9\. Using bullock constantly as a vehicle through wild jungles, Uccātana (exorcising) and Abhicāra (black magic) acceptance of grains as gift, working as a physician.

## \|\| 9.6.10 \|\|

*jihvākāmopabhogārthaṃ yasyāraṃbhaḥ sukarmasu \|*  
*mūlenakhyāpako nityaṃ vedajñānādikaṃ ca yat \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Engagement in sacred rites to satisfy the palate or the sexual urge, teaching only the text of the Vedas (not the meaning).

## \|\| 9.6.11 \|\|

*brāhmyādivratasaṃtyāgaścānyācāraniṣevaṇam \|*  
*asacchāstrādhigamanaṃ śuṣkatarkāvalambanam \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Eschewing of Brahma and other Vratas and adoption of the modes and customs, learning of spurious holy lore, engagement in hair-splitting arguments.

## \|\| 9.6.12 \|\|

*devāgnigurusādhūnāṃ nindayā brāhmaṇasya ca \|*  
*pratyakṣaṃ vā parokṣaṃ vā rājñāṃ maṇḍalināmapi \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. Censuring gods, fire-god, preceptors and virtuous men openly and the kings and his officers indirectly.

## \|\| 9.6.13 \|\|

*utsannapitṛdevejyā svakarmmatyāginaśca ye \|*  
*duḥśīlā nāstikāḥ pāpāssadā vā'satyavādinaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. Those who have ceased to perform sacrifices to the gods and the manes, those who have abandoned their duties and rites, persons of evil conduct, atheists, sinners, and habitual liars.

## \|\| 9.6.14 \|\|

*parvakāle divā vāpsu viyonau paśuyoniṣu \|*  
*rajasvalāyā yonau ca maithunaṃ yaḥ samācaret \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. He who indulges in sexual intercourse during new moon and Tull moon days during day time, or in the vaginal passages of animals, or through other passages or emits semen in water, or cohabits with a woman in her monthly course.

## \|\| 9.6.15 \|\|

*strīputramitrasaṃprāptāvāśācchedakarāśca ye \|*  
*janasyāpriya vaktāraḥ krūrā samayavedinaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. Those who shatter the hopes of obtaining wives, sons and friends; those who speak displeasing words; ruthless persons and those who break agreements.

## \|\| 9.6.16 \|\|

*bhettā taḍāgakūpānāṃ saṃkrayāṇāṃ rasasya ca \|*  
*ekapaṃktisthitānāṃ ca pākabhedaṃ karoti yaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. Those who damage or demolish lakes, wells or water causeways; he who serves different kinds of food among persons sitting in the same row.

## \|\| 9.6.17 \|\|

*ityetaiḥ strīnarāḥ pāpairupapātakinaḥ smṛtāḥ \|*  
*yuktā ebhistathānye'pi śṛṇu tāṃstu bravīmi te \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. The foregoing are some of the minor sins. Men or women guilty of the same are minor sinners, I shall mention some others also. Listen.

## \|\| 9.6.18 \|\|

*ye gobrāhmaṇakanyānāṃ svāmimitratapasvinām \|*  
*vināśayaṃti kāryyāṇi te narā nārakāḥ smṛtāḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. Those who cause injury to cows, brahmins, virgins, master, friends or ascetics are sure to go to hell.

## \|\| 9.6.19 \|\|

*parastriyābhitapyaṃte ye paradravyasūcakāḥ \|*  
*paradravyaharā nityaṃ taulamithyānusārakāḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. Those who undergo agony due to another man’s wife; those who have an eye on another man’s wealth; those who steal that and those who make use of false weights.

## \|\| 9.6.20 \|\|

*dvijaduḥkhakarā ye ca prahāraṃ coddharaṃti ye \|*  
*sevante tu dvijāśśūdrāṃ surāṃ badhnaṃti kāmataḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. Those who cause misery to brahmins by means of kicks and blows; the brahmins who serve Śūdra women and drink wine due to passion.

## \|\| 9.6.21 \|\|

*ye pāpaniratāḥ krūrāḥ ye'pi hiṃsāpriyā narāḥ \|*  
*vṛttyarthaṃ ye'pi kurvaṃti dānayajñādikāḥ kriyāḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. Those who are cruel and engaged in sinful acts; those who are fond of violence and those who perform Dāna, Yajña and other rites as a profession for livelihood.

## \|\| 9.6.22 \|\|

*goṣṭhāgnijalarathyāsu tarucchāyā nageṣu ca \|*  
*tyajaṃti ye purīṣādyānārāmāyataneṣu ca \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. Those who evacuate their bowels in cowpens, streets, near water and fire, shades of trees, mountains, parks and temples.

## \|\| 9.6.23 \|\|

*lajjāśramaprāsādeṣu mayapānaratāśca ye \|*  
*kṛtakelibhujaṃgāśca randhrānveṣaṇatatparāḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. Those who are engaged in drinking bouts in hermitages and palaces; those who are in search of weak points in others; those who are in association with others.

## \|\| 9.6.24 \|\|

*vaṃśeṣṭakā śilākāṣṭhaiḥ śṛṅgaiśśaṃkubhireva ca \|*  
*ye mārgamanuruṃdhaṃti parasīmāṃ haraṃti ye \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. Those who block roads by means of bamboos, bricks, logs of wood, horns or poles and those who violate the boundaries of others’ fields.

## \|\| 9.6.25 \|\|

*kūṭaśāsanakartāraḥ kūṭakarmakriyāratāḥ \|*  
*kūṭapākānnavastrāṇāṃ kūṭasaṃvyavahāriṇaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. Those who make counterfeit documents, those who are engaged in fraudulent activities, those who indulge in fraud in dealing in food and clothing and in law suits.

## \|\| 9.6.26 \|\|

*dhanuṣaḥ śastraśalyānāṃ kartā yaḥ krayavikrayī \|*  
*nirddayo'tīvabhṛtyeṣu paśūnāṃ damanaśca yaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. The maker, buyer and seller of bows, weapons and darts, he who is merciless to servants and he who ill-treats animals.

## \|\| 9.6.27 \|\|

*mithyā pravadato vāca ākarṇayati yaśśanaiḥ \|*  
*svāmimitragurudrohī māyāvī capalaśśaṭhaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. He who listens slowly to the words of liars, who is traitor to masters, friends and teachers, a cheat, a fickle-minded and a rogue

## \|\| 9.6.28 \|\|

*ye bhāryyāputramitrāṇi bālavṛddhakṛśāturān \|*  
*bhṛtyānatithibaṃdhūṃśca tyaktvāśnaṃti bubhukṣitān \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. Those who leave their wives, sons, friends, children, the aged, lean and sick persons, servants, guests and kinsmen hungry but take food themselves.

## \|\| 9.6.29 \|\|

*yaḥ svayaṃ miṣṭamaśnāti viprebhyo na prayacchati \|*  
*vṛthāpākassa vijñeyo brahmavādiṣu garhitaḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. He who sumptuously feeds himself on delicacies but does not give anything to brahmins, shall be known as Vṛthāpāka (a man of fruitless cooking). He is despised by those who propound Brahman.

## \|\| 9.6.30 \|\|

*niyamānsvayamādāya ye tyajaṃtyajitendriyāḥ \|*  
*pravrajyāvāsitā ye ca harasyāsyaprabhedakāḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. Those who voluntarily decide to perform certain rites with self-imposed checks and restraints but leave them off because they have not conquered their sense-organs; those who renounce but again come back to householder’s life; breakers of the idols of Śiva.

## \|\| 9.6.31 \|\|

*ye tāḍayaṃti gāṃ krūrā damayaṃte muhurmuhuḥ \|*  
*durbalānye na puṣṇaṃti satataṃ ye tyajaṃti ca \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. Cruel persons who beat cows and bulls; suppress them; do not feed them properly and let them alone weak and feeble.

## \|\| 9.6.32 \|\|

*pīḍayaṃtyatibhāreṇā'sahaṃtaṃ vāhayaṃti ca \|*  
*yojayannakṛtāhārānna vimuṃcaṃti saṃyatān \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. Those who ill-treat bullocks with weighty burden; those who make them draw heavily-laden carts; those who do not let them off free for leisure.

## \|\| 9.6.33 \|\|

*ye bhārakṣatarogārtāngovṛṣāṃśca kṣudhāturān \|*  
*na pālayaṃti yatnena goghnāste nārakāssmṛtāḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. Those who do not rear cows and bullocks properly, let them starve; ill treat them with heavy burdens; do not treat their wounds and bruises are called the killers of cows. They are sure to fall into hell.

## \|\| 9.6.34 \|\|

*vṛṣāṇāṃ vṛṣaṇānye ca pāpiṣṭhā gālayaṃti ca \|*  
*vāhayaṃti ca gāṃ vaṃdhyāṃ mahānārakino narāḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. The most sinful persons who castrate bulls by squeezing out their scrotum and those who make heifers draw carts are great sinners sure to fall into hell.

## \|\| 9.6.35-36 \|\|

*āśayā samanuprāptānkṣuttṛṣṇāśramakarśitān \|*  
*atithīṃśca tathānāthānsvatantrā gṛhamāgatān \|\| 35 \|\|*

*annābhilāṣāndīnānvā bālavṛddhakṛśāturān \|*  
*nānukaṃpaṃti ye mūḍhāste yāṃti narakārṇavam \|\| 36 \|\|*

35-36. Those fools who do not take pity oft guests, helpless persons, independent casual guests, children, old men, emaciated and sick persons overwhelmed by hunger, thirst and weariness and desirous of food surely go to hell.

## \|\| 9.6.37 \|\|

*gṛheṣvarthā nivartante smaśānādapi bāṃdhavāḥ \|*  
*sukṛtaṃ duṣkṛtaṃ caiva gacchaṃtamanugacchati \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. The assets of a man take leave of him at the house itself as the kinsmen do at the cremation ground but his merits and sins follow wherever he goes.

## \|\| 9.6.38 \|\|

*ajāviko māhiṣikassāmudro vṛṣalīpatiḥ \|*  
*śūdravatkṣatravṛttiśca nārakī syād dvijādhamaḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. The base brahmin who rears goats, sheep and buffaloes and who marries a Śūdra woman and who lives on fishing etc. is a Śudra. If he follows the occupation of a Kṣattriya he is sure to go to hell.

## \|\| 9.6.39 \|\|

*śilpinaḥ kāravo vaidyā hemakārā nṛpadhvajāḥ \|*  
*bhṛtakā kūṭasaṃyuktāḥ sarve te nārakāḥ smṛtāḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. Sculptors, blacksmiths, physicians, goldsmiths and royal pretenders and deceitful servants are sure to go to hell.

## \|\| 9.6.40 \|\|

*yaścocitamatikramya svecchayai vāharetkaram \|*  
*narake pacyate so'pi yopi daṇḍarucirnaraḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. The king who imposes improper taxes out of his own will and takes undue delight in punishment is tortured in hell.

## \|\| 9.6.41 \|\|

*utkocakai rucikrītaistaskaraiśca prapīḍyate \|*  
*yasya rājñaḥ prajā rāṣṭre pacyate narakeṣu saḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. The king whose subjects are harassed in the following way is tortured in hell. The way they are harassed may be due to bribery, favouritism of the officers and robbery.

## \|\| 9.6.42 \|\|

*ye dvijāḥ parigṛhṇaṃti nṛpasyānyāyavartinaḥ \|*  
*te prayāṃti tu ghoreṣu narakeṣu na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. There is no doubt that the brahmins who take monetary gifts from an unjust king fall into terrible hells.

## \|\| 9.6.43 \|\|

*anyāyātsamupādāya dvijebhyo yaḥ prayacchati \|*  
*prajābhyaḥ pacyate so'pi narakeṣu nṛpo yathā \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. The king who confiscates the properties of brahmins unjustly and passes them on to others certainly falls into hell.

## \|\| 9.6.44 \|\|

*pāradārikacaurāṇāṃ caṃḍānāṃ vidyate tvagham \|*  
*paradāraratasyāpi rājño bhavati nityaśaḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. Sin accrues to the fierce robbers ānd to those cohabiting with other men’s wives. It accrues to the king who indulges in amorous sports with other’s women.

## \|\| 9.6.45 \|\|

*acauraṃ cauravatpaśyeccauraṃ vācaurarūpiṇam \|*  
*avicārya nṛpastasmāddhātayannarakaṃ vrajet \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. If the king does not discriminate between a nonthief and a thief and kills the wrong person he is sure to fall in hell.

## \|\| 9.6.46-49 \|\|

*ghṛtatailānnapānāni madhumāṃsasurāsavam \|*  
*guḍekṣuśākadugdhāni dadhimūlaphalāni ca \|\| 46 \|\|*

*tṛṇaṃ kāṣṭhaṃ patrapuṣpamauṣadhaṃ cātmabhojanam \|*  
*upānatchatraśakaṭamāsanaṃ ca kamaṃḍalum \|\| 47 \|\|*

*tāmrasīsatrapuḥ śastraṃ śaṃkhādyaṃ ca jalodbhavam \|*  
*vaidyaṃ ca vaiṇavaṃ cānyadgṛhopaskaraṇāni ca \|\| 48 \|\|*

*aurṇṇakārpāsakauśeyapaṭṭasūtrodbhavāni ca \|*  
*sthūlasūkṣmāṇi vastrāṇi ye lobhāddhi haraṃti ca \|\| 49 \|\|*

46-49. If people steal even a small quantity of these things they will fall into hell—ghee, oil, food and drink, honey, flesh, wine, toddy, jaggery, sugarcane, vegetables, milk, curds, roots, fruits, grass, firewood, leaves, flowers, medicine, shoes, umbrella, cart, seat, water-pot, copper, tin, lead, weapon, conch, and aquatic products, medicinal concoctions, bamboo, household articles, etc. Those who steal coarse or fine clothes out of greed fall into hell.

## \|\| 9.6.50 \|\|

*evamādīni cānyāni dravyāṇi vividhāni ca \|*  
*narakeṣu dhruvaṃ yānti cāpahṛtyālpakāni ca \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. There are other similar things the stealing of which even in small quantities causes fall into hell.

## \|\| 9.6.51 \|\|

*tadvā yadvā paradravyamapi sarṣapamātrakam \|*  
*apahṛtya narā yāṃti narakaṃ nātra saṃśayaḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

51\. There is no doubt that stealing another man’s possession, whatever it is, be it of the size of mustard seed, causes fall into hell.

## \|\| 9.6.52 \|\|

*evamādyairnaraḥ pāpairutkrāṃtisamanaṃtaram \|*  
*śarīrayātanārthāya sarvākāramavāpnuyāt \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. By these sins the man after his death is born as a serpent for suffering the tortures.

## \|\| 9.6.53 \|\|

*yamalokaṃ vrajaṃtyete śarīreṇa yamājñayā \|*  
*yamadūtairmahāghorainīyamānāssuduḥkhitāḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. At the behest of Yama the sinners go to the world of Yama in their bodies, which are dragged by Yama’s terrible emissaries and are subjected to grief.

## \|\| 9.6.54 \|\|

*devatiryaṅmanuṣyāṇāmadharmaniratātmanām \|*  
*dharmarājaḥ smṛtaśśāstā sughorairvividhairvadhaiḥ \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. Yama is the chastiser of all gods, human beings and animals engaged in evil practices. He chastises them by inflicting diverse terrible punishments.

## \|\| 9.6.55 \|\|

*niyamācārayuktānāṃ pramādātskhalitātmanām \|*  
*prāyaścittairguruśśāstā na budhairiṣyate yamaḥ \|\| 55 \|\|*

55\. For those who regularly observe restraints and disciplined life but who err slightly due to oversight, the preceptor is the chastiser through expiatory rites and not Yama as explained by learned men.

## \|\| 9.6.56 \|\|

*pāradārikacaurāṇāmanyāyavyavahāriṇām \|*  
*nṛpatiśśāsakaḥ proktaḥ pracchannānāṃ sa dharmmarāṭ \|\| 56 \|\|*

56\. The king is the chastiser for outragers of others’ wives and thieves and unjust persons. But for those who are hidden Yama is the chastiser.

## \|\| 9.6.57 \|\|

*tasmātkṛtasya pāpasya prāyaścittaṃ samācaret \|*  
*nābhuktasyānyathānāśaḥ kalpakoṭiśatairapi \|\| 57 \|\|*

57\. Hence one should perform expiatory rites for the commission of sins. If the sin is not consumed it will not perish even in hundreds of crores of Kalpas.

## \|\| 9.6.58 \|\|

*yaḥ karoti svayaṃ karmma kārayeccānumodayet \|*  
*kāyena manasā vācā tasya pāpagatiḥ phalam \|\| 58 \|\|*

58\. If the action is by oneself or through one’s incitement or instigation or if it is applauded by one later, physically, mentally or verbally he reaps the fruit of the sin.

## Chapter 7 - Pathway to Hell and the Emissaries of Yama

## \|\| 9.7.1 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*atha pāpairnarā yāṃti yamalokaṃ caturvidhaiḥ \|*  
*saṃtrāsajananaṃ ghoraṃ vivaśāssarvadehinaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

1\. As a result of the four kinds of sins, all living beings go to Yamaloka. They are helpless.

## \|\| 9.7.2 \|\|

*garbhasthairjāyamānaiśca bālaistaruṇamadhyamaiḥ \|*  
*strīpunnapuṃsakairjīvairjñātavyaṃ sarvajaṃtuṣu \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. It shall be known to all living beings whether in the womb, in the process of birth, as children, youth or middling whether women, man or a eunuch.

## \|\| 9.7.3 \|\|

*śubhāśubhaphalaṃ cātra dehināṃ saṃvicāryate \|*  
*citraguptādibhissarvairvasiṣṭhapramukhaistathā \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. Here the result of the auspicious and the inauspicious actions of all embodied beings is being considered by Citragupta, Vasiṣṭha and others.

## \|\| 9.7.4 \|\|

*na kecitprāṇinassaṃti ye na yāṃti yamakṣayam \|*  
*avaśyaṃ hi kṛtaṃ karma bhoktavyaṃ tadvicāryyatām \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. There are no living beings who do not go to Yama’s abode. The result of the action must of necessity be undergone. Let that be considered.

## \|\| 9.7.5 \|\|

*tatra ye śubhakarmāṇassaumyacittā dayānvitāḥ \|*  
*te narā yāṃti saumyena pūrvaṃ yamaniketanam \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Men who have performed auspicious rites who are of gentle minds, endowed with the quality of compassion, go to Yama’s abode through the gentle entrance at the East.

## \|\| 9.7.6 \|\|

*ye punaḥ pāpakarmmāṇaḥ pāpā dānavivarjitāḥ \|*  
*te ghoreṇa pathā yāṃti dakṣiṇena yamālayam \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. Sinners, habitually performing sinful actions and devoid of charitable nature, pass through the terrible path and enter Yama’s abode by the southern gate.

## \|\| 9.7.7 \|\|

*ṣaḍaśītisahasrāṇi yojanānāmatītya tat \|*  
*vaivasvatapuraṃ jñeyaṃ nānārūpamavasthitam \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. It must be known that the multiformed city of Yama is situated at a distance of eighty-six thousand Yojanas from the Earth.

## \|\| 9.7.8 \|\|

*samīpasthamivābhāti narāṇāṃ puṇyakarmaṇām \|*  
*pāpināmatidūrasthaṃ pathā raudreṇa gacchatām \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. To men of auspicious deeds it appears to be very near, but to the sinners who go along the terrible path it is situated far off.

## \|\| 9.7.9 \|\|

*tīkṣṇakaṃṭakayuktena śarkarāvicitena ca \|*  
*kṣuradhārānibhaistīkṣṇaiḥ pāṣāṇai racitena ca \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. At places the path is strewn with sharp thorns, at places it is full of sand, elsewhere it is full of pebbles sharp like the razor-edge.

## \|\| 9.7.10 \|\|

*kvacitpaṃkena mahatā urutokaiśca pātakaiḥ \|*  
*lohasūcīnibhairdarbhaissampannena pathā kvacit \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Somewhere the place is marshy, somewhere full of long or short Darbha grass like iron pins that split the legs.

## \|\| 9.7.11 \|\|

*taṭaprāyātiviṣamaiḥ parvatairvṛkṣasaṃkulaiḥ \|*  
*prataptāṃgārayuktena yāṃti mārgeṇa duḥkhitāḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Elsewhere it is full of mountains overgrown with trees and resembling impassable bunds. The distressd persons go along the path full of glowing coal.

## \|\| 9.7.12 \|\|

*kvacidviṣamagartaiśca kvacilloṣṭaissuduṣkaraiḥ \|*  
*sutaptavālukābhiśca tathā tīkṣṇaiśca śaṃkubhiḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. In other places it is full of deep irregular chasms and canyons, elsewhere, of rugged lumps of clay; of burning sands here and sharp spikes there.

## \|\| 9.7.13 \|\|

*aneka śākhāvitatairvyāptaṃ vaṃśavanaiḥ kvacit \|*  
*kaṣṭena tamasā mārge nānālambena kutracit \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. Somewhere it is pervaded by bamboo groves with many branches spreading, elsewhere the path is enveloped in darkness, in some places (he path has no support at all.

## \|\| 9.7.14 \|\|

*ayaśśṛṃgāṭakaistīkṣṇaiḥ kvaciddāvāgninā punaḥ \|*  
*kvacittaptaśilābhiśca kvacidvyāptaṃ himena ca \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. Somewhere the cross-roads are full of sharp iron pieces, elsewhere there is a forest-fire; in other places these are hot rocks; in some places snow spreads over it.

## \|\| 9.7.15 \|\|

*kvacidvālukayā vyāptamākaṃṭhāṃtaḥ praveśayā \|*  
*kvacidduṣṭāmbunā vyāptaṃ kvacicca kariṣāgninā \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. Some places are full of fine sand where the persons sink up to the neck; elsewhere it is full of stinking muddy water and some places are covered with burning balls of dry cowdung.

## \|\| 9.7.16 \|\|

*kvacitsiṃhairvṛkairvyāghrairmaśakaiśca sudāruṇaiḥ \|*  
*kvacinmahājalaukābhiḥ kvaciccājagaraistathā \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. Different places are infested with different terrible beasts of prey such as lions, wolves, tigers and huge pythons or terrible mosquitoes or huge leeches.

## \|\| 9.7.17 \|\|

*makṣikābhiśca raudrābhiḥ kvacitsarpairviṣolbaṇaiḥ \|*  
*mattamātaṃgayūthaiśca balonmattaiḥ pramāthibhiḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. Terrible flics, extremely poisonous serpents, herds of elephants in rut mad and mighty, crushing everything under their feet infest the paths.

## \|\| 9.7.18-19 \|\|

*paṃthānamullikhadbhiśca sūkaraistīkṣṇadaṃṣṭribhiḥ \|*  
*tīkṣṇaśṛṃgaiśca mahiṣaissarvabhūtaiśca śvāpadaiḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

*ḍākinībhiśca raudrābhirvikarālaiśca rākṣasaiḥ \|*  
*vyādhibhiśca mahāghoraiḥ pīḍyamānā vrajaṃti hi \|\| 19 \|\|*

18-19. The persons who go that way are tortured and harassed by big boars digging and butting against the path with their sharp fangs, buffaloes with sharp horns, all sorts of beasts of prey, terrible evil spirits like Dākinīs, horrible Rākṣasas and pernicious diseases.

## \|\| 9.7.20 \|\|

*mahādhūlivimiśreṇa mahācaṇḍena vāyunā \|*  
*mahāpāṣāṇavarṣeṇa hanyamānā nirāśrayāḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. They are without any shelter when fierce gusts of wind raising huge columns of dust blow sharply against them or showers of massive stones smother them.

## \|\| 9.7.21 \|\|

*kvacidvidyutprapātena dahyamānā vrajanti ca \|*  
*mahatā bāṇavarṣeṇa vidhyamānāśca sarvataḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. They go on, burnt and scorched by lightning falls and pierced through by heavy showers of arrows.

## \|\| 9.7.22 \|\|

*patadbhirvajrapātaiśca ulkāpātaiśca dāruṇaiḥ \|*  
*pradīptāṃgāravarṣeṇa dahyamānāśca saṃti hi \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. They are scorched and burnt by the showers of burning coal, the falling terrible thunderbolts and meteors.

## \|\| 9.7.23 \|\|

*mahatā pāṃsuvarṣeṇa pūryamāṇā rudaṃti ca \|*  
*mahāmegharavairghoraistrasyaṃte ca muhurmuhuḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. They cry when heavy showers of dust envelop them. They tremble with fear ever and anon at the terrible rumbling sounds of massive clouds.

## \|\| 9.7.24 \|\|

*niśitāyudhavarṣeṇa bhidyamānāśca sarvataḥ \|*  
*mahākṣārāmbudhārābhissicyamānā vrajaṃti ca \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. They are split by the shower of sharp weapons and drenched with acid-currents as they go on.

## \|\| 9.7.25 \|\|

*mahīśītena marutā rūkṣeṇa paruṣeṇa ca \|*  
*samaṃtādbādhyamānāśca śuṣyaṃte saṃkucanti ca \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. They shrink and wither when oppressed by the rough and chill wind all round.

## \|\| 9.7.26-28 \|\|

*itthaṃ mārgeṇa raudreṇa pātheyarahitena ca \|*  
*nirālambena durgeṇa nirjalena samaṃtataḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

*viṣameṇaiva mahatā nirjanāpāśrayeṇa ca \|*  
*tamorūpeṇa kaṣṭena sarvaduṣṭāśrayeṇa ca \|\| 27 \|\|*

*nīyaṃte dehinassarve ye mūḍhāḥ pāpakarmiṇaḥ \|*  
*yamadūtairmahāghoraistadājñākāribhirbalāt \|\| 28 \|\|*

26-28. All those foolish persons who habitually sin are led through such paths by the terrible emissaries of Yama forcefully, in carrying out his behests.—the paths that are terrible, devoid of feeding, devoid of basic support, impassable, lacking in water, rugged, desolate, dark and gloomy and full of pain and misery and all sorts of evil things.

## \|\| 9.7.29 \|\|

*ekākinaḥ parādhīnā mitrabandhuvivarjitāḥ \|*  
*śocaṃtassvāni karmmāṇi rudaṃtaśca muhurmuhuḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. They are lonely, devoid of friends and relatives. They are dependent on others. They bewail their evil actions. They cry again and again.

## \|\| 9.7.30 \|\|

*pretā bhūtvā vivastrāśca śuṣkakaṃṭhauṣṭhatālukāḥ \|*  
*asaumyā bhayabhītāśca dahyamānāḥ kṣudhānvitāḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. They have by this time become ghosts. They have no cloth on. Their throats, lips and palates are parched. They are frightened of terror. They are hungry and feel a burning sensation all over.

## \|\| 9.7.31 \|\|

*baddhāśśṛṃkhalayā keciduttā napādakā narāḥ \|*  
*kṛṣyaṃte kṛṣyamāṇāśca yamadūtairbalotkaṭaiḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. Some are bound with fetters and forced to keep their legs lifted up. They are dragged again and again by the extraordinarily powerful emissaries of Yama.

## \|\| 9.7.32 \|\|

*urasādhomukhāścānye ghṛṣyamāṇāssuduḥkhitāḥ \|*  
*keśapāśani baṃdhena saṃskṛṣyaṃte ca rajjunā \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. Others with downcast faces are against the chest. They are distressed. They are dragged with a rope fastened to their tresses.

## \|\| 9.7.33 \|\|

*lalāṭe cāṃkuśenānye bhinnā duṣyaṃti dehinaḥ \|*  
*uttānāḥ kaṃṭakapathā kvacidaṃgāravartmanā \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. Men in supine position are dragged along the thorny path or that covered with burning coal, by means of a goad clutching at their foreheads.

## \|\| 9.7.34 \|\|

*paścādbāhunibaddhāśca jaṭhareṇa prapīḍitāḥ \|*  
*pūritāśśṛṃkhalābhiśca hastayośca sukīlitāḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. The hands of some are tied behind and they are hit in the belly. Others are completely bound with iron fetters. Still others are nailed in their hands.

## \|\| 9.7.35 \|\|

*grīvāpāśena kṛṣyaṃte prayāṃtyanye suduḥkhitāḥ \|*  
*jihvāṃkuśapraveśena rajjvākṛṣyanta eva te \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. A few others are dragged with a noose tied round their necks. They go ahead in distress. Others are dragged by ropes. Their tongues are pierced through by goads.

## \|\| 9.7.36 \|\|

*nāsābhedena rajjvā ca tvākṛśyante tathāpare \|*  
*bhinnāḥ kapolayo rajjvākṛṣyaṃte'nye tathauṣṭhayoḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. The noses of others are bored and ropes are tied through them and they are dragged. Similarly others have their cheeks and lips bored and are dragged by ropes.

## \|\| 9.7.37 \|\|

*chinnāgrapādahastāśca cchinnakarṇoṣṭhanāsikāḥ \|*  
*saṃchinnaśiśnavṛṣaṇāḥ chinnabhinnāṃgasaṃdhayaḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. The tips of the hands and the legs of some are cut off. The ears, noses and lips of others are cut. The penis and the scrotum of some are cut. A few others have all their limbs and joints cut.

## \|\| 9.7.38 \|\|

*ābhidyamānāḥ kuṃtaiśca bhidyamānāśca sāyakaiḥ \|*  
*itaścetaśca dhāvaṃtaḥ kraṃdamānā nirāśrayāḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. Pushed, pierced and thrust by spears and arrows some run about helplessly here and there shrieking and squealing.

## \|\| 9.7.39-40 \|\|

*mudgarairlohadaṇḍaiśca hanyamānā muhurmuhuḥ \|*  
*kaṃṭakairvividhairghorairjvalanārkasamaprabhaiḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

*bhindipālairvibhiyaṃte sravataḥ pūyaśoṇitam \|*  
*śakṛtā kṛmidigdhāśca nīyaṃte vivaśā narāḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

39-40. Hit and thrashed by iron clubs and rods, bruised by terrible thorns of various sorts, luminous like fire and sun and pierced through by javelins, some men shed putrid blood or evacuate faeces infested with worms. They are taken ahead thus.

## \|\| 9.7.41-42 \|\|

*yācamānāśca salilamannaṃ vāpi bubhukṣitāḥ \|*  
*chāyāṃ prārthayamānāśca śītārtāścānalaṃ punaḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

*dānahīnāḥ prayāṃtyevaṃ prārthayaṃtassukhaṃ narāḥ \|*  
*gṛhītadāna pātheyāssukhaṃ yāṃti yamālayam \|\| 42 \|\|*

41-42. Those who had not made any charitable gifts in the world feel thirsty along this path and vainly beg for water, feeling hungry they beg for food; oppressed by sunshine they beg for shade and distressed with dullness they request for fire. They vainly beg for happiness. But those who had made charitable gifts in the world have all the food and drink necessary for this journey and go ahead to Yama’s abode happily.

## \|\| 9.7.43 \|\|

*evaṃ nyāyena kaṣṭena prāptāḥ pretapuraṃ yadā \|*  
*prajñāpitāstato dūtairniveśyaṃte yamāgrataḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. Having thus traversed the path they finally reach the city of the dead with great hardship. They are then ushered into the presence of Yama by the emissaries after due announcement.

## \|\| 9.7.44 \|\|

*tatra ye śubhakarmmāṇastāṃstu sammānayedyamaḥ \|*  
*svāgatāsanadānena pādyārghyeṇa priyeṇa ca \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. Yama welcomes with pleasure and due honour, all those who had performed auspicious rites in this world. He offers them seat, pādya and Arghya.

## \|\| 9.7.45 \|\|

*dhanyā yūyaṃ mahātmāno nigamoditakāriṇaḥ \|*  
*yaiśca divyasukhārthāya bhavadbhissukṛtaṃ kṛtam \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. Yama tells them—“You are noble souls duly blessed, since you have performed what is ordained in the Vedas. Good deeds that are conducive to divine happiness have been performed by you.

## \|\| 9.7.46 \|\|

*divyaṃ vimānamāruhya divyastrībhogabhūṣitam \|*  
*svargaṃ gacchadhvamamalaṃ sarvakāmasamanvitam \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. Ascend the celestial aerial chariot and go to heaven to enjoy the pleasures in the company of celestial damsels and fulfil your cherished desires.

## \|\| 9.7.47 \|\|

*tatra bhuktvā mahābhogānaṃte puṇyasya saṃkṣayāt \|*  
*yatkiṃcidalpamaśubhaṃ punastadiha bhokṣyatha \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. After enjoying pleasures there, in the end when the merit is exhausted return to this place for reaping the fruit of what little evil you may have committed.”

## \|\| 9.7.48 \|\|

*dharmmātmāno narā ye ca mitrabhūtvā ivātmanaḥ \|*  
*saumyaṃ sukhaṃ prapaśyaṃti dharmarājatvameva ca \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. Men who have been virtuous are treated as friends by Yama. They see Yama with a gentle face.

## \|\| 9.7.49-53 \|\|

*ye punaḥ krūrakarmmāṇaste paśyaṃti bhayānakam \|*  
*daṃṣṭrākarālavadanaṃ bhṛkuṭīkuṭilekṣaṇam \|\| 49 \|\|*

*ūrdhvakeśaṃ mahāśmaśrumūrdhvaprasphuritādharam \|*  
*aṣṭādaśabhujaṃ kruddhaṃ nīlāṃjanacayopamam \|\| 50 \|\|*

*sarvāyudhoddhatakaraṃ sarvadaṇḍena tarjayan \|*  
*mahāmahiṣamārūḍhaṃ dīptāgnisamalocanam \|\| 51 \|\|*

*raktamālyāṃbaradharaṃ mahāmerumivocchritam \|*  
*pralayāmbudanirghoṣaṃ pibanniva mahodadhim \|\| 52 \|\|*

*grasaṃtamiva śailendramudgiraṃtamivānalam \|*  
*mṛtyuścaiva samīpasthaḥ kālānalasamaprabhuḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

49-53. Men who have been guilty of cruelties sec him in a terrible form. His face is terrible with curved fangs. His eyes are cruel with knit eyebrows. The hair on his head stand lifted up. He has a big moustache. His lips are pouted and they throb. He has eighteen hands. He is furious. He resembles black collyrium. His uplifted hands hold all weapons. He threatens with punishment. He is seated on a great buffalo. His eyes resemble blazing fire. He wears red garlands and garments. He is as tall as the mountain Mahā Meru. His voice resembles the rumbling sound of the clouds at the time of dissolution. He appears ready to drink up even the big ocean.

## \|\| 9.7.53 \|\|

*grasaṃtamiva śailendramudgiraṃtamivānalam \|*  
*mṛtyuścaiva samīpasthaḥ kālānalasamaprabhuḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. He appears ready to swallow even great mountains. He appears to vomit fire. Very near him is Mṛtyu, whose lustre is like that of black fire.

## \|\| 9.7.54 \|\|

*kālaścāṃjanasaṃkāśaḥ kṛtāṃtaśca bhayānakaḥ \|*  
*mārīcogramahāmārī kālarātriśca dāruṇā \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. Black in colour resembling collyrium he is very terrifying. Mārī, Ugramahāmārī and Kālarātri are terrible spirits attending on Yama.

## \|\| 9.7.55 \|\|

*vividhā vyādhayaḥ kuṣṭhā nānārūpā bhayāvahāḥ \|*  
*śaktiśūlāṃkuśadharāḥ pāśacakrāsipāṇayaḥ \|\| 55 \|\|*

55 Various ailments, leprosies of various forms are terrible. They hold Śaktis, tridents, goads, nooses, discuses and swords in their hands.

## \|\| 9.7.56 \|\|

*vajatuṃḍadharā rudrā kṣuratūṇadhanurddharāḥ \|*  
*nānāyudhadharāssarve mahāvīrā bhayaṃkarāḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

56\. All of them are heroic and terrible. They have curved snouts. They bear shafts, quivers and bows and weapons of various sorts.

## \|\| 9.7.57 \|\|

*asaṃkhyātā mahāvīrāḥ kālāñjanasamaprabhāḥ \|*  
*sarvāyudhodyatakarā yamadūtā bhayānakāḥ \|\| 57 \|\|*

57\. The attendants of Yama are innumerable. They are great heroes. They have complexions like the black collyrium. They appear terrifying with weapons lifted up.

## \|\| 9.7.58 \|\|

*anena paricāreṇa vṛtaṃ taṃ ghoradarśanam \|*  
*yamaṃ paśyaṃti pāpiṣṭhāścitraguptaṃ ca bhīṣaṇam \|\| 58 \|\|*

58\. The sinners see Yama terrific to behold, surrounded by his attendants and they see Citragupta too equally terrible.

## \|\| 9.7.59 \|\|

*nirbhartsayati cātyaṃtaṃ yamastānpāpakarmmaṇaḥ \|*  
*citraguptaśca bhagavāndharmmavākyaiḥ prabodhayet \|\| 59 \|\|*

59\. Yama rebukes and reproaches the sinners. Lord Citragupta enlightens them with statements on virtue.

## Chapter 8 - Description of the Hell (naraka)

## \|\| 9.8.1-2 \|\|

*citragupta uvāca \|*  
*bho bho duṣkṛtakarmmāṇaḥ para dravyāpahārakāḥ \|*  
*garvitā rūpavīryeṇa paradārāvamarddakāḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

*yastvayaṃ kriyate karma tadidaṃ bhujyate punaḥ \|*  
*tatkimātmopaghātārthaṃ bhavadbhirduṣkṛtaṃ kṛtam \|\| 2 \|\|*

Note: The chapter mentions several hells (naraka) and their cells to each of which it assigns a group of five emmissaries who look after the tortures inflicted upon the wicked in accordance with their sins. The Purāṇas vary greatly as to the number and names of the hells. Manu mentions twenty-one hells only.

Citragupta said:—

1-2. O ye sinners, of evil actions, stealers of other man’s riches, outragers of the modesty of other’s wives, arrogant by your comeliness and might, why was sin committed by you all, for your own ruination? The actions committed by you shall be endured and the fruits thereof shall be reaped.

## \|\| 9.8.3 \|\|

*idānīṃ kiṃ pralapyadhve pīḍyamānāssvakarmabhiḥ \|*  
*bhujyaṃtāṃ svāni karmmāṇi nāsti doṣo hi kasyacit \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. Why do you lament now? You are being tortured by your own actions. Reap the fruits thereof. This is not the fault of anyone else.

## \|\| 9.8.4-5 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*evaṃ te pṛthivīpālāssaṃprāptāstatsamīpataḥ \|*  
*svakīyaiḥ karmmabhighaurairduṣkarmmabaladarpiṇaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

*tānapi krodhasaṃyuktaścitragupto mahāprabhuḥ \|*  
*saṃśikṣayati dharmajño yamarājānuśikṣayā \|\| 5 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

4-5. Similarly, the infuriated great lord Citragupta, the knower of virtuousness, at the behest and instruction of Yamarāja imparts the following advice to the kings who proud of their might had committed many misdeeds and had been brought near him ultimately.

## \|\| 9.8.6 \|\|

*citragupta uvāca \|*  
*bho bho nṛpā durācārāḥ prajā vidhvaṃsakāriṇaḥ \|*  
*alpakālasya rājyasya kṛte kiṃ duṣkṛtaṃ kṛtam \|\| 6 \|\|*

Citragupta said:—

6\. O ye wicked kings, who had tyrannised over and repressed your subjects, why were misdeeds committed for your rule of a very short duration?

## \|\| 9.8.7 \|\|

*rājyabhogena mohena balādanyāyataḥ prajāḥ \|*  
*yaddaṇḍitāḥ phalaṃ tasya bhujyatāmadhunā nṛpāḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. O kings, let the fruits be reaped now (of the evil actions) in having punished your subjects unjusticiably due to your fascination for kingly pleasures. You have misused your might.

## \|\| 9.8.8 \|\|

*kva tadrājyaṃ kalatraṃ ca yadarthamaśubhaṃ kṛtam \|*  
*tatsarvaṃ saṃparityajya yūyamekākinaḥ sthitāḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. Where is that kingdom? Where is your mistress? Where are those for whom sin and evil have been perpetrated? You have left them all and stand here alone.

## \|\| 9.8.9 \|\|

*paśyāmi tadbalaṃ naṣṭaṃ yena vidhvaṃsitāḥ prajāḥ \|*  
*yamadūtairyojyamānā adhunā kīdṛśaṃ bhavet \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. I see that might quelled, the might that suppressed the subjects. How will you fare when you too are subjected to the same treatment by the emissaries of Yama?

## \|\| 9.8.10 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*evaṃ bahuvidhairvākyairupalabdhā yamena te \|*  
*svāni karmāṇi śocaṃti tūṣṇīṃ tiṣṭhaṃti pārthivāḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

10\. The kings who are treated with these and other similar taunts by Yama bewail their own lot and stand silent.

## \|\| 9.8.11 \|\|

*iti karmma samuddiśya nṛpāṇāṃ dharmmarāḍyamaḥ \|*  
*tatpāpapaṃkaśuddhyarthamidaṃ dūtānbravīti ca \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. After having proclaimed their evil actions, Yama, Dharmarāja speaks to the messengers thus, for wiping off the sins of those kings.

## \|\| 9.8.12 \|\|

*yamarāja uvāca \|*  
*bhobhoścaṇḍa mahācaṃḍa gṛhītvā nṛpatīnbalāt \|*  
*niyamena viśudyadhvaṃ krameṇa narakāgniṣu \|\| 12 \|\|*

Yamarāja said:—

12\. O Caṇḍa, O Mahācaṇḍa, seize these kings forcibly and purify them gradually in the fires of hell (naraka).

## \|\| 9.8.13-14 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*tataśśīghraṃ samādāya nṛpānsaṃgṛhya pādayoḥ \|*  
*bhrāmayitvā tu vegena nikṣipyordhvaṃ pragṛhya ca \|\| \|\| 13 \|\|*

*sarvaprāyeṇa mahatātīva tapte śilātale \|*  
*āsphālayaṃti tarasā vajreṇeva mahādrumān \|\| 14 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

13-14. Then they immediately catch hold of the kings by their feet, whirl them with great velocity, throw them up and when they fall they catch them again and dash them against a heated rock. They are thus felled like great trees smitten with the thunderbolt.

## \|\| 9.8.15 \|\|

*tatassaraktaṃ śrotreṇa sravate jarjarīkṛtaḥ \|*  
*nissaṃjñassa sadā dehī niśceṣṭassaṃprajāyate \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. Then the man sheds blood through his cars. He is wholly shattered. He becomes unconscious and motionless.

## \|\| 9.8.16 \|\|

*tatassa vāyunā spṛṣṭassatairujjīvitaḥ punaḥ \|*  
*tataḥ pāpaviśuddhyarthaṃ kṣipaṃti narakārṇave \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. When the wind blows against him he is resuscitated to life again. In order to purify him of his sins they throw him into the ocean of hell.

## \|\| 9.8.17 \|\|

*aṣṭāviṃśatisaṃkhyābhiḥ kṣityadhassaptakoṭayaḥ \|*  
*saptamasya talasyāṃte ghore tamasi saṃsthitaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. At the end of the seventh nether-world Tala, there are twenty-eight Narakakotis situated in terrible darkness.

## \|\| 9.8.18 \|\|

*ghorākhyā prathamā koṭiḥ sughorā tadadhaḥ sthitā \|*  
*atighorā mahāghorā ghorarūpā ca paṃcamī \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. The first cell is called Ghorā. Sughorā is situated beneath it. Atighorā and Mahāghorā come next and Ghorarūpā is the fifth.

## \|\| 9.8.19 \|\|

*ṣaṣṭhī talātalākhyā ca saptamī ca bhayānakā \|*  
*aṣṭamī kālarātriśca navamī ca bhayotkaṭā \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. The sixth is named Talātalā. The seventh is Bhayānakā. The eighth is Kālarātri and the ninth is Bhayotkaṭā.

## \|\| 9.8.20-21 \|\|

*daśamī tadadhaścaṇḍā mahācaṇḍā tato'pyadhaḥ \|*  
*caṇḍakolāhalā cānyā pracaṇḍā caṇḍanāyikā \|\| 20 \|\|*

*padmā padmāvatī bhītā bhīmā bhīṣaṇanāyikā \|*  
*karālā vikarālā ca vajrāviṃśatimā smṛtā \|\| 21 \|\|*

20-21. The tenth beneath the previous is Caṇḍā. Mahācaṇḍā is lower still. Caṇḍakolāhalā is another. Pracaṇḍā, Caṇḍanāyikā, Padmā, Padmāvatī, Bhītā, Bhīmā, Bhīṣaṇanāyikā and Vajrā are terrible, very terrible.

## \|\| 9.8.22 \|\|

*trikoṇā pañcakoṇā ca sudīrghā cākhilārtidā \|*  
*samā bhīmabalā bhogrā dīptaprāyeti cāntimī \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. The next eight cells are Trikoṇā, Pañcakoṇā, Sūdīrghā, Akhilārtidā, Samā, Bhīmabalā, Atyugrā and the eighth Dīptaprāyā.

## \|\| 9.8.23 \|\|

*iti te nāmataḥ proktā ghorā narakakoṭayaḥ \|*  
*aṣṭāviṃśatirevaitāḥ pāpānāṃ yātanātmikāḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23 Thus the cells of Naraka have been mentioned to you by their names. Each of these is meant for the torture for a particular sin. Thus the twenty-eight cells for twenty-eight type of sins.

## \|\| 9.8.24 \|\|

*tāsāṃ krameṇa vijñeyāḥ paṃca pañcaiva nāyakāḥ \|*  
*pratyekaṃ sarvakoṭīnāṃ nāmatassaṃnibodhata \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. For each of these cells there are five officers in charge. Now I shall mention the names of the hells of each of these cells. Understand them well.

## \|\| 9.8.25 \|\|

*rauravaḥ prathamasteṣāṃ ruvaṃte yatra dehinaḥ \|*  
*mahārauravapīḍābhirmahāṃto'pi rudaṃti ca \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. Raurava is the first where the embodied beings cry. Mahāraurava is the next. Due to the tortures here even great men cry.

## \|\| 9.8.26-42 \|\|

*tataśśītaṃ tathā coṣṇaṃ paṃcādyā nāyakāssmṛtāḥ \|*  
*sughorastu mahātīkṣṇastathā saṃjīvanaḥ smṛtaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

*mahātamo vilomaśca vilopaścāpi kaṃṭaka \|*  
*tīvravegaḥ karālaśca vikarālaḥ prakaṃpanaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

*mahāvakraśca kālaśca kālasūtraḥ pragarjanaḥ \|*  
*sūcīmukhassunetiśca khādakassuprapīḍanaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

*kumbhīpākasupākau ca krakacaścātidāruṇaḥ \|*  
*aṃgārarāśibhavanaṃ merurasṛkprahitastataḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

*tīkṣṇatuṇḍaśca śakunirmahāsaṃvartakaḥ kratuḥ \|*  
*taptajaṃtuḥ paṃkalepaḥ pratimāṃsastrapūdbhavaḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

*ucchvāsassunirucchvāso sudīrghaḥ kūṭaśālmaliḥ \|*  
*duriṣṭassumahāvādaḥ pravādassupratāpanaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

*tato megho vṛṣaḥ śālmassiṃhavyāghragajānanāḥ \|*  
*śvasūkarājamahiṣaghūkakokavṛkānanāḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

*grāhakuṃbhīnanakrākhyā ssarpakūrmākhyavāyasāḥ \|*  
*gṛdhrolūkahalokākhyāḥ śārdūlakrathakarkaṭāḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

*maṃḍūkāḥ pūtivaktrāśca raktākṣaḥ pūtimṛttikāḥ \|*  
*kaṇadhūmrastathāgniśca kṛmigandhivapustathā \|\| 34 \|\|*

*agnīdhraścāpratiṣṭhaśca rudhirābhaśśvabhojanaḥ \|*  
*lālā bhekṣāṃtrabhakṣau ca sarvabhakṣaḥ sudāruṇaḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

*kaṃṭakassuviśālaśca vikaṭaḥ kaṭapūtanaḥ \|*  
*aṃbarīṣaḥ kaṭāhaśca kaṣṭā vaitaraṇī nadī \|\| 36 \|\|*

*sutaptalohaśayana ekapādaḥ prapūraṇaḥ \|*  
*asitālavanaṃ ghoramasthibhaṃgaḥ supūraṇaḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

*vilātaso'suyaṃtro'pi kūṭapāśaḥ pramardanaḥ \|*  
*mahācūrṇṇo sucūrṇṇo'pi taptalohamayaṃ tathā \|\| 38 \|\|*

*parvataḥ kṣuradhārā ca tathā yamalaparvataḥ \|*  
*mūtraviṣṭhāśrukūpaśca kṣārakūpaśca śītalaḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

*musalolūkhalaṃ yantraṃ śilāśakaṭalāṃgalam \|*  
*tālapatrāsigahanaṃ mahāśakaṭamaṇḍapam \|\| 40 \|\|*

*saṃmohamasthibhaṃgaśca taptaścalamayo guḍam \|*  
*bahudukhaṃ mahākleśaḥ kaśmalaṃ samalaṃ malāt \|\| 41 \|\|*

*hālāhalo virūpaśca svarūpaśca yamānugaḥ \|\| \|*  
*ekapādastripādaśca tīvraścācīvaraṃ tamaḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

26-42. There are cool and hot hells (naraka). The first five are the leading hells. The hells are named thus: Sughora, Sumahātīkṣṇa, Sañjīvana, Mahātamas. Viloma, Vilopa, Kaṇṭaka, Tīvravega, Karāla, Vikarāla, Prakampana, Mahāvaktra, Kāla, Kālasūtra, Pragarjana, Sūcīmukha, Suneti, Khādaka, Suprapīḍana, Kumbhīpāka, Supāka, Krakaca, Atidāruṇa, Aṅgārarāśibhavanam, Medaprahita, Asṛkprahita, Tīkṣṇatuṇḍa, Śakuni, Mahāsaṃvartaka, Kratu, Taptajantu, Paṅkalepa, Pratimāṃśa, Trapūdbhava, Ucchvāsa, Sunirucchvāsa, Sudīrgha, Kūṭaśālmali, Duriṣṭa, Sumahāvāda, Pravāha, Supratāpana, Megha, Vṛṣa, Śālma, Siṃhānana, Vyāghrānana, Gajānana, Śvānana, Sūkarānana, Ajānana, Mahiṣānana, Ghūkānana, Kokānana, Vṛkānana, Grahākhya, Kumbhīnākhya, Nakrāskhya, Sarpākhya, Kūrmākhya, Vāyasākhya, Gṛdhrākhya, Ulūkākhya, Jalaukākhya, Śārdūla, Kratha, Karkaṭa, Maṇḍūka, Pūtivaktra, Raktākṣa, Pūtimṛttika, Kaṇadhūmra, Agni, Kṛmigandhivapus, Agnīdhra, Apratiṣṭha, Rudhirābha, Śvabhojana, Lālābhakṣa, Antrabhakṣa, Sarvabhakṣa, Sudāruṇa, Kaṇṭaka, Suviśāla, Vikaṭa, Kaṭapūtana, Ambarīṣa, Kaṭāha, the grievous river Vaitaraṇī, Sutaptalohaśayana, Ekapāda, Prapūraṇa, Asitālavana, Asthibhaṅga, Supūraṇa Vilātasa, Asuyantra, Kūṭapāśa, Pramardana, Mahācūrṇa, Asucūrṇa, Taptalohamaya, Parvata, Kṣuradhārā, Yamalaparvata, Mūtrakūpa, Viṣṭhākūpa, Aśrukūpa, Kṣārakūpa, Śītala, Musalolūkhala, Yantra, Śilā, Śakaṭalāṅgala, Tālapatrāsigahana, Mahāśakaṭa, Maṇḍapa, Sammoha, Asthibhaṅga, Tapta, Cala, Ayoguḍa, Bahuduḥkha, Mahākleśa, Kaśmala, Samala, Mala, Hālāhala, Virūpa, Svarūpa, Yamānuga, Ekapāda, Tripāda, Tīvra, Ācīvara and Tamas.

## \|\| 9.8.43-45 \|\|

*aṣṭāviṃśatirityete kramaśaḥ paṃcapaṃcakam \|*  
*koṭīnāmānupūrvyeṇa paṃca paṃcaiva nāyakāḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

*rauravāya prabodhyaṃte narakāṇāṃ śataṃ smṛtam \|*  
*catvāriṃśacchataṃ proktaṃ mahānarakamaṇḍalam \|\| 44 \|\|*

*iti te vyāsa saṃproktā narakasya sthitirmayā \|*  
*prasaṃkhyānācca vairāgyaṃ śṛṇu pāpagatiṃ ca tām \|\| 45 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

43-45. Thus for twenty-eight cells there are five officers in charge of each. Raurava and other hells are thus hundred and more. The great zones of hell (naraka-maṇḍala) are forty hundreds. Thus O Vyāsa, I have described the hells to you. The enumeration arouses detachment from the sin. Now listen to the pangs of sinners.

## Chapter 9 - Pangs of hell

## \|\| 9.9.1 \|\|

*\|\| sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*eṣu pāpāḥ prapacyaṃte śoṣyaṃte narakāgniṣu \|*  
*yātanābhirvicitrābhirāsvakarmmakṣayādbhṛśam \|\| 1 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

1\. In the hellish fires, the sinners are roasted and dried up by diverse tortures till their action is exhausted completely.

## \|\| 9.9.2 \|\|

*svamalaprakṣayādyadvadagnau dhāsyaṃti dhātavaḥ \|*  
*tatra pāpakṣayātpāpā narāḥ karmānurūpataḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. Just as metals are melted in fire to remove their impurities so also sinners are put in hells in order to remove their sins.

## \|\| 9.9.3 \|\|

*sugāḍhaṃ hastayorbaddhvā tataśśṛṃkhalayā narāḥ \|*  
*mahāvṛkṣāgraśākhāsu lambyante yamakiṃkaraiḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. The hands are tightly tied and the men are battered. Then they are suspended from the branches of tall trees by the attendants of Yama.

## \|\| 9.9.4 \|\|

*tataste sarvayatnena kṣiptā dolaṃti kiṃkaraiḥ \|*  
*dolaṃtaścātivegena visaṃjñā yāṃti yojanam \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. Then they are pushed by the servants till they begin to oscillate a Yojana unconscious by their rapidity.

## \|\| 9.9.5 \|\|

*aṃtarikṣasthitānāṃ ca lohabhāraśataṃ punaḥ \|*  
*pādayorbadhyate teṣāṃ yamadūtairmahābalaiḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Even as they are suspended in mid air, iron hundred Bhāras in weight is tied to their feet by the emissaries of Yama.

## \|\| 9.9.6 \|\|

*tena bhāreṇa mahatā prabhṛśaṃ tāḍitā narāḥ \|*  
*dhyāyaṃti svāni karmāṇi tūṣṇīṃ tiṣṭhanti niścalāḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. Afflicted by such a heavy weight the sinners begin to think about their evil actions and keep quiet and motionless.

## \|\| 9.9.7 \|\|

*tatoṃ'kuśairagnivarṇairloha daṇḍaiśca dāruṇaiḥ \|*  
*hanyaṃte kiṃkaraighoraissamantātpāpakarmmiṇaḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. Then the sinners are beaten with goads of fiery colour and terrible iron rods by the terrible servants of Yama.

## \|\| 9.9.8 \|\|

*tataḥ kṣāreṇa dīptena vahnerapi viśeṣataḥ \|*  
*samaṃtataḥ pralipyaṃte tīveṇa tu punaḥ punaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. Then they are again smeared with glowing acid more unbearable than fire.

## \|\| 9.9.9-11 \|\|

*drutenātyaṃtaliptena kṛttāṃgā jarjarīkṛtāḥ \|*  
*punarvidāryya cāṃgāni śirasaḥ prabhṛti kramāt \|\| 9 \|\|*

*vṛtākavatprapacyaṃte taptalohakaṭāhakaiḥ \|*  
*viṣṭhāpūrṇe tathā kūpe kṛmīṇāṃ nicaye punaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

*medo'sṛkpūyapūrṇāyāṃ vāpyāṃ kṣipyaṃti te punaḥ \|*  
*bhakṣyaṃte kṛmibhistīkṣṇairloṃhatuṃḍaiśca vāyasaiḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

9-11. Their limbs are cut and smothered, gradually torn and severed and smeared with molten metal. They are then roasted like brinjal in red-hot iron cauldrons. They are then cast into wells full of filth, swarms of worms or in tanks full of putrid fat and blood. They are eaten by worm and crows with beaks strong as iron.

## \|\| 9.9.12 \|\|

*śvabhirddaṃśairvṛkairvyāghrairraudraiśca vikṛtānanaiḥ \|*  
*pacyaṃte matsyavaccāpi pradīptāṃgārarāśiṣu \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. Dogs, mosquitoes, wolves and tigers of terrible and hideous faces too devour them. They are roasted like fish over glowing heaps of coal.

## \|\| 9.9.13-14 \|\|

*bhinnāḥ śūlaistu tīkṣṇaiśca narāḥ pāpena karmmaṇā \|*  
*tailayantreṣu cākramya ghoraiḥ karmmabhirātmanaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

*tilā iva prapīḍyaṃte cakrākhye janapiṃḍakāḥ \|*  
*bhrajyaṃte cātape tapte lohabhāṇḍeṣvanekadhā \|\| 14 \|\|*

13-14. Due to their sinful actions men are pierced through with sharp spears. They are put in oil extracting machines called Cakra and crushed like gingelly seeds and beaten into pulp. They are fried in iron cauldrons red hot in the blazing sunlight.

## \|\| 9.9.15 \|\|

*tailapūrṇakaṭāheṣu sutapteṣu punaḥpunaḥ \|*  
*bahudhā pacyate jihvā prapīḍyorasi pādayoḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. They are fried in boiling oil in cauldrons again and again. Their tongues, chests and feet are struck in many ways.

## \|\| 9.9.16 \|\|

*yātanāśca mahatyo'tra śarīrasyāti sarvataḥ \|*  
*niśśeṣanarakeṣvevaṃ kramaṃti kramaśo narāḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. Here the tortures to the body are very severe. Men thus go from one hell to another and are tortured in all the hells.

## \|\| 9.9.17 \|\|

*narakeṣu ca sarveṣu vicitrā yamayātanā \|*  
*yāmyaiśca dīyate vyāsa sarvāṃgeṣu sukaṣṭadā \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. O vyāsa, terrible tortures are inflicted in diverse ways in all the hells by Yama’s emissaries. They are grievous and painful to every part of the body.

## \|\| 9.9.18-19 \|\|

*jvaladaṃgāramādāya mukhamāpūryya tāḍyate \|*  
*tataḥ kṣāreṇa dīptena tāmreṇa ca punaḥpunaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

*ghṛtenātyantataptena tadā tailena tanmukham \|*  
*itastataḥ pīḍayitvā bhṛśamāpūryya hanyate \|\| 19 \|\|*

18-19. Their mouths are filled with the burning coals and again with acid red hot, copper hot clarified butter and oil and they are beaten afterwards.

## \|\| 9.9.20 \|\|

*viṣṭhābhiḥ kṛmibhiścāpi pūryamāṇāḥ kvacitkvacit \|*  
*pariṣvajaṃti cātyugrāṃ pradīptāṃ lohaśālmalīm \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. They are filled with faeces and worms and are forced to embrace the red hot fierce iron-silk cotton tree.

## \|\| 9.9.21-22 \|\|

*hanyaṃte pṛṣṭhadeśe ca punardīptairmahāghanaiḥ \|*  
*dantureṇādikaṃṭhena krakacena balīsayā \|\| 21 \|\|*

*śiraḥprabhṛti pīḍyaṃte ghoraiḥ karmabhirātmajaiḥ \|*  
*khādyaṃte ca svamāṃsāni pīyate śoṇitaṃ svakam \|\| 22 \|\|*

21-22. They are then beaten on the back with heated hammer. Their heads and their limbs are crushed with too blunt tooth-edged strong scissors. They are thus tortured by their own actions. Their flesh is eaten and their blood is drunk by them.

## \|\| 9.9.23 \|\|

*annaṃ pānaṃ na dattaṃ yaissarvadā svātmapoṣakaiḥ \|*  
*ikṣuvatte prapīḍyaṃte jarjarīkṛtya mudgaraiḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. Those who fed and nurtured only themselves and never made any gift of food or drink are crushed with iron rods like sugarcane.

## \|\| 9.9.24 \|\|

*asitālavane ghore chidyante khaṇḍaśastataḥ \|*  
*sūcībhirbhinnasarvāṅgāstaptaśūlāgraropitāḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. In the terrible hell Asitālavana they are cut into pieces. Their limbs are pierced through with needles. They are staked on to heated spearheads.

## \|\| 9.9.25 \|\|

*saṃcālyamānā bahuśaḥ kliśyaṃte na mriyanti ca \|*  
*tathā ca taccharīrāṇi sukhaduḥkhasahāni ca \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. Tossed about many times their bodies do not perish but are severely pained. They become used to bear the pleasure or pain.

## \|\| 9.9.26 \|\|

*dehādutpāṭya māṃsāni bhidyaṃte svaiśca mudgaraiḥ \|*  
*daṃturākṛtibhirrghorairyamadūtairbalotkaṭaiḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. Flesh is torn off their bodies and they are pounded with iron clubs of tooth-edged shapes by terribly mighty emissaries of Yama.

## \|\| 9.9.27 \|\|

*nirucchvāse niruchvāsāstiṣṭhaṃti narake ciram \|*  
*uttāḍyaṃte tathochvāse vālukāsadane narāḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. In the hell Nirucchvāsa they are forced to stand without breathing, for a long time. In the hell Ucchvāsa they are hit and thrashed in a house of sand.

## \|\| 9.9.28 \|\|

*raurave rodamānāśca pīḍyaṃte vividhai vadhaiḥ \|*  
*mahārauravapīḍābhirmahāṃto'pi rudaṃti ca \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. In the hell Raurava they are made to cry and inflicted pain by various tortures. Due to the tortures in the hell, Mahāraurava, even great men cry.

## \|\| 9.9.29 \|\|

*patsu vaktre gude muṃḍe netrayoścaiva mastake \|*  
*nihanyaṃte ghanaistīkṣṇaissutaptairloha śaṃkubhiḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. They are beaten on the faces, feet, anus, skull, eyes and forehead with blocks of iron and sharp-edged red-hot iron spikes.

## \|\| 9.9.30 \|\|

*sutaptāvālukāyāṃ tu prayojyaṃte muhurmuhuḥ \|*  
*jaṃtupaṃke bhṛśaṃ tapte kṣiptāḥ krandaṃti visvaram \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. They are again and again rolled on hot sand. Cast into boiling marsh of creatures they make harsh shrieking sounds.

## \|\| 9.9.31 \|\|

*kuṃbhīpākeṣu ca tathā taptataileṣu vai mune \|*  
*pāpinaḥ kūrakarmmāṇo'sahyeṣu sarvathā punaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. O sage, in the hell kumbhīpāka sinners of ruthless misdeeds are fried in unbearable boiling oil.

## \|\| 9.9.32 \|\|

*lālābhakṣeṣu pāpāste pātyaṃte duḥkhadeṣu vai \|*  
*nānāsthāneṣu pacyaṃte narakeṣu punaḥpunaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. The sinners are felled and dragged along various torturing places again and again in the hell named Lālābhakṣa.

## \|\| 9.9.33 \|\|

*sūcīmukhe mahākleśe narake pātyate naraḥ \|*  
*pāpī puṇyavihīnaśca tāḍyate yamakiṃkaraiḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. Sinners devoid of merit are beaten by Yama’s servants. They are hurled into the torturous hell Sūcīmukha.

## \|\| 9.9.34 \|\|

*lauhakumbhe viniḥkṣiptāḥ śvasantaśca śanaiḥśanaiḥ \|*  
*mahāgninā prapacyaṃte svapāpaireva mānavāḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. They are put into iron pots. They breathe hard and slow. They are scorched by great fire, as if by their own sins.

## \|\| 9.9.35 \|\|

*dṛḍhaṃ rajjvādibhirbaddhvā prapīḍyaṃte śilāsu ca \|*  
*kṣipyaṃte cāndhakūpeṣu daśyaṃte bhramarairbhṛśam \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. They are tied tightly with ropes and are rolled on rocks and harassed. They are cast into deep gloomy wells. They are subjected to bee-bites.

## \|\| 9.9.36 \|\|

*kṛmibhirbhinnasarvāṃgāśśataśo jarjarīkṛtāḥ \|*  
*sutīkṣṇakṣārakūpeṣu kṣipyaṃte tadanaṃtaram \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. When their bodies are cut, bitten and gnawed by worms in a hundred places, they are hurled into wells of pungent acid.

## \|\| 9.9.37 \|\|

*mahājvāle'tra narake pāpāḥ krandaṃti duḥkhitāḥ \|*  
*itaścetaśca dhāvaṃti dahyamānāstadarciṣā \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. In the blazing hell the distressed sinners, shriek and run about here and there scorched by its flames.

## \|\| 9.9.38-39 \|\|

*pṛṣṭhe cānīya tuṇḍābhyāṃ vinyasta skaṃdhayojite \|*  
*tayormadhyena vākṛṣya bāhupṛṣṭhena gāḍhataḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

*baddhvā parasparaṃ sarve subhṛśaṃ pāparajjubhiḥ \|*  
*baddhapiṃḍāstu dṛśyaṃte mahā jvāle tu yātanāḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

38-39. People are clubbed and tied together with mouths resting on shoulders and hands brought through the hollow and tied with nooses and ropes to the back. Such bundles of bodies are seen in the Mahājvāla undergoing tortures.

## \|\| 9.9.40 \|\|

*rajjubhirveṣṭitāścaiva praliptāḥ karddamena ca \|*  
*karīṣatuṣavahnau ca pacyaṃte na mriyaṃti ca \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. They are tied with ropes, smeared with mud and scorched in husk and cowdung fire but do not perish.

## \|\| 9.9.41 \|\|

*sutīkṣṇaṃ caritāste hi karkaśāsu śilāsu ca \|*  
*āsphālya śataśaḥ pāpāḥ pacyaṃte tṛṇavattataḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. Dragged and rolled through rough and coarse rocks, beaten and burnt like straw, the sinners of ruthless conduct, are tortured.

## \|\| 9.9.42-43 \|\|

*śarīrābhyaṃtaragataiḥ prabhūtaiḥ kṛmibhirnarāḥ \|*  
*bhakṣyaṃte tīkṣṇavadanairātmadehakṣayādbhṛśam \|\| 42 \|\|*

*kṛmīṇāṃ nicaye kṣiptāḥ pūyamāṃsāsthirāśiṣu \|*  
*tiṣṭhaṃtyudvignahṛdayāḥ parvatābhyāṃ nipīḍitāḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

42-43. The worms eat through their bodies with their sharp mouths and teeth. With their bodies gradually hurled over swarms of worms, heaps of putrid flesh and bones they stay there distressed, dejected and crushed between two mountains

## \|\| 9.9.44 \|\|

*taptena vajralepena śarīramanulipyate \|*  
*adhomukhordhvapādāśca tātapyaṃte sma vahninā \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. They are kept with face down and legs up and scorched in fire. Their bodies are smeared with hot adamantine solution.

## \|\| 9.9.45 \|\|

*vadanāṃtaḥ pravinyastāṃ suprataptāmayogadām \|*  
*te khādanti parādhīnāstaistāḍyaṃte samudgaraiḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. Red hot iron clubs are thrust into their mouths. They are helplessly forced to gnaw at it. They are then beaten with iron threshing rods.

## \|\| 9.9.46 \|\|

*itthaṃ vyāsa kukarmmāṇo narakeṣu pacaṃti hi \|*  
*varṇayāmi vivarṇatvaṃ teṣāṃ tattvāya karmmiṇām \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. O Vyāsa, thus the sinners of misdeeds are cooked and scorched in hells. I shall now describe their various modes of undergoing the tortures of hell.

## Chapter 10 - The mode of sufferings in the Hell

## \|\| 9.10.1 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*mithyāgamaṃ pravṛttastu dvijihvākhye ca gacchati \|*  
*jihvārddhakośavistīrṇahalaistīkṣṇaḥ prapīḍyate \|\| 1 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

1\. He who follows false scriptures goes to the hell Dvijihva. He is tortured by the tongue-like ploughshares extending to one and a half kilometres.

## \|\| 9.10.2 \|\|

*nirbhartsayati yaḥ krūro mātaraṃ pitaraṃ gurum \|*  
*viṣṭhābhiḥ kṛmimiśrābhirmukhamāpūryya hanyate \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. The mouth of the man who had been cruel to his parents and had reproached them is filled with faeces and worms and he is beaten.

## \|\| 9.10.3-5 \|\|

*ye śivāyatanārāmavāpīkūpataḍāgakān \|*  
*vidravaṃti dvijasthānaṃ narāstatra ramaṃti ca \|\| 3 \|\|*

*kāmāyodvartanābhyaṃga snānapānāmbubhojanam \|*  
*krīḍanaṃ maithunaṃ dyūtamācaranti madoddhatā \|\| 4 \|\|*

*pecire vividhairgherairikṣuyaṃtrādipīḍanaiḥ \|*  
*nirayāgniṣu pacyaṃte yāvadābhūtasaṃplavam \|\| 5 \|\|*

3-5. Men who defile the temple of Śiva, the park surrounding it and the wells, tanks and lakes therein, in various ways such as playing there, applying oil and unguents over the body, drinking bouts, taking food, indulgence in sexual intercourse, game of dice etc. are tortured by being crushed in the sugercane-crushing machine and other mechanical contrivances. They are scorched in hellish fire till the final day of dissolution.

## \|\| 9.10.6 \|\|

*tena tenaiva rūpeṇa tāḍyate pāradārikāḥ \|*  
*gāḍhamāliṃgya te nārīṃ sutaptāṃ lohanirmitām \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. Sinners who indulge in illicit union with other’s wives are forced to embrace red-hot iron images of those women with whom they had illicit relation and struck in various ways.

## \|\| 9.10.7 \|\|

*pūrvākārāśca puruṣāḥ prajvalanti samaṃtataḥ \|*  
*duścāriṇīṃ striyaṃ gāḍhamāliṃgaṃti rudaṃti ca \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. Images of those men are made of red hot iron and the erring women are forced to embrace those images. They cry and shriek.

## \|\| 9.10.8-10 \|\|

*ye śṛṇvaṃti satāṃ niṃdāṃ teṣāṃ karṇaprapūraṇam \|*  
*agnivarṇairayaḥkīlaistaptaistāmrādinirmitaiḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

*trapusīsārakūṭādbhiḥ kṣīreṇa ca punaḥpunaḥ \|*  
*sutaptatīkṣṇatailena vajralepena vā punaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

*kramādāpūryyakarṇāṃstu narakeṣu ca yātanāḥ \|*  
*anukrameṇa sarveṣu bhavaṃtyetāḥ samaṃtataḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

8-10. Those who listen to the censure of good people are punished thus: ears filled with red hot pins of iron, copper, tin, lead or brass, or with boiling milk or oil or adamantine liquid again and again. The tortures in the hells are inflicted by filling the cars with these and other parts one after the other. The procedure here too is the same as in the case of ears.

## \|\| 9.10.11 \|\|

*sarvendriyāṇāmapyevaṃ kramātpāpena yātanāḥ \|*  
*bhavaṃti ghorāḥ pratyekaṃ śarīreṇa kṛtena ca \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Similar tortures are inflicted on all the organs of the body with which the acts of sin had been committed.

## \|\| 9.10.12 \|\|

*sparśadoṣeṇa ye mūḍhāsspṛśaṃti ca parastriyam \|*  
*teṣāṃ karo'gnivarṇābhiḥ pāṃśubhiḥ pūryyate bhṛśam \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. The hands of those sinners who touch other’s wives are filled with red hot fillings.

## \|\| 9.10.13 \|\|

*teṣāṃ kṣārādibhissarvaiśśarīramanulipyate \|*  
*yātanāśca mahākaṣṭāssarveṣu narakeṣu ca \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. Their bodies are smeared with corrosive substances such as acid etc. Tortures are acute and grievous in all the hells.

## \|\| 9.10.14 \|\|

*kurvanti pitrorbhṛkuṭiṃ karanetrāṇi ye narā \|*  
*vaktrāṇi teṣāṃ sāṃtāni kīryyaṃte śaṃkubhirdṛḍham \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. The faces of men who show wry faces with knit eye-brows to their parents are scratched from end to end with sharp pikes.

## \|\| 9.10.15 \|\|

*yairindriyairnarā ye ca kurvanti parastriyam \|*  
*indriyāṇi ca teṣāṃ vai vikurvaṃti tathaiva ca \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. The organs wherewith men defile or harass women are respectively tortured.

## \|\| 9.10.16 \|\|

*paradārāṃśca paśyanti lubdhāsstabdhena cakṣuṣā \|*  
*sūcībhiścāgnivarṇābhisteṣāṃ netraprapūraṇam \|\| 16 \|\|*

16 If they had looked at other women with greedy gaping eyes, red hot needles are stuck in their eyes.

## \|\| 9.10.17 \|\|

*kṣārādyaiśca kramātsarvā ihaiva yamayātanāḥ \|*  
*bhavaṃti muniśārdūla satyaṃsatyaṃ na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. O excellent sage, it is true, true. There is no doubt. Tortures of Yama by acids etc. take place here itself in the hells.

## \|\| 9.10.18 \|\|

*devāgniguruviprebhyaścānivedya prabhuṃjate \|*  
*lohakīlaśataistaptaistajjihvāsyaṃ ca pūryyate \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. If people take food without first offering it to the gods, firegod, preceptors and brahmins, their tongues and mouths are pierced through and filled with hundreds of red-hot nails of iron.

## \|\| 9.10.19-20 \|\|

*ye devārāmapuṣpāṇi lobhātsaṃgṛhya pāṇinā \|*  
*jighraṃti ca narā bhūyaḥ śirasā dhārayaṃti ca \|\| 19 \|\|*

*āpūryyate śirasteṣāṃ taptairlohasya śaṃkubhiḥ \|*  
*nāsikā vātibahulaistataḥ kṣārādibhirbhṛśam \|\| 20 \|\|*

19-20. If men, out of greed, pluck and sniff at flowers of the temple parks, or wear them on their heads, their heads are covered with iron spikes and their noses are filled with plenty of acid and other things.

## \|\| 9.10.21-23 \|\|

*ye niṃdanti mahātmānaṃ vācakaṃ dharmmadeśikam \|*  
*devāgnigurubhaktāṃśca dharmaśāstraṃ ca śāśvatam \|\| 21 \|\|*

*teṣāmurasi kaṇṭhe ca jihvāyāṃ daṃtasandhiṣu \|*  
*tālunyoṣṭhe nāsikāyāṃ mūrdhni sarvāṅgasandhiṣu \|\| 22 \|\|*

*agnivarṇāstu taptāśca triśākhā lohaśaṃkavaḥ \|*  
*ākhidyaṃte ca bahuśaḥ sthāneṣveteṣu mudgaraiḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

21-23. Red-hot three-pointed iron rods are thrust and pushed through the chest, neck, tongue, tooth-joints, palate, lips, nostrils and all the limb-joints of those persons who slander and censure the noble-preacher of virtue, devotees of the gods, fire-god and preceptor as well as the eternal scriptural texts.

## \|\| 9.10.24 \|\|

*tataḥ kṣāreṇa dīptena pūryate hi samaṃ tataḥ \|*  
*yātanāśca mahatyo vai śarīrasyāti sarvataḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. The crevices of the body are filled with corrosive acid. Severe tortures are inflicted all over the body.

## \|\| 9.10.25-26 \|\|

*aśeṣanarakeṣveva kramaṃti kramaśaḥ punaḥ \|*  
*ye gṛhṇanti paradravyaṃ padbhyāṃ vipraṃ spṛśaṃti ca \|\| 25 \|\|*

*śivopakaraṇaṃ gāṃ ca jñānādilikhitaṃ ca yat \|*  
*hastapādādibhisteṣāmāpūryyaṃte samaṃtataḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

25-26. Those who take other’s wealth or kick or even touch a brahmin with their legs go from hell to hell serially. Those who touch illegally or stamp with the foot the materials for Śiva’s worship, cow, or a Manuscript in which words of wisdom are written, are tortured by filling—(as mentioned before).

## \|\| 9.10.27 \|\|

*narakeṣu ca sarveṣu vicitrā dehayātanāḥ \|*  
*bhavaṃti bahuśaḥ kaṣṭāḥ pāṇipādasamudbhavāḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. In all hells various tortures of grievous nature are inflicted on hands and feet.

## \|\| 9.10.28-30 \|\|

*śivāyatanaparyyaṃte devārāmeṣu kutracit \|*  
*samutsṛjaṃti ye pāpāḥ purīṣaṃ mūtrameva ca \|\| 28 \|\|*

*teṣāṃ śiśnaṃ savṛṣaṇaṃ cūrṇyate lohamudgaraiḥ \|*  
*sūcībhiragnivarṇābhiskathā tvāpūryyate punaḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

*tataḥ kṣāreṇa mahatā tīvreṇa ca punaḥ punaḥ \|*  
*drutena pūryate gāḍhaṃ gude śiśne ca dehinaḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

28-30. If sinners evacuate their bowels or pass urine near Śiva’s temple or in the premises of the lord's parks their penis along with the scrotum is pounded into powder by iron-threshing rods. Red hot needles are stuffed into his anus and penis. Acute corrosive acid or molten metals are poured into them.

## \|\| 9.10.31-32 \|\|

*manassarvendriyāṇāṃ ca yasmā dduḥkhaṃ prajāyate \|*  
*dhane satyapi ye dānaṃ na prayacchaṃti tṛṣṇayā \|\| 31 \|\|*

*atithiṃ cāvamanyate kāle prāpte gṛhāśrame \|*  
*tasmātte duṣkṛtaṃ prāpya gacchaṃti niraye'śucau \|\| 32 \|\|*

31-32. As a result of the previous tortures their minds and all sense-organs are put to great misery. Those who, despite being rich do not make monetary gifts due to greed, and those who dishonour guests visiting their houses at the proper time, commit sins and fall into dirty hell.

## \|\| 9.10.33-34 \|\|

*ye'nnaṃ dattvā hi bhuṃjaṃti na śvabhyassaha vāyasaiḥ \|*  
*teṣāṃ ca vivṛtaṃ vaktraṃ kīlakadvayatāḍitam \|\| 33 \|\|*

*kṛmibhiḥ prāṇibhiścograirlohatuṇḍaiśca vāyasaiḥ \|*  
*upadravairbahuvidhairugrairaṃtaḥ prapīḍyate \|\| 34 \|\|*

33-34. Those who take food before offering oblations to dogs and crows are tortured by beating two nails into their open mouths. They are further harassed through worms, fierce living beings and by means of crows with iron beaks. Various other sorts of tortures too are in store for them.

## \|\| 9.10.35-40 \|\|

*śyāmaśca śabalaścaiva yamamārgānurodhakau \|*  
*yau stastābhyāṃ prayacchāmi tau gṛhṇītāmimaṃ balim \|\| 35 \|\|*

*ye vā varuṇavāyavyā yāmyā nairṛtyavāyasāḥ \|*  
*vāyasā puṇyakarmāṇaste pragṛhṇaṃtu me balim \|\| 36 \|\|*

*śivāmabhyarcya yatnena hutvāgnau vidhipūrvakam \|*  
*śaivairmantrairbaliṃ ye ca dadaṃte na ca te yamam \|\| 37 \|\|*

*paśyaṃti tridivaṃ yāṃti tasmāddadyāddinedine \|*  
*maṇḍalaṃ caturasraṃ tu kṛtvā gaṃdhādivāsitam \|\| 38 \|\|*

*dhanvantaryarthamīśānyāṃ prācyāmindrāya niḥkṣipet \|*  
*yāmyāṃ yamāya vāruṇyāṃ sudakṣomāya dakṣiṇe \|\| 39 \|\|*

*pitṛbhyastu vinikṣipya prācyāmaryamaṇe tataḥ \|*  
*dhātuścaiva vidhātuśca dvāradeśe viniḥkṣipet \|\| 40 \|\|*

35-40. He who is dark and he who is multi-coloured—these two are the obstacles to the path of Yama. To these two dogs I am offering this oblation. Let them take this oblation. Let the pious crows in the direction of Varuṇa (west), of Vāyu (north-west), of Yama (south) and of Nairṛti (south-west), accept this oblation of mine”. Those who offer this Bali with Śiva’s mantras, after worshipping Śiva with devotion and performing Homa duly, do not face Yama. They go straight to heaven. Hence this oblation shall be offered daily. A square mystic diagram is made and sweet scents are offered. The oblation to Dhanvantari is made in the Īśāna corner (north-east); that to Indra in the east; that to Yama in the south; that to Śiva with Dakṣa and Umā in the west; that to the Pitṛs in the south; that to Aryaman in the cast and to Dhātṛ and Vidhātṛ at the entrance.

## \|\| 9.10.41-44 \|\|

*śvabhyaśca śvapatibhyaśca vayobhyo vikṣipeddhuvi \|*  
*devaiḥ pitṛmanuṣyaiśca pretairbhūtaissaguhyakai \|\| 41 \|\|*

*vayobhiḥ kṛmikīṭaiśca gṛhasthaścopajīvyate \|*  
*svāhākāraḥ svadhākāro vaṣaṭkārastṛtīyakaḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

*haṃtakārastathaivānyo dhenvā stanacatuṣṭayam \|*  
*svāhākāraṃ stanaṃ devāssvadhāṃ ca pitarastathā \|\| 43 \|\|*

*vaṣaṭkāraṃ tathaivānye devā bhūteśvarāstathā \|*  
*haṃtakāraṃ manuṣyāśca pibaṃti satataṃ sta nam \|\| 44 \|\|*

41-44. The offerings to dogs and lords of dogs and to the crows are made on the ground. A householder is depended upon by the gods, Pitṛs, ghosts, all sorts of living beings, Guhyakas, birds, worms and insects. The four nipples of the cow are respectively Svāhākāra, Svadhākāra, Vaṣaṭkāra and Hantakāra. The Svāhākāra nipple is sucked by gods; the Svadhākāra by the manes, the Vaṣaṭkāra by the other gods as well as lords of the Bhūtas and the Hantakāra nipple is drunk by human beings.

## \|\| 9.10.45-46 \|\|

*yastvetāṃ mānavo dhenuṃ śraddhayā hyanupūrvikām \|*  
*karoti satataṃ kāle sāgnitvāyopakalpyate \|\| 45 \|\|*

*yastāṃ jahāti vā svasthastāmisre sa tu majjati \|*  
*tasmāddattvā baliṃ tebhyo dvārasthaściṃtayetkṣaṇam \|\| 46 \|\|*

45-46. He who serves the cow thus with faith and reverence deserves to maintain the sacrificial fires. He who forsakes it is drowned in the hell Tāmisra. Hence after offering Bali to these at the door, the householder shall meditate for a short while.

## \|\| 9.10.47 \|\|

*kṣudhārtamatithiṃ samyagekagrāmanivāsinam \|*  
*bhojayettaṃ śubhānnena yathāśaktyātmabhojanāt \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. One shall feed the hungry guest staying in the same village with auspicious food, in accordance with his ability and with the same dishes as he partakes of himself.

## \|\| 9.10.48 \|\|

*atithiryasya bhagnāśo gṛhātpratinivartate \|*  
*sa tasmai duṣkṛtaṃ dattvā puṇyamā dāya gacchati \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. If a guest turns back from a house disappointed he takes away all the merits of the householder and leaves his own sins behind.

## \|\| 9.10.49 \|\|

*tato'nnaṃ priyamevāśnannaraḥ śṛṃkhalavānpunaḥ \|*  
*jihvāvegena viddhotra ciraṃ kālaṃ sa tiṣṭhati \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. A man eating delicious food by himself stays in the hell for a long time fettered in the body and pierced in the tongue with force.

## \|\| 9.10.50-51 \|\|

*yatastaṃ māṃsamuddhatya tilamātrapramāṇataḥ \|*  
*khādituṃ dīyate teṣāṃ bhittvā caiva tu śoṇitam \|\| 50 \|\|*

*niśśeṣataḥ kaśābhistu pīḍyate kramaśaḥ punaḥ \|*  
*bubhukṣayātikaṣṭaṃ hi tathāyāti pipāsayā \|\| 51 \|\|*

50-51. Bits of his own flesh, of the size of a small gingelly seed, are cut off from his body and are given to him for eating. Blood is similarly taken and given for drinking. He is then beaten with whips. Thus he is tormented with hunger and thirst.

## \|\| 9.10.52 \|\|

*evamādyā mahāghorā yātanāḥ pāpakarmaṇām \|*  
*aṃte yatpratipannaṃ hi tatsaṃkṣepeṇa saṃśṛṇu \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. These and similar things constitute the terrible tortures inflicted on sinners. What comes off in the end may be listened to in brief.

## \|\| 9.10.53 \|\|

*yaḥ karoti mahāpāpaṃ dharmmaṃ carati vai laghu \|*  
*dharmmaṃ gurutaraṃ vāpi tathāvasthe tayoḥ śṛṇu \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. There may be a person who has done more sin than good or a person who has done more good than evil. Now listen to the result of their actions.

## \|\| 9.10.54 \|\|

*sukṛtasya phalaṃ noktaṃ gurupā paprabhāvataḥ \|*  
*na minoti sukhaṃ tatra bhogairbahubhiranvitaḥ \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. The fruit of good actions is immaterial because it is the sin that is prominent. His pleasure is insignificant since he has to undergo manifold suffering due to his bad deeds.

## \|\| 9.10.55 \|\|

*tathodvignotisaṃtapto na bhakṣyairmanyate sukham \|*  
*abhāvādagrato'nyasya pratikalpaṃ dinedine \|\| 55 \|\|*

55\. He is put to misery and distress. As sumptuous food is not pleasing to a person if that is not repeated every day so his small joys are of no avail in the face of various sufferings he has to undergo.

## \|\| 9.10.56 \|\|

*pumānyo gurudharmmā'pi sopavāso yathā gṛhī \|*  
*vittavānna vijānāti pīḍāṃ niyamasaṃsthitaḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

56\. On the other hand a man of more good actions is not distressed by a few sufferings due to small sins just as a rich householder is not distressed by hunger when he fasts for a day.

## \|\| 9.10.57 \|\|

*tāni pāpāni ghorāṇi saṃti yaiśca naro bhuvi \|*  
*śatadhā bhedamāpnoti girirvajrahato yathā \|\| 57 \|\|*

57\. There are great sins in the world which shatter a man into hundreds of pieces like a mountain struck down by thunderbolt.

## Chapter 11 - The glory of the gift of food

## \|\| 9.11.1 \|\|

*\|\| vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*kṛtapāpā narā yāṃti duḥkhena mahatānvitāḥ \|*  
*yamamārge sukhaṃ yaiśca tāndharmānvada me prabho \|\| 1 \|\|*

Vyāsa said:—

1\. Those who commit sins go along (he path to Yama with great misery. O holy lord, now narrate the righteous deeds which render pleasure in the path of Yama.

## \|\| 9.11.2 \|\|

*\|\| sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*avaśyaṃ hi kṛtaṃ karma bhoktavyamavicārataḥ \|*  
*śubhāśubhamatho vakṣye tāndharmmānsukhadāyakān \|\| 2 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

2\. Auspicious or inauspicious, one has of necessity to reap the fruit of actions committed by him without thinking. I shall now mention those sacred rites that yield happiness.

## \|\| 9.11.3 \|\|

*atra ye śubhakarmmāṇaḥ saumyacittā dayānvitāḥ \|*  
*sukhena te narā yāṃti yamamārgaṃ bhayāvaham \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. Men who perform auspicious rites here, whose minds are gentle and who are merciful pass through the terrible path of Yama happily.

## \|\| 9.11.4 \|\|

*yaḥ pradadyād dvijendrāṇāmupānatkāṣṭhapāduke \|*  
*sa naro'śvena mahatā sukhaṃ yāti yamālayam \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. He who makes gifts of sandals of leather or wood to leading brahmins goes happily to the abode of Yama on a great horse.

## \|\| 9.11.5 \|\|

*chatradānena gacchaṃti yathā chatreṇa dehinaḥ \|*  
*śibikāyāḥ pradānena tadrathena sukhaṃ vrajet \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. By making gifts of umbrella they go holding an umbrella. By making gifts of palanquin he goes by means of a chariot.

## \|\| 9.11.6 \|\|

*śayyāsanapradānena sukhaṃ yāti suviśramam \|*  
*ārāmacchāyākartāro mārge vā vṛkṣaropakāḥ \|*  
*vrajanti yamalokaṃ ca ātape'ti gataklamāḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. By making gifts of beds and seats he goes with ample rest. Those who make shady parks or those who plant trees on the wayside go without weariness even in the hottest region.

## \|\| 9.11.7 \|\|

*yāṃti puṣpagayānena puṣpārāmakarā narāḥ \|*  
*devāyatanakartāraḥ krīḍaṃti ca gṛhodare \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. Men making flower gardens go by the aerial chariot Puṣpaka. Makers of temples feel at home on the way.

## \|\| 9.11.8 \|\|

*kartāraśca tathā ye ca yatīnāmāśramasya ca \|*  
*anāthamaṇḍapānāṃ tu krīḍaṃti ca gṛhodare \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. The founders of hermitages of ascetics and Rest houses for the orphans feel as if they play in the house.

## \|\| 9.11.9 \|\|

*devāgniguruviprāṇāṃ mātāpitrośca pūjakāḥ \|*  
*pūjyamānā narā yāṃti kāmukena yathāsukham \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Worshippers of the gods, fire-god brahmins and their parents go along that path as they please. They are honoured on the way.

## \|\| 9.11.10 \|\|

*dyotayaṃto diśassarvā yāṃti dīpapradāyinaḥ \|*  
*pratiśrayapradānena sukhaṃ yāṃti nirāmayāḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Those who gift lamps, go brightly illuminating all the ten quarters. By giving dwellings and asylums they go without ailments on the way.

## \|\| 9.11.11 \|\|

*viśrāmyamāṇā gacchaṃti guruśuśrūṣakā narāḥ \|*  
*ātodyavipradātārassukhaṃ yāṃti svake gṛhe \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Those who habitually serve their preceptors go without difficulty. They get ample rest on the way. Those who gift musical instruments to brahmins go happily feeling at home on the way.

## \|\| 9.11.12 \|\|

*sarvakāmasamṛddhena yathā gacchaṃti gopradāḥ \|*  
*atra dattānnapānāni tānyāpnoti naraḥ pathi \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. Givers of cows go along the path flourishing with all their desires. They get those food-stuffs and drinks which they give away here, on that path also.

## \|\| 9.11.13 \|\|

*pādaśaucapradānena sajalena pathā vrajet \|*  
*pādābhyaṃgaṃ ca yaḥ kuryādaśvapṛṣṭhena gacchati \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. By giving water for washing the feet one goes along the path full of water. He who gifts oils for massaging the feet goes on horseback along that way.

## \|\| 9.11.14 \|\|

*pādaśaucaṃ tathābhyaṃgaṃ dīpamannaṃ pratiśrayam \|*  
*yo dadāti sadā vyāsa nopasarpati taṃ yamaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. O Vyāsa, Yama never comes near the man who always makes gifts of water for washing the feet, oil for massaging, lamps, foodstuffs and dwellings.

## \|\| 9.11.15 \|\|

*hemaratnapradānena yāti durgāṇi nistaran \|*  
*raupyānaḍutsragdānena yamalokaṃ sukhena saḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. By making gifts of gold and gems he crosses all impassable hurdles. By gifting silver and oxen he goes to Yamaloka happily.

## \|\| 9.11.16 \|\|

*ityevamādibhirdānaissukhaṃ yāṃti yamālayam \|*  
*svarge tu vividhānbhogānprāpnuvaṃti sadā narāḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. By these and similar charitable gifts people go happily to Yama’s abode. They attain different pleasures in the heaven.

## \|\| 9.11.17 \|\|

*sarveṣāmeva dānānāmannadānaṃ paraṃ smṛtam \|*  
*sadyaḥ prītikaraṃ hṛdyaṃ balabuddhivivardhanam \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. Of all charitable gifts the gift of food is the greatest. It is pleasing. It gives pleasure immediately. It enhances strength and intellect.

## \|\| 9.11.18 \|\|

*nānnadānasamaṃ dānaṃ vidyate munisattama \|*  
*annādbhavaṃti bhūtāni tadabhāve mriyaṃti ca \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. O excellent sage, there is no other charitable gift on a par with the gift of food. All living beings originate from food. In its absence they die.

## \|\| 9.11.19 \|\|

*raktaṃ māṃsaṃ vasā śukraṃ kramādannātpravardhate \|*  
*śukrādbhavaṃti bhūtāni tasmādannamayaṃ jagat \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. Food gives blood, flesh, fat and semen and out of semen living beings are born. Hence the universe is identical with food.

## \|\| 9.11.20 \|\|

*hemaratnāśvanāgendrairnārīsrakcaṃdanādibhiḥ \|*  
*samastairapi saṃprāptairna ramaṃti bubhukṣitāḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. Hungry men are not delighted even if they possess these things viz—gold, gems, horses, elephants, women, garlands, sandal paste and the like.

## \|\| 9.11.21 \|\|

*garbhasthā jāyamānāśca bālavṛddhāśca madhyamāḥ \|*  
*āhāramabhikāṃkṣaṃti devadānavarākṣasāḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. All persons, the child in the womb, the new born babe, the child, the middle aged man, the old man, the gods, the dānavas or Rākṣasas desire food.

## \|\| 9.11.22 \|\|

*kṣudhā niśśeṣarogāṇāṃ vyādhiḥ śreṣṭhatamaḥ smṛtaḥ \|*  
*sa cānnauṣadhilepena naśyatīha na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. Hunger is the acutest of all ailments. There is no doubt that it perishes on the application of the medicinal ointment viz food.

## \|\| 9.11.23 \|\|

*nāsti kṣudhāsamaṃ duḥkhaṃ nāsti rogaḥ kṣudhāsamaḥ \|*  
*nāstyarogasamaṃ saukhyaṃ nāsti krodhasamo ripuḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. There is no misery equal to hunger; no sickness equal to hunger; no happiness on a par with being free from sickness and no enemy equal to anger.

## \|\| 9.11.24 \|\|

*ataeva mahatpuṇyamannadāne prakīrtitam \|*  
*tathā kṣudhāgninā taptā mriyaṃte sarvadehinaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. All embodied beings die when scorched by the fire of hunger. That is why it is glorified that there is great merit in the charitable gift of food.

## \|\| 9.11.25 \|\|

*annadaḥ prāṇadaḥ proktaḥ prāṇadaścāpi sarvadaḥ \|*  
*tasmādannapradānena sarvadānaphalaṃ labhet \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. The giver of food is the giver of life. The giver of life is the giver of all. Hence one attains the benefit of gifting everything by means of the charitable gift of food.

## \|\| 9.11.26 \|\|

*yasyānnapānapuṣṭāṅgaḥ kurute puṇyasaṃcayam \|*  
*annapradātustasyārddhaṃ kartuścārddhaṃ na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. If a man performs holy and sacred rites after being nourished with the food gifted by someone, the merit of the sacred rites is shared half and half by the two—namely by the giver of food and the performer of the rites. There is no doubt in this.

## \|\| 9.11.27 \|\|

*trailokye yāni ratnāni bhogastrīvāhanāni ca \|*  
*annadānapradassarvamihāmutra ca tallabhet \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. The giver of charitable gift of food obtains here and hereafter whatever means of pleasure there are in the three worlds such as gems, women and vehicles.

## \|\| 9.11.28 \|\|

*dharmmārthakāmamokṣāṇāṃ dehaḥ paramasādhanam \|*  
*tasmādannena pānena pālayeddehamātmanaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. This body is the greatest of the means of achievement of virtue, wealth, love and salvation. Hence one should preserve one’s body with food and drink.

## \|\| 9.11.29 \|\|

*annameva praśaṃsaṃti sarvameva pratiṣṭhitam \|*  
*annena sadṛśaṃ dānaṃ na bhūtaṃ na bhaviṣyati \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. They praise only food. Everything is founded on food. There has never been a charitable gift like that of food, nor will there ever be any.

## \|\| 9.11.30 \|\|

*annena dhāryyate sarvaṃ viśvaṃ jagadidaṃ mune \|*  
*annamūrjaskaraṃ loke prāṇā hyanne pratiṣṭhitāḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. O sage, the entire universe is sustained by food. It is food that infuses virility in the people. The vital airs are founded on food.

## \|\| 9.11.31 \|\|

*dātavyaṃ bhikṣave cānnaṃ brāhmaṇāya mahātmane \|*  
*kuṭuṃbaṃ pīḍayitvāpi hyātmano bhūtimicchatā \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. Even by putting one’s family to inconvenience and trouble one should give food to a mendicant brahmin of noble soul, if one wishes for one’s prosperity.

## \|\| 9.11.32 \|\|

*vidadāti nidhiśreṣṭhaṃ yo dadyādannamarthine \|*  
*brāhmaṇāyārtarūpāya pāralaukikamātmanaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. He who gives food to a suppliant brahmin in distress actually makes an excellent deposit facilitating his benefit in the other world.

## \|\| 9.11.33 \|\|

*arcayedbhūtimanvicchankāle dvijamupasthitam \|*  
*śrāṃtamadhvani vṛttyarthaṃ gṛhastho gṛhamāgatam \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. A householder, desirous of prosperity shall worship a brahmin who has approached him at the proper time for his livelihood, or who is travel-weary and comes to his house as a guest.

## \|\| 9.11.34-35 \|\|

*annadaḥ pūjayedvyāsaḥ suśīlastu vimatsaraḥ \|*  
*krodhamutpatitaṃ hitvā divi ceha mahatsukham \|\| 34 \|\|*

*nābhiniṃdedadhigataṃ na praṇudyātkathaṃcana \|*  
*api śvapāke śuni vā nānnadānaṃ praṇaśyati \|\| 35 \|\|*

34-35. O Vyāsa, a giver of food, of good conduct and free from malice, is honoured. A gift of food never goes in-vain be it to a dog or a person who cooks dogs. If a person eschews anger that surges up he derives happiness here and hereafter. He should not encourage it even if it is natural. One shall try to eschew it somehow.

## \|\| 9.11.36 \|\|

*śrāṃtāyādṛṣṭapūrvāya hyannamadhvani vartate \|*  
*yo dadyādaparikliṣṭaṃ sa samṛddhimavāpnuyāta \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. He who gives food to a weary, unknown traveller, without feeling distressed for the same will derive prosperity.

## \|\| 9.11.37 \|\|

*pitṝndevāṃstathā viprānatithīṃśca mahāmune \|*  
*yo naraḥ prīṇayatyannaistasya puṇyaphalaṃ mahat \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. The meritorious benefit of the man, O great sage, who delights Pitṛs, gods, brahmins and guests with food-stuffs, in really immense.

## \|\| 9.11.38 \|\|

*annaṃ pānaṃ ca śūdre'pi brāhmaṇe ca viśiṣyate \|*  
*na pṛcchedgotracaraṇaṃ svādhyāyaṃ deśameva ca \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. Gift of food and drink whether to a Śūdra or to a brahmin is equally excellent. One should not ask the spiritual lineage,\[1\] branch of the Vedas or the country of the recipient.

## \|\| 9.11.39 \|\|

*bhikṣito brāhmaṇeneha dadyādannaṃ ca yaḥ pumān \|*  
*sa yāti paramaṃ svargaṃ yāvadābhūtasaṃplavam \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. He who gives food when begged by a brahmin, goes to the highest heaven and remains there till the final dissolution when all living beings are destroyed.

## \|\| 9.11.40 \|\|

*annadasya ca vṛkṣāśca sarvakāmaphalānvitāḥ \|*  
*bhavaṃtīha yathā viprā harṣayuktāstriviṣṭape \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. The garden of a food-giver acquires the fruits of his cherished desire as the brahmins do in the highest heaven.
