## \|\| 9.11.41 \|\|

*annadānena ye lokāssvarge viracitā mune \|*  
*annadāturmahādivyāstāñchṛṇuṣva mahāmune \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. O sage, O great sage, listen to the divine regions which are created in the heaven by the gifts of food, for the giver of food.

## \|\| 9.11.42 \|\|

*bhavanāni prakāśaṃte divi teṣāṃ mahātmanām \|*  
*nānāsaṃsthānarūpāṇi nānā kāmānvitāni ca \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. The abodes of those noble souls in the heaven shine in various shapes and are equipped with every desirable object.

## \|\| 9.11.43 \|\|

*sarvakāmaphalāścāpi vṛkṣā bhavanasaṃsthitāḥ \|*  
*hemavāpyaḥ śubhāḥ kūpā dīrghikāścaiva sarvaśaḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. The trees bear the fruits of all desires. The golden lakes in the mansions, the auspicious wells and tanks are all excellent.

## \|\| 9.11.44 \|\|

*ghoṣayaṃti ca pānāni śubhānyatha sahasraśaḥ \|*  
*bhakṣyabhojyamayāśśailā vāsāṃsyābharaṇāni ca \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. The auspicious drinks of various varieties are proclaimed. There are huge mountains of food-stuffs, garments and ornaments.

## \|\| 9.11.45 \|\|

*kṣīraṃ sravaṃtyassaritastathaivājyasya parvatāḥ \|*  
*prāsādāḥ pāṇḍurābhāsāśśayyāśca kanakojjvalāḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. Milk flows in rivers there. There are huge mountains of ghee. The palaces are white in colour. Beds are brilliant like gold.

## \|\| 9.11.46 \|\|

*tānannadāśca gacchaṃti tasmādannaprado bhavet \|*  
*yadīcchedātmano bhavyamiha loke paratra ca \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. The giver of food goes to these regions. One should be a giver of food if one wishes for one’s own good in this or in the other world.

## \|\| 9.11.47 \|\|

*ete lokāḥ puṇyakṛtāmannadānāṃ mahāprabhāḥ \|*  
*tasmādannaṃ viśeṣeṇa dātavyaṃ mānavairdhruvam \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. These regions of the meritorious givers of food are very brilliant. Hence food shall be given as gift by the people.

## \|\| 9.11.48 \|\|

*annaṃ prajāpatissākṣādannaṃ viṣṇussvayaṃ haraḥ \|*  
*tasmādannasamaṃ dānaṃ na bhūtaṃ na bhaviṣyati \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. Food is Brahma himself. Food is Viṣṇu himself. Food is Śiva. There has never been nor will there ever be a gift on a par with that of food

## \|\| 9.11.49 \|\|

*kṛtvāpi sumahatpāpaṃ yaḥ paścādannado bhavet \|*  
*vimuktassarvapāpebhyassvargalokaṃ sa gacchati \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. Even after committing a great sin if a man gives food to the needy he becomes rid of all sins. He goes to heaven.

## \|\| 9.11.50 \|\|

*annapānāśvagovastraśayyācchatrāsanāni ca \|*  
*pretaloke praśastāni dānānyaṣṭau viśeṣataḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. The eight gifts are honoured most in the land of the dead viz food, drink, horse, cow, garments, bed, umbrella and scat.

## \|\| 9.11.51 \|\|

*evaṃ dānaviśeṣeṇa dharmarājapuraṃ naraḥ \|*  
*yasmādyāti vimānena tasmāddānaṃ samācaret \|\| 51 \|\|*

51\. If a man wishes to go to the city of Yama in the aerial chariot, he shall necessarily make these special gifts.

## \|\| 9.11.52 \|\|

*etadākhyānamanaghamannadānaprabhāvataḥ \|*  
*yaḥ paṭhetpāṭhayedanyānsa samṛddhaḥ prajāyate \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. This sacred narrative gives instances of the virtue of gifts of foodstuffs; whoever reads this or teaches this to others flourishes indeed.

## \|\| 9.11.53 \|\|

*śṛṇuyācchrāvayecchrāddhe brāhmaṇānyo mahāmune \|\| \|*  
*akṣayyamannadānaṃ ca pitṝṇāmupatiṣṭhati \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. O great sage, if a person listens to this or narrates this at the performance or Śrāddha to brahmins, it results in a never-ending gift of food to the Pitṛs.

## Chapter 12 - The glory of penance (tapas)

## \|\| 9.12.1 \|\|

*\|\| sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*pānīyadānaṃ paramaṃ dānānāmuttamaṃ sadā \|*  
*sarveṣāṃ jīvapuṃjānāṃ tarpaṇaṃ jīvanaṃ smṛtam \|\| 1 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

1\. The gift of drink is the greatest and the most excellent of all gifts. Water the enlivener is the most satisfying to all living beings.

## \|\| 9.12.2 \|\|

*prapādānamataḥ kuryātsusnehādanivāritam \|*  
*jalāśrayavinirmāṇaṃ mahānandakaraṃ bhavet \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. Hence one should make gifts of drinking watersheds lovingly and unrestrictedly. Making of water bases, tanks etc. is the cause of great bliss.

## \|\| 9.12.3 \|\|

*iha loke pare vāpi satyaṃ satyaṃ na saṃśayaḥ \|*  
*tasmādvāpīśca kūpāṃśca taḍāgānkārayennaraḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. It is truthful, really truthful. There is no doubt in this, here and hereafter. Men should cause tanks, wells and lakes to be dug therefore.

## \|\| 9.12.4 \|\|

*arddhaṃ pāpasya harati puruṣasya vikarmaṇaḥ \|*  
*kūpaḥ pravṛttapānīyaḥ supravṛttasya nityaśaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. A well that abounds in water removes half of the sin of the man who causes it to be dug.

## \|\| 9.12.5 \|\|

*sarvaṃ tārayate vaṃśaṃ yasya khāte jalāśaye \|*  
*gāvaḥ pibaṃti viprāśca sādhavaśca narāssadā \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. If cows drink and good men and brahmins make use of the water in a water-base dug by a man, the whole family is redeemed.

## \|\| 9.12.6 \|\|

*nidāghakāle pānīyaṃ yasya tiṣṭhatyavāritam \|*  
*sudurgaṃ viṣamaṃ kṛcchraṃ na kadācidavāpyate \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. If there is plenty of water in the water-base dug by a man, during summer, he never suffers distress or mishaps at any time.

## \|\| 9.12.7 \|\|

*taḍāgānāṃ ca vakṣyāmi kṛtānāṃ ye guṇāḥ smṛtā \|*  
*triṣu lokeṣu sarvatra pūjito yastaḍāgavān \|\| 7 \|\|*

7‘I shall mention the benefits of lakes dug by men. He who has a lake to his credit is honoured in all the three worlds.

## \|\| 9.12.8 \|\|

*athavā mitrasadane maitraṃ mitrārtivarjitam \|*  
*kīrtisaṃjananaṃ śreṣṭhaṃ taḍāgānāṃ niveśanam \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. Or the digging of lakes is conducive to the advent of fame and attainment of friendship in the abode of the sun but without distressing heat.

## \|\| 9.12.9 \|\|

*dharmasyārthasya kāmasya phalamāhurmanīṣiṇaḥ \|*  
*taḍāgaṃ sukṛte yena tasya puṇyamanantakam \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. He who digs a lake attains an endless merit. The wise say that it is the fruit of virtue, wealth and love.

## \|\| 9.12.10 \|\|

*caturvidhānāṃ bhūtānāṃ taḍāgaḥ paramāśrayaḥ \|*  
*taḍāgādīni sarvāṇi diśanti śriyamuttamām \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. The lake is the greatest resort of the four classes of living beings. The construction of the lake etc. indicates excellent glory.

## \|\| 9.12.11 \|\|

*devā manuṣyā gandharvāḥ pitaro nāgarākṣasāḥ \|*  
*sthāvarāṇi ca bhūtāni saṃśrayaṃti jalāśayam \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. The gods, human beings, Gandharvas, Pitṛs, serpents, Rākṣasas and the immobile beings depend upon water bases.

## \|\| 9.12.12 \|\|

*prāvṛḍṛtau taḍāge tu salilaṃ yasya tiṣṭhati \|*  
*agnihotraphalaṃ tasya bhavatītyāha cātmabhūḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. Brahmā says that the benefit of the sacred rite Agnihotra is derived by a man in the lake dug by whom there is plenty of water in the rainy season.

## \|\| 9.12.13 \|\|

*śaratkāle tu salilaṃ taḍāge yasya tiṣṭhati \|*  
*gosahasraphalaṃ tasya bhavennaivātra saṃśayaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. There is no doubt at all in this that the benefit of gifting away a thousand cows is derived by a man, in the lake dug by whom there is plenty of water in the Autumn.

## \|\| 9.12.14 \|\|

*hemante śiśire caiva salilaṃ yasya tiṣṭhati \|*  
*sa vai bahusuvarṇasya yajñasya labhate phalam \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. If there is plenty of water in the lake dug during the seasons of early winter and late winter, he derives the benefit of that sacrifice in which plenty of gold is given as a sacrificial gift.

## \|\| 9.12.15 \|\|

*vasaṃte ca tathā grīṣme salilaṃ yasya tiṣṭhati \|*  
*atirātrāśvamedhānāṃ phalamāhurmanīṣiṇaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. If there is plenty of water in the lake dug during the seasons of Spring and Summer, the wise say that he derives the benefit of the holy rites of Atirātra\[1\] and Aśvamedha sacrifices.

## \|\| 9.12.16 \|\|

*mune vyāsātha vṛkṣāṇāṃ ropaṇe ca guṇāñchṛṇu \|*  
*proktaṃ jalāśayaphalaṃ jīvaprīṇanamuttamam \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. O sage Vyāsa, the merit accruing from the digging of water bases, excellent and pleasing to all living beings, has been narrated to you. Now listen to the merits of planting trees.

## \|\| 9.12.17 \|\|

*atītānāgatānsarvānpitṛvaṃśāṃstu tārayet \|*  
*kāṃtāre vṛkṣaropī yastasmādvṛkṣāṃstu ropayet \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. If a man plants trees in the forests, he enables his ancestors and the successors of his family, to gain redemption. Hence one should plant trees.

## \|\| 9.12.18 \|\|

*tatra putrā bhavaṃtyete pādapā nātra saṃśayaḥ \|*  
*paraṃ lokaṃ gatasso'pi lokānāpnoti cākṣayān \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. There is no doubt that these trees become his sons. When he goes to the other world he attains the undying regions.

## \|\| 9.12.19 \|\|

*puṣpaissuragaṇānsarvānphalaiścāpi tathā pitṝn \|*  
*chāyayā cātithīnsarvānpūjayaṃti mahīruhāḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. Trees honour and worship the gods through flowers, the Pitṛs through fruits, and the guests through shade.

## \|\| 9.12.20 \|\|

*kinnaroragarakṣāṃsi devagaṃdharvamānavāḥ \|*  
*tathaivarṣigaṇāścaiva saṃśrayaṃti mahīruhān \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. The Kinnaras, the serpents, the Rākṣasas, the gods, the Gandharvas, the human beings and the sages depend upon trees.

## \|\| 9.12.21 \|\|

*puṣpitāḥ phalavaṃtaśca tarpayaṃtīha mānavān \|*  
*iha loke pare caiva putrāste dharmataḥ smṛtāḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. While in bloom or when they bear fruits, the trees delight human beings. They are as sons by way of virtue in this world and hereafter.

## \|\| 9.12.22 \|\|

*taḍāgakṛdvṛkṣaropī ceṣṭayajñaśca yo dvijaḥ \|*  
*ete svargānna hīyaṃte ye cānye satyavādinaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. These people never fail to attain heaven—viz. the digger of lakes, the planter of trees, the brahmin who performs sacrifices and those who speak truth.

## \|\| 9.12.23 \|\|

*satyameva paraṃ brahma satyameva paraṃ tapaḥ \|*  
*satyameva paro yajñassatyameva paraṃ śrutam \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. Truth alone is the great Brahman, the greatest penance (tapas), the greatest sacrifice and the greatest learning.

## \|\| 9.12.24 \|\|

*satyaṃ supteṣu jāgarti satyaṃ ca paramaṃ padam \|*  
*satyenaiva dhṛtā pṛthvī satye sarvaṃ pratiṣṭhitam \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. When others sleep, truth is wakeful. Truth is the greatest base, it is truth alone by which the earth is sustained, everything is founded in truth.

## \|\| 9.12.25 \|\|

*tato yajñaśca puṇyaṃ ca devarṣipitṛpūjane \|*  
*āpo vidyā ca te sarve sarvaṃ satye pratiṣṭhitam \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. Penance, sacrifice, merit, the worship of the gods, sages and the Pitṛs, the waters, theVidyās—all these things are established in truth.

## \|\| 9.12.26 \|\|

*satyaṃ yajñastapo dānaṃ maṃtrā devī sarasvatī \|*  
*brahmacaryyaṃ tathā satyamoṃkārassatyameva ca \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. Truth is sacrifice, penance, charitable gift, mantras, goddess Sarasvatī, celibacy and Oṃkāra. These are really truth.

## \|\| 9.12.27 \|\|

*satyena vāyurabhyeti satyena tapate raviḥ \|*  
*satyenāgnirnirdahati svargassatyena tiṣṭhati \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. It is truth whereby the wind blows, the sun blazes, the fìre burns and the heaven is upheld.

## \|\| 9.12.28 \|\|

*pālanaṃ sarvavedānāṃ sarvatīrthāvagāhanam \|*  
*satyena vahate loke sarvamāpnotyasaṃśayam \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. By upholding truth the people can attain the benefits of keeping up the traditions of all the Vedas and taking the ceremonial ablutions in all holy centres.

## \|\| 9.12.29 \|\|

*aśvamedhasahasraṃ ca satyaṃ ca tulayā dhṛtam \|*  
*lakṣāṇi kratavaścaiva satyameva viśiṣyate \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. When weighed against truth in a balance, a thousand horse-sacrifices and a hundred thousand other sacrifices do not equal it. Truth alone excels.

## \|\| 9.12.30 \|\|

*satyena devāḥ pitaro mānavoragarākṣasāḥ \|*  
*prīyaṃte satyatassarve lokāśca sacarācarāḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. The gods, the Pitṛs, the human beings, the serpents, the Rākṣasas and the worlds including the mobile and immobile beings are delighted by Truth.

## \|\| 9.12.31 \|\|

*satyamāhuḥ paraṃ dharmaṃ satyamāhuḥ paraṃ padam \|*  
*satyamāhuḥ paraṃ brahma tasmātsatyaṃ sadā vadet \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. They say that Truth is the greatest virtue, the greatest region, the greatest Brahman; hence one shall tell the Truth always.

## \|\| 9.12.32 \|\|

*munayassatyaniratāstapastaptvā suduścaram \|*  
*satyadharmaratāssiddhāstatassvargaṃ ca te gatāḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. After performing very difficult penance, it is by depending solely on Truth, by maintaining truthful virtue that the sages have attained Siddhis and reached heaven.

## \|\| 9.12.33 \|\|

*apsarogaṇasaṃviṣṭairvimānaiḥparimātṛbhiḥ \|*  
*vaktavyaṃ ca sadā satyaṃ na satyādvidyate param \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. The sages have attained heaven travelling in aerial chariots along with the celestial damsels. Truth shall be ever spoken, for there is nothing greater than Truth.

## \|\| 9.12.34 \|\|

*agādhe vipule siddhe satyatīrthe śucihrade \|*  
*snātavyaṃ manasā yuktaṃ sthānaṃ tatparamaṃ smṛtam \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. In the deep, extensive and pure eddy, the holy centre of Truth, one shall mentally take one’s ceremonial ablution. That is said to be the highest bliss.

## \|\| 9.12.35 \|\|

*ātmārthe vā parārthe vā putrārthe vāpi mānavāḥ \|*  
*anṛtaṃ ye na bhāṣaṃte te narāssvargagāminaḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. Men who never utter a lie whether for their own sake, or for other’s sake, or even for their own son’s sake, do go to heaven.

## \|\| 9.12.36 \|\|

*vedā yajñāstathā maṃtrāssaṃti vipreṣu nityaśaḥ \|*  
*nobhāṃtyapi hyasatyeṣu tasmātsatyaṃ samācaret \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. The Veda’s, the sacrifices and the Mantras exist in brahmins for ever. They are never visible to the untruthful. Hence one shall always speak truth.

## \|\| 9.12.37 \|\|

*\|\| vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*tapaso me phalaṃ brūhi punareva viśeṣataḥ \|*  
*sarveṣāṃ caiva varṇānāṃ brāhmaṇānāṃ tapodhana \|\| 37 \|\|*

Vyāsa said:—

37\. O ascetic, please explain again with special emphasis on the fruit of penance for the brahmins as well as other castes.

## \|\| 9.12.38 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*pravakṣyāmi tapo'dhyāyaṃ sarva kāmārthasādhakam \|*  
*suduścaraṃ dvijātīnāṃ tanme nigadataḥ śṛṇu \|\| 38 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

38\. I shall explain the section on penance (tapas) which is the means of achieving all the objects of desire, very difficult to be performed even by brahmins. Please listen even as I explain.

## \|\| 9.12.39 \|\|

*tapo hi paramaṃ proktaṃ tapasā vidyate phalam \|*  
*taporatā hi ye nityaṃ modaṃte saha daivataiḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. Penance is the greatest rite. Fruits are achieved by penance. Those who are engaged in penance incessantly rejoice along with the gods.

## \|\| 9.12.40 \|\|

*tapasā prāpyate svargastapasā prāpyate yaśaḥ \|*  
*tapasā prāpyate kāmastapassarvārthasādhanam \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. Heaven, fame and love are achieved through penance. Penance is the means of achievement for all objects.

## \|\| 9.12.41 \|\|

*tapasā mokṣamāpnoti tapasā viṃdate mahat \|*  
*jñānavijñānasaṃpattiḥ saubhāgyaṃ rūpameva ca \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. One achieves great things through penance, One attains salvation, knowledge, perfect wisdom, good fortune and beauty by penance.

## \|\| 9.12.42 \|\|

*nānāvidhāni vastūni tapasā labhate naraḥ \|*  
*tapasā labhate sarvaṃ manasā yadyadicchati \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. Man obtains different things through penance; he gets everything, whatever he wishes in his mind through penance.

## \|\| 9.12.43 \|\|

*nātaptatapaso yāṃti brahmalokaṃ kadācana \|*  
*nātaptatapasāṃ prāpyaśśaṃkaraḥ parameśvaraḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. Those who have never performed penance (tapas) do not attain Brahmaloka; lord Śiva is not accessible to those who have never performed penance.

## \|\| 9.12.44 \|\|

*yatkāryaṃ kiṃcidāsthāya puruṣastapate tapaḥ \|*  
*tatsarvaṃ samavāpnoti paratreha ca mānavaḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. After deciding on a particular thing, if a man performs penance he attains it here as well as hereafter.

## \|\| 9.12.45 \|\|

*surāpaḥ pāradārī ca brahmahā gurutalpagaḥ \|*  
*tapasā tarate sarvaṃ sarvataśca vimuṃcati \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. The wine-addict, the defiler of other women, the brahmin-slayer, the defiler of teacher’s bed overcome all sins through penance and get rid of them.

## \|\| 9.12.46-47 \|\|

*api sarveśvaraḥ sthāṇurviṣṇu ścaiva sanātanaḥ \|*  
*brahmā hutāśanaḥ śakro ye cānye tapasānvitāḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

*aṣṭāśītisahasrāṇi munīnāmūrddhvaretasām \|*  
*tapasā divi modaṃte sametā daivataissaha \|\| 47 \|\|*

46-47. Śiva, the lord of all, the eternal Viṣṇu, Brahmā, fire god, Indra and others are endowed with penance. The eighty-eight thousand sages of sublimated semen rejoice in heaven through penance, along with the gods.

## \|\| 9.12.48 \|\|

*tapasā labhyate rājyaṃ sa ca śakrassureśvaraḥ \|*  
*tapasā'pālayatsarvamahanyahani vṛtrahā \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. Kingdoms are achieved through penance. Indra, the lord of gods and the slayer of Vṛtra, protects everything day by day through penance.

## \|\| 9.12.49 \|\|

*sūryyācandramasau devau sarvalokahite ratau \|*  
*tapasaiva prakāśaṃte nakṣatrāṇi grahāstathā \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. Due to penance (tapas), the lords Sun and the Moon are engaged in the welfare of the worlds. The stars and the planets too shine by virtue of their penance.

## \|\| 9.12.50 \|\|

*na cāsti tatsukhaṃ loke yadvinā tapasā kila \|*  
*tapasaiva sukhaṃ sarvamiti vedavido viduḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. There is no happiness in the world that can be gained without penance. The knowers of the Vedas understand that all happiness is attained through penance alone.

## \|\| 9.12.51 \|\|

*jñānaṃ vijñānamārogyaṃ rūpavattvaṃ tathaiva ca \|*  
*saubhāgyaṃ caiva tapasā prāpyate sarvadā sukham \|\| 51 \|\|*

51\. Knowledge, perfect wisdom, health, beauty, good fortune and perpetual happiness are the offshoots of penance.

## \|\| 9.12.52 \|\|

*tapasā sṛjyate viśvaṃ brahmā viśvaṃ vināśramam \|*  
*pāti viṣṇurharo'pyatti dhatte śeṣo'khilāṃ mahīm \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. It is by penance that Brahmā creates the universe; Viṣṇu protects it without any strain; Śiva annihilates and Śeṣa upholds the entire earth.

## \|\| 9.12.53 \|\|

*viśvāmitro gādhisutastapasaiva mahāmune \|*  
*kṣatriyo'thābhavadvipraḥ prasiddhaṃ tribhavetvidam \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. O great sage, the Kṣatriya king Viśvāmitra, the son of Gādhi, became a brahmin through penance. This is well known in the three worlds.

## \|\| 9.12.54 \|\|

*ityuktaṃ te mahāprājña tapomāhātmyamuttamam \|*  
*śṛṇvadhyayanamāhātmyaṃ tapaso'dhikamuttamam \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. O intelligent one, thus the excellent glory of penance (tapas) has been mentioned to you. Now listen to the glory of the study of the Vedas which is far more excellent than penance itself.

## Chapter 13 - The glory of the Purāṇas

## \|\| 9.13.1 \|\|

*\|\| sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*tapastapati yo'raṇye vanyamūlaphalāśanaḥ \|*  
*yo'dhīte ṛcamekāṃ hi phalaṃ syāttatsamaṃ mune \|\| 1 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

1\. O sage, the fruit is the same whether one performs penance in a forest restricting his diet to roots and fruits there or studies a single Vedic verse.

## \|\| 9.13.2 \|\|

*śruteradhyayanātpuṇyaṃ yadāpnoti dvijottamaḥ \|*  
*tadadhyāpanataścāpi dviguṇaṃ phalamaśnute \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. By teaching the Veda, an excellent brahmin attains twice the merit that he attains by studying it.

## \|\| 9.13.3 \|\|

*jagadyathā nirālokaṃ jāyate'śaśibhāskaram \|*  
*vinā tathā purāṇaṃ hyadhyeyamasmānmune sadā \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. The universe will go without light if the moon and the sun were not present. The same will happen if the Purāṇas too, O sage, were not.

## \|\| 9.13.4 \|\|

*tapyamānaṃ sadājñānānniraye yo'pi śāstrataḥ \|*  
*sambodhayati lokaṃ taṃ tasmātpūjyaḥ purāṇagaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. A Purāṇist enlightens the people through Śāstras, the people who are in distress in the hell due to ignorance. Hence he shall be worshipped.

## \|\| 9.13.5 \|\|

*sarveṣāṃ caiva pātrāṇāṃ madhye śreṣṭhaḥ purāṇavit \|*  
*patanāttrāyate yasmāttasmātpātramudāhṛtam \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Among all the deserving men the knower of Purāṇa is the most excellent. He is considered worthy because he saves people from fall

## \|\| 9.13.6 \|\|

*martyabuddhirna kartavyā purāṇajñe kadācana \|*  
*purāṇajñassarvavettā brahmā viṣṇurharo guruḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. Never shall a Purāṇist be considered an ordinary man. A preceptor proficient in Purāṇa is omniscient. He is Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva.

## \|\| 9.13.7 \|\|

*dhanaṃ dhānyaṃ hiraṇyaṃ ca vāsāṃsi vividhāni ca \|*  
*deyaṃ purāṇavijñāya paratreha ca śarmmaṇe \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. For the welfare here and hereafter, wealth, food-grain, gold, different clothes etc. shall be given to a Purāṇist as gift,

## \|\| 9.13.8 \|\|

*yo dadāti mahāprītyā purāṇajñāya sajjanaḥ \|*  
*pātrāya śubhavastūni sa yāti paramāṃ gatim \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. The good man who out of pleasure gives auspicious things to a Purāṇist who is a deserving person, attains the greatest salvation.

## \|\| 9.13.9 \|\|

*mahīṃ gāṃ vā syaṃdanāṃśca gajānaśvāṃśca śobhanān \|*  
*yaḥ prayacchati pātrāya tasya puṇyaphalaṃ śṛṇu \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Listen to the merit that a person derives from lands, cows, chariots, elephants and good horses to a deserving person.

## \|\| 9.13.10 \|\|

*akṣayānsarvakāmāṃśca paratreha ca janmani \|*  
*aśvamedhamakhasyāpi sa phalaṃ labhate pumān \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. That man derives the fruit of horse-sacrifice, and realises all his cherished desires without wastage, both here and hereafter.

## \|\| 9.13.11 \|\|

*mahī dadāti yastasmai kṛṣṭāṃ phalavatīṃ śubhām \|*  
*sa tārayati vai vaṃśyāndaśa pūrvāndaśāparān \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. He who gives him a good fertile and well ploughed land facilitates the redemption of the members of his family upto ten generations before him and after him.

## \|\| 9.13.12 \|\|

*iha bhuktvākhilānkāmānaṃte divyaśarīravān \|*  
*vimānena ca divyena śivalokaṃ sa gacchati \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. After enjoying all the pleasures here, he becomes endowed with a divine body on death and goes to Śivaloka, by a divine aerial chariot.

## \|\| 9.13.13 \|\|

*na yajñaistuṣṭimāyāṃti devāḥ prokṣaṇakairapi \|*  
*balibhiḥ puṣpapūjābhiryathā pustakavācanaiḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. The gods are not satisfied so much with sacrifices, Prokṣaṇakas (immolations of animals at the sacrifices), oblations and floral worships as with the recitation of books.

## \|\| 9.13.14 \|\|

*śaṃbhorāyatane yastu kārayeddharmapustakam \|*  
*viṣṇorarkasya kasyāpi śṛṇu tasyāpi tatphalam \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. Listen to the meritorious benefits of that man who makes arrangement for the Sacred Text book in the temple of Śiva, Viṣṇu, the Sun or any one else.

## \|\| 9.13.15 \|\|

*rājasūyāśvamedhānāṃ phalamāpnoti mānavaḥ \|*  
*sūryalokaṃ ca bhittvāśu brahmalokaṃ sa gacchati \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. The man derives the benefit of Rājasūyas and Aśvamedhas. Piercing through the sun’s sphere he goes to to the Brahmaloka

## \|\| 9.13.16 \|\|

*sthitvā kalpaśatānyatra rājā bhavati bhūtale \|*  
*bhuṃkte niṣkaṃṭakaṃ bhogānnātra kāryā vicāraṇā \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. After remaining there for hundreds of Kalpas he becomes a king on the earth. He enjoys the pleasures without pinpricks. No hesitation need be entertained in this regard.

## \|\| 9.13.17 \|\|

*aśvamedhasahasrasya yatphalaṃ samudāhṛtam \|*  
*tatphalaṃ samāvāpnoti devāgre yo japaṃ caret \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. He who performs Japas in front of the deity obtains that benefit which is mentioned as the fruit of a thousand horse-sacrifices.

## \|\| 9.13.18 \|\|

*itihāsapurāṇābhyāṃ śambhorāyatane śubhe \|*  
*nānyatprītikaraṃ śaṃbhostathānyeṣāṃ divaukasām \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. Nothing else Is delightful to Śiva and the heaven-dwellers more than the recitation of Itihāsas and Purāṇas in the auspicious temples of Śiva.

## \|\| 9.13.19 \|\|

*tasmātsarvaprayatnena kāryaṃ pustakavācanam \|*  
*tathāsya śravaṇaṃ premṇā sarvakāmaphalapradam \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. Hence arrangements shall be assiduously made for the reciting of the book. Listening to it with devotion and love yields the fruit of all cherished desires.

## \|\| 9.13.20 \|\|

*purāṇaśravaṇācchaṃbhorniṣpāpo jāyate naraḥ \|*  
*bhuktvā bhogānsuvipulāñchivalokamavāpnuyāt \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. Man becomes free from sins on listening to Śivapurāṇa. After enjoying immense pleasures he attains the world of Śiva.

## \|\| 9.13.21 \|\|

*rājasūyena yatpuṇyamagniṣṭomaśatena ca \|*  
*tatpuṇyaṃ labhate śaṃbhoḥ kathāśravaṇamātrataḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. Merely by listening to the story of Śiva one derives the merit similar to that resulting from Rājasūya and a hundred Agniṣṭoma rites.

## \|\| 9.13.22 \|\|

*sarvatīrthāvagāhena gāṃ koṭipradānataḥ \|*  
*tat phalaṃ labhate śaṃbhoḥ kathāśravaṇato mune \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. O sage, by listening to the story of Śiva one derives the benefit and merit derived from ablution in the sacred rivers and from gifting away a crore of cows.

## \|\| 9.13.23 \|\|

*ye śṛṇvaṃti kathāṃ śaṃbhossadā bhuvanapāvanīm \|*  
*te manuṣyā na maṃtavyā rudrā eva na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. Those who constantly listen to Śiva’s story that sanctifies the worlds are not mere human beings, they are Rudras. There is no doubt in this.

## \|\| 9.13.24 \|\|

*śṛṇvatāṃ śivasatkīrtiṃ satāṃ kīrtayatāṃ ca tām \|*  
*padāmbujarajāṃsyeva tīrthāni munayo viduḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. Sages consider the dust in the lotus-feet of those who listen to the holy glory of Śiva and those who constantly glorify it, to be as holy as the holy centres.

## \|\| 9.13.25 \|\|

*gaṃtuṃ niḥśreyasaṃ sthānaṃ ye'bhivāṃchanti dehinaḥ \|*  
*kathāṃ paurāṇikīṃ śaivīṃ bhaktyā śṛṇvaṃtu te sadā \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. Let those embodied beings who desire to attain salvation listen always the Purāṇic story of Śiva with devotion.

## \|\| 9.13.26 \|\|

*kathāṃ paurāṇikīṃ śrotuṃ yadyaśaktassadā bhavet \|*  
*niyatātmā pratidinaṃ śṛṇuyādvā muhūrtakam \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. If any one is unable to listen to the Purāṇic story let him listen at least for a Muhūrta every day with pure mind.

## \|\| 9.13.27 \|\|

*yadi pratidinaṃ śrotumaśakto mānavo bhavet \|*  
*puṇyamāsādiṣu mune śṛṇuyācchāṃkarīṃ kathām \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. O sage, if any man, is unable to listen to the story of Śiva every day let him listen to it in the course of holy months, days etc.

## \|\| 9.13.28 \|\|

*śaivīṃ kathāṃ hi śṛṇvānaḥ puruṣo hi munīśvara \|*  
*sa nistarati saṃsāraṃ dagdhvā karmamahāṭavīm \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. O great sage, he who regularly listens to Śiva’s story crosses the ocean of worldly existence after burning away the great forest of Karman.

## \|\| 9.13.29 \|\|

*kathāṃ śaivīṃ muhūrtaṃ vā tadarddhaṃ kṣaṇaṃ ca vā \|*  
*ye śṛṇvati narā bhaktyā na teṣāṃ durgatirbhavet \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. No mishap befalls those men who listen to Śiva’s story even for a Muhūrta, or half that time or even for a moment with devotion.

## \|\| 9.13.30 \|\|

*yatpuṇyaṃ sarvadāneṣu sarvayajñeṣu vā mune \|*  
*śaṃbhoḥ purāṇaśravaṇāttatphalaṃ niścalaṃ bhavet \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. O sage, certainly the fruit derived from listening to Śivapurāṇa is the same as that derived from making all charitable gifts or from performing all sacrifices.

## \|\| 9.13.31 \|\|

*viśeṣataḥ kalau vyāsa purāṇaśravaṇādṛte \|*  
*paro dharmo na puṃsāṃ hi muktidhyānaparaḥ smṛtaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. O Vyāsa, especially in the age of Kali there is no greater virtue aimed at salvation and meditation than listening to the Purāṇas.

## \|\| 9.13.32 \|\|

*purāṇaśravaṇaṃ śambhornāmasaṃkīrtanaṃ tathā \|*  
*kalpadrumaphalaṃ ramyaṃ manuṣyāṇāṃ na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. There is no doubt that the listening to Śiva-Purāṇa and the holy recital of his names yield fruits readily like the Kalpa tree to human beings.

## \|\| 9.13.33 \|\|

*kalau durmedhasāṃ puṃsāṃ dharmācārojhjhitātmanām \|*  
*hitāya vidadhe śambhuḥ purāṇākhyaṃ sudhārasam \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. Śiva has created the nectarine juice in the form of Purāṇa for the benefit of men of poor intellect in the Kali age who have eschewed virtue and good conduct.

## \|\| 9.13.34 \|\|

*eko'jarāmarasyādvai pibannevāmṛtaṃ pumān \|*  
*śambhoḥ kathāmṛtāpānātkulamevājarāmaram \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. Only one person becomes free from old age and death by drinking the nectar Amṛta. But by drinking the nectarine story of Śiva the entire family becomes free from death and old age.

## \|\| 9.13.35 \|\|

*yā gatiḥ puṇyaśīlānāṃ yajvanāṃ ca tapasvinām \|*  
*sā gatissahasā tāta purāṇaśravaṇātkhalu \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. O dear, one instantaneously attains by listening to the Purāṇas that salvation which meritorious ascetics and performers of sacrifices derive.

## \|\| 9.13.36 \|\|

*jñānavāptiryadā na syādyogaśāstrāṇi yatnataḥ \|*  
*adhyetavyāni paurāṇaṃ śāstraṃ śrotavyameva ca \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. As long as perfect knowledge is not attained, Yogaśāstras shall be learnt assiduously and the Purāṇic text be listened to.

## \|\| 9.13.37 \|\|

*pāpaṃ saṃkṣīyate nityaṃ dharmmaścaiva vivarddhate \|*  
*purāṇaśravaṇājjñānī na saṃsāraṃ prapadyate \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. By listening to the Purāṇas, sin is reduced and eliminated; virtue is enhanced; and the wise man does not re-enter worldly life,

## \|\| 9.13.38 \|\|

*ataeva purāṇāni śrotavyāni prayatnataḥ \|*  
*dharmārthakāmalābhāya mokṣamārgāptaye tathā \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. Hence indeed, the Purāṇas shall be assiduously listened to for the achievement of virtue, wealth, love and salvation.

## \|\| 9.13.39 \|\|

*yajñairdānaistapobhistu yatphalaṃ tīrthasevayā \|*  
*tatphalama samavāpnoti purāṇaśravaṇānnaraḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. Man attains, thanks to listening to the Purāṇas, that benefit which is attained by sacrifices, charitable gifts, penances and pilgrimages.

## \|\| 9.13.40 \|\|

*na bhaveyuḥ purāṇāni dharmamārge kṣaṇāni tu \|*  
*yadyatra yadvratī sthātā cātra pāratrakī kathām \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. If there be no purāṇa showing the path of Dharma, there will be no person observing Vrata or interested in the life hereafter.

## \|\| 9.13.41 \|\|

*ṣaḍviṃśatipurāṇānāṃ madhye'pyekaṃ śṛṇoti yaḥ \|*  
*paṭhedvā bhaktiyuktastu sa mukto nātra saṃśayaḥ \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. He who listens to at least one among the thirty six Purāṇas or reads it with devotion is liberated. There is no doubt in this.

## \|\| 9.13.42 \|\|

*anyo na dṛṣṭassukhado hi mārgaḥ purāṇamārgo hi sadā variṣṭhaḥ \|*  
*śāstraṃ vinā sarvamidaṃ na bhāti sūryyeṇa hīnā iva jīvalokāḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. No other path so pleasing is there. The path of the Purāṇas is the most excellent. Nothing in the world shines without the Śāstra as the worlds of living beings without the sun.

## Chapter 14 - General charitable gifts

## \|\| 9.14.1 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*śastāni ghoradānāni mahādānāni nityaśaḥ \|*  
*pātrebhyastu pradeyāni ātmānaṃ tārayaṃti ca \|\| 1 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

1\. Ten charitable gifts are called great gifts (mahādāna) and are praiseworthy. They shall be given to deserving persons every day. They enable the soul to cross the ocean of worldly existence.

## \|\| 9.14.2 \|\|

*hiraṇyadānaṃ godānaṃ bhūmidānaṃ dvijottama \|*  
*gṛhṇaṃto vai pavitrāṇi tārayaṃti svameva tam \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. The charitable gifts of gold (hiraṇyadāna), cow (godāna) and land (bhūmidāna), O excellent brahmin, are very holy. Those who make them redeem themselves.

## \|\| 9.14.3 \|\|

*suvarṇadānaṃ godānaṃ pṛthivīdānameva ca \|*  
*etāni śreṣṭhadānāni kṛtvā pāpaiḥ pramucyate \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. There are gifts of gold, cows and land, by making these excellent gifts one is liberated from sins.

## \|\| 9.14.4 \|\|

*tulādānāni śastāni gāvaḥ pṛthvī sarasvatī \|*  
*dve tu tulyabale śaste hyadhikā ca sarasvatī \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. Tulādānas are highly praised like the gifts of cows. Gifts of lands and of speech are of equal potency and deserve praise. But gifts of speech shall be greater.

## \|\| 9.14.5 \|\|

*nitya hyanuḍuho gāvacchatraṃ vastramupānahau \|*  
*deyāni yācamānebhyaḥ pānamannaṃ tathaiva ca \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. These things shall be given everyday to those who beg for them—viz. oxen, cows, umbrella, cloth, pairs of shoes, drink and food.

## \|\| 9.14.6 \|\|

*saṃkalpavihitoyo'rtho brāhmaṇebhyaḥ pradīyate \|*  
*arthibhyo'pīḍitebhyaśca manasvī tena jāyate \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. An object which is given to brahmins, suppliants and distressed people after performing the Saṃkalpa rites enables the donee to become greatly intelligent.

## \|\| 9.14.7 \|\|

*kanakaṃ ca tilā nāgāḥ kanyā dāsī gṛhaṃ rathaḥ \|*  
*maṇayaḥ kapilā gāvo mahādānāni vai daśa \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. There are ten great gifts—viz. gold, gingelly seeds, elephants, virgins, servant-maid, house, chariot, jewels, the tawny coloured cow and ordinary cows.

## \|\| 9.14.8 \|\|

*gṛhītvaitāni sarvāṇi brāhmaṇo jñānavitsadā \|*  
*vadānyāṃstārayetsadyo hyātmānaṃ ca na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. After taking all these a brahmin of great wisdom enables the donee to cross the ocean of worldly existence as well as himself.

## \|\| 9.14.9 \|\|

*suvarṇaṃ ye prayacchaṃti narāśśuddhena cetasā \|*  
*devatāstaṃ prayacchaṃti samaṃtāditi me śruvam \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. If men make gifts of gold with pious mind, the gods bestow the same on him. Thus I have heard.

## \|\| 9.14.10 \|\|

*agnirhi devatāssarvāḥ suvarṇaṃ ca hutāśanaḥ \|*  
*tasmātsuvarṇaṃ dattvā ca dattāssyussarvadevatāḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Gold is fìre. Indeed fire represents all gods. Hence by making the gift of gold one makes the gift of all gods.

## \|\| 9.14.11 \|\|

*pṛthvīdānaṃ mahāśreṣṭhaṃ sarvakāmaphalapradam \|*  
*sauvarṇaṃ ca viśeṣeṇa yatkṛtaṃ pṛthunā purā \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Gift of lands is very excellent and it yields the fruits of all desires. The gift of gold is also excellent and it was made by king Pṛthu formerly.

## \|\| 9.14.12 \|\|

*dīyamānāṃ prapaśyaṃti pṛthvīṃ rukmasamanvitām \|*  
*sarvapāpavinirmuktāste yāṃti paramāṃ gatim \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. If plots of lands are given along with gold it is praiseworthy. They become liberated from all sins and attain the greatest goal.

## \|\| 9.14.13 \|\|

*athānyacca pravakṣyāmi dānaṃ sarvottamaṃ mune \|*  
*kāṃtāraṃ yanna paśyaṃti yamasya bahuduḥkhadam \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. O sage, I shall mention another gift which is the most excellent and whereby people do not see the forest of Yama, the cause of many miseries.

## \|\| 9.14.14 \|\|

*kuryātkāṃtāradānaṃ hi vidhinā śuddhamānasaḥ \|*  
*nyāyārjitena dravyeṇa vittaśāṭhyavivarjitaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. One shall make the charitable gift of forest with due observance of rules and pious mind. The wealth shall be earned by justiciable means. The donee shall be devoid of stinginess associated with affluence.

## \|\| 9.14.15-19 \|\|

*tilaprasthamayīṃ kṛtvā dhenuṃ sarvaguṇānvitām \|*  
*dhenuvatsaṃ suvarṇaṃ ca sudivyaṃ sarvalakṣaṇam \|\| 15 \|\|*

*padmamaṣṭadalaṃ kṛtvā kuṃkumāktākṣataiśśubhaiḥ \|*  
*pūjayettatra rudrādīnsarvāndevānsubhaktitaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

*evaṃ saṃpūjya tāṃ dadyādbrāhmaṇāya svaśaktitaḥ \|*  
*saratnāṃ sahiraṇyāṃ ca sarvābharaṇabhūṣitām \|\| 17 \|\|*

*tato naktaṃ samaśnīyāddīpāndadyāttu vistarāt \|*  
*kārtikyāmiti kartavyaṃ pūrṇimāyāṃ prayatnataḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

*evaṃ yaḥ kurute samyagvidhānena svaśaktitaḥ \|*  
*yamamārgabhayaṃ ghoraṃ narakaṃ ca na paśyati \|\| 19 \|\|*

15-19. With a Prastha measure of gingelly seeds an image of a cow endowed with all qualities shall be made. The calf shall be made of gold. It shall be divine in shape and it shall possess all characteristics. The mystic diagram of the eight-petalled lotus shall be made with auspicious Vermillion and raw rice grains. The devotee shall worship Rudra and all other gods there with great devotion. After the worship the cow and the calf shall be given to a brahmin along with gems and gold according to ability and bedecked too in all ornaments. The devotee shall take food only in the night. He shall then make elaborate gifts of lamps. This shall be done on the full moon day in the month of Kārttika ‘strenuously. He who does thus according to his ability but strictly observing the rules does not see the terrible hell or the path of Yama.

## \|\| 9.14.20 \|\|

*kṛtvā pāpānyaśeṣāṇi sabaṃdhussasuhṛjjanaḥ \|*  
*divi saṃkrīḍate vyāsa yāvadindrāścaturdaśa \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. O Vyāsa, even after committing sins, the man enjoys in the heaven along with his kinsmen and friends as long as fourteen Indras rule over it

## \|\| 9.14.21 \|\|

*vidhito gośca dānaṃ vai sarvottamamiha smṛtam \|*  
*na tena sadṛśaṃ vyāsa paraṃ dānaṃ prakīrtitam \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. The gift of the cow duly performed is the most excellent. O Vyāsa, no other gift is glorified so much as this.

## \|\| 9.14.22-23 \|\|

*prayacchate yaḥ kapilāṃ savatsāṃ svarṇaśṛṃgikām \|*  
*kāṃsyapātrāṃ raupyakhurāṃ sarvalakṣaṇalakṣitām \|\| 22 \|\|*

*taistairguṇaiḥ kāmadughā bhūtvā sā gaurupaiti tam \|*  
*pradātāraṃ naraṃ vyāsa paratreha ca janmani \|\| 23 \|\|*

22-23. If any one makes the gift of a tawny cow along with its calf adorning the horns with gold, the hoofs with silver and endowed with other characteristics and also gives a bell metal vessel, that cow returns to him in the form of the wish-yielding celestial cow. O Vyāsa, the donee is thus blessed both here and hereafer.

## \|\| 9.14.24 \|\|

*yadyadiṣṭatamaṃ loke yadasti dayitaṃ gṛhe \|*  
*tattadguṇavate deyaṃ tadevākṣayamicchatā \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. One shall make gifts of such things to a man of good qualities, such thing as he likes most in the world, as are very dear to him in the house and as are wished for by him to be his permanent possessions.

## \|\| 9.14.25 \|\|

*tulāpuruṣadānaṃ hi dānānāṃ dānamuttamam \|*  
*tulāsaṃrohaṇaṃ kāryaṃ yadīcchecchreya ātmanaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. Gifts of anything equal in weight to the giver is the most excellent of all gifts. If he wishes for his prosperity he shall ascend the weighing balance.

## \|\| 9.14.26 \|\|

*yatkṛtvā mucyate pāpairvadhabaṃdhakṛtodbhavaiḥ \|*  
*tulādānaṃ mahatpuṇyaṃ sarvapāpakṣayaṃkaram \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. The weighing balance is highly meritorious. It eliminates all sins. On making the gift of it one gets rid of sins arising from slaughter and captivity.

## \|\| 9.14.27 \|\|

*kṛtvā pāpānyaśeṣāṇi tulādānaṃ karoti yaḥ \|*  
*sarvaistu pātakairmuktaḥ sa divaṃ yātyasaṃśayam \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. Even after committing sins if a man makes the gift of weighing balance he becomes free from all sins and goes to heaven.

## \|\| 9.14.28-30 \|\|

*pāpaṃ kṛtaṃ yaddivase niśāyāṃ dvisaṃdhyormadhyadine niśāṃte \|*  
*kālatraye kāyamanovacobhistulāpumānvai tadapākaroti \|\| 28 \|\|*

*bālena vṛddhena mayā hi yūnā vijānatā jñānapareṇa pāpam \|*  
*tatsarvamevāśu kṛtaṃ madīyaṃ tulāpumānvai haratu smarāriḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

*pātre prayuktaṃ draviṇaṃ mayā'dya pramāṇapūrṇaṃ nihitaṃ tulāyām \|*  
*tenaiva sārdhaṃ tu mamāvaśeṣaṃ kṛtākṛtaṃ yatsukṛtaṃ sametu \|\| 30 \|\|*

28-30. (The following shall be recited before making the gift). “May the presiding deity of the weighing balance remove the sin committed by me physically, mentally or verbally during the day, night, the twilights, midday) the close of the night or in any of the three times. May the presiding deity of the balance, lord Śiva, enemy of the cupid, remove all the sins done by me, whether as a boy or a youth, or an old man wakefully and consciously. Wealth has been consigned by me to a deserving person. It has been deposited in the balance according to my weight. May merit accrue to me along with this.”

## \|\| 9.14.31 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*evamuccāryya taṃ dadyāt dvijebhyaḥ sarvadā hitaḥ \|*  
*naikasyāpi pradātavyaṃ na nistārastato bhavet \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. The presiding deity shall be made after reciting the above formula It shall not be given to a single person. There is no benefit in it. It shall be distributed among many brahmins.

## \|\| 9.14.32 \|\|

*dadātyevaṃ tu yo vyāsa tulāpuruṣamuttamam \|*  
*hatvā pāpaṃ divyaṃ tiṣṭhedyāvadindrāścaturddaśa \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. O Vyāsa, he who makes this excellent gift of the presiding deity of the balance destroys his sins and attains heaven He stays there as long as the fourteen Indras rule over it.

## Chapter 15 - Description of the Nether worlds (pātāla)

## \|\| 9.15.1 \|\|

*vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*yenaikena hi dattena sarveṣāṃ prāpyate phalam \|*  
*dānānāṃ tanmamākhyā hi mānuṣāṇāṃ hitārthataḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

Vyāsa said:—

1\. For the benefit of human beings tell me that which when given as gift makes it possible to derive the benefit of all gifts.

## \|\| 9.15.2 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*śṛṇu kāleḥ pradattādvai phalaṃ viṃdaṃti mānavāḥ \|*  
*ekasmādapi sarveṣāṃ dānānāṃ tadvadāmi te \|\| 2 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

2\. Listen. I shall mention that gift on giving which, at the proper time, men obtain the benefit of all the gifts

## \|\| 9.15.3 \|\|

*dānānāmuttamaṃ dānaṃ brahmāṇḍaṃ khalu mānavaiḥ \|*  
*dātavyaṃ muktikāmaistu saṃsārottāraṇāya vai \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. This is the most excellent of all gifts. The whole universe shall be given as gift by those who desire salvation for crossing the ocean of worldly existence.

## \|\| 9.15.4 \|\|

*brahmāṃḍe sakalaṃ dattaṃ yatphalaṃ labhate naraḥ \|*  
*tadekabhāvādāpnoti saptalokādhipo bhavet \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. A man becomes the lord of the seven worlds, with a fraction of the benefit that he obtains when the whole universe is given as a gift.

## \|\| 9.15.5 \|\|

*yāvaccandradivākarau nabhasi vai yāvatsthirā medinī \|*  
*tāvatso'pi naraḥ svabāṃdhavayutassvavargaukasāmokasi \|*  
*sarveṣveva manonugeṣu kakubhirbrahmāṇḍadaḥ krīḍate \|*  
*paścādyāti padaṃ sudurlabhataraṃ devairmude mādhavam \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. The man who gifts away the universe sports in the abode of the gods along with his kinsmen as long as the moon and the sun are in the sky or the earth is steady or every one in the abode of the heaven-dwellers is favourable. Afterwards, for rejoicing, he attains the region of Viṣṇu which is difficult of access even to the gods.

## \|\| 9.15.6 \|\|

*vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*bhagavanbrūhi brāhmāṇḍaṃ yatpramāṇaṃ yadātmakam \|*  
*yadādhāraṃ yathābhūtaṃ yena me pratyayo bhavet \|\| 6 \|\|*

Vyāsa said:—

6\. O holy lord, please explain the universe, its extent, its main soul, its support and its structure so that I can have a full idea.

## \|\| 9.15.7 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*mune śṛṇu pravakṣyāmi yadutsedhaṃ tu vistaram \|*  
*brahmāṇḍaṃ tattu saṃkṣepācchrutvā pāpātpramucyate \|\| 7 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

7\. O sage, listen. I shall explain how high and how extensive is the universe. On hearing it succinctly one gets rid of sins.

## \|\| 9.15.8 \|\|

*yattatkāraṇamavyaktaṃ vyaktaṃ śivamanāmayam \|*  
*tasmātsaṃjāyate brahmā dvidhābhūtāddhi kālataḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. That which is the unmanifest ultimate cause becomes manifest as Śiva, free from ailment. When that becomes dichotomised, Brahma is born of that in due course.

## \|\| 9.15.9 \|\|

*brāhmāṇḍaṃ sṛjati brahmā caturddaśabhavātmakam \|*  
*tadvacmi kramatastāta samāsācchṛṇu yatnataḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Brahmā creates the universe consisting of the fourteen worlds. O dear, I shall succinctly explain that in proper order. Listen to it assiduously.

## \|\| 9.15.10 \|\|

*pātālāni tu saptaiva bhuvanāni tathorddhvataḥ \|*  
*ucchrāyo dviguṇastasya jalamadhye sthitasya ca \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. The nether worlds (pātāla) are seven only. The upper worlds are also seven (bhuvana). The height of the universe is twice as such and it stands in the middle of water.

## \|\| 9.15.11 \|\|

*tasyādhāraḥ sthito nāgassa ca viṣṇuḥ prakīrtitaḥ \|*  
*brahmaṇo vacaso hetorbibharti sakalaṃ tvidam \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Its support is the serpent Śeṣa. He is glorified as Viṣṇu. At the instance of Brahmā he supports all this.

## \|\| 9.15.12 \|\|

*śeṣasyāsya guṇān vaktuṃ na śaktā devadānavāḥ \|*  
*yonaṃtaḥ paṭhyate siddhairdevarṣigaṇapūjitaḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. Even the gods and the Asuras are incompetent to describe the attributes of Śeṣa who is worshipped by the gods, sages and the Gaṇas and who is called as Anante by the Siddhas.

## \|\| 9.15.13 \|\|

*śiraḥsāhasrayuktassa sarvā vidyotayandiśaḥ \|*  
*phaṇāmaṇisahasreṇa svastikāmalabhūṣaṇaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. He has a thousand hoods. By means of the thousand jewels on his hoods, he illuminates all the quarters. He has the brilliant ornament Svastika.

## \|\| 9.15.14 \|\|

*madāghūrṇitanetro'sau sāgniśśveta ivācalaḥ \|*  
*sragvī kirīṭī hyābhāti yassadaivaika kuṃḍalaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. He has only a single ear-ring. He has a crown and wears garlands. His eyes are rolling due to inebriation. He shines like a white fiery mountain.

## \|\| 9.15.15 \|\|

*sāyaṃ gaṃgāpravāheṇa śvetaśailopaśobhitaḥ \|*  
*nīlavāsā madodriktaḥ kailāsādririvāparaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. He is like a Kailāsa mountain which shines white due to the flow of the Gaṅgā. But he wears a blue cloth and is inebriated.

## \|\| 9.15.16-17 \|\|

*lāṃgalāsaktahastāgro bibhranmusalamuttamam \|*  
*yo'rcyate nāgakanyābhissvarṇavarṇābhirādarāt \|\| 16 \|\|*

*saṃkarṣaṇātmako rudro viṣānalaśikhojjvalaḥ \|*  
*kalpāṃte niṣkramante yadvaktrebhyo'gniśikhā muhuḥ \|*  
*dagdhvā jagattrayaṃ śāntā bhavaṃtītyanuśuśruma \|\| 17 \|\|*

16-17. He is worshipped by the gold-complexioned Nāga virgins (nāgakanyā) in this aspect—viz. the tips of his hands are in contact with the ploughshare. He is bearing an excellent threshing rod. He is Rudra in the form of Saṅkarṣaṇa. He is brilliant with the flame of the poisonous fire. At the end of the Kalpa, flames of fire come out of his mouths. After consuming the three worlds they subside.

## \|\| 9.15.18 \|\|

*āste pātālamūlasthassa śeṣaḥ kṣitimaṇḍalam \|*  
*bibhratsvapṛṣṭhe bhūteśaśśeṣo'śeṣaguṇārcitaḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. Holding the sphere of the earth on his back, Śeṣa, the lord of Bhūtas stands at the root of Pātāla. He is worshipped for his endless attributes.

## \|\| 9.15.19 \|\|

*tasya vīryaprabhāvaśca sākāṃkṣaistridaśairapi \|*  
*na hi varṇayituṃ śakyaḥ svarūpaṃ jñātumeva vā \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. The power of his virility cannot be adequately described even by the ambitious gods nor can his form be known.

## \|\| 9.15.20 \|\|

*āste kusumamāleva phaṇāmaṇiśilāruṇā \|*  
*yasyaiṣā sakalā pṛthvī kastadvīryaṃ vadiṣyati \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. Who will adequately express his prowess? The series of his hoods, pink with the jewels go round the entire earth like a garland of flowers.

## \|\| 9.15.21 \|\|

*yadā vijṛmbhate'naṃto madāghūrṇitalocanaḥ \|*  
*tadā calati bhūreṣā sādritoyādhikānanā \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. With his eyes rolling due to inebriation when Śeṣa stretches himself, the earth quakes along with all its mountains, oceans and forests.

## \|\| 9.15.22-23 \|\|

*daśasāhasramekaikaṃ pātālaṃ muni sattama \|*  
*atalaṃ vitalaṃ caiva sutalaṃ ca rasātalam \|\| 22 \|\|*

*talaṃ talātalaṃ cāgryaṃ pātālaṃ saptamaṃ matam \|*  
*bhūmeradhassapta lokā ime jñeyā vicakṣaṇaiḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

22-23. O excellent sage, each of the nether worlds (pātāla) extends to ten thousand Yojanas. The seven nether worlds are Atala, Vitala, Sutala, Rasātala, Tala, Talātala and the Pātāla. The seven worlds are beneath the earth. So say the learned people.

## \|\| 9.15.24 \|\|

*ucchrāyo dviguṇaścaiṣāṃ sarveṣāṃ ratnabhūmayaḥ \|*  
*ratnavanto'tha prāsādā bhūmayo hemasaṃbhavāḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. The height of each of these is twice its extent. The surfaces of all are grounds of gems. The palaces are full of gems and their terraces are made of gold.

## \|\| 9.15.25 \|\|

*teṣu dānavadaiteyā nāgānāṃ jātayastathā \|*  
*nivasaṃti mahānāgā rākṣasā daityasaṃbhavāḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. Dānavas, Daiteyas, Serpents, and Rākṣasas of the Daitya origin reside there.

## \|\| 9.15.26 \|\|

*prāha svargasadomadhye pātālānīti nāradaḥ \|*  
*svarlokādati ramyāṇi tebhyo'sāvāgato divi \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. Nārada who came to heaven from the nether region announced in the middle of the celestial assembly that the nether worlds (pātāla) are more beautiful than heaven.

## \|\| 9.15.27 \|\|

*nānābhūṣaṇabhūṣāsu maṇayo yatra suprabhāḥ \|*  
*āhlādakāni śubhrāṇi pātālaṃ kena tatsamam \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. There, in all sorts of ornaments, lustrous jewels are present. They are white and delightful. What is there equal to it?

## \|\| 9.15.28 \|\|

*pātāle kasya na prītiritaścetaśca śobhitam \|*  
*devadānavakanyābhirvimuktasyābhijāyate \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. The nether region is here and there brightened up by the daughters of Daityas and Dānavas. Even to the liberated soul, the nether region is pleasing. Which liberated soul does not like it?

## \|\| 9.15.29 \|\|

*divārkaraśmayo yatra na bhavaṃti vidho niśi \|*  
*na śītamātapo yatra maṇitejo'tra kevalam \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. There during (he daytime there are no sun’s rays, nor the moon’s rays during the night. There is neither chillness nor bright sunlight. There is only the lustre of the jewels.

## \|\| 9.15.30 \|\|

*bhakṣyabhojyānnapānāni bhujyaṃte muditairbhṛśam \|*  
*yatra na jāyate kālo gato'pi munisattama \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. O excellent sage, all sorts of foodstuffs and drinks are consumed there by extremely joyous persons. The time that passes by is not known there at all.

## \|\| 9.15.31 \|\|

*puṃskokilarutaṃ yatra padmāni kamalākarāḥ \|*  
*nadyassarāṃsi ramyāṇi hyanyonyavicarāṇi ca \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. The cooings of the male cuckoo are there. Lotuses are there. There are lotus-ponds. There are rivers and lakes. Each of them is more excellent than the other.

## \|\| 9.15.32 \|\|

*bhūṣaṇānyatiśubhrāṇi gaṃdhāḍhyaṃ cānulepanam \|*  
*vīṇāveṇumṛdaṃgānāṃ svanā geyāni ca dvija \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. There the ornaments are very brilliant. The unguents are sweet-smelling. O brahmin, the sound of Vīṇās, flutes and Mṛdaṅgas can be heard there. There are songs everywhere.

## \|\| 9.15.33 \|\|

*daityoragaiśca bhujyaṃte pātāle vai sukhāni ca \|*  
*tapasā samavāpnoti dānavaissiddhamānavaiḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. There the Daityas and serpents rejoice and enjoy pleasures. It is by performing penances that Dānavas and Siddha beings attain the same.

## Chapter 16 - Uplift from the hell

## \|\| 9.16.1 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*teṣāṃ mūrddhopariṣṭādvai narakāṃstāñchṛṇuṣva ca \|*  
*matto munivaraśreṣṭha pacyaṃte yatra pāpinaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

1\. O excellent sage, above the nether regions, are the hells where the sinners are scorched. Listen to their description from me.

## \|\| 9.16.2 \|\|

*rauravaśśūkaro rodhastālo vivasanastathā \|*  
*mahājvālastaptakuṃbho lavaṇopi vilohitaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. They are—Raurava, Śūkara, Rādha, Tāla, Vivasvat, Mahājvāla, Taptakumbha, Lavaṇa, Vilohita.

## \|\| 9.16.3 \|\|

*vaitaraṇī pūyavahā kṛmiṇaḥ kṛmibhojanaḥ \|*  
*asipatravanaṃ ghoraṃ lālābhakṣaśca dāruṇaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. The river Vaitaraṇī consists of putrid stuff flowing in it, Kṛmiṇa. Kṛmibhojana, Asipatravana and Lālābhakṣa are the terrible hells:

## \|\| 9.16.4 \|\|

*tathā pūyavahaḥ prāyo bahirjvālo hyadhaśśirāḥ \|*  
*saṃdaṃśaḥ kālasūtraśca tamaścāvīciro dhanaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. Pūyavaha has usually flames of fire outside and is topsyturvy. Sandaṃśa, Kālasūtra, Tamas, Avīcirodhana

## \|\| 9.16.5 \|\|

*śvabhojano'tha ruṣṭaśca mahārauravaśālmalī \|*  
*ityādyā bahavastatra narakā duḥkhadāyakāḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Śvabhojana, Ruṣṭa, Mahāraurava and Śālmali—these and other bells are there; all of these are very grievous.

## \|\| 9.16.6 \|\|

*pacyaṃte teṣu puruṣāḥ pāpakarmaratāstu ye \|*  
*kramādvakṣye tu tān vyāsa sāvadhānatayā śṛṇu \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. Men who are indulgent in sins are scorched there. O Vyāsa, I shall mention them in order. Listen attentively,

## \|\| 9.16.7 \|\|

*kūṭasākṣyaṃ tu yo vakti vinā viprān surāṃśca gāḥ \|*  
*sadā'nṛtaṃ vadedyastu sa naro yāti rauravam \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. He who commits perjury except for the brahmins, gods and cows and he who utters a lie always goes to Raurava

## \|\| 9.16.8-10 \|\|

*bhrūṇahā svarṇahartā ca gorodhī viśvaghātakaḥ \|*  
*surāpo brahmahaṃtā ca paradravyāpahārakaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

*yastatsaṃgī sa vai yāti mṛto vyāsa gurorvadhāt \|*  
*tataḥ kuṃbhe svasurmāturgoścaiva duhitustathā \|\| 9 \|\|*

*sādhvyā vikrayakṛccātha vārddhakī keśavikrayī \|*  
*taptaloheṣu pacyaṃte yaśca bhaktaṃ parityajet \|\| 10 \|\|*

8-10. The following sinners viz the destroyer of the child in the womb, the stealer of gold, of cows, one who commits the breach of trust, the wine-addict, the brahmin slayer, the stealer of other’s wealth and he who associates with these—go to the hell Kumbha, O Vyāsa. He who kills his preceptor, sister, mother, daughter or a cow too goes there. He who tells his chaste wife, he who is addicted to usury, he who sells tresses of hair and he who forsakes a devotee—all these are scorched in redhot iron.

## \|\| 9.16.11-13 \|\|

*avamaṃtā gurūṇāṃ yaḥ paścādbhoktā narādhamaḥ \|*  
*devadūṣayitā caiva devavikrayikaśca yaḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

*agamyagāmī yaścāṃte yāti saptabalaṃ dvija \|*  
*cauro goghno hi patito maryādādūṣakastathā \|\| 12 \|\|*

*devadvijapitṛdveṣṭā ratnadūṣayitā ca yaḥ \|*  
*sa yāti kṛmibhakṣaṃ vai kṛmīnatti duriṣṭakṛt \|\| 13 \|\|*

11-13. He who insults preceptors, he who dismisses visitors and then dines, he who commits blasphemy, he who sells idols and he who cohabits with forbidden women—all these, O brahmin, go to Saptabala hell. A thief, a slayer of cows, a fallen man, a defiler of boundaries, the hater of gods, brahmins and Pitṛs and the defiler of gems go to Kṛmibhakṣa hell. They eat worms and other displeasing things.

## \|\| 9.16.14-16 \|\|

*pitṛdevasurān yastu paryaśnāti narādhamaḥ \|*  
*lālābhakṣaṃ sa yātyajño yaśśastrakūṭakṛnnaraḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

*yaścāṃtyajena saṃsevyo hyasadgrāhī tu yo dvijaḥ \|*  
*ayājyayājakaścaiva tathaivābhakṣya bhakṣakaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

*rudhiraughe pataṃtyete somavikrayiṇaśca ye \|*  
*madhuhā grāmahā yāti krūrāṃ vaitaraṇīṃ nadīm \|\| 16 \|\|*

14-16. The base man who eats before Pitṛs, deities and gods and he who ignorantly or deceitfully misquotes sacred texts—these go to Lālābhakṣa hell. The brahmin who associates with evil men and is surrounded by outcastes, the brahmin who officiates as priest in the sacrifice of undeserving persons, and eats forbidden food, and he who sells Soma juice—these fall into Rudhiraugha hell. He who spoils honey and creates disturbances in the village falls in the ruthless river Vaitaraṇī.

## \|\| 9.16.17 \|\|

*navayauvanamattāśca maryādābhedinaśca ye \|*  
*te kṛtyaṃ yāṃtyaśaucāśca kulakājīvinaśca ye \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. Those who are arrogant in the freshness of youth, who transgress the bounds of decency, who are unclean and who maintain themselves on the earnings of unchaste women go to Kṛmya hell.

## \|\| 9.16.18 \|\|

*asipatravanaṃ yāti vṛkṣacchedī vṛthaiva yaḥ \|*  
*kṣurabhrakā mṛgavyādhā vahnijvāle pataṃti te \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. He who cues off trees without purpose goes to Asipatravana hell. Those who hunt deer with Kṣuraprakas (arrows with horse-shoe-shaped heads) fall into Vahnijvāla hell.

## \|\| 9.16.19 \|\|

*bhraṣṭācāro hi yo vipraḥ kṣatriyo vaiśya eva ca \|*  
*yātyaṃte dvija tatraiva yaḥ śvapākeṣu vahnidaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. O brahmin, the brahmin, the Kṣatriya or the Vaiśya who swerves from the path of good conduct ultimately fall into the hell prescribed for dog-cooking cāṇḍālas.

## \|\| 9.16.20 \|\|

*vratasya lopakā ye ca svāśramādvicyutāśca ye \|*  
*saṃdaṃśayātanāmadhye pataṃti bhṛśadāruṇe \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. Those who drop sacred rites in the middle and those who are fallen off from their duties in accordance with the stages of their life fall into the hell Sandaṃśa where the tortures are very terrible.

## \|\| 9.16.21 \|\|

*vīryaṃ svapneṣu skaṃdeyurye narā brahmacāriṇaḥ \|*  
*putrā nādhyāpitā yaiśca te pataṃti śvabhojane \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. The students who are guilty of nocturnal emission of their semen and the fathers who do not properly educate their sons fall into the Śvabhojana hell.

## \|\| 9.16.22 \|\|

*ete cānye ca narakāḥ śataśo'tha sahasraśaḥ \|*  
*yeṣu duṣkṛtakarmāṇaḥ pacyate yātanāgatāḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. There are hundreds and thousands of these and other hells where thousands of sinners undergo tortures and are scorched.

## \|\| 9.16.23 \|\|

*tathaiva pāpānyetāni tathānyāni sahasraśaḥ \|*  
*bhujyaṃte yāni puruṣairnarakāṃtaragocaraiḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. These sins and others are thousands in number similarly, which the beings in the hells experience and try to wipe off.

## \|\| 9.16.24 \|\|

*varṇāśramaviruddhaṃ ca karma kurvaṃti ye narāḥ \|*  
*karmaṇā manasā vācā niraye tu pataṃti te \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. Those who perpetrate misdeeds contrary to the injunctions of caste and stages of life whether physically, mentally or verbally fall into hell.

## \|\| 9.16.25 \|\|

*adhaśśirobhirdṛśyaṃte nārakā divi daivataiḥ \|*  
*devānadhomukhānsarvānadhaḥ paśyaṃti nārakāḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. The beings in the hells are seen standing upside down by the gods in the heaven. These beings see the gods also similarly with heads down, beneath them.

## \|\| 9.16.26-27 \|\|

*sthāvarāḥ kṛmipākāśca pakṣiṇaḥ paśavo mṛgāḥ \|*  
*dhārmikāstridaśāstadvanmokṣiṇaśca yathākramam \|\| 26 \|\|*

*yāvaṃto jaṃtavassvarge tāvaṃto narakaukasaḥ \|*  
*pāpakṛdyāti narakaṃ prāyaścittaparāṅmukhaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

26-27. Immovable beings—plants etc. and movable beings worms, insects, birds, beasts, righteous men, gods and liberated beings, all these are equal in number in heaven as also in hell. A sinner who is averse to the performance of expiatory rites goes to hell.

## \|\| 9.16.28 \|\|

*gurūṇi gurubhiścaiva laghūni laghubhistathā \|*  
*prāyaścittāni kāleya manussvāyambhuvo'bravīt \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. Svāyambhuva Manu has ordained expiatory rites for great sins and small expiatory rites for small sins in the Kali age

## \|\| 9.16.29 \|\|

*yāni teṣāmaśeṣāṇāṃ karmārṇyuktāni teṣu vai \|*  
*prāyaścittamaśeṣeṇa harānusmaraṇaṃ param \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. Of the many rites mentioned by way of expiation the remembrance of Śiva is the greatest.

## \|\| 9.16.30 \|\|

*prāyaścittaṃ tu yasyaiva pāpaṃ puṃsaḥ prajāyate \|*  
*kṛte pāpe'nutāpo'pi śivasaṃsmaraṇaṃ param \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. If a person commits a sin but repents after committing it, it is also an expiation. There also the remembrance of Śiva is the greatest expiation.

## \|\| 9.16.31 \|\|

*māheśvaramavāpnoti madhyāhnādiṣu saṃsmaran \|*  
*prātarniśi ca saṃdhyāyāṃ kṣīṇapāpo bhavennaraḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. A man remembering lord Śiva in the midday or other occasions attains lord Śiva. By remembering the lord in the morning, night or dusk, he gets his sins eliminated.

## \|\| 9.16.32 \|\|

*muktiṃ prayāti svargaṃ vā samastakleśasaṃkṣayama \|*  
*śivasya smaraṇādeva tasya śaṃbhorumāpateḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. It is only by remembering Śiva, the lord of Umā that a man attains salvation, the annihilation of all pains or heaven.

## \|\| 9.16.33 \|\|

*pāpantarāyo viprendra japahomārcanādi ca \|*  
*bhavatyeva na kutrāpi trailokye munisattama \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. O leading brahmin, Japas, Homas, worship etc. are obstacles in the path of sins. O excellent sage, they do not occur anywhere in the three worlds.

## \|\| 9.16.34 \|\|

*maheśvare matiryasya japahomārcanādipu \|*  
*yatpuṇyaṃ tatkṛtaṃ tena devendratvādikaṃ phalam \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. The attainment of the status of Indra, lord of the gods, is the fruit of the merit attained in Japa, Homa, worship etc. performed by the man whose mind is in lord Siva.

## \|\| 9.16.35 \|\|

*pumānna narakaṃ yāti yaḥ smaranbhaktito mune \|*  
*aharniśaṃ śivaṃ tasmātsa kṣīṇāśeṣapātakaḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. O sage, he who devoutly remembers Śiva day and night never goes to hell since his sins are eliminated without any vestige.

## \|\| 9.16.36 \|\|

*narakasvargasaṃjñāye pāpapuṇye dvijottama \|*  
*yayostvekaṃ tu duḥkhāyānyatsukhāyodbhavāya ca \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. O excellent brahmin, sin and merit indicate hell and heaven. One is conducive to misery and the other to pleasure and rebirth.

## \|\| 9.16.37 \|\|

*tadeva prītaye bhūtvā punarduḥkhāya jāyate \|*  
*tatsyādduḥkhātmakaṃ nāsti na ca kiṃcitsukhātmakam \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. That at the outset is conducive to happiness but later brings in misery. Hence ultimately everything is of the nature of misery. There is nothing in fact of an invariably pleasurable nature.

## \|\| 9.16.38 \|\|

*manasaḥ pariṇāmo'yaṃ sukhaduḥkhopalakṣaṇaḥ \|*  
*jñānameva paraṃ brahma jñānaṃ tattvāya kalpate \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. Happiness and misery constitute only a temporary transformation of the mind. But knowledge is the greatest Brahman. Knowledge is conducive to the understanding of reality.

## \|\| 9.16.39 \|\|

*jñānātmakamidaṃ viśvaṃ sakalaṃ sacarācaram \|*  
*paravijñānataḥ kiṃcidvidyate na paraṃ mune \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. The entire universe consisting of the mobile and immobile beings has knowledge as its soul. O sage, there is nothing greater than the perfect knowledge of the greatest being.

## \|\| 9.16.40 \|\|

*evametanmayākhyātaṃ sarvaṃ narakamaṇḍalam \|*  
*ata ūrdhvaṃ pravakṣyāmi sāṃprataṃ maṃḍalaṃ bhuvaḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. Thus the entire sphere of the hell has been explained by me. Hereafter I shall explain the sphere of the earth.

## Chapter 17 - Description of the Jambūdvīpa (jambū-dvīpa)

## \|\| 9.17.1 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*pārāśaryya susaṃkṣepācchṛṇu tvaṃ vadato mama \|*  
*maṇḍalaṃ ca bhuvassamyak saptadvīpādisaṃyutam \|\| 1 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

1\. O son of Parāśara, listen succinctly to what I explain, the sphere of the earth consisting of seven Dvīpas (continents).

## \|\| 9.17.2 \|\|

*jaṃbū plakṣaśśālmaliśca kuśaḥ krauñcaśca śākakaḥ \|*  
*puṣpakassaptamassarve samudraissaptabhirvṛtāḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. The seven continents \[see notes\] are Jambū, Plakṣa, Śālmali, Kuśa, Krauñca, Śāka, Puṣkara. These seven are surrounded by seven oceans.

## \|\| 9.17.3 \|\|

*lavaṇekṣurasau sarpirdavidugdhajalāśayāḥ \|*  
*jambudvīpassamastānāmeteṣāṃ madhyataḥ sthitaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. The seven oceans are salt sea, sugarcane juice sea, wine sea, ghee sea, curd sea, milk sea and pure water sea. Jambū Dvīpa is situated in the middle of all these.

## \|\| 9.17.4-6 \|\|

*tasyāpi meruḥ kāleyamadhye kanakaparvataḥ \|*  
*praviṣṭaḥ ṣoḍaśādhastādyojanaistasya cocchrayaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

*caturaśītimānaistairdvātriṃśanmūrdhni vistṛtaḥ \|*  
*bhūmipṛṣṭhasthaśailo'yaṃ vistarastasya sarvataḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

*mūle ṣoḍaśasāhasraḥ karṇikākāra saṃsthitaḥ \|*  
*himavān hemakūṭaśca niṣadhaścāsya dakṣiṇe \|\| 6 \|\|*

4-6. In the middle of it is the golden mountain Meru, O Kāleya. It extends below to sixteen Yojanas and its height is eighty-four Yojanas. Its expanse on the top is thirty-two Yojanas. This mountain is situated on the back of the earth and expands on all sides. It is sixteen thousand Yojanas at the base. It has the shape of a pericarp. The mountains Himavat,\[1\] Hemakūṭa\[2\] and Niṣadha are to its south.

## \|\| 9.17.7 \|\|

*nīlaḥ śvetaśca śṛṅgī ca uttare varṣaparvatāḥ \|*  
*daśasāhasrikaṃ hyete ratnavaṃto'ruṇaprabhāḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. The Varṣa mountains in the north are Nīla, Śveta and Śṛṅgin. The extent of these is ten thousand Yojanas. They are full of gems and have pinky lustre.

## \|\| 9.17.8 \|\|

*sahasrayojanotsedhāstāvadvistāriṇaśca te \|*  
*bhārataṃ prathamaṃ varṣaṃ tataḥ kiṃpuruṣaṃ smṛtam \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. Their height is a thousand Yojanas and their width too is the same. Bharata is the first sub-continent. Then there is the Kimpuruṣa.

## \|\| 9.17.9 \|\|

*harivarṣaṃ tato 'nyadvai merordakṣiṇato mune \|*  
*ramyakaṃ cottare pārśve tasyāṃśe tu hiraṇmayam \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Hari Varṣa is another which, O sage, is to the south of Meru. Ramyaka is on the northern side. In its part is Hiraṇmaya Varṣa.

## \|\| 9.17.10 \|\|

*uttare kuravaścaiva yathā vai bhārataṃ tathā \|*  
*navasāhasramekaikameteṣāṃ munisattama \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. In the north are the Kurus as well. O excellent sage, the extent of each of these, as in the case of Bharata, is nine thousand Yojanas.

## \|\| 9.17.11 \|\|

*ilāvṛtaṃ tu tanmadhye tanmadhye merurucchritaḥ \|*  
*meroścaturddiśaṃ tatra navasāhasramucchritam \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Ilāvṛta Varṣa is in its middle and Meru rises up in its middle. In all the four quarters, peaks nine thousand Yojanas in height rise up.

## \|\| 9.17.12 \|\|

*ilāvṛtamṛṣiśreṣṭha catvāraścātra parvatāḥ \|*  
*viṣkaṃbhā racitā meroryojitāḥ punarucchritāḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. O excellent sage, such is Ilāvṛta and there are four mountains here which appear as supporting beams to Meru, which are joined to it and rise above.

## \|\| 9.17.13 \|\|

*pūrve hi mandaro nāma dakṣiṇe gandhamādanaḥ \|*  
*vipulaḥ paścime bhāge supārśvaścottare sthitaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. Mandara is in the east. Gandhamādana is in the south. Vipula is in the western portion and Supārśva is situated in the north.

## \|\| 9.17.14 \|\|

*kadaṃbo jaṃbuvṛkṣaśca pippalo vaṭa eva ca \|*  
*ekādaśaśatāyāmāḥ pādapā giriketavaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. Trees acting as the flagstaff of the mountain are Kadamba, Jambu, Pippala and Vaṭa. Their height b eleven hundred Yojanas.

## \|\| 9.17.15 \|\|

*jambūdvīpasya nāmno vai hetuṃ śṛṇu mahāmune \|*  
*virājaṃte mahāvṛkṣāstatsvabhāvaṃ vadāmi te \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. O great sage, listen to the origin of the name Jambūdvīpa. There are great trees shining here. I shall explain their nature to you.

## \|\| 9.17.16 \|\|

*mahāgaja pramāṇāni jambvāstasyāḥ phalāni ca \|*  
*pataṃti bhūbhṛtaḥ pṛṣṭhe śīryyamāṇāni sarvataḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. Fruits of the Jambū tree are of the size of a huge elephant. They fall on the top of the mountain and become shattered and scattered everywhere.

## \|\| 9.17.17 \|\|

*rasena teṣāṃ vikhyātā jambūnadīti vai \|*  
*parito vartate tatra pīyate tannivāsibhiḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. The famous river Jambū is made up of the juice of Jambū fruits. It flows there and is drunk by the residents of the locality.

## \|\| 9.17.18 \|\|

*na svedo na ca daurgaṃdhyaṃ na jarā cendriyagrahaḥ \|*  
*tasyāstaṭe sthitānāntu janānāṃ tanna jāyate \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. People staying on its banks do not perspire. They do not emit bad odour and they do not want in the exercise of their sense-organs.

## \|\| 9.17.19 \|\|

*tīramṛtsnāṃ ca samprāpya mukhavāyuviśoṣitām \|*  
*jāmbūnadākhyaṃ bhavati suvarṇaṃ siddhabhūṣaṇam \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. The mud of the stream dried by blowing with the mouth transforms itself into golden ornaments for the Siddhas. It is called Jāmbūnada.

## \|\| 9.17.20 \|\|

*bhadrāśvaṃ pūrvato meroḥ ketumālaṃ ca paścime \|*  
*varṣe dve tu muniśreṣṭha tayormadhya ilāvṛtam \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. Bhadrāśva Varṣa is on the cast of Meru. Ketumāla Varṣa is on the west. Between these two there is Ilāvṛta.

## \|\| 9.17.21 \|\|

*vanaṃ caitrarathaṃ pūrve dakṣiṇe gandhamādanaḥ \|*  
*vibhrājaṃ paścime tadvaduttare nandanaṃ smṛtam \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. The forest in the east is Caitraratha, that in the south in Gandhamādana, that in the west is Vibhrāja and that in the north is Nandana.

## \|\| 9.17.22 \|\|

*aruṇodaṃ mahābhadraṃ śītodaṃ mānasaṃ smṛtam \|*  
*sarāṃsyetāni catvāri devabhogyāni sarvaśaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. There are four lakes viz. Aruṇoda, Mahābhadra, Śitoda and Mānasa. These four lakes are worthy of being enjoyed by all the gods.

## \|\| 9.17.23 \|\|

*śītāṃjanaḥ kuruṃgaśca kuraro mālyavāṃstathā \|*  
*caikaikapramukhā meroḥ pūrvataḥ kesarācalāḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. The filament-mountains of Meru on its east are Śātāñjana, Kuruṅga, Kurara and Mālyavat. Every one of them is important.

## \|\| 9.17.24 \|\|

*trikūṭaśśiśiraścaiva pataṃgo rucakastathā \|*  
*niṣadhaḥ kapilāyāśca dakṣiṇe kesarācalāḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. The filament-mountains on the south are Trikūṭa, Śiśira, Pataṅga, Rucaka, Niṣadha, Kapila and others.

## \|\| 9.17.25 \|\|

*sinī vāsaḥ kusuṃbhaśca kapilo nāradastathā \|*  
*nāgādayaśca girayaḥ paścime kesarācalāḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. The filament-mountains on the west are Sinīvāsa, Kusumbha, Kapila, Nārada, Nāga and other mountains.

## \|\| 9.17.26 \|\|

*śaṃkhacūḍo'tha ṛṣabho haṃso nāma mahīdharaḥ \|*  
*kālaṃjarādyāśca tathā uttare kesarācalāḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. The filament-mountains on the north are Śaṅkhacūḍa, Ṛṣabha, the mountain named Haṃsa, Kālañjara and others.

## \|\| 9.17.27 \|\|

*merorupari madhye hi śātakauṃbhaṃ vidheḥ puram \|*  
*caturddaśasahasrāṇi yojanāni ca saṃkhyayā \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. Śātakaumbha, the golden city of Brahmā, is on the top of Meru and in the middle. It extends to fourteen thousand Yojanas.

## \|\| 9.17.28 \|\|

*aṣṭānāṃ lokapālānāṃ paritastadanukramāt \|*  
*yathādiśaṃ yathārūpaṃ puro'ṣṭāvupakalpitāḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. All round that city are the eight cities of the eight guardians of the quarters, which have been assigned to them according to their quarters and forms.

## \|\| 9.17.29 \|\|

*tasyāṃ ca brahmaṇaḥ puryyāṃ pātayitvendumaṇḍalam \|*  
*viṣṇupādaviniṣkrāṃtā gaṃgā patati vai nadī \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. Issuing from Viṣṇu’s feet, the river Gaṅgā falls in that city of Brahmā flowing through the sphere of the moon.

## \|\| 9.17.30 \|\|

*sītā cālakanaṃdā ca cakṣurbhadrā ca vai kramāt \|*  
*sā tatra patitā dikṣu caturddhā pratyapadyata \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. Falling in four directions the river Gaṅgā flows into four streams viz. Sītā,\[9\] Alakanandā,\[10\] Cakṣus,\[11\] and Bhadrā.

## \|\| 9.17.31 \|\|

*sītā pūrveṇa śailaṃ hi nandā caiva tu dakṣiṇe \|*  
*sā cakṣuḥ paścime caiva bhadrā cottarato vrajet \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. Sītā flows to the east of the mountain, Alakanandā flows to the south, the Cakṣus flows to the west and the Bhadrā flows to the north.

## \|\| 9.17.32 \|\|

*girīnatītya sakalāṃścaturddikṣu mahāṃbudhim \|*  
*sā yayau prayatā sūtā gaṃgā tripathagāminī \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. Thus the holy river Gaṅgā flowing along three paths, flows into the great ocean in the four quarters after crossing all the mountains.

## \|\| 9.17.33 \|\|

*sunīlaniṣadhau yau tau mālyavadgandhamādanau \|*  
*teṣāṃ madhyagato meruḥ karṇikākārasaṃsthitaḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. Meru is situated in the middle of the four mountains: Sunīla, Niṣadha, Mālyavat and Gandhamādana. It has the shape of a pericarp of a lotus.

## \|\| 9.17.34 \|\|

*bhārataḥ ketumālaśca bhadrāśvaḥ kuravastathā \|*  
*patrāṇi lokapadmasya maryādālokaparvatāḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. The four Varṣas Bhārata, Ketumāla, Bhadrāśva and the Kurus are the petals of the world-lotus. The Lokaparvatas constitute the boundary.

## \|\| 9.17.35 \|\|

*jaṭharaṃ devakūṭaśca āyāme dakṣiṇottare \|*  
*gandhamādanakailāsau pūrvapaścimato gatau \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. The Devakūṭa constitutes its belly. Its extent is from south to north. Gandhamādana and Kailāsa extend from east to west.

## \|\| 9.17.36 \|\|

*pūrvapaścimato merorniṣadho nīlaparvataḥ \|*  
*dakṣiṇottaramāyātau karṇikāṃtarvyavasthitau \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. The Niṣadha and the Nīla mountains issue from the east and the west of Meru, curl down to south and north and terminate within the pericarp.

## \|\| 9.17.37 \|\|

*jaṭharādyāḥ sthitā meroryeṣāṃ dvau dvau vyavasthitau \|*  
*kesarāḥ parvatā ete śvetādyāḥ sumanoramāḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. These Kesara mountains Śveta and others, constituting the belly of Meru in twos, are very beautiful.

## \|\| 9.17.38 \|\|

*śailānāmuttare droṇyassiddhacāraṇasevitāḥ \|*  
*suramyāṇi tathā tāsu kānanāni purāṇi ca \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. To the north of the mountains are water-troughs resorted to by the Siddhas and Cāraṇas. The forests and the cities beside them are very fascinating.

## \|\| 9.17.39 \|\|

*sarveṣāṃ caiva devānāṃ yakṣagaṃdharvarakṣasām \|*  
*krīḍaṃti devadaiteyāśśailaprāyeṣvaharniśam \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. The cities are of the gods, Yakṣas, Gandharvas and Rākṣasas. The gods and the Daityas sport about on the mountains day and night.

## \|\| 9.17.40 \|\|

*dharmiṇāmālayā hyete bhaumāssvargāḥ prakīrtitāḥ \|*  
*na teṣu pāpakartāro yāṃti paśyaṃti kutracit \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. These are the abodes of righteous men. They are glorified as heavens on the earth. Sinners can never go there. They cannot be seen in their vicinity.

## \|\| 9.17.41 \|\|

*yāni kiṃpuruṣādīni varṣāṇyaṣṭau mahāmune \|*  
*na teṣu śoko nāpattyo nodvegaḥ kṣudbhayādikam \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. O great sage, in the eight Varṣas beginning with the Kimpuruṣa etc. there is no sorrow, danger, agony, hunger or fear.

## \|\| 9.17.42 \|\|

*svasthāḥ prajā nirātaṃkāssarvaduḥkhavivarjitāḥ \|*  
*daśadvādaśavarṣāṇāṃ sahasrāṇi sthirāyuṣaḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. The people are healthy, without terror and devoid of miseries. They have the longevity of ten to twelve thousand years.

## \|\| 9.17.43 \|\|

*kṛtatretādikāścaiva bhaumānyaṃbhāṃsi sarvataḥ \|*  
*na teṣu varṣate devasteṣu sthāneṣu kalpanā \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. Time is calculated there as Kṛta, Tretā etc. The lord does not shower earthly waters there.

## \|\| 9.17.44 \|\|

*saptasveteṣu nadyaśca sujātāssvarṇavā lukāḥ \|*  
*śataśassaṃti kṣudrāśca tāsu krīḍāratā janāḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. In all these seven Varṣas, the rivers are well arranged. They have sands of gold. There are hundreds of small rivers too. Auspicious men sport in them.

Notes on the seven continents (dvīpa):

ŚP. mentions seven continents. Their identifìcation is uncertain. Scholars hold different views: See Geography of the Purāṇas Ch. II. Each of the continents is surrounded by one of the seven seas and divided into several Varṣas. For instance, Jambu comprises nine varṣas of which Bharata is the one. The continents comprise mountains variously classed as maryādā, varṣa, kula, viṣkambha and kṣudra. These give rise to innumerable streams and rivers that flow in the lands and fall info the sea.

## Chapter 18 - Seven continents (varṣa)

## \|\| 9.18.1-2 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*vakṣye'haṃ bhārataṃ varṣaṃ himādreścaiva dakṣiṇe \|*  
*uttare tu samudrasya bhāratī yatra saṃsṛtiḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

*navayojanasāhasro vistāro'sya mahāmune \|*  
*svargāpavargayoḥ karmabhūmireṣā smṛtā budhaiḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

1-2. I shall explain the Bhāratavarṣa. South of the Himālaya mountain and north of the ocean is the land of Bharata. O great sage, its extent is nine thousand Yojanas. This is the land of sacred rites for attaining heaven and salvation. So say the wise people.

## \|\| 9.18.3 \|\|

*yatassaṃprāpyate puṃbhissvargo naraka eva ca \|*  
*bhāratasyāpi varṣasya nava bhedānbravīmi te \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. Heaven and hell are attained by men from here only. I shall mention nine subdivisions of the Bhārata Varṣa to you.

## \|\| 9.18.4-5 \|\|

*iṃdradyumnaḥ kaseruśca tāmravarṇo gabhastimān \|*  
*nāgadvīpastathā saumyo gandharvastvatha vāruṇaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

*ayaṃ tu navamasteṣāṃ dvīpassāgarasaṃbhṛtaḥ \|*  
*yojanānāṃ sahasraṃ tu dvīpo'yaṃ dakṣiṇottaraḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

4-5. Indradyumna, Kaseru, Tāmravarṇa Gabhastimat, Nāgadvīpa, Saumya, Gandharva, Vāruṇa and the ninth is the sub-continent Sāgarasambhṛta. This sub-continent lies North to South extending to a thousand Yojanas.

## \|\| 9.18.6 \|\|

*pūrve kirātā yasya syurdakṣiṇe yavanāḥ sthitāḥ \|*  
*paścime ca tathā jñeyā uttare hi tapasvinaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. To the east of Bhārata live the Kirātas, to the south the Yavanas; to the west Khaśas and to the north the ascetics.

## \|\| 9.18.7 \|\|

*brāhmaṇāḥ kṣatriyā vaiśyā madhye śūdrāśca bhūyaśaḥ \|*  
*ijyā yuddhapaṇā sevā vartayanto vyavasthitāḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. In the middle live the Brahmins, Kṣatriyas, Vaiśyas and Śūdras maintaining themselves by sacrifices, battles, merchandise and service.

## \|\| 9.18.8 \|\|

*maheṃdro malayassahyaḥ sudāmā carkṣaparvataḥ \|*  
*viṃdhyaśca pāriyātraśca saptātra kulaparvatāḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. There are seven Kulaparvatas viz Mahendra,\[2\] Malaya,\[3\] Sahya,\[4\] Sudāmā,\[5\] Ṛkṣaparvata,\[6\] Vindhya\[7\] and Pāriyātra.

## \|\| 9.18.9 \|\|

*vedasmṛtipurāṇādyāḥ pāriyātrodbhavā mune \|*  
*sarvapāpaharā jñeyā darśanātsparśanādapi \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. O sage, Vedas, Smṛtis, Purāṇas and other things issue from Pāriyātra. They dispel all sins, it should be known, by their very sight or touch.

## \|\| 9.18.10 \|\|

*narmadā surasādyāśca saptānyāśca sahasraśaḥ \|*  
*viṃdhyodbhavā mahānadyassarvapāpaharāśśubhāḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. The great rivers issuing from Vindhya are seven, such as Narmadā,\[9\] Surasā etc, and thousands of smaller ones are all auspicious. They quell all sins.

## \|\| 9.18.11 \|\|

*godāvarī bhīmarathī tāpīpramukhanimnagāḥ \|*  
*girervinirgatā ṛkṣātsadyaḥ pāpabhayāpahāḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Godāvarī,\[11\] Bhīmarathī,\[12\] Tāpī, and other important rivers issue from the mountain Ṛkṣa. They dispel fear of sins immediately.

## \|\| 9.18.12 \|\|

*sahyapādodbhavā nadyaḥ kṛṣṇāveṇyādikāstathā \|*  
*kṛtamālā tāmraparṇī pramukhā malayodbhavāḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. The rivers Kṛṣṇā Veṇī\[14\] and others issue from Sahya Kṛtamālā,\[15\] Tāmraparṇī and others issue from Malaya.

## \|\| 9.18.13 \|\|

*triyāmā carṣikulyādyā mahendraprabhavā smṛtāḥ \|*  
*ṛṣikulyā kumāryyādyāḥ śuktimatpādasaṃbhavāḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. Triyāmā,\[17\] Ṛṣikulyā\[18\] and others issue from Mahendra. Ṛṣikulyā,\[19\] Kumārī\[20\] and others issue from Śuktimat.

## \|\| 9.18.14 \|\|

*nānājanapadāsteṣu maṃḍaleṣu vasanti vai \|*  
*āsāṃ pibaṃti pānīyaṃ saratsu vividheṣu ca \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. Many territories, countries are situated in their basins. People drink from these rivers as well as from other lakes.

## \|\| 9.18.15 \|\|

*catvāri bhārate varṣe yugānyāsanmahāmune \|*  
*kṛtādīni na cānyeṣu dvīpeṣu prabhavaṃti hi \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. O great sage, all the four Yugās Kṛta and others are followed in Bhārata and not in the other Dvīpas.

## \|\| 9.18.16 \|\|

*dānāni cātra dīyaṃte sukṛtaiścātra yājñikaiḥ \|*  
*tapastapaṃti yatayaḥ paralokārthamādarāt \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. Charitable gifts are offered here by meritorious sacrifices. Ascetics perform penances strenuously for the attainment of better worlds.

## \|\| 9.18.17 \|\|

*yato hi karmabhūreṣā jambūdvīpe mahāmune \|*  
*atrāpi bhārataṃ śreṣṭhamato'nyā bhogabhūmayaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. O great sage, it is so because this is the land of sacred rites in the Jambūdvīpa. Here too Bhārata is the most excellent. The others are lands of pleasure.

## \|\| 9.18.18 \|\|

*kadācillabhate martyassahasrairmunisattama \|*  
*atra janmasahasrāṇāṃ mānuṣyaṃ puṇyasaṃcayaiḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. O excellent sage, after a thousand births rarely docs a man get birth as a human being here and that too due to hoarded merits.

## \|\| 9.18.19 \|\|

*svargāpavargāspadamārgabhūte dhanyāstu te bhāratabhūmibhāge \|*  
*gāyaṃti devāḥ kila gītakāni bhavaṃti bhūyaḥ puruṣāssurāste \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. It is said that the gods sing songs like this:—It is only the blessed who are able to live in the land of Bhārata which is the pathway leading to heaven and salvation. They are born as men there.

## \|\| 9.18.20 \|\|

*avāpya mānuṣyamayaṃ kadācidvihṛtya śaṃbhoḥ paramātmarūpe \|*  
*phalāni sarvāṇi tu karmajāni yāsyāmyahaṃ tattanutāṃ hi tasya \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. After getting human birth once in a way, and sporting about in the great soul form of Śiva I shall attain the fruits of all sacred rites in that form.

## \|\| 9.18.21 \|\|

*āpsyaṃti dhanyāḥ khalu te manuṣyāḥ sukhairyutāḥ karmaṇi sanniviṣṭāḥ \|*  
*janurhi yeṣāṃ khalu bhārate'sti te svargamokṣobhayalābhavantaḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. Only blessed human beings derive this pleasure. They are happy and their minds are interested in sacred rites. Those who take birth in Bhārata have the two gains of attaining heaven and salvation.

## \|\| 9.18.22 \|\|

*lakṣayojanavistārassamastaparimaṇḍalaḥ \|*  
*jambūdvīpo mayā khyātaḥ kṣārodadhisusaṃvṛtaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. This Jambūdvīpa extends to a hundred thousand Yojanas including all its internal zones. It is well surrounded by the salt ocean.

## \|\| 9.18.23 \|\|

*saṃveṣṭya kṣāramudadhiṃ śatasāhasrasammitam \|*  
*tato hi dviguṇo brahmanplakṣadvīpaḥ prakīrtitaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. The continent of Plakṣa is glorified as surrounding the briny sea extending to a hundred thousand Yojanas. O brahmin the Dvīpa is twice that in extent.

## \|\| 9.18.24 \|\|

*gomaṃtaścaiva candraśca nārado dardurastathā \|*  
*somakassumanāśśailo vaibhrājaścaiva sattamaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. The seven Varṣa mountains are Gomanta, Candra, Nārada, Dardura, Somaka, Sumanas and Vaibhrāja.

## \|\| 9.18.25 \|\|

*varṣācaleṣu ramyeṣu sahitāssatataṃ prajāḥ \|*  
*vasaṃti devagaṃdharvā varṣeṣveteṣu nityaśaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*
