25\. On these beautiful Varṣa mountains, all the subjects, gods, Gandharvas etc. live together for ever.

## \|\| 9.18.26 \|\|

*nādhayo vyādhayo vāpi janānāṃ tatra kutracit \|*  
*daśa varṣasahasrāṇi tatra jīvaṃti mānavāḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. There the life expectation for men is ten thousand years. Neither physical sickness nor worries nor agonies torment the people anywhere there.

## \|\| 9.18.27 \|\|

*anutaptā śikhī caiva pāpaghnī tridivā kṛpā \|*  
*amṛtā sukṛtā caiva saptaivātra ca nimnagāḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. The seven holy rivers here are Anutaptā, Śikhī, Pāpaghnī, Tridivā, Kṛpā, Amṛtā and Sukṛtā.

## \|\| 9.18.28 \|\|

*kṣudranadyastathā śailāstatra saṃti sahasraśaḥ \|*  
*tāḥ pibaṃti susaṃhṛṣṭā nadīrjanapadāstu te \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. Insignificant rivers and mountains are there in thousands. The people in the countries are delighted in drinking the waters of these rivers.

## \|\| 9.18.29 \|\|

*na tatrāpi yugāvasthā yathāsthāneṣu saptasu \|*  
*tretāyugasamaḥ kālassarvadaiva mahāmune \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. In all the seven subdivisions in it there is no differentiation among the various Yugas. O great sage, the time there is on a par with Tretā Yuga for ever.

## \|\| 9.18.30 \|\|

*viprakṣatriyavaiśyāste śūdrāśca munisattama \|\| \|*  
*kalpavṛkṣasamānastu tanmadhye sumahātaruḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. O excellent sage, there are brahmins, Kṣatriyas, Vaiśyas and Śūdras there. In its middle there is a big tree like the celestial Kalpa tree.

## \|\| 9.18.31 \|\|

*plakṣastannāmasaṃjño vai plakṣadvīpo dvijottama \|*  
*ijyate tatra bhagavāñchaṃkaro lokaśaṃkaraḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. That tree is called Plakṣa and hence, O excellent brahmin, the continent is called Plakṣadvīpa. Lord Śiva, the benefactor of the worlds, performs a sacrifice there.

## \|\| 9.18.32 \|\|

*hariśca bhagavānbrahmā yantrairmantraiśca vaidikaiḥ \|*  
*saṃkṣepeṇa tathā bhūyaśśālmaliṃ tvaṃ niśāmaya \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. Lord Viṣṇu and Brahmā too perform worships here with Vedic mantras and Tantras. Now listen again to the description of Śālmali in brief.

## \|\| 9.18.33-34 \|\|

*saptavarṣāṇi tatraiva teṣāṃ nāmāni me śṛṇu \|*  
*śveto'tha haritaścaiva jīmūto rohitastathā \|\| 33 \|\|*

*vaikalo mānasaścaiva suprabhassaptamo mune \|*  
*śālmalena tu vṛkṣeṇa dvīpaḥ śālmalisaṃjñakaḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

33-34. There are seven Upa-varṣas (sub-continents) there too. Please listen to their names. They are Śveta, Harita, Jīmūta, Rohita, Vaikala, Mānasa and Suprabha the seventh one. The continent is called Śālmali because of the Śālmala tree.

## \|\| 9.18.35-36 \|\|

*dviguṇena samudreṇa satataṃ saṃvṛtaḥ sthitaḥ \|*  
*varṣābhivyaṃjakā nadyastāsāṃ nāmāni me śṛṇu \|\| 35 \|\|*

*śuklā raktā hiraṇyā ca candrā śubhrā vimocanā \|*  
*nivṛttiḥ saptamī tāsāṃ puṇyatoyā suśītalāḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

35-36. It is always surrounded by the ocean, twice extensive as it. The rivers indicate the Varṣas significantly. Listen to their names. They are Śuklā, Raktā, Hiraṇyā, Candrā, Śubhrā, Vimocanā and the Nivṛtti. All these have cool sacred waters.

## \|\| 9.18.37 \|\|

*saptaiva tāni varṣāṇi caturvarṇāyutāni ca \|*  
*bhagavantaṃ sadā śaṃbhuṃ yajaṃte vividhairmakhaiḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. All the seven Varṣas contain people of the four castes. They worship lord Śiva always with various kinds of sacrifices.

## \|\| 9.18.38 \|\|

*devānāṃ tatra sānnidhyamatīva sumanorame \|*  
*eṣa dvīpassamudreṇa surodena samāvṛtaḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. In this beautiful place the gods are always present. This Dvīpa is surrounded by the ocean of wine.

## \|\| 9.18.39-40 \|\|

*dviguṇena kuśadvīpaḥ samaṃtādbāhyataḥ sthitaḥ \|*  
*vasaṃti tatra daiteyā manujaissaha dānavāḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

*tathaiva devagandharvā yakṣāḥ kiṃpuruṣādayaḥ \|*  
*varṇāstatraiva catvāro nijānuṣṭhānatatparāḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

39-40. The ocean of wine is twice as much as the Śālmali Dvīpa. Kuśa Dvīpa is all round it externally. Daityas and Dānavas live there along with human beings. So also the gods, the Gandharvas, the Yakṣas, the Kimpuruṣas and others too. There also the castes are devoted to their respective duties and conduct.

## \|\| 9.18.41 \|\|

*tatraiva ca kuśadvīpe brahmāṇaṃ ca janārddanam \|*  
*yajaṃti ca tatheśānaṃ sarvakāmaphalapradam \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. People in Kuśa Dvīpa worship Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Śiva, the bestower of the fruits of all desires.

## \|\| 9.18.42 \|\|

*kuśeśayo hariścaiva dyutimānpuṣpavāṃstathā \|*  
*maṇidrumo hemaśailassaptamo mandarācalaḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. The seven mountains are Kuśeśaya, Hari, Dyutimat, Puṣpavat, Maṇidruma, Hemaśaila and Mandarācala.

## \|\| 9.18.43-44 \|\|

*nadyaśca sapta tāsāṃ tu nāmāni śṛṇu tattvataḥ \|*  
*dhūtapāpā śivā caiva pavitrā saṃmitistathā \|\| 43 \|\|*

*vidyā daṃbhā mahī cānyā sarvapāpaharāstvimāḥ \|*  
*anyāssahasraśassaṃti śubhāpo hemavālukāḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

43-44. The rivers are seven. Listen to their names factually. Dhūtapāpā, Sivā, the holy Sammiti, Vidyā, Dambhā, Mahī and Alpā. These quell all sins. There are thousands of other rivers of auspicious waters and golden sands.

## \|\| 9.18.45 \|\|

*kuśadvīpe kuśastambo ghṛtodena samāvṛtaḥ \|*  
*krauñcadvīpo mahābhāga śrūyatāṃ cāparo mahān \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. In the Kuśadvīpa the main plant is the stump of Kuśa grass. It is surrounded by the ocean of clarified butter. O fortunate one, listen to the description of Krauñca Dvīpa, the other great continent.

## \|\| 9.18.46 \|\|

*dviguṇena samudreṇa dadhimaṃḍena cāvṛtaḥ \|*  
*varṣācalā mahābuddhe teṣāṃ nāmāni me śṛṇu \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. It is surrounded by the ocean of curds and whey twice as large. O intelligent one, listen to the names of the Varṣa mountains.

## \|\| 9.18.47 \|\|

*krauñcaśca vāmanaścaiva tṛtīyaścāṃdhakārakaḥ \|*  
*divāvṛtirmanaścaiva puṇḍarīkaśca dundubhiḥ \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. They are Krauñca, Vāmana, Andhakāraka, Divāvṛti, Manas, Puṇḍarīka and Dundubhi.

## \|\| 9.18.48 \|\|

*nivasaṃti nirātaṃkā varṣaśaileṣu teṣu vai \|*  
*sarvasauvarṇaramyeṣu suhṛddevagaṇaiḥ prajāḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. The subjects live on these Varṣa mountains which are very beautiful and golden. Free from terror, they are accompanied by their friends and the gods.

## \|\| 9.18.49 \|\|

*brāhmaṇāḥ kṣattriyā vaiśyāśśūdrāścānukramoditāḥ \|*  
*saṃti tatra mahānadyassaptānyāstu sahasraśaḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. Brahmins, Kṣatriyas, Vaiśyas and Śūdras live there in their due order. There are seven great rivers and thousands of other small rivers there.

## \|\| 9.18.50 \|\|

*gaurī kumudvatī caiva sandhyā rātrirmanojavā \|*  
*śāṃtiśca puṃḍarīkā ca yāḥ pibanti payaśśubham \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. They are Gaurī, Kumudvatī, Sandhyā, Rāś?, Manojavā, Śānti and Puṇḍarīka and they make others drink their holy water.

## \|\| 9.18.51-53 \|\|

*bhagavānpūjyate tatra yogarudrasvarūpavān \|*  
*dadhimaṇḍodakaścāpi śākadvīpena saṃvṛtaḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

*dviguṇenādrayassapta teṣāṃ nāmāni me śṛṇu \|*  
*pūrve tatrodayagirirjaladhāraḥ pare yataḥ \|\| 52 \|\|*

*pṛṣṭhato'stagiriścaiva hyavikeśaśca kesarī \|*  
*śākastatra mahāvṛkṣassiddhagaṃdharvasevitaḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

51-53. The lord in the form of Yogarudra is worshipped there. The ocean of curds and whey is surrounded by Śākadvīpa twice as large. The mountains are seven. Listen to their names from me. In the east is the mountain Udayagiri. In the south is the mountain Jaladhāra. The Astagiri is in the west and Avikeśa in the north. The great tree is Śāka there, worshipped by Siddhas and Gandharvas.

## \|\| 9.18.54 \|\|

*tatra puṇyā janapadāścāturvarṇyasamanvitāḥ \|*  
*nadyaścātra mahāpuṇyāssarvapāpabhayāpahāḥ \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. The countries are very sacred populated by the people of all the four castes. The rivers too are very holy, dispelling all terror due to sins.

## \|\| 9.18.55 \|\|

*sukumārī kumārī ca nalinī veṇukā tathā \|*  
*ikṣuśca reṇukā caiva gabhastissaptamī tathā \|\| 55 \|\|*

55\. They are Sukumārī, Kumārī, Nalinī, Veṇukā, Ikṣu, Reṇukā and Gabhasti.

## \|\| 9.18.56 \|\|

*anyāssahasraśastatra kṣudranadyo mahāmune \|*  
*mahīdharāstathā saṃti śataśo'tha sahasraśaḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

56\. O great sage, there are thousands of small rivers there. The mountains too are in hundreds and thousands.

## \|\| 9.18.57 \|\|

*dharmahānirna teṣvasti svargādāgatya mānavāḥ \|*  
*varṣeṣu teṣu pṛthivīṃ viharanti parasparam \|\| 57 \|\|*

57\. There is no question of loss of virtue in them. Men come from heaven to those Varṣas on the earth and sport about with one another.

## \|\| 9.18.58 \|\|

*śākadvīpe tu vai sūryyaḥ prītyā janapadaissadā \|*  
*yathoktairijyate samyakkarmabhirniyatātmabhiḥ \|\| 58 \|\|*

58\. In the Śākadvīpa, the sun is lovingly worshipped by the people of all countries pious in mind and activities as mentioned before.

## \|\| 9.18.59-60 \|\|

*kṣīrodenāvṛtasso'pi dviguṇena samaṃtataḥ \|*  
*kṣīrābdhissarvato vyāsa puṣkarākhyena saṃvṛtaḥ \|\| 59 \|\|*

*dviguṇena mahāvarṣastatra khyāto'tra mānasaḥ \|*  
*yojanānāṃ sahasrāṇi paṃcaivordhvasamucchritaḥ \|\| 60 \|\|*

59-60. It is surrounded on all sides by the milk-ocean twice as large. O Vyāsa, the milk-ocean is surrounded by Puṣkara continent twice as large. (?) There at Varṣa there is famous by the name Mānasa. It is five thousand Yojanas in height.

## \|\| 9.18.61 \|\|

*tāni caiva tu lakṣāṇi sarvato valayākṛti \|*  
*puṣkaradvīpavalayo madhyena vibhajaṃti ca \|\| 61 \|\|*

61\. There is a great ring round it, five hundred thousand Yojanas in length. The Puṣkara Dvīpa ring divides it into two.

## \|\| 9.18.62 \|\|

*tenaiva valayā kārā dvīpavarṣasamākṛtiḥ \|*  
*daśavarṣasahasrāṇi tatra jīvaṃti mānavāḥ \|\| 62 \|\|*

62\. The Dvīpa and the Varṣas are of similar shapes namely gyrated. Men live there up to ten thousand years.

## \|\| 9.18.63 \|\|

*nirāmayā vītaśokā rāgadveṣavivarjitāḥ \|*  
*adharmo na matasteṣāṃ na baṃdhavadhakau mune \|\| 63 \|\|*

63\. They are free from ailment. They are not unhappy. They are devoid of passion and hatred. Evil is never approved of by them. O sage, nor do they imprison or kill anyone.

## \|\| 9.18.64 \|\|

*satyānṛte na tasyāstāṃ sadaiva vasatissadā \|*  
*tulyaveṣāstu manujā hemavarṇaikarūpiṇaḥ \|\| 64 \|\|*

64\. There is neither truth or lie there. It is always night there. Men are of equal dress and features, all being gold-complexioned.

## \|\| 9.18.65 \|\|

*varṣaścāyaṃ tu kāleya bhauma svargopamo mataḥ \|*  
*sarvasya sukhadaḥ kāle jarārogavivarjitaḥ \|\| 65 \|\|*

65\. This Varṣa, O sage, is Bhauma which is like a heaven on this earth and the life herein is pleasing to all, free from old age and sickness,

## \|\| 9.18.66 \|\|

*puṣkare dhātakīkhaṇḍe mahāvīte mahāmune \|\| \|*  
*nyagrodhaṃ puṣkaradvīpe brahmaṇaḥ sthānamuttamam \|\| 66 \|\|*

66\. O great sage, in the Dhātakīkhaṇḍa and the Mahāvīta in the Puṣkaradvīpa, the holy fig tree is the excellent abode of Brahmā.

## \|\| 9.18.67 \|\|

*tasminnivasate brahmā pūjyamānassurāsuraiḥ \|*  
*svādūdakenāṃbudhinā puṣkaraḥ pariveṣṭitaḥ \|\| 67 \|\|*

67\. Brahmā resides there, worshipped by gods and Asuras. Puṣkara is surrounded by the ocean of sweet water.

## \|\| 9.18.68 \|\|

*evaṃ dvīpāssamudraistu sapta saptabhirāvṛtāḥ \|*  
*dvīpāścaiva samudrāśca samānā dviguṇaiḥ paraiḥ \|\| 68 \|\|*

68\. Thus the seven continents are surrounded by seven oceans each twice as large as the one within.

## \|\| 9.18.69 \|\|

*uktātiriktatā teṣāṃ samudreṣu samāni vai \|*  
*payāṃsi sarvadā'lpatvaṃ jāyate na kadācana \|\| 69 \|\|*

69\. The speciality of the oceans is mentioned thus. The water is always equal in quantity. It is never reduced.

## \|\| 9.18.70 \|\|

*sthālīsthamagnisaṃyogādadhaḥsthaṃ munisattamaḥ \|*  
*tathenduvṛddhau salilamūrddhvagaṃ bhavati dhruvam \|\| 70 \|\|*

70\. O excellent sage, if the water kept in a pot is heated it goes up. Similarly when the moon rises the water of the ocean rises up.

## \|\| 9.18.71 \|\|

*udayāstamanetviṃdorvarddhaṃtyāpo hrasanti ca \|*  
*ato nyūnātiriktāśca pakṣayoḥ śuklakṛṣṇayoḥ \|\| 71 \|\|*

71\. At the time of rising and setting of the moon waters flow and ebb. Hence in the bright and dark halves the waters vary in quantity; they increase and decrease.

## \|\| 9.18.72 \|\|

*apāṃ vṛddhikṣayau dṛṣṭau śataśastu daśottaram \|*  
*samudrāṇāṃ muniśreṣṭho sarveṣāṃ kathitaṃ tava \|\| 72 \|\|*

72\. O great sage, the increase and decrease of the waters of the oceans are seen hundred and thousands of times. This has been mentioned to you.

## \|\| 9.18.73-74 \|\|

*bhojanaṃ puṣkaradvīpe prajāssarvāḥ sadaiva hi \|*  
*khaṃḍasya kurvate vipra tatra svayamupasthitam \|\| 73 \|\|*

*svāṃgado yasya purato nāsti lokasya saṃsthitiḥ \|*  
*dviguṇā hiraṇmayī bhūmissarvajaṃtuvivarjitā \|\| 74 \|\|*

73-74. All the people in the Puṣkaradvīpa, O brahmin, take as food Sugarcandy which appears there by itself for ever. Beyond that there are no living beings. The land is golden and devoid of living creatures.

## \|\| 9.18.75 \|\|

*lokālokastataśśailassahasrāṇyacalo hi saḥ \|*  
*ucchrayeṇa hi tāvaṃti yojanāyutavistṛtaḥ \|\| 75 \|\|*

75\. Beyond that is the mountain Lokāloka. It is a thousand Yojanas in height and an equal number of Yojanas in extent.

## \|\| 9.18.76 \|\|

*tamaścāṃḍakaṭāhena seyamurvī mahāmune \|*  
*paṃcāśatkoṭivistārā sadvīpā samahīdharā \|\| 76 \|\|*

76\. O great sage, therefore, along with this universe the earth is fifty crores of Yojanas in extent inclusive of all the continents and mountains.

## \|\| 9.18.77 \|\|

*ādhārabhūtā sarveṣāṃ sarvabhūtaguṇādhikā \|*  
*seyaṃ dhātrī ca kāleya sarveṣāṃ jagatāmilā \|\| 77 \|\|*

77\. This world is the support in due course of all living beings. It has more attributes than all other elements have.

## Chapter 19 - Worlds (loka) and Planets (graha)

## \|\| 9.19.1 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*ravicandramasoryāvanmayūkhā bhāsayaṃti hi \|*  
*tāvatpramāṇā pṛthivī bhūlokassa tu gīyate \|\| 1 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

1\. The extent to which the rays of the sun and the moon shine and illuminate is the size of the earth. It is called Bhūloka.

## \|\| 9.19.2-3 \|\|

*bhūmeryojanalakṣe tu saṃsthitaṃ ravimaṇḍalam \|*  
*yojanānāṃ sahasrāṇi sadaiva parisaṃkhyayā \|\| 2 \|\|*

*śaśinastu pramāṇāya jagataḥ paricakṣate \|*  
*raverūrdhvaṃ śaśī tasthau lakṣayojanasaṃkhyayā \|\| 3 \|\|*

2-3. The sun’s sphere is situated a hundred thousand Yojanas from the earth. The moon is situated thousands of Yojanas from this earth and a hundred thousand Yojanas from the sun.

## \|\| 9.19.4 \|\|

*grahāṇāṃ maṇḍalaṃ kṛtsnaṃ śaśerupari saṃsthitam \|*  
*sanakṣatraṃ sahasrāṇi daśaiva paritopari \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. The spheres of the planets are entirely situated above the moon along with the stars about ten thousand Yojanas one above the other.

## \|\| 9.19.5 \|\|

*budhastasmādatho kāvyastasmādbhaumasya maṇḍalam \|*  
*bṛhaspatistadūrdhvaṃ tu tasyopari śanaiścaraḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Budha (Mercury); above that is Kāvya (Venus): above that is the sphere of Bhauma (Mars). Bṛhaspati (Jupiter) is above that and Śanaiścara (Saturn) is above that.

## \|\| 9.19.6 \|\|

*saptarṣimaṇḍalaṃ tasmāllakṣeṇaikena saṃsthitam \|*  
*ṛṣibhya tu sahasrāṇāṃ śatādūrdhvaṃ dhruvaḥ sthitaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. The sphere of the seven sages (The Ursa Major) is situated a hundred thousand Yojanas above it. The Pole Star (Dhruva) is situated a hundred thousand Yojanas above the sages.

## \|\| 9.19.7 \|\|

*meḍhībhūtassa yastasya jyotiścakrasya vai dhruvaḥ \|*  
*bhūrbhuvaḥsvariti jñeyaṃ bhuva ūrdhvaṃ dhruvādavāk \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. The Pole star is the prop of the circle of luminary bodies. The Bhuvarloka and Svarloka are above the earth but beneath the Pole star.

## \|\| 9.19.8 \|\|

*ekayojanakoṭistu yatra te kalpavāsinaḥ \|*  
*dhruvādūrdhvaṃ maharlokassaptaite brahmaṇassutāḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. A crore Yojanas above the Pole star is the Maharloka where the seven sons of Brahmā are stationed. They live even during the time of Kalpa.

## \|\| 9.19.9 \|\|

*sanakaśca sanandaśca tṛtīyaśca sanātanaḥ \|*  
*kapilaścāsuriścaiva voḍhuḥ paṃcaśikhastathā \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. The seven sons are—Sanaka, Sananda, Sanātana, Kapila, Āsuri, Voḍhu and Pañcaśikha.

## \|\| 9.19.10 \|\|

*upariṣṭāttataśśukro dvilakṣābhyaṃtare sthitaḥ \|*  
*dvilakṣayojanaṃ tasmādadhaḥ somasutaḥ smṛtaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Above that is stationed Śukra (Venus) at a distance of two hundred thousand Yojanas. Two hundred thousand Yojanas below it is stationed Budha (Mercury) the son of the moon.

## \|\| 9.19.11 \|\|

*dvilakṣayojanaṃ tasmādūrdhvaṃ bhaumassthito mune \|*  
*dvilakṣayojanaṃ tasmādūrdhvaṃ jīvaḥ sthito guru \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. O sage, Bhauma (Mars) is stationed two hundred thousand Yojanas above it. Bṛhaspati (Jupiter) is stationed two hundred thousand Yojanas above it.

## \|\| 9.19.12 \|\|

*dvilakṣayojanaṃ jīvādūrdhvaṃ saurirvyavasthitaḥ \|*  
*ete saptagrahāḥ proktāssvasvarāśivyavasthitā \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. Two hundred thousand Yojanas above Jupiter, Saturn is stationed. These are the seven planets that stay in their respective zodiacs.

## \|\| 9.19.13 \|\|

*rudralakṣairyojanatassaptordhvamṛṣayaḥ sthitāḥ \|*  
*viśvalakṣairyojanato dhruvasthitirudāhṛtā \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. The seven sages (Ursa Major) are stationed eleven hundred thousand Yojanas above the planet Saturn. The Dhruva star is said to be stationed fourteen hundred thousand Yojanas above the great star.

## \|\| 9.19.14 \|\|

*caturguṇottare cārddhe janalokāttapaḥ smṛtam \|*  
*vairājā yatra devā vai sthitā dāhavivarjitāḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. Sixty-three hundred thousand Yojanas from Janaloka is stationed the Tapoloka where the gods called Vairājas stay. They are free from burning sensation.

## \|\| 9.19.15-16 \|\|

*ṣaḍguṇena tapolokātsatyaloko vyavasthitaḥ \|*  
*brahmalokaḥ sa vijñeyo vasaṃtyamalacetasaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

*satyadharmaratāścaiva jñānino brahmacāriṇaḥ \|*  
*yadgāmino'tha bhūlokānnivasaṃti hi mānavāḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

15-16. Eighty four thousand Yojanas from Tapoloka is stationed the Satyaloka. It is known as Brahmaloka where the pure-minded, perfectly wise Brahmacārins engaged in truthful virtue stay. Men who go there from the earth also stay there.

## \|\| 9.19.17-18 \|\|

*bhuvarloke tu saṃsiddhā munayo devarūpiṇaḥ \|*  
*svargaloke surādityā maruto vasavo'śvinau \|\| 17 \|\|*

*viśvedevāstathā rudrāssādhyā nāgāḥ khagādayaḥ \|*  
*navagrahāstatastatra ṛṣayo vītakalmaṣāḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

17-18. In the Bhuvarloka, the sages of great Siddhis stay in the form of gods. In the Svargaloka, the gods, the Ādityas, the Maruts, the Vasus, the twin Aśvins, Viśvedevas, Rudras, Sādhyas, Nāgas, Khgas etc. stay. Above that are the nine planets and above them are the seven sages free from sins.

## \|\| 9.19.19 \|\|

*ete sapta mahālokāḥ kāleya kathitāstava \|*  
*pātālāni ca saptaiva brahmāṇḍasya ca vistaraḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. O sage, thus the seven worlds have been mentioned to you. The Pātālas too are also seven. Thus the universe has been explained to you.

## \|\| 9.19.20 \|\|

*dadhivṛkṣaphalaṃ yadvadvṛttiścordhvamadhastathā \|*  
*etadaṃḍakaṭāhena sarvato vai samāvṛtam \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. As the wood-apple is both above and below the earth, so the universe is enveloped by the cauldron of the Cosmic Egg.

## \|\| 9.19.21-23 \|\|

*daśaguṇena payasā sarvatastatsamāvṛtam \|*  
*vahninā vāyunā cāpi nabhasā tamasā tathā \|\| 21 \|\|*

*bhūtādināpi mahatā digguṇottaraveṣṭitaḥ \|*  
*mahāṃtaṃ ca samāvṛtya pradhānaṃ puruṣaḥ sthitaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

*anaṃtasya na tasyāsti saṃkhyāpi paramātmanaḥ \|*  
*tenānaṃta iti khyātaḥ pramāṇaṃ nāsti vai yataḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

21-23. It is surrounded by water ten times as large all round the fire, wind, ether and darkness. It is encompassed by the elements and the principle of Mahat, eight times as large. There surrounded by the Mahat and Pradhāna is stationed the Puruṣa. The supreme soul is infinite. He is therefore called eternal since he has no limitation.

## \|\| 9.19.24-27 \|\|

*hetubhūtassamastasya prakṛtissā parā mune \|*  
*aṃḍānāṃ tu sahasrāṇāṃ sahasrāṇyayutāni ca \|\| 24 \|\|*

*īdṛśānāṃ prabhūtāni tasmādavyaktajanmanaḥ \|*  
*dāruṇyagnistile tailaṃ payassu ca yathā ghṛtam \|\| 25 \|\|*

*tathāsau paramātmā vai sarvaṃ vyāpyātmavedanaḥ \|*  
*ādibījātprasuvate tatastebhyaḥ pareṇḍajāḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

*tebhyaḥ putrāstathānyeṣāṃ bījānyanyāni vai tataḥ \|*  
*mahadādayo viśeṣāṃtāstadbhavaṃti surādayaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

24-27. This eternal is the cause of all. O sage, it is the great Prakṛti. From that infinite of unmanifest origin thousands and hundreds of thousands of cosmic eggs are born. The self-known great soul contains everything just as the wood contains latent fire, the gingelly seed the latent oil and the milk the latent ghee. From this primordial seed emerge all those beginning with Mahat and ending with Viśeṣa. Then the gods and others are born. Then are born birds, their progenies and the seeds of others.

## \|\| 9.19.28-29 \|\|

*bījādvṛkṣapraroheṇa yathā nāpacayastaroḥ \|*  
*sūryyakāṃtamaṇeḥ sūryyādyadvadvahniḥ prajāyate \|\| 28 \|\|*

*tadvatsaṃjāyate sṛṣṭiḥ śivastatraḥ na kāmayet \|*  
*śivaśaktisamāyoge devādyāḥ prabhavaṃti hi \|\| 29 \|\|*

28-29. When the tree comes out of seed it docs not suffer any depreciation or diminution. Just as fire appears from the solar stone coming in contact of the sun so also the creation takes place. Śiva has no desire for it. As a result of the contact of Śiva with Śakti, the gods and others are born naturally.

## \|\| 9.19.30 \|\|

*tathā svakarmaṇaikena prarohamupayāṃti vai \|*  
*brahmā viṣṇuśca rudrāśca sa śivaḥ parigīyate \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. They spring up through their own action. Śiva manifests himself as Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Rudra.

## \|\| 9.19.31 \|\|

*tasmāduddharate sarvaṃ yasmiṃśca layameṣyati \|*  
*kartā kriyāṇāṃ sarvāsāṃ sa śivaḥ parigīyate \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. Every thing springs up from him and finally merges in him. Śiva is sung as the performer of all activities.

## \|\| 9.19.32 \|\|

*vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*sanatkumāra sarvajña chiṃdhi me saṃśayaṃ mahat \|*  
*santi lokā hi brahmāṃḍādupariṣṭānna vā mune \|\| 32 \|\|*

Vyāsa said:—

32\. O sage Sanatkumāra, O omniscient, please clear my doubts. Are there worlds above this universe?

## \|\| 9.19.33 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*brahmāṃḍādupariṣṭācca saṃti lokā munīśvara \|*  
*tāñchṛṇu tvaṃ viśeṣeṇa vacmi te'haṃ samāgataḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

33\. O great sage, there are worlds above the universe. Please listen. I shall explain them clearly but briefly.

## \|\| 9.19.34 \|\|

*vidhilokātparo loko vaikuṃṭha iti viśrutaḥ \|*  
*virājate mahādīptyā yatra viṣṇuḥ pratiṣṭhitaḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. The world Vaikuṇṭha is beyond the world of Brahmā. It is here that Viṣṇu is stationed and it shines with great brilliance.

## \|\| 9.19.35 \|\|

*tasyopariṣṭātkaumāro loko hi paramādbhutaḥ \|*  
*senānīḥ śaṃbhutanayo rājate yatra suprabhaḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. Above it is the extremely wonderful world Kaumāra. The general Kārttikeya, the brilliant son of Śiva shines here.

## \|\| 9.19.36 \|\|

*tataḥ paramumāloko mahādivyo virā jate \|*  
*yatra śaktirvibhātyekā tridevajananī śivā \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. Beyond that shines the excessively divine world of Umā, where Śakti-Śivā, the mother of the three deities shines.

## \|\| 9.19.37 \|\|

*parātparā hi prakṛtī rajassattvatamomayī \|*  
*nirguṇā ca svayaṃ devī nirvikārā śivātmikā \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. She is the Prakṛti, greater than the greatest, with the attributes of Rajas, Sattva and Tamas. But the goddess Śivā herself is devoid of attributes, free from aberrations.

## \|\| 9.19.38-39 \|\|

*tasyopariṣṭādvijñeyaśśivalokassanātanaḥ \|*  
*avināśī mahādivyo mahāśobhānvitassadā \|\| 38 \|\|*

*virājate paraṃ brahma yatra śaṃbhurmaheśvaraḥ \|*  
*tridevajanakasvāmī sarveṣāṃ triguṇātparaḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

38-39. The eternal Śivaloka is above that. Here lord Śiva, the great Brahman, shines. He is indestructible, excessively divine and endowed with great brilliance. He is the lord of all, greater than the three attributes. He is the progenitor of the three deities.

## \|\| 9.19.40 \|\|

*tata ūrdhvaṃ na lokāśca golokastatsamīpataḥ \|*  
*gomātarassuśīlākhyāstatra saṃti śivapriyā \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. There are no worlds above it. The Goloka is near it. Mother cows named Suśīlā are there. They are favourites of Śiva.

## \|\| 9.19.41 \|\|

*tatpālaḥ kṛṣṇanāmā hi rājate śaṃkarājñayā \|*  
*pratiṣṭhitaśśivenaiva śaktyā svacchandacāriṇā \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. The protector of that world is Kṛṣṇa. He is established there at the behest of Śiva by Śiva himself who moves about as he pleases due to his power.

## \|\| 9.19.42 \|\|

*śivaloko'dbhuto vyāsa nirādhāro manoharaḥ \|*  
*atinirvacanīyaśca nānāvastuvirājitaḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. O Vyāsa, Śiva’s region is wonderful and beautiful. It has no support. It shines with different objects. It cannot be specifically described.

## \|\| 9.19.43 \|\|

*śivastu tadadhiṣṭhātā sarvadevaśiromaṇiḥ \|*  
*viṣṇubrahmaharaissevyaḥ paramātmā nirañjanaḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. The presiding deity of that region is Śiva. He is the crest-jewel of all the gods. He is worthy of being served by Viṣṇu, Brahmā and Śiva. He is the unsullied great soul.

## \|\| 9.19.44 \|\|

*iti te kathitā tāta sarvabrahmāṃḍasaṃsthitiḥ \|*  
*tadūrdhvaṃ lokasaṃsthānaṃ kimanyacchrotumicchasi \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. O dear, thus the situation of the entire universe is narrated to you. What more do you wish to hear about the position of the worlds.

## Chapter 20 - The statement of a special Mantra

## \|\| 9.20.1 \|\|

*\|\| vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*sanatkumāra sarvajña tatprāptiṃ vada sattama \|*  
*yadgatvā na nivartaṃte śivabhaktiyutā narāḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

Vyāsa said:—

1\. O omniscient Sanatkumāra, O the most excellent of the good, explain the acquisition of that by attaining which men endowed with devotion to Śiva do not return again.

## \|\| 9.20.2 \|\|

*\|\| sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*parāśarasuta vyāsa śṛṇu prītyā śubhāṃ gatim \|*  
*vrataṃ hi śuddhabhaktānāṃ tathā śuddhaṃ tapasvinām \|\| 2 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

2\. O Vyāsa, son of Parāśara, listen with pleasure to the auspicious salvation and the holy rite of pious devotees and the sacred rite of ascetics.

## \|\| 9.20.3 \|\|

*ye śivaṃ śuddhakarmāṇassuśuddhatapasānvitāḥ \|*  
*samarcayanti taṃ nityaṃ vandyāste sarvathānvaham \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. Those who perform holy rites, who are endowed with very pious penance and who always worship Śiva should be honoured in every way always.

## \|\| 9.20.4 \|\|

*nātaptatapaso yāṃti śivalokamanāmayam \|*  
*śivānugrahasaddhetustapa eva mahāmune \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. Those who have not performed austerities do not go to Śiva’s region which is free from ailments. O great sage, penance alone leads to Śiva’s blessings.

## \|\| 9.20.5 \|\|

*tapasā divi modante pratyakṣaṃ devatāgaṇāḥ \|*  
*ṛṣayo munayaścaiva satyaṃ jānīha madvacaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. The gods and Gaṇas rejoice in heaven due to penance. So also the sages and the ascetics. Know my statement to be true.

## \|\| 9.20.6 \|\|

*sudurddharaṃ durāsādhyaṃ sudhuraṃ duratikramam \|*  
*tatsarvaṃ tapasā sādhyaṃ tapo hi duratikramam \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. Every thing can be achieved through penance, whether it is difficult to be sustained, or propitiated or is burdensome or difficult to be transgressed. Penance cannot be transgressed.

## \|\| 9.20.7 \|\|

*susthitastapasi brahmā nityaṃ viṣṇurharastathā \|*  
*devā devyo'khilāḥ prāptāstapasā durlabhaṃ phalam \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. Brahmā stays steady in his penance. So also Viṣṇu and Śiva. All the gods and the goddesses have attained rare benefits through penance.

## \|\| 9.20.8 \|\|

*yena yena hi bhāvena sthitvā yatkriyate tapaḥ \|*  
*tatassaṃprāpyate'sau tairiha loke na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. There is no doubt that in this world the particular goal is realised through penance by those who aspire for it

## \|\| 9.20.9 \|\|

*sāttvikaṃ rājasaṃ caiva tāmasaṃ trividhaṃ smṛtam \|*  
*vijñeyaṃ hi tapo vyāsa sarvasādhanasādhanam \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. O Vyāsa, penance is the means for achieving everything. It is of three kinds viz. Sāttvika, Rājasa and Tāmasa.

## \|\| 9.20.10 \|\|

*sāttvikaṃ daivatānāṃ hi yatīnāmūrddhvaretasām \|*  
*rājasaṃ dānavānāṃ hi manuṣyāṇāṃ tathaiva ca \|*  
*tāmasaṃ rākṣasānāṃ hi narāṇāṃ krūrakarmaṇām \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. The Sāttvika penance is performed by the gods and the ascetics of sublimated prowess; the Rājasa penance is by the Dānavas and human beings.

## \|\| 9.20.11-12 \|\|

*trividhaṃ tatphalaṃ proktaṃ munibhistattvadarśibhiḥ \|*  
*japo dhyānaṃ tu devānāmarcanaṃ bhaktitaśśubham \|\| 11 \|\|*

*sāttvikaṃ taddhi nirdiṣṭamaśeṣaphalasādhakam \|*  
*iha loke pare caiva manobhipretasādhanam \|\| 12 \|\|*

11-12. Their result too is threefold, achievable by the sages who have truthful vision. The Sāttvika penance consists of Japa, meditation and auspicious worship of the deities with devotion. It is the means of achieving all benefits and all desires here and hereafter.

## \|\| 9.20.13 \|\|

*kāmanāphalamuddiśya rājasaṃ tapa ucyate \|*  
*nijadehaṃ susaṃpīḍya dehaśoṣakadussahaiḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. The Rājasa penance is performed with a specific desire as the fruit. Here the body is subjected to tortures and unbearable whithering and macerating processes.

## \|\| 9.20.14 \|\|

*tapastāmasamuddiṣṭaṃ manobhipretasādhanam \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. That is Tāmasa penance which has a passionate end in view.

## \|\| 9.20.15-21 \|\|

*uttamaṃ sāttvikaṃ vidyāddharmabuddhiśca niścalā \|*  
*snānaṃ pūjā japo homaḥ śuddhaśaucamahiṃsanam \|\| 15 \|\|*

*vratopavāsacaryā ca maunamindriyanigrahaḥ \|*  
*dhīrvidyā satyamakrodho dānaṃ kṣāṃtirdamo dayā \|\| 16 \|\|*

*vāpīkūpataḍāgādeḥ prasādasya ca kalpanā \|*  
*kṛcchraṃ cāṃdrāyaṇaṃ yajñassutīrthānyāśramāḥ punaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

*dharmasthānāni caitāni sukhadāni manīṣiṇām \|*  
*sudharmaḥ paramo vyāsaḥ śivabhakteśca kāraṇam \|\| 18 \|\|*

*saṃkrātiviṣuvadyogo nādamukte niyujyatām \|*  
*dhyānaṃ trikālikaṃ jyotirunmanībhāvadhāraṇā \|\| 19 \|\|*

*recakaḥ pūrakaḥ kumbhaḥ prāṇāyāmastridhā smṛtaḥ \|*  
*nāḍīsaṃcāravijñānaṃ pratyāhāranirodhanam \|\| 20 \|\|*

*turīyaṃ tadadho buddhiraṇimādyaṣṭasaṃyutam \|*  
*pūrvottamaṃ samuddiṣṭaṃ parajñānaprasādhanam \|\| 21 \|\|*

15-21. The Sāttvika penance is the most excellent. It makes righteous intellect firm and steady. It has following characteristics—performance of ceremonial ablution, worship, Japa, sacrifice, scrupulous purity and cleanliness, non-violence, sacred rites, observance of fasts, silence, control of the sense-organs, pure intelligence, learning, truthfulness, refraining from anger, charitable gifts, forgiveness, suppression of the sense-organs, mercifulness, digging of wells, tanks and lakes, building of mansions for public cause observing Kṛcchra, Cāndrāyaṇa and other rites, Yajñas, pilgrimages, hermitages, holy places and other acts pleasing to sensible persons. O Vyāsa, good virtue is the cause of Śiva’s devotion. Celebration of the day of sun’s transit, the conjunction of equinoxes the Nādamukta, Dhyāna or meditation shall be in all the three occasions. It is the retention of the mind in the brilliance. Prāṇāyāma is three-fold viz. Recaka, Pūraka and Kumbhaka. This along with the knowledge of the course of the veins, withdrawal or obstruction of senses are the means of perfect knowledge and the achievement of the eight Siddhis, Aṇimā, etc.

## \|\| 9.20.22 \|\|

*kāṣṭhāvasthā mṛtāvasthā haritāveti kīrtitāḥ \|*  
*nānopalabdhayo hyetāssarvapāpapraṇāśanāḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. In the state of trance there are various stages such as the stage of the log of wood, the stage of the dead and the stage of Harita. All these are destructive of all sins.

## \|\| 9.20.23 \|\|

*nārī śayyā tathā pānaṃ vastradhūpavilepanam \|*  
*tāmbūlabhakṣaṇaṃ paṃca rājaiśvaryyavibhūtayaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. These five are the prosperities of royal glory-women, bed, drink, good garments unguents and incense, and the chewing of betel leaves.

## \|\| 9.20.24-25 \|\|

*hemabhārastathā tāmraṃ gṛhāśca ratnadhenavaḥ \|*  
*pāṃḍityaṃ vedaśāstrāṇāṃ gītanṛtyavibhūṣaṇam \|\| 24 \|\|*

*śaṃkhavīṇāmṛdaṃgāśca gajendraśchatracāmare \|*  
*bhogarūpāṇi caitāni ebhiśśakto'nurajyate \|\| 25 \|\|*

24-25. The forms of various pleasures are—blocks of gold, copper, houses, gems, cows, scholarship in the Vedas and Śāstras, ornaments, ability to sing and dance, conches, Vīṇās, Mṛdaṅgas, lordly elephants, umbrellas and chowries. Man yearning for these takes delight in these.

## \|\| 9.20.26 \|\|

*ādarśavanmunesnehaistilavatsa nipīḍyate \|*  
*araṃ gaccheti cāpyenaṃ kurute jñānamohitaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. O sage, but he does not know that these are only reflections in a mirror, not real substances. Just as the gingelly seeds are crushed to yield oil he gets himself crushed to yield things of pleasure. He madly rushes after them deluded by false knowledge. Some one seems to tell him, “Go on quickly.”

## \|\| 9.20.27 \|\|

*jānannapīha saṃsāre bhramate ghaṭiyaṃtravat \|*  
*sarvayoniṣu duḥkhārtassthāvareṣu careṣu ca \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. In spite of realising this he moves up and down in the world like the water-raising wheel. He takes various births in the mobile and immobile forms of life. He is distressed throughout.

## \|\| 9.20.28 \|\|

*evaṃ yoniṣu sarvāsu pratikramya bhrameṇa ta \|*  
*kālāṃtaravaśādyāti mānuṣyamatidurlabham \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. After the transmigration through various forms of life ultimately he attains the rare human birth.

## \|\| 9.20.29 \|\|

*vyutkrameṇāpi mānuṣyaṃ prāpyate puṇyagauravāt \|*  
*vicitrā gatayaḥ proktāḥ karmaṇāṃ gurulāghavāt \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. Human birth īs attained even out of turn if the merit is weighty enough. As a result of the weightiness or otherwise of the actions, the goals achieved are of diverse nature.

## \|\| 9.20.30 \|\|

*mānuṣyaṃ ca samāsādya svargamokṣaprasādhanam \|*  
*nācaratyātmanaḥ śreyassa mṛtaśśocate ciram \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. If anyone does not strive for salvation even after obtaining human birth which is capable of achieving heaven and salvation, surely he bemoans his lot for a long time, when dead.

## \|\| 9.20.31 \|\|

*devāsurāṇāṃ sarveṣāṃ mānuṣyaṃ cāti durlabhaṃ \|*  
*tatsaṃprāpya tathā kuryānna gacchennarakaṃ yathā \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. Even to the god and the Asuras the birth as human being is very rare to be achieved. After attaining the same he should do such things as would prevent his fall in the hell.

## \|\| 9.20.32 \|\|

*svargāpavargalābhāya yadi nāsti samudyamaḥ \|*  
*durlabhaṃ prāpya mānuṣyaṃ vṛthā tajjanma kīrtitam \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. If there is no endeavour for the acquisition of heaven and salvation even after attaining the rare human form that birth is to be dubbed as useless.

## \|\| 9.20.33 \|\|

*sarvasya mūlaṃ mānuṣyaṃ caturvargasya kīrtitam \|*  
*saṃprāpya dharmato vyāsa tadyattādanupālayet \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. Human birth is glorified as the root-cause for the four aims of life. O Vyāsa, after getting it, one shall assiduously and virtuously maintain it.

## \|\| 9.20.34 \|\|

*dharmamūlaṃ hi mānuṣyaṃ labdhvā sarvārthasādhakam \|*  
*yadi lābhāya yatnaḥ syānmūlaṃ rakṣetsvayaṃ tataḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. Human birth has virtue as its root. It shall achieve all objects. Even if there is no endeavour to achieve any special aim after getting human birth at least one should preserve the root.

## \|\| 9.20.35 \|\|

*mānuṣye'pi ca vipratvaṃ yaḥ prāpya khalu durlabham \|*  
*nācaratyātmanaḥ śreyaḥ ko'nyastasmādacetanaḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. Who can be a more senseless man than one who does not strive for the welfare of the soul after attaining the rare birth as a brahmin in the course of human birth?

## \|\| 9.20.36 \|\|

*dvīpānāmeva sarveṣāṃ karmabhūmiriyamucyate \|*  
*itassvargaśca mokṣaśca prāpyate samupārjitaḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. Of all the continents, this continent is called the land of sacred rites. It is from here that heaven and salvation are attained.

## \|\| 9.20.37 \|\|

*deśe'sminbhārate varṣe prāpya mānuṣyamadhruvam \|*  
*na kuryādātmanaḥ śreyastenātmā khalu vaṃcitaḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. Indeed the soul is deceived by that man who does not strive for welfare despite attaining human form in this land of Bhārata Varṣa.

## \|\| 9.20.38 \|\|

*karmabhūmiriyaṃ vipra phalabhūmirasau smṛtā \|*  
*iha yatkriyate karma svarge tadanubhujyate \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. O brahmin, this is the land of sacred riles and that is the land of enjoyment. The fruit of the sacred rite performed is enjoyed in heaven.

## \|\| 9.20.39 \|\|

*yāvatsvāsthyaṃ śarīrasya tāvaddharmaṃ samācaret \|*  
*asvasthaścodito'pyanyairna kiṃcitkartumutsahet \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. One should continue to perform virtuous rites as long as the health and normalcy of the body is maintained. A sick man is not eager to perform anything even when urged by others.

## \|\| 9.20.40 \|\|

*adhruveṇa śarīreṇa dhruvaṃ yo na prasādhayet \|*  
*dhruvaṃ tasya paribhraṣṭamadhruvaṃ naṣṭameva ca \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. If anyone does not try to achieve the permanent through the non-permanent body, the permanent is lost for him and the non-permanent has already perished.

## \|\| 9.20.41 \|\|

*āyuṣaḥ khaṃḍakhaṃḍāni nipataṃti tadagrataḥ \|*  
*ahorātropadeśena kimarthaṃ nāvabudhyate \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. Under the pretext of passing days and nights pieces of life fall off. Why is it not realised?

## \|\| 9.20.42 \|\|

*yadā na jñāyate mṛtyuḥ kadā kasya bhaviṣyati \|*  
*ākasmike hi maraṇe dhṛtiṃ viṃdati kastathā \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. When it is not known who will die and when, when the death is so sudden and unexpected who can have self-confidence?

## \|\| 9.20.43 \|\|

*parityajya yadā sarvamekākī yāsyati dhruvam \|*  
*na dadāti kadā kasmātpātheyārthamidaṃ dhanam \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. It is certain that one will ultimately go away leaving off everything. Why then docs he not give off in charity, all the wealth, so that it can be the packet of fooding on his way to Yama’s abode?

## \|\| 9.20.44 \|\|

*gṛhītadānapātheyaḥ sukhaṃ yāti yamālayam \|*  
*anyathā kliśyate jaṃtuḥ pātheyarahite pathi \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. A man who has taken the packet of fooding with him by virtue of giving charitable gifts, goes to Yama’s abode without difficulty. Otherwise the creature suffers on the way without the packet of food.

## \|\| 9.20.45 \|\|

*yeṣāṃ kāleya puṇyāni paripūrṇāni sarvataḥ \|*  
*gacchatāṃ svargadeśaṃ hi teṣāṃ lābhaḥ padepade \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. O Kāleya, they gain at every step whose merits are full and perfect and who go to heaven.

## \|\| 9.20.46 \|\|

*iti jñātvā naraḥ puṇyaṃ kuryātpāpaṃ vivarjayet \|*  
*puṇyena yāti devatvamapuṇyo narakaṃ vrajet \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. Realising this, man should perform meritorious rites and avoid sinful activities. Thanks to the merit one attains godhood. A person devoid of merit goes to hell.

## \|\| 9.20.47 \|\|

*ye manāgapi deveśaṃ prapannāśśaraṇaṃ śivam \|*  
*te'pi ghoraṃ na paśyaṃti yamaṃ na narakaṃ tathā \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. They who take refuge in Śiva, lord of the gods, even slightly, do not see the terrible Yama nor the terrible hell.

## \|\| 9.20.48 \|\|

*kiṃtu pāpairmahāmohaiḥ kiṃcitkāle śivājñayā \|*  
*vasaṃti tatra mānuṣyāstato yāṃti śivāspadam \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. But at the behest of Śiva, due to sins and great delusions, those men stay there for sometime and then go to the region of Śiva.

## \|\| 9.20.49 \|\|

*ye punassarvabhāvena pratipannā maheśvaram \|*  
*na te limpaṃti pāpena padmapatramivāmbhasā \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. But those who have taken refuge in lord Śiva wholly are not smeared with sin like the leaf of the lotus with water.

## \|\| 9.20.50 \|\|

*uktaṃ śiveti yairnāma tathā harahareti ca \|*  
*na teṣāṃ narakādbhītiryamāddhi munisattama \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. O excellent sage, they who repeat the names of Śiva need not be afraid of hell or Yama.

## \|\| 9.20.51 \|\|

*paralokasya pātheyaṃ mokṣopāyamanāmayam \|*  
*puṇyasaṃghaikanilayaṃ śiva ityakṣaradvayam \|\| 51 \|\|*

51\. The two syllables “Śiva” constitute the packet of flooding for the journey to the other world; it is the means of salvation, free from worries and is the abode of all merits.

## \|\| 9.20.52 \|\|

*śivanāmaiva saṃsāramahārogekaśāmakam \|*  
*nānyatsaṃsārarogasya śāmakaṃ dṛśyate mayā \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. It is only the name of Śiva that subdues and quells the great ailment of worldly existence. Nothing else does the same, as I see.

## \|\| 9.20.53 \|\|

*brahmahatyāsahasrāṇi purā kṛtvā tu pulkasaḥ \|*  
*śiveti nāma vimalaṃ śrutvā mokṣaṃ gataḥ purā \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. Formerly Pulkaśa committed thousands of murders of Brahmins but on hearing the sacred name Śiva, he attained salvation.

## \|\| 9.20.54 \|\|

*tasmādvivarddhayedbhaktimīśvare satataṃ budhaḥ \|*  
*śivabhaktyā mahāprājña bhuktiṃ muktiṃ ca viṃdati \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. Hence a sensible man shall enhance his devotion to Śiva incessantly. O intelligent one, one attains worldly pleasures and salvation due to the devotion to Śiva.

## Chapter 21 - The fruits of righteous war

## \|\| 9.21.1-2 \|\|

*\|\| vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*brāhmaṇatvaṃ hi duṣprāpyaṃ nisargādbrāhmaṇo bhavet \|*  
*īśvarasya mukhātkṣatraṃ bāhubhyāmūruto viśaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

*padbhyāṃ śūdrassamutpanna iti tasya mukhācchrutiḥ \|*  
*kimu sthitimadhaḥsthānādāpnuvanti hyato vada \|\| 2 \|\|*

Vyāsa said:—

1-2. Brahminhood is difficult to be achieved. Brahmin is born of the face of Śiva; Kṣatra, of the arms; Vaiśya of the thighs and Śūdra of the feet of the lord—so says the Veda. But do people attain salvation from the lower position? Please tell me.

## \|\| 9.21.3 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*duṣkṛtena tu kāleya sthānādbhraśyanti mānavāḥ \|*  
*śreṣṭhaṃ sthānaṃ samāsādya tasmādrakṣeta paṇḍitaḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

3\. O Kāleya, men fall off their status due to evil deeds. Hence, after attaining the high status, the learned man shall preserve it.

## \|\| 9.21.4 \|\|

*yastu vipratvamutsṛjya kṣatrayonyāṃ prasūyate \|*  
*brāhmaṇyātsa paribhraṣṭaḥ kṣatriyatvaṃ niṣevate \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. He who forsakes brahminhood and is born as a Kṣatriya falls off the status of a brahmin and assumes the status of a Kṣatriya.

## \|\| 9.21.5 \|\|

*adharmasevanānmūḍhastathaiva parivartate \|*  
*janmāntarasahasrāṇi tamasyāviśate yataḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Due to his resorting to evil, the foolish fellow goes on changing thus. He takes thousands of births and plunges himself into gloom.

## \|\| 9.21.6 \|\|

*tasmātprāpya paraṃ sthānaṃ pramādyanna tu nāśayet \|*  
*svasthānaṃ sarvadā rakṣetprāpyāpi vipado naraḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. Hence after attaining the highest status one should not err or destroy it. Even in the face of adversity one shall always preserve his status.

## \|\| 9.21.7 \|\|

*brāhmaṇatvaṃ śubhaṃ prāpya brāhmaṇyaṃ yo'vamanyate \|*  
*bhojyābhojyaṃ na jānāti sa pumānkṣatriyo bhavet \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. After attaining the auspicious brahminhood if a person disrespects brahminhood and does not differentiate between what could be eaten and what could not be eaten he becomes a Kṣatriya.

## \|\| 9.21.8 \|\|

*karmaṇā yena medhāvī śūdro vaiśyo hi jāyate \|*  
*tatte vakṣyāmi nikhilaṃ yena varṇottamo bhavet \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. I shall explain to you entirely that which gives a higher caste whereby an intelligent Śūdra becomes a Vaiśya.

## \|\| 9.21.9 \|\|

*śūdrakarma yathoddiṣṭaṃ śūdro bhūtvā samācaret \|*  
*yathāvatparicaryyāṃ tu triṣu varṇeṣu nityadā \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Being born a Śūdra, one shall take up the duties of Śūdra as ordained, viz, the due service to the people of the other three castes.

## \|\| 9.21.10-15 \|\|

*kurute kāmayānastu śūdro'pi vaiśyatāṃ vrajet \|*  
*yo yojayeddhanairvaiśyo juhvānaśca yathāvidhi \|\| 10 \|\|*

*agnihotramupādāya śeṣānna kṛtabhojanaḥ \|*  
*sa vaiśyaḥ kṣatriyakule jāyate nātra saṃśayaḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

*kṣattriyo jāyate yajñaisaṃskṛtairāttadakṣiṇaiḥ \|*  
*adhīte svargamanvicchaṃstretāgniśaraṇaṃ sadā \|\| 12 \|\|*

*ārdrahastapado nityaṃ kṣitiṃ dharmeṇa pālayet \|*  
*ṛtukālābhigāmī ca svabhāryyādharmatatparaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

*sarvātithyaṃ trivargasya bhūtebhyo dīyatāmiti \|*  
*gobrāhmaṇātmano'rthaṃ hi saṃgrāmābhihato bhavet \|\| 14 \|\|*

*tenāgnimantrapūtātmā kṣattriyo brāhmaṇo bhavet \|*  
*vidhito brāhmaṇo bhūtvā yājakastu prajāyate \|\| 15 \|\|*

10-15. By doing this regularly and aspiring for Vaiśya-hood, even a Śūdra attains it, A Vaiśya who uses his wealth for sacred rites, who performs sacrifices duly, who performs the Agnihotra and partakes of the remnants of food therefrom, is reborn in a Kṣatriya family. There is no doubt in this. He is born as a Kṣatriya by means of consecrated sacrifices with proper gifts. A Kṣatriya shall do like this. He shall study the Vedas desirous of heaven. He shall maintain the three sacrificial fires. He shall wash his hands and feet before entering shrines. He shall protect the earth virtuously. He shall cohabit with his wife during the prescribed period after the monthly menses. He shall remain devout and pious. He shall be hospitable to all. He shall fight and get killed in battle for acquiring facilities to achieve the three aims of life (virtue, wealth and love) to all living beings or for the sake of cows and brahmins. Thus when his soul is purified by fires and mantras he becomes a brahmin. After becoming a brahmin, if he follows the rules he becomes a priest in a sacrifice.

## \|\| 9.21.16 \|\|

*svakarmanirato nityaṃ satyavādī jitendriyaḥ \|*  
*prāpyate vipulassvargo devānāmapi vallabhaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. Strictly observing his rites, speaking truth and controlling the sense-organs he derives vast pleasures of heaven. He becomes a favourite of the gods.

## \|\| 9.21.17-18 \|\|

*brahmaṇatvaṃ hi duṣprāpyaṃ kṛcchreṇa sādhyate naraiḥ \|*  
*brāhmaṇyātsakalaṃ prāpya mokṣaścāpi munīśvara \|\| 17 \|\|*

*tasmātsarvaprayatnena brāhmaṇo dharmatatparaḥ \|*  
*sādhanaṃ sarvavargasya rakṣedbrāhmaṇyamuttamam \|\| 18 \|\|*

17-18. O great sage, brahminhood is very difficult to achieve. It is attained by men who undergo hardships. Salvation is achieved after attaining brahminhood entirely. Hence a brahmin shall be virtuous. With great effort he shall preserve his excellent brahminhood which is a good means of achieving all the aims of life.

## \|\| 9.21.19 \|\|

*vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*saṃgrāmasyeha māhātmyaṃ tvayoktaṃ munisattama \|*  
*etadicchāmyahaṃ śrotuṃ brūhi tvaṃ vadatāṃ vara \|\| 19 \|\|*

Vyāsa said:—

19\. O excellent sage, you have mentioned about the glory. O foremost among the eloquent, I wish to hear this. Please mention.

## \|\| 9.21.20-21 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*agniṣṭomādibhiryajñairiṣṭvā vipuladakṣiṇaiḥ \|*  
*na tatphalamavāpnoti saṃgrāme yadavāpnuyāt \|\| 20 \|\|*

*iti tattvavidaḥ prāhuryajñakarmavidassadā \|*  
*tasmāttatte pravakṣyāmi yatphalaṃ śastrajīvinām \|\| 21 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

20-21. Those who know the principles, who understand sacrifices and sacred rites say thus—“By performing the Agniṣṭoma and other sacrifices with profuse gifts one does not attain that benefit which is attained through battle.” Hence I shall explain to you the fruit attained by those who maintain themselves by means of weapons.

## \|\| 9.21.22-24 \|\|

*dharmalābho'rthalābhaśca yaśolābhastathaiva ca \|*  
*yaśśūro vāṃchate yuddhaṃ vimṛndanparavāhinīm \|\| 22 \|\|*

*tasya dharmārtha kāmāśca yajñaścaiva sadakṣiṇaḥ \|*  
*paraṃ hyabhimukhaṃ dattvā tadyānaṃ yo'dhirohati \|\| 23 \|\|*

*viṣṇuloke sa jāyeta yaśca yuddhe'parājitaḥ \|*  
*aśvamedhānavāpnoti caturo na mṛtassa cet \|\| 24 \|\|*

22-24, The hero who offers battle subduing the army of the enemy attains virtue, wealth and fame. He achieves the fruit of a sacrifice with due gifts and the three aims of life, virtue, wealth and love. He who faces the enemy, kills him and gets on to the enemy’s chariot goes to Viṣṇuloka. He who is not defeated in war, nor killed in it attains the fruit of four horse-sacrifices.

## \|\| 9.21.25 \|\|

*yastu śastramanutsṛjya mriyate vāhinī mukhe \|*  
*sammukho vartate śūrassa svargānna nivartate \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. He who dies in the war-front without surrendering his weapons attains heaven from which there is no return.

## \|\| 9.21.26 \|\|

*rājā vā rājaputro vā senāpatirathāpi vā \|*  
*hatakṣātreṇa yaḥ śūrastasya loko'kṣayo bhavet \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. The king, or prince or the general who heroically fights and is killed by Kṣatriya attains everlasting regions.

## \|\| 9.21.27 \|\|

*yāvaṃti tasya romāṇi bhidyante'strairmahāhave \|*  
*tāvato labhate lokānsarvakāmadughā'kṣayān \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. He attains as many worlds as the number of his hairs split by arrows in the battle. All of these will be wish-yielding and eternal.

## \|\| 9.21.28 \|\|

*vīrāsanaṃ vīraśayyā vīrasthānasthitissthirā \|*  
*sarvadā bhavati vyāsa iha loke paratra ca \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. O Vyāsa, both here and hereafter he will have heroic seat, heroic bed, heroic status and heroic stability always.

## \|\| 9.21.29 \|\|

*gavārthe brāhmaṇārthe ca sthānasvāmyarthameva ca \|*  
*ye mṛtāste sukhaṃ yāṃti yathā sukṛtinastathā \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. Those who die for the protection of cows, brahmins, status and the master go happily to the other world like virtuous persons.{GL\_NOTE::}

## \|\| 9.21.30 \|\|

*yaḥ kaścidbrāhmaṇaṃ hatvā paścātprāṇānparityajet \|*  
*tatrāsau svapateryuddhe sa svargānna nivartate \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. Even a slayer of a brahmin fighting for his master in a battle and dying there attains heaven which becomes his permanent abode.

## \|\| 9.21.31 \|\|

*kravyādairdatibhiścaiva hatasya gatiruttamā \|*  
*dvijagosvāmināmarthe bhavedvipuladākṣayā \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. The goal realised by the person who dies for the brahmins, cows and his master, being killed by carnivorous beasts or tusked beasts is excellent. It yields him extensive and never-ending benefits.

## \|\| 9.21.32 \|\|

*śaknotviha samarthaśca yaṣṭuṃ kratuśatairapi \|*  
*ātmadehaparityāgaḥ kartuṃ yudhi suduṣkaraḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. A man may be competent to perform hundreds of sacrifices, but it is very difficult to renounce his own body in a righteous war.

## \|\| 9.21.33 \|\|

*yuddhaṃ puṇyatamaṃ svargyaṃ rūpajñaṃ sarvatomukham \|*  
*sarveṣāmeva varṇānāṃ kṣatriyasya viśeṣataḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. A righteous war is conducive to the attainment of heaven. It is sanctifying. It makes known the multi-faced beauty of a man. This is the case with the people of all castes, particularly so in the case of a Kṣatriya.

## \|\| 9.21.34 \|\|

*bhṛśaṃ caiva pravakṣyāmi yuddhadharmaṃ sanātanam \|*  
*yādṛśāya prahartavyaṃ yādṛśaṃ parivarjayet \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. Now I shall explain clearly the eternal virtue concerning war, as to who can be attacked and hit and who shall be spared.

## \|\| 9.21.35 \|\|

*ātatāyinamāyāṃtamapi vedāṃtagaṃ dvijam \|*  
*jighāṃsaṃtaṃ jighāṃsettu na tena brahmahā bhavet \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. Even if a brahmin well-versed in Vedānta doctrine were to attack one violently with lethal weapons one can fight him in return. He will not become guilty of brahminicide thereby.

## \|\| 9.21.36 \|\|

*haṃtavyo'pi na haṃtavyaḥ pānīyaṃ yaśca yācate \|*  
*raṇe hatvāturānvyāsa sa naro brahmahā bhavet \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. Even a murderer shall not be attacked and killed if he is thirsty and begs for water. O Vyāsa, by killing the sick and the distressed even in battle a man becomes guilty of brahminicide.

## \|\| 9.21.37 \|\|

*vyādhitaṃ durbalaṃ bālaṃ stryanāthau kṛpaṇaṃ dhruvam \|*  
*dhanurbhagnaṃ chinnaguṇaṃ hatvā vai brahmahā bhavet \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. One becomes a murderer of a brahmin by killing the sick, the feeble, the child, the hen-pecked, the miserly and one whose bow or bowstring is broken.

## \|\| 9.21.38 \|\|

*evaṃ vicāryya saddhīmānbhavetprītyāḥ raṇapriyaḥ \|*  
*sajanmanaḥ phalaṃ prāpya paratreha pramodate \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. After considering all this, an intelligent man shall strenuously indulge in battles. He will achieve the purpose of his life and rejoice here and hereafter.

## Chapter 22 - The origin and development of the body (deha)

## \|\| 9.22.1 \|\|

*vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*vidhiṃ tāta vadedānīṃ jīva janmavidhānataḥ \|*  
*garbhe sthitiṃ ca tasyāpi vairāgyārthaṃ munīśvara \|\| 1 \|\|*

Vyāsa said:—

1\. O dear, O great sage, for realizing the worldly detachment please mention the detailed mode of the birth of living beings and their stay in the womb.

## \|\| 9.22.2 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*śṛṇu vyāsa samāsena śāstrasāramaśeṣataḥ \|*  
*vadiṣyāmi suvairāgyaṃ mumukṣorbhavabaṃdhakṛt \|\| 2 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

2\. O Vyāsa, listen succinctly to the essence of the sacred lore. I shall explain the excellent mode of worldly detachment which removes the bondage with the world, of a man desirous of liberation.

## \|\| 9.22.3-4 \|\|

*pākapātrasya madhye tu pṛthagannaṃ pṛthagjalam \|*  
*agnerūrdhvaṃ jalaṃ sthāpyaṃ tadannaṃ ca jalopari \|\| 3 \|\|*

*jalasyādhassa cāgnirhi sthito'gniṃ dhamate śanaiḥ \|*  
*vāyunādhamyamāno'gniratyuṣṇaṃ kurute jalam \|\| 4 \|\|*

3-4. When we cook rice the process is as follows. In the cooking vessel the rice and water remain separate. Water is above fire. The rice is above water. The wind slowly blows against the fire beneath the water. The fire kindled by the wind makes the water boil.

## \|\| 9.22.5 \|\|

*tadannamuṣṇatoyena samantātpacyate punaḥ \|*  
*dvidhā bhavati tatpakvaṃ pṛthakkiṭṭaṃ pṛthagrasaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. The rice with hot-boiling water all round it becomes cooked. When cooked it becomes separated into sediment and juice.

## \|\| 9.22.6 \|\|

*malairdvādaśabhiḥ kiṭṭaṃ bhinnaṃ dehādbahirbhavet \|*  
*rasastu dehe sarati sa puṣṭastena jāyate \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. (More or less a similar process takes place in the body.) The sediment in the body (deha) consists of twelve forms of waste matter. These are split and sent out of the body. The juice matter circulates in the body. Man gets nourished through it.

## \|\| 9.22.7 \|\|

*karṇākṣināsikā jihvā dantāḥ śiśno gudaṃ nakhāḥ \|*  
*malāśrayaḥ kaphaḥ svedo viṇmūtraṃ dvādaśa smṛtāḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. The ears, the eyes, the now, the tongue, the teeth, the penis, the anus, and the nails are the receptacles of dirt secreted by them. The phlegm, the sweat, the faeces and the urine—these together make up the twelve.

## \|\| 9.22.8 \|\|

*hṛtpadme pratibaddhāśca sarvanāḍyassamaṃtataḥ \|*  
*jñeyā rasapravāhinyastatprakāraṃ bruve mune \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. All the vessels scattered all over the body are joined centrally at the heart. These vessels shall be understood to convey Rasa. O sage, I shall mention their process of working.

## \|\| 9.22.9 \|\|

*tāsāṃ mukheṣu taṃ sūkṣmaṃ prāṇassthāpayet rasam \|*  
*rasena tena nāḍīstāḥ prāṇaṃ pūrayate punaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Prāṇa (the vital air) places the subtle lymph at the mouths of the vessels. Then it fills the entire vessels with the lymph.

## \|\| 9.22.10-11 \|\|

*punaḥ prayāṃti saṃpūrṇāstāśca dehaṃ samaṃtataḥ \|*  
*tatassa nāḍīmadhyasthaśśarīreṇātmanā rasaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

*pacyate pacyamānācca bhavetpākadvayaṃ punaḥ \|*  
*tvak tayā veṣṭyate pūrvaṃ rudhiraṃ ca prajāyate \|\| 11 \|\|*

10-11. Thus filled, the vessels circulate all over the body. Then the lymph inside the vessels gets cooked by the fire in the body. There are two modes of cooking. \[Probably the separation of sediment and watery part\]. The skin is covered thereby. \[The cells constituting the skin are generated\]. Blood is produced.

## \|\| 9.22.12 \|\|

*raktāllomāni māṃsaṃ ca keśāḥ snāyuśca māṃsataḥ \|*  
*snāyutaśca tathāsthīni nakhā majjāsthisaṃbhavāḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. The hair of the body and flesh are produced from the blood. The hair on the head and the sinews are produced from the flesh. From the sinews the bones are produced. The nails and marrow are produced from the bones.

## \|\| 9.22.13 \|\|

*majjākāraṇavaikalyaṃ śukraṃ hi prasavātmakam \|*  
*iti dvādaśadhānnasya pariṇāmaḥ prakīrtitāḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. An alternative cause of marrow, the semen itself becomes progeny. Thus the twelvefold metabolism of blood is explained.

## \|\| 9.22.14-16 \|\|

*śukro'nnājjāyate śukrāddivyadehasya saṃbhavaḥ \|*  
*ṛtukāle yadā śukraṃ nirdoṣaṃ yonisaṃsthitam \|\| 14 \|\|*

*tadvā tadvāyusaṃspṛṣṭaṃ strīraktenaikatāṃ vrajet \|*  
*visargakāle śukrasya jīvaḥ kāraṇasaṃyutaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

*saṃvṛtaḥ praviśedyoniṃ karmabhissvairniyojitaḥ \|*  
*tacchukraraktamekasthamekāhātkalilaṃ bhavet \|\| 16 \|\|*

14-16. The semen is secreted from the food eaten From the semen, the birth of another body is made possible. When the semen unalloyed is deposited in the vaginal passage during the prescribed period of cohabitation after the monthly menses, then the semen blown by the vital wind mingles with the blood of the woman. At the time when the semen is discharged the individual soul with the causal body or unit of sense-organs etc. enters the vaginal passage fully covered and urged by its past actions. The semen and blood in the unified state become foetus in a day.

## \|\| 9.22.17 \|\|

*paṃcarātreṇa kalilaṃ budbudākāratāṃ vrajet \|*  
*budbudassaptarātreṇa māṃsapeśī bhavetpunaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. In five nights the foetus assumes the shape cf bubbles and the bubbly mass becomes muscular mass in seven nights.

## \|\| 9.22.18-19 \|\|

*grīvā śiraśca skaṃdhau ca pṛṣṭhavaṃśastathodaram \|*  
*pāṇipādantathā pārśve kaṭirgātraṃ tathaiva ca \|\| 18 \|\|*

*dvimāsābhyantareṇaiva kramaśassaṃbhavediha \|*  
*tribhirmāsaiḥ prajāyaṃte sarve hyaṃkurasaṃdhayaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

18-19. Within two months the following parts of the body are formulated in order Viz. the neck, the head the shoulders, the backbone, the belly, hands and feet, the sides the lips etc. Within three months all the joints are completed.

## \|\| 9.22.20 \|\|

*māsaiścaturbhiraṃgulyaḥ prajāyaṃte yathākramam \|*  
*mukhaṃ nāsā ca karṇau māsaiḥ paṃcabhireva ca \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. Within the fourth month the fingers are duly formed. The mouth, nose, and cars are ready within the fifth month,

## \|\| 9.22.21 \|\|

*dantapaṃktistathā guhyaṃ jāyaṃte ca nakhāḥ punaḥ \|*  
*karṇayostu bhavecchidraṃ ṣaṇmāsābhyaṃtareṇa tu \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. Within six months the row of teeth, the private parts and the nails and the orifice of the ears take shape.

## \|\| 9.22.22 \|\|

*pāyurmehamupasthaṃ ca nābhiścābhyupajāyate \|*  
*saṃdhayo ye ca gātreṣu māsairjāyaṃti saptabhiḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. The rectum, or the penis of the vaginal passage and the navel too are formulated. Within the seventh all the limb-joints are completed.

## \|\| 9.22.23 \|\|

*aṃgapratyaṃgasaṃpūrṇaḥ paripakvassa tiṣṭhati \|*  
*udare māturācchanno jarāyau muni sattama \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. O excellent sage, thus the complete child with all the limbs stays in the womb covered by the embryonic skin.

## \|\| 9.22.24 \|\|

*māturāhāracauryyeṇa ṣaḍvidhena rasena tu \|*  
*nābhinālanibaddhena varddhate sa dinedine \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. The child increases and develops day by day, stealthily taking in the food of the mother, of all the six tastes, through the umbilical cord.

## \|\| 9.22.25 \|\|

*tatasmṛtiṃ labhejjīvassaṃpūrṇe'smiñśarīrake \|*  
*sukhaṃ duḥkhaṃ vijānāti nidrāsvapnaṃ purākṛtam \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. Then the soul complete in this body recollects all the pleasures and sorrows and every thing done by it formerly as if in a dream.

## \|\| 9.22.26 \|\|

*mṛtaścāhaṃ punarjāto jātaścāhaṃ punarmṛtaḥ \|*  
*nānāyonisahasrāṇi mayā dṛṣṭāni jāyatā \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. “I am dead and born many times In these different births I have seen many types of wombs.

## \|\| 9.22.27 \|\|

*adhunā jātamātro'haṃ prāptasaṃskāra eva ca \|*  
*śreyo'munā kariṣyāmi yena garbhe na saṃbhavaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. This time when I am born and consecrated I shall strive for welfare by means of this body so that I may not have to stay in another womb in future.

## \|\| 9.22.28 \|\|

*garbhasthaściṃtayatyevamahaṃ garbhādvinissṛtaḥ \|*  
*anveṣyāmi śivajñānaṃ saṃsāravinivartakam \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. So thinks the child in the womb “As soon as I am released from this womb I shall seek Śiva’s perfect knowledge that repels all worldly existence.”

## \|\| 9.22.29 \|\|

*evaṃ sa garbhaduḥkhena mahatā paripīḍitaḥ \|*  
*jīvaḥ karmavaśādāste mokṣopāyaṃ viciṃtayan \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. Troubled and tormented by the distress in the womb as a result of his past actions the individual soul stays there pondering over the means of liberation.

## \|\| 9.22.30 \|\|

*yathā girivarākrāṃtaḥ kaścidduḥkhena tiṣṭhati \|*  
*tathā jarāyuṇā dehī duḥkhaṃ tiṣṭhati veṣṭitaḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. Just as a traveller surrounded by huge impassable mountains on all sides stands helplessly in his distress, so also the creature in the womb enveloped by the embryonic skin stays miserably.

## \|\| 9.22.31 \|\|

*patitassāgare yadvadduḥkhamāste samākulaḥ \|*  
*garbhodakena siktāṃgassarvadākulitastadā \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. Just as a man drowning in the sea is very much distressed, so also the child in the womb completely drenched by the liquid secretion in the womb is always distressed excessively.

## \|\| 9.22.32 \|\|

*lohakuṃbhe yathā nyastaḥ pacyate kaścidagninā \|*  
*garbhakuṃbhe tathā kṣiptaḥ pacyate jaṭharāgninā \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. Just as a person is cooked and boiled in an iron cauldron by means of fire, so also the child hurled into the pot of womb is. cooked by the gastric fire.

## \|\| 9.22.33 \|\|

*sūcībhiragnivarṇābhinirbhinnasya niraṃtaram \|*  
*yadduḥkhaṃ jāyate tasya tatra saṃsthasya cādhikam \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. For him staying inside the womb the misery is equal to, nay, more than the misery of a person incessantly pierced by means of redhot needles.

## \|\| 9.22.34 \|\|

*garbhāvāsātparaṃ duḥkhaṃ kaṣṭaṃ naivāsti kutracit \|*  
*dehināṃ duḥkhabahulaṃ sughoramatisaṃkaṭam \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. There is nowhere a greater misery than staying within the womb. To all embodied souls it is full of misery, very terrible and grievous.

## \|\| 9.22.35 \|\|

*ityetatsumahadduḥkhaṃ pāpināṃ parikīrtitam \|*  
*kevalaṃ dharmabudīnāṃ saptamāsairbhavassadā \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. This type of misery is for the sinners. But those of virtuous intellect take birth within seven months.

## \|\| 9.22.36 \|\|

*garbhātsudurlabhaṃ duḥkhaṃ yoniyaṃtranipīḍanāt \|*  
*bhavetpāpātmanāṃ vyāsa na hi dharmayutātmanām \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. Getting crushed in the vaginal passage as if within a machine is a misery more grievous than the stay within the womb. O Vyāsa, this takes place in regard to the sinners and not the virtuous.

## \|\| 9.22.37 \|\|

*ikṣuvatpīḍyamānasya yaṃtreṇaiva samaṃtataḥ \|*  
*śirasā tāḍyamānasya pāpa mudgarakeṇa ca \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. Just as the sugarcane is crushed in the machine, the sinner is crushed and beaten on the head by the ironclub of sin.

## \|\| 9.22.38 \|\|

*yaṃtreṇa pīḍitā yadvannissārā ssyustilāḥ kṣaṇāt \|*  
*tathā śarīraṃ nissāraṃ yoniyaṃtranipīḍanāt \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. Just as the gingelly seeds pressed in the oil machine become a worthless residue, so also the body becomes a waste being crushed by the muscular walls of the vaginal passage.

## \|\| 9.22.39-43 \|\|

*asthipādatulāstaṃbhaṃ snāyubandhena yaṃtritam \|*  
*raktamāṃsamṛdāliptaṃ viṇmūtradravyabhājanam \|\| 39 \|\|*

*keśaromanakhacchannaṃ rogāyatanamāturam \|*  
*vadanaikamahādvāraṃ gavākṣāṣṭakabhūṣitam \|\| 40 \|\|*

*oṣṭhadvayakapāṭaṃ ca tathā jihvārgalānvitam \|*  
*bhogatṛṣṇāturaṃ mūḍhaṃ rāgadveṣavaśānugam \|\| 41 \|\|*

*saṃvartitāṃgapratyaṃgaṃ jarāyupariveṣṭitam \|*  
*saṃkaṭenāviviktena yonimārgeṇa nirgatam \|\| 42 \|\|*

*viṇmūtraraktasiktāṃgaṃ vikośikasamudbhavam \|*  
*asthipañjaravikhyātamasmiñjñeyaṃ kalevaram \|\| 43 \|\|*

39-43. This body in the womb is in an unenviable position. The hands and legs are like suspended pillars. Sinews tie them up in various ways. The whole body is smeared with dirty blood and flesh. It is the receptacle of faeces and urine. It is covered with hairs and nails. It is sickly and an abode of ailments. The only great opening is the mouth. It has eight small apertures like windows. The two lips are like two doors. The tongue is the bolt for them. The body is sick due to its thirst for pleasures. It is subservient to passion and hatred and utterly deluded. The various limbs overlap one another. It is surrounded by the embryonic membrane. With very great pain and difficulty it has ṭo come out of the vaginal passage. It is drenched with urine, blood and faeces. It comes out as from a sheath. It is a mass of skeleton.

## \|\| 9.22.44 \|\|

*śatatrayaṃ ṣaṣṭyadhikaṃ paṃcapeśīśatāni ca \|*  
*sārddhābhistisṛbhiśchannaṃ samaṃtādromakoṭibhiḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. The bones are three hundred and sixty in number. There are five hundred muscles. The body is covered with thirty-fìve million hairs.

## \|\| 9.22.45 \|\|

*śarīraṃ sthūlasūkṣmābhirdṛśyā'dṛśyā hi tāssmṛtāḥ \|*  
*etāvatībhirnāḍībhiḥ koṭibhistatsamaṃtataḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. The hairs are subtle and gross, visible and invisible. The veins and vessels within the body too are so many in number.

## \|\| 9.22.46 \|\|

*asvedamadhubhiryābhiraṃtasthaḥ sravate bahiḥ \|*  
*dvātriṃśaddaśanāḥ proktā viṃśatiśca nakhāḥ smṛtāḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. The internal secretions circulate within and go out through these vessels. The teeth are thirty two in number and the nails are twenty.

## \|\| 9.22.47 \|\|

*pittasya kuḍavaṃ jñeyaṃ kaphasyāthāḍhakaṃ smṛtam \|*  
*vasāyāśca palaṃ viṃśattadardhaṃ kapilasya ca \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. The measure of bile content in the body is one Kuḍava. (1/4 of Prastha or 12 handfuls). The phlegm is one Āḍhaka (256 fistfuls). The vasā fat weighs 20 palas. Half of that is the tawny matter.

## \|\| 9.22.48 \|\|

*paṃcārddhaṃ tu tulā jñeyā palāni daśa medasaḥ \|*  
*palatrayaṃ mahāraktaṃ majjāyāśca caturguṇam \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. The weight of solid fat is 2½ Tulās and ten palas. Blood is three palas and it is four times the marrow.

## \|\| 9.22.49 \|\|

*śukrorddhaṃ kuḍavaṃ jñeyaṃ tadbījaṃ dehināṃ balam \|*  
*māṃsasya caikapiṃḍena palasāhasramucyate \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. The semen is half a Kuḍava. The strength of the living beings depends on it. A mass of flesh weighs a thousand palas.

## \|\| 9.22.50 \|\|

*raktaṃ palaśataṃ jñeyaṃ viṇmūtraṃ yatpramāṇata \|*  
*aṃjalayaśca catvāraścatvāro munisattama \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. O excellent sage, the blood is hundred palas according to which the faeces and the urine form four handfuls each.

## \|\| 9.22.51 \|\|

*iti dehagṛhaṃ hyetannityasyānityamātmanaḥ \|*  
*aviśuddhaṃ viśuddhasya karmabaṃdhādvinirmitam \|\| 51 \|\|*

51\. Such is the transient abode, the body of the eternal soul. It is impure while the soul is pure. It is generated due to his past actions and their bondage.

## Chapter 23 - The description of infancy (bālya)

## \|\| 9.23.1 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*śṛṇu vyāsa mahābuddhe dehasyāśucitāṃ mune \|*  
*mahattvaṃ ca svabhāvasya samāsātkathayāmyaham \|\| 1 \|\|*

Summary: The description of infancy and other stages and the impurity of the body.

Sanatkumāra said:—

1\. O Vyāsa, O highly intelligent sage, listen to the impurity of the body and the greatness of good feelings. I shall explain briefly.

## \|\| 9.23.2 \|\|

*śukraśoṇitasaṃyogāddehassaṃjāyate yataḥ \|*  
*nityaṃ viṇmūtrasaṃpūrṇastenāyamaśucissmṛtaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. The body is considered impure because it is produced by the mingling of semen and blood and is always full of faeces and urine.

## \|\| 9.23.3 \|\|

*yathāṃtarviṣṭhayā pūrṇaśśucimānna bahirghaṭaḥ \|*  
*śodhyamāno hi deho'yaṃ tenāyamaśucistataḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. Just as a pot, full of faeces cannot become pure even when washed outside, so also the body washed outside cannot become pure. Hence it is impure.

## \|\| 9.23.4 \|\|

*saṃprāpyātipavitrāṇi paṃcagavyahavīṃṣi cā \|*  
*aśucitvaṃ kṣaṇādyāṃti kimanyadaśucistataḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. Even after taking in the sanctifying Pañcagavya, Havis (Ghee oblation) etc. the body becomes impure the next moment. What else is more impure than this body?

## \|\| 9.23.5 \|\|

*hṛdyānyapyannapānāni yaṃ prāpya surabhīṇi ca \|*  
*aśucitvaṃ prayāṃtyāśu kimanyadaśucistataḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Even after taking in sweet, fragrant and delicious foodstuffs and drinks the body becomes impure quickly. What else is more impure than this.

## \|\| 9.23.6 \|\|

*he janāḥ kinna paśyaṃti yanniryāti dinedine \|*  
*svadehātkaśmalaṃ pūtistadādhāraḥ kathaṃ śuciḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. O men, don’t you see dirty and foetal matter coming out of your bodies day by day. How can the receptacle of such matter be pure.?

## \|\| 9.23.7 \|\|

*dehassaṃśodhyamāno'pi paṃcagavyakuśāṃbubhiḥ \|*  
*ghṛṣyamāṇa ivāṃgāro nirmalatvaṃ na gacchati \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. Even if the body is sprinkled with the waters of the holy Kuśa grass and smeared with Pañcagavya, it will not become free from dirt like charcoal even when it is rubbed and polished.

## \|\| 9.23.8 \|\|

*srotāṃsi yasya satataṃ prabhavaṃti gireriva \|*  
*kaphamūtrapurīṣādyaissa dehaśśudhyate katham \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. Like the perennial flow of the springs of a mountain the vessels of the body discharge phlegm, urine, faeces and other types of filth. How can that body become clean?

## \|\| 9.23.9 \|\|

*sarvāśucinidhānasya śarīrasya na vidyate \|*  
*śucirekaḥ pradeśo'pi viṇmūtrasya dṛteriva \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. There is not even a single clean spot on the body—the receptacle of all types of filth and a leather bag of faeces and urine.

## \|\| 9.23.10 \|\|

*sṛṣṭvātmadehasrotāṃsi mṛttoyaiḥ śodhyate karaḥ \|*  
*tathāpyaśucibhāṃḍasya na vibhraśyati kiṃ karaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. After touching the tips of the vessels of one’s own body the hand is washed with sand and water. Still the persons who are slaves to this filthy body do not feel.

## \|\| 9.23.11 \|\|

*kāyassugaṃdhadhūpādyairya nnenāpi susaṃskṛtaḥ \|*  
*na jahāti svabhāvaṃ sa śvapucchamiva nāmitam \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Inspite of being smeared and sanctified assiduously by sweet scents, incense and other things the body does not eschew its nature like the curved tail of a dog.

## \|\| 9.23.12 \|\|

*yathā jātyaiva kṛṣṇorthaḥ śuklassyānna hyupāyataḥ \|*  
*saṃśoddhyamānāpi tathā bhavenmūrtirna nirmalā \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. Just as an object, naturally black, cannot become white by any means, so also the physical body does not become clean though it is scrubbed and cleaned.

## \|\| 9.23.13 \|\|

*jighrannapi svadurgaṃdhaṃ paśyannapi svakaṃ malam \|*  
*na virajyeta loko'yaṃ pīḍayannapi nāsikām \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. Though he smells his own noxious odour, though he sees his own dirt, no man becomes detached and keeps his hand on his nose.

## \|\| 9.23.14 \|\|

*aho mohasya māhātmyaṃ yenedaṃ chāditaṃ jagat \|*  
*śīghraṃ paśyansvakaṃ doṣaṃ kāyasya na virajyate \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. Wonderful indeed is this great delusion whereby the whole universe is enveloped. He does not observe the impurities of his body and become disgusted with it.

## \|\| 9.23.15 \|\|

*svadehasya vigaṃdhena na virajyeta yo naraḥ \|*  
*virāgakāraṇaṃ tasya kimetadupadiśyate \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. If a man is not detached from the world due to the foetal smell of his own what other cause of detachment can be imparted to him?

## \|\| 9.23.16 \|\|

*sarvasyaiva jaganmadhye deha evāśucirbhavet \|*  
*tanmalāvayavasparśācchucirapyaśucirbhavet \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. In the midst of the entire universe, the body alone is the most unclean. By touching its filthy limbs even the pure becomes impure.

## \|\| 9.23.17 \|\|

*gaṃdhalepāpanodārtha śaucaṃ dehasya kīrtitam \|*  
*dvayasyāpagamācchuddhiśśuddhasparśādviśudhyati \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. The purification of the body is for removing bad odour and sliminess. If both are removed there is purity. The touch of a pure thing also sanctifies it.

## \|\| 9.23.18 \|\|

*gaṃgātoyena sarveṇa mṛdbhāraiḥ parvatopamaiḥ \|*  
*āmṛtyorācarecchaucaṃ bhāvaduṣṭo na śudhyati \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. But let him be cleaned with all the waters of the Gaṅgā, with heaps of sand resembling a mountain or let him observe purificatory rites till death, if a man has wicked feelings and emotions he can never be purified.

## \|\| 9.23.19 \|\|

*tīrthasnānaistapobhirvā duṣṭātmā naiva śudhyati \|*  
*śvadṛtiḥ kṣālitā tīrthe kiṃ śuddhimadhigacchati \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. A vicious soul is not sanctified by the waters in the pilgrim spots nor by austerities. Does the canine hide become pure when washed in a holy river?

## \|\| 9.23.20 \|\|

*aṃtarbhāvapraduṣṭasya viśato'pi hutāśanam \|*  
*na svargo nāpavargaśca dehanirdahanaṃ param \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. Neither heaven nor salvation can be attained by a man of vicious emotion even if he immolates himself in the holy fire. The only result will be that his body is entirely burnt off.

## \|\| 9.23.21 \|\|

*sarveṇa gāṃgena jalena samyaṅ mṛtparvatenāpyatha bhāvaduṣṭaḥ \|*  
*ājanmanaḥ snānaparo manuṣyo na śudhyatītyeva vayaṃ vadāmaḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. We assert that a man of vicious instinct and feeling never becomes pure though he may indulge in ablutions throughout his life with all the waters of the Gaṅgā and a mountain of sand.

## \|\| 9.23.22 \|\|

*prajvālya vahniṃ ghṛtatailasiktaṃ pradakṣiṇāvartaśikhaṃ mahāṃtam \|*  
*praviśya dagdhastvapi bhāvaduṣṭo na dharmamāpnoti phalaṃ na cānyata \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. If a man of defiled emotion gets himself burnt after entering a huge blazing fire kindled by sprinkling ghee and oil and with flames of circular motion he does not become pure.

## \|\| 9.23.23 \|\|

*gaṃgāditīrtheṣu vasaṃti matsyā devālaye pakṣigaṇāśca nityam \|*  
*bhāvojjhitāste na phalaṃ labhaṃte tīrthāvagāhācca tathaiva dānāt \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. Fishes stay in the holy Gaṅgā and other sacred rivers. Flocks of birds stay in the temple. They do not attain any special benefit from the ceremonial ablutions and charitable gifts since they are devoid of holy feelings.

## \|\| 9.23.24 \|\|

*bhāvaśuddhiḥ paraṃ śaucaṃ pramāṇe sarvakarmasu \|*  
*anyathā''liṃgyate kāṃtā bhāvena duhitānyathā \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. It is the purity of the feelings that is the criterion for the sanctity of rites. The wife is embraced with one feeling and the daughter is embraced with another.

## \|\| 9.23.25 \|\|

*manaso bhidyate vṛttirabhinneṣvapi vastuṣu \|*  
*anyathaiva sutaṃ nārī cintayatyanyathā patim \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. Feelings are different in the mind even if the objects are not different. The same woman is looked at in one way by the son and in another way by the husband.

## \|\| 9.23.26 \|\|

*paśyadhvamasya bhāvasya mahābhāgyamaśeṣataḥ \|*  
*pariṣvaktopi yannāryyā bhāvahīnaṃ na kāmayet \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. O observe the great fortune in the difference of emotions. The man, though embraced, does not love the woman who is devoid of the feelings of love.

## \|\| 9.23.27 \|\|

*nādyādvividhamannādyaṃ bhakṣyāṇi surabhīṇi ca \|*  
*yadi ciṃtāṃ samādhatte citte kāmādiṣu triṣu \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. If the three emotions, passion etc. were to create worries in the mind no one would relish different delicious or fragrant stuffs.

## \|\| 9.23.28 \|\|

*gṛhyate tena bhāvena naro bhāvādvimucyate \|*  
*bhāvataśśuci śuddhātmā svargaṃ mokṣaṃ ca viṃdati \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. Man is caught hold of by emotions and man can rid himself of emotions. A man purified by pure emotions attains heaven and salvation.

## \|\| 9.23.29 \|\|

*bhāvenaikātmaśuddhātmā dahañjuhvanstuvanmṛtaḥ \|*  
*jñānāvāpteravāpyāśu lokānsubahuyājinām \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. Performing Japas, sacrifices and eulogies after purifying the soul with pure emotions a man acquires perfect knowledge and after death he attains the worlds of persons who perform many sacrifices.

## \|\| 9.23.30 \|\|

*jñānāmalāṃbhasā puṃsāṃ sadvairāgyamṛdā punaḥ \|*  
*avidyārāgaviṇmūtralepagaṃdhaviśodhanam \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. The purification and removal of the sliminess and bad odour of the faeces and urine of ignorance and passion of men is effected by the pure water of knowledge and the clay of detachment from the world.

## \|\| 9.23.31 \|\|

*evametaccharīraṃ hi nisargādaśuci smṛtam \|*  
*tvaṅmātrasāraṃ niḥsāraṃ kadalīsārasannibham \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. Thus as the body is naturally unclean it is worthless. Like the inner stump of the plantain tree it has only the outer skin as substantial.

## \|\| 9.23.32-33 \|\|

*jñātvaivaṃ doṣavaddehaṃ yaḥ prājñaśśithilo bhavet \|*  
*deha bhogodbhavādbhāvācchamacittaḥ prasannadhīḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

*so'tikrāmati saṃsāraṃ jīvanmuktaḥ prajāyate \|*  
*saṃsāraṃ kadalīsāradṛḍhagrāhyavatiṣṭhate \|\| 33 \|\|*

32-33. An intelligent man shall understand that the body is defective and faulty. He shall be disinterested in such emotions as revolve round the pleasures of the body. With the mind in restraint and intellect delighted he surmounts the worldly existence and becomes a living liberated soul. He who firmly grasps the inner stump of the plantain tree of worldly existence is not liberated.

## \|\| 9.23.34 \|\|

*evametanmahākaṣṭaṃ janma duḥkhaṃ prakīrtitam \|*  
*puṃsāmajñānadoṣeṇa nānākārmavaśena ca \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. Thus the misery of life is proclaimed to be grievous and painful to men due to the fault of ignorance and the influence of different activities.

## \|\| 9.23.35 \|\|

*ślokārdhena tu vakṣyāmi yaduktaṃ granthakoṭibhiḥ \|*  
*mameti paramaṃ duḥkhaṃ na mameti paraṃ sukham \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. I shall explain in half a stanza what is mentioned in crores of books. The feeling of “My-ness” is the greatest affliction. The feeling of “not-my-ness” is the greatest joy.

## \|\| 9.23.36 \|\|

*bahavopīha rājānaḥ paraṃ loka mito gatāḥ \|*  
*nirmamatvasametāstu baddhāśśatasahasraśaḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. Hundreds and thousands of monarchs who had been in bondage had attained the greatest world on reaching the Nirmamatva (not-my-ness) state.

## \|\| 9.23.37 \|\|

*garbhasthasya smṛtiryāsītsā ca tasya praṇaśyati \|*  
*saṃmūrchitena duḥkhena yoniyantranipīḍanāt \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. What little recollection the child had while within the womb perishes when his misery develops further on being crushed by the muscular walls of the vaginal passage as if within a machine.

## \|\| 9.23.38 \|\|

*bāhyena vāyunā vāsya mohasaṅgena dehinaḥ \|*  
*spṛṣṭamātreṇa ghoreṇa jvarassamupajāyate \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. A great fever overtakes him as soon as the external air touches him or delusion overwhelms him.

## \|\| 9.23.39 \|\|

*tena jvāreṇa mahatā sammohaśca prajāyate \|*  
*sammūḍhasya smṛtibhraṃśaśśīghraṃ saṃjāyate punaḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. Due to that fever a great fascination overpowers him and deludes him. After being deluded he soon suffers from the loss of memory.

## \|\| 9.23.40 \|\|

*smṛtibhraṃśāttatastasya smṛtirnno'pūrvakarmaṇaḥ \|*  
*ratiḥ saṃjāyate tūrṇaṃ jantostatraiva janmani \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. Loss of memory leads to forgetfulness of all previous activities. The creature soon begins to love the present birth.

## \|\| 9.23.41 \|\|

*rakto mūḍhaśca loko'yaṃ na kāryye sampravartate \|*  
*na cātmānaṃ vijānāti na paraṃ na ca daivatam \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. The passionate and deluded world does not engage in activities beneficial to itself. It does not realise itself nor others nor even the deity.

## \|\| 9.23.42 \|\|

*na śṛṇoti paraṃ śreyassati karṇe'pi sanmune \|*  
*na paśyati paraṃ śreyassati cakṣuṣi tatkṣame \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. O good sage, though he has ears man does not listen to what is beneficial to him. Even when he has competent eyes he does not see what is good.

## \|\| 9.23.43 \|\|

*same pathi śanairgacchan skhalatīva padepade \|*  
*satyāṃ buddhau na jānāti bodhyamāno budhairapi \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. Walking slowly on the level, even path he falters at every step. Even when he has intellect, even when he is enlightened by scholars he does not understand.

## \|\| 9.23.44 \|\|

*saṃsāre kliśyate tena garbhalobhavaśānugaḥ \|*  
*garbhasmṛtena pāpena samujjhitamatiḥ pumān \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. Thereby, urged and prompted by great greed he suffers pain and distress in the world. His intellect no longer remembers the sins he committed which he remembered in the womb.

## \|\| 9.23.45 \|\|

*itthaṃ mahatparaṃ divyaṃ śāstramuktaṃ śivena tu \|*  
*tapasaḥ kathanārthāya svargamokṣaprasādhanam \|\| 45 \|\|*
