45\. This great divine Śāstra has been narrated by Śiva in the context of penance as a means to achieving heaven and salvation.

## \|\| 9.23.46 \|\|

*ye satyasmicchive jñāne sarvakāmārtha sādhane \|*  
*na kurvantyātmanaḥ śreyastadatra mahadadbhutam \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. Even when the perfect knowledge of Śiva that leads to the achievement of desire is available, it is really a great surprise if people do not strive for its attainment in their own interest.

## \|\| 9.23.47 \|\|

*avyaktendriyavṛttitvādbālye duḥkhaṃ mahatpunaḥ \|*  
*icchannapi na śaknoti vaktuṃ karttuṃ pratikriyām \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. There is again this great misery in infancy (bālya) because the operative power of the sense-organs is not clearly manifest or developed. Even if he wishes he is unable to express or take remedial or preventive measures.

## \|\| 9.23.48 \|\|

*daṃtotthāne mahadduḥkhamalpena vyādhinā tathā \|\| \|*  
*bālarogaiśca vividhai pīḍā bālagrahairapi \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. There is a great trouble when the child cuts the teeth. There is pain due to minor childhood ailments, different kinds of children’s diseases and adverse evil planets and other evils.

## \|\| 9.23.49 \|\|

*kvacitkṣuttṛṭparītāṃgaḥ kvacittiṣṭhati saṃraṭan \|*  
*viṇmūtrabhakṣaṇādyaṃ ca mohādbālassamācaret \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. The infant boy may remain somewhere very hungry and thirsty, or lying somewhere he may be crying or he may be eating or drinking faeces and the urine due to delusion.

## \|\| 9.23.50-51 \|\|

*kaumāre karṇapīḍāyāṃ mātāpitrośca sādhanaḥ \|*  
*akṣarādhyayanādyaiśca nānāduḥkhaṃ pravartate \|\| 50 \|\|*

*bālye duḥkhamatītyaiva paśyannapi vimūḍhadhīḥ \|*  
*na kurvītātmanaḥ śreyastadatra mahadadbhutam \|\| 51 \|\|*

50-51. During childhood when the rite of earboring is performed or when the parents beat him or when he feels difficulty in learning the alphabet it is surprising that the stupid fellow does not strive for his welfare, even after seeing and somehow surmounting the miseries of boyhood.

## \|\| 9.23.52 \|\|

*pravṛttendriyavṛttitvātkāmarogaprapīḍanāt \|*  
*tadaprāpte tu satataṃ kutassaukhyaṃ tu yauvane \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. Where is happiness during youth, when all the sense-organs are developed fully and begin to function, and there is an attack from the sickness of lust that remains unrealised ever?

## \|\| 9.23.53 \|\|

*īrṣyayā ca mahadduḥkhaṃ mohādraktasya tasya ca \|*  
*netrasya kupitasyeva tyāgī duḥkhāya kevalam \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. Then, he feels misery from malice and rivalry. By delusion his eyes become red like those of an infuriated man. Its abandonment is only conducive to misery.

## \|\| 9.23.54 \|\|

*na rātrau viṃdate nidrāṃ kāmāgnipariveditaḥ \|*  
*divāpi ca kutassaukhyamarthopārjanaciṃtayā \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. During nights he does not get proper sleep because he is scorched by the fire of lust. Where is pleasure even during the day because he is worried over the ways and means of earning wealth?

## \|\| 9.23.55 \|\|

*strīṣvadhyāsitacittasya ye puṃsaḥ śukrabindavaḥ \|*  
*te sukhāya na manyante svedajā iva te tathā \|\| 55 \|\|*

55\. The drops of semen of a man whose mind is dwelling on women do not bring him pleasure. They are like his own sweat drops, mere dirt.

## \|\| 9.23.56 \|\|

*kṛmibhistudyamānasya kuṣṭhino vānarasya ca \|*  
*kaṃḍūyanābhitāpena yadbhavetstriṣu tadvidaḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

56\. What happen? in women is exactly what happens to a leprous monkey when it scratches itself on being afflicted by worms.

## \|\| 9.23.57 \|\|

*yādṛśaṃ manyate saukhyaṃ gaṃḍe pūtivinirgamāt \|*  
*tādṛśaṃ strīṣu mantavyaṃ nādhikaṃ tāsu vidyate \|\| 57 \|\|*

57\. The pleasure in women is similar to the pleasure that is felt when the pus comes out of a swollen tumour. It is nothing more than that.

## \|\| 9.23.58 \|\|

*viṇmūtrasya samutsargātsukhaṃ bhavati yādṛśam \|*  
*tādṛśaṃ strīṣu vijñeyaṃ mūḍhaiḥ kalpitamanyathā \|\| 58 \|\|*

58\. The pleasure in women is similar to the pleasure one feels when faeces and urine are discharged. But it has been considered otherwise by the stupid.

## \|\| 9.23.59 \|\|

*nārīṣvavastubhūtāsu sarvadoṣāśrayāsu vā \|*  
*nāṇumātraṃ sukhaṃ tāsu kathitaṃ paṃcacūḍayā \|\| 59 \|\|*

59\. It has been mentioned by Pañcacūḍā that there is not even a particle of pleasure in women who are the storehouses of all blemishes and who are creatures unsubstantial.

## \|\| 9.23.60 \|\|

*sammānanāvamānābhyāṃ viyogeneṣṭasaṃgamāt \|*  
*yauvanaṃ jarayā grastaṃ kva saukhyamanupadravam \|\| 60 \|\|*

60\. Due to honour and disrespect, due to union with and separation from the beloved people youth is soon devoured by old age. Where is pleasure without torment?

## \|\| 9.23.61-62 \|\|

*valīpalitakhālityaiśśithilikṛtavigraham \|*  
*sarvakriyāsvaśaktiṃ ca jarayā jarjarīkṛtam \|\| 61 \|\|*

*strīpuṃsayauvanaṃ hṛdyamanyo'nyasya priyaṃ purā \|*  
*tadeva jarayāgrastamanayorapi na priyam \|\| 62 \|\|*

61-62. The youth of women and men which had been mutually pleasurable formerly is not so now when it is overwhelmed by old age, when the body is rendered shattered and rickety by wrinkles, grey hairs and baldness and when the man is incompetent to perform any serious task.

## \|\| 9.23.63 \|\|

*apūrvavatsvamātmānaṃ jarayā parivartitam \|*  
*yaḥ paśyannapi rajyeta ko'nyastasmādacetanaḥ \|\| 63 \|\|*

63\. Who can be a more senseless person than the man who feels attracted to worldly pleasures even after seeing himself completely transformed due to old age as if he were a new person not existing before?

## \|\| 9.23.64 \|\|

*jarābhibhūtaḥ puruṣaḥ putrīputrādibāṃdhavaiḥ \|*  
*āsaktatvāddurādharṣairbhṛtyaiśca paribhūyate \|\| 64 \|\|*

64\. A man overwhelmed by old age is tormented by daughters, sons and other kinsmen and even by servants who cannot be kept under control because they are not attached to him.

## \|\| 9.23.65 \|\|

*dharmamarthaṃ ca kāmaṃ vā mokṣaṃ vātijarāturaḥ \|*  
*aśaktassādhituṃ tasmādyuvā dharmaṃ samācaret \|\| 65 \|\|*

65\. A sick and an aged man is incompetent to strive for virtue, wealth, love and salvation. Hence a young man shall strive for these virtues.

## Chapter 24 - Nature of women (strī)

## \|\| 9.24.1 \|\|

*vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*kutsitaṃ yoṣidarthaṃ yatsaṃproktaṃ paṃcacūḍayā \|*  
*tanme brūhi samāsena yadi tuṣṭo'si me mune \|\| 1 \|\|*

Vyāsa said:—

1\. O sage, if you are pleased with me you shall narrate succinctly what was mentioned by Pañcacūḍā viz., that women are despicable.

## \|\| 9.24.2 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*strīṇāṃ svabhāvaṃ vakṣyāmi śṛṇu vipra yathātatham \|*  
*yasya śravaṇamātreṇa bhavedvairāgyamuttamam \|\| 2 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

2\. I shall explain the nature of women (strī) as it is. O dear, listen to it, merely by hearing which excellent detachment results.

## \|\| 9.24.3 \|\|

*striyo mūlaṃ hi doṣāṇāṃ laghucittāḥ sadā mune \|*  
*tadāsaktirna kartavyā mokṣepsubhiratandritaiḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. O sage, women are light-minded. They are at the root of all troubles. Attachment towards them should not be pursued by wakeful persons who desire liberation.

## \|\| 9.24.4 \|\|

*atrāpyudāharaṃtīmamitihāsaṃ purātanam \|*  
*nāradasya ca saṃvādaṃ puṃścalyā paṃcacūḍayā \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. In this respect they quote an ancient tradition, the conversation of Nārada with the unchaste woman Pañcacūḍā.

## \|\| 9.24.5 \|\|

*lokānparicarandhīmāndevarṣirnāradaḥ purā \|*  
*dadarśāpsarasaṃ bālāṃ paṃcacūḍāmanuttamām \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Formerly, while the intelligent celestial sage Nārada was wandering in the worlds he saw the beautiful celestial damsel Pañcacūḍā.

## \|\| 9.24.6 \|\|

*papracchāpsarasaṃ subhrūṃ nārado munisattamaḥ \|*  
*saṃśayo hṛdi me kaścittanme brūhi sumadhyame \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. The excellent sage Nārada asked the beautiful woman, the Apsaras—“O lady of beautiful waist, I have a certain doubt. Please explain it to me.”

## \|\| 9.24.7 \|\|

*evamuktā tu sā vipraṃ pratyuvāca varāpsarā \|*  
*viṣaye sati vakṣyāmi samarthāṃ manyase'tha mām \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. O brahmin, thus addressed the excellent celestial damsel replied—“If you consider me competent and if there is a proper subject, I shall explain.”

## \|\| 9.24.8 \|\|

*nārada uvāca \|*  
*na tvāmaviṣaye bhadre niyokṣyāmi kathaṃcana \|*  
*strīṇāṃ svabhāvamicchāmi tvattaḥ śrotuṃ sumadhyame \|\| 8 \|\|*

Nārada said:—

8\. O gentle lady, I shall never engage you in a subject beyond your scope. O lady of slender waist, I wish to hear from you the nature of women.

## \|\| 9.24.9 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*etacchrutvā vacastasya devarṣerapsarottamā \|*  
*pratyuvāca munīśaṃ taṃ devarṣiṃ munisattamam \|\| 9 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

9\. On hearing these words of that celestial sage the excellent Apsaras replied to that lord of sages, Nārada the excellent sage.

## \|\| 9.24.10 \|\|

*paṃcacūḍovāca \|*  
*mune śṛṇu na śakyā strī satī vai niṃdituṃ striyā \|*  
*viditāste striyo yāśca yādṛśyaśca svabhāvataḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

Pañcacūdā said:—

10\. “O sage listen, being a woman I cannot censure women. You already know what women are and what their nature is.

## \|\| 9.24.11 \|\|

*na māmarhasi devarṣe niyoktuṃ praśnamīdṛśam \|*  
*ityuktvā sā'bhavattūṣṇīṃ paṃcacūḍāpsarovarā \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. O celestial sage, it does not behove you to urge me in such a question.” After saying this the excellent Apsaras Pañcacūḍā kept quiet.

## \|\| 9.24.12 \|\|

*atha devarṣivaryo hi śrutvā tadvākyamuttamam \|*  
*pratyuvāca punastāṃ vai lokānāṃ hitakāmyayā \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. On hearing her excellent statement the most excellent of the celestial sages replied to her with a desire for the benefit of the worlds.

## \|\| 9.24.13 \|\|

*nārada uvāca \|*  
*mṛṣāvāde bhaveddoṣassatye doṣo na vidyate \|*  
*iti jānīhi satyaṃ tvaṃ vadātastatsumadhyame \|\| 13 \|\|*

Nārada said:—

13\. It may be wrong to make a false statement. There is no defect in speaking the truth. Know this, O lady of good waist and speak the truth.

## \|\| 9.24.14 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*ityuktā sā kṛtamatī rabhasā cāruhāsinī \|*  
*strīdoṣāñśāśvatānsatyānbhāṣituṃ saṃpracakrame \|\| 14 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

14\. Thus prompted, the sweet-smiling lady, resolved and immediately began to explain truthfully the permanent defects of women.

## \|\| 9.24.15 \|\|

*pañcacūḍovāca \|*  
*kulīnā nāthavaṃtyaśca rūpavaṃtyaśca yoṣitaḥ \|*  
*maryādāsu na tiṣṭhaṃti sa doṣaḥ strīṣu nārada \|\| 15 \|\|*

Pañcacūḍā said:—

15\. O Nārada, this is the defect in women. Even women of noble families, women with husbands and women endowed with beauty do not stand within the limits of decency.

## \|\| 9.24.16 \|\|

*na strībhyaḥ kiṃcidanyadvai pāpīyastaramasti hi \|*  
*striyo mūlaṃ hi pāpānāṃ tathā tvamapi vettha ha \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. There is none more sinning and more sinful than women. Women are at the root of all sins. This you know already.

## \|\| 9.24.17 \|\|

*samājñātānarthavataḥ pratirūpān yathepsitān \|*  
*yatīnantaramāsādya nālaṃ nāryyaḥ pratīkṣitum \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. They might have husbands of good knowledge, of ample wealth, of great comeliness and pleasing to them. But when they get opportunities for erring, they do not wait.

## \|\| 9.24.18 \|\|

*asaddharmastvayaṃ strīṇāmasmākaṃ bhavati prabho \|*  
*pāpīyaso narān yadvai lajjāṃ tyaktvā bhajāmahe \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. O holy lord, this is the evil practice of all of us, women, that we resort to sinful men casting off all shame and shyness.

## \|\| 9.24.19 \|\|

*striyaṃ ca yaḥ prārthayate sannikarṣaṃ ca gacchati \|*  
*īṣacca kurute sevāṃ tamevecchati yoṣitaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. Women love only those persons who solicit their company, who approach them intimately and who render them a little bit of service.

## \|\| 9.24.20 \|\|

*anarthitvānmanuṣyāṇāṃ bhayātpatijanasya ca \|*  
*maryādāyāmamaryādāḥ striyastiṣṭhaṃti bhartṛṣu \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. Women usually do not observe the limitations of conventional decency. If at all they stand by them with their husbands it is because no man makes advances to them or because they are afraid of their husbands.

## \|\| 9.24.21 \|\|

*nāsāṃ kaścidamānyo'sti nāsāṃ vayasi niścayaḥ \|*  
*surūpaṃ vā kurūpaṃ vā pumāṃsamupabhuṃjate \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. There is no man not worthy of honour to women. They are not mindful of the age of the man. They carry on their dalliances with any man ugly or beautiful.

## \|\| 9.24.22 \|\|

*na bhayādatha vākrośānnārthahetoḥ kathaṃcana \|*  
*na jñātikulasambandhāstriyastiṣṭhaṃti bhartṛṣu \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. It is not due to fear, taunts or affection for their husbands or regard for their lineage that women remain loyal to their husbands.

## \|\| 9.24.23 \|\|

*yauvane vartamānānāmiṣṭābharaṇavāsasām \|*  
*nārīṇāṃ svairavṛttīnāṃ spṛhayanti kulastriyaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. Even women of noble families aspire for the life of lascivious women who in their prime of youth adorned with lovable ornaments and beautiful wearing garments move about frivolously.

## \|\| 9.24.24 \|\|

*yā hi śaśvadbahumatā rakṣyante dayitāḥ striyaḥ \|*  
*api tāḥ samprasajjante kubjāndhajaḍavāmane \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. Even the women who are honoured well, loved intimately and looked after with care become attached to hunchbacks, blind men, imbeciles and dwarfs.

## \|\| 9.24.25 \|\|

*paṃguṣvapi ca devarṣe ye cānye kutsitā narāḥ \|*  
*strīṇāmagamyo lokeṣu nāsti kaścinmahāmune \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. O celestial sage, they become attached to lame and even despicable persons. O great sage, there is none in the world who cannot be approached by women with solicitations of lust.

## \|\| 9.24.26 \|\|

*yadi puṃsāṃ gatirbrahmankathaṃcinnopapadyate \|*  
*apyanyonyaṃ pravartante na ca tiṣṭhanti bhartṛṣu \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. O brahmin, if women do not get men for their dalliance they begin to indulge in abnormal sexual activity with one another. They do not stand by their husbands.

## \|\| 9.24.27 \|\|

*alābhātpuruṣāṇāṃ ca bhayātparijanasya ca \|*  
*vadhabandhabhayāccaiva tā bhagnāśā hi yoṣitaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. Women become desperate when they do not get men, when they are afraid of servants, when they are frightened of being killed or imprisoned.

## \|\| 9.24.28 \|\|

*calasvabhāva duśceṣṭā durgāhyā bhavatastathā \|*  
*prājñasya puruṣasyeha yathā ratiparigrahāt \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. Because they indulge in sexual intercourse as they please they are fickle-minded, of evil deeds and emotionally incomprehensible even to an intelligent man.

## \|\| 9.24.29 \|\|

*nāgnistuṣyati kāṣṭhānāṃ nāpagānāṃ mahodadhi \|*  
*nāntakassarvabhūtānāṃ na puṃsāṃ vāmalocanāḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. Fire is not satiated with the logs of wood it consumes; the ocean is not satiated with the rivers that flow into it. The god of death is not satiated with the living beings he kills and women are not satiated with the number of men they cohabit with.

## \|\| 9.24.30 \|\|

*idamanyacca devarṣe rahasyaṃ sarvayoṣitām \|*  
*dṛṣṭvaiva puruṣaṃ sadyo yoniḥ praklidyate striyāḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. O excellent sage, there is another secret of all women that immediately on seeing a man their vaginal passage begins to exude slimy secretions.

## \|\| 9.24.31 \|\|

*susnātaṃ puruṣaṃ dṛṣṭvā sugandhaṃ malavarjitam \|*  
*yoniḥ praklidyate strīṇāṃ dṛteḥ pātrādivodakam \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. On seeing a man fresh and clean from his bath with his body perfumed with sweet scents, the vaginal passage of women begins to exude like water dripping from a leather bag.

## \|\| 9.24.32 \|\|

*kāyānāmapi dātāraṃ karttāraṃ mānasāṃtvayoḥ \|*  
*rakṣitāraṃ na mṛṣyaṃti bhartāraṃ paramaṃ striyaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. Women do not brook their husbands who may give all that they love, who may honour and console them and who may look after them well.

## \|\| 9.24.33 \|\|

*na kāmabhogātparamānnālaṃkārārthasaṃcayāt \|*  
*tathā hitaṃ na manyante yathā ratiparigrahāt \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. Women do not remain satisfied and contented so much with simple loves and pleasures and with ornaments and money as with illicit love pursued by them with other men.

## \|\| 9.24.34 \|\|

*antakaśśamano mṛtyuḥ pātālaṃ vaḍavāmukham \|*  
*kṣuradhārā viṣaṃ sarpo vahnirityekataḥ striyaḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. Women can be kept equally balanced against all these put together viz., god of death, Yama, Antaka, Pātāla, the submarine fire, the sharp edge of razor, poison, serpent and fire.

## \|\| 9.24.35 \|\|

*yataśca bhūtāni mahāṃti paṃca yataśca loko vihito vidhātrā \|*  
*yataḥ pumāṃsaḥ pramadāśca nirmitāḥ sadaiva doṣaḥ pramadāsu nārada \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. Ever since the five elements, the world, the men and women were created by Brahmā, O Nārada, the defect lies in women always.

## \|\| 9.24.36 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*iti śrutvā vacastasyā nāradastuṣṭamānasaḥ \|*  
*tathyaṃ matvā tatastadvai viraktobhūddhi tāsu ca \|\| 36 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

36\. On hearing her words Nārada was satisfied in his mind. Considering it to be the truth he became disinterested in them.

## \|\| 9.24.37 \|\|

*ityuktaḥ strī svabhāvaste paṃcacūḍokta ādarāt \|*  
*vairāgyakāraṇaṃ vyāsa kimanyacchrotumarhasi \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. O Vyāsa, thus the nature of women as mentioned by Pañcacūḍā has been narrated to you. What other cause of detachment do you wish to hear?

## Chapter 25 - Ascertainment of the time of death

## \|\| 9.25.1 \|\|

*vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*sanatkumāra sarvajña tvatsakāśānmayā mune \|*  
*strīsvabhāvaḥ śrutaḥ prītyā kālajñānaṃ vadasva me \|\| 1 \|\|*

Vyāsa said:—

1\. O omniscient Sanatkumāra, O sage, the nature of women has been heard by me from you. Please narrate the knowledge of Kāla with pleasure.

## \|\| 9.25.2 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*idameva purā'pṛcchatpārvatī parameśvaram \|*  
*śrutvā nānākathāṃ divyāṃ prasannā supraṇamya tam \|\| 2 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

2\. Formerly, this very same thing Pārvatī asked lord Śiva after hearing various divine stories and delightedly bowing to him.

## \|\| 9.25.3 \|\|

*pārvatyuvāca \|*  
*bhagavaṃstvatprasādena jñātaṃ me sakalaṃ matam \|*  
*yathārcanaṃ tu te deva yairmaṃtraiśca yathāvidhi \|\| 3 \|\|*

Pārvati said:—

3\. O lord, thanks to your grace, everything has been understood by me and pondered over, such as your due worship, O lord, and the mantra befitting the same.

## \|\| 9.25.4 \|\|

*adyāpi saṃśayastvekaḥ kālacakraṃ prati prabho \|*  
*mṛtyucihnaṃ yathā deva kiṃ pramāṇaṃ yathāyuṣaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. But still, O lord, a doubt regarding the wheel of time lingers in my mind. What are the signs of death? What is the extent of life?

## \|\| 9.25.5 \|\|

*tathā kathaya me nātha yadyahaṃ tava vallabhā \|*  
*iti pṛṣṭastayā devyā pratyuvāca maheśvaraḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. O lord, please tell me everything if I am your beloved. On being asked thus by the goddess, lord Śiva replied.

## \|\| 9.25.6 \|\|

*\|\| īśvara uvāca \|*  
*satyaṃ te kathayiṣyāmi śāstraṃ sarvottamaṃ priye \|*  
*yena śāstreṇa deveśi naraiḥ kālaḥ prabudhyate \|\| 6 \|\|*

Lord Śiva said:—

6\. O beloved, I shall tell you the truth, the Śāstra that is the most excellent of all, by which, O goddess, men can understand their time of death.

## \|\| 9.25.7 \|\|

*ahaḥ pakṣaṃ tathā māsamṛtuṃ cāyanavatsarau \|*  
*sthūlasūkṣmagataiścihnairbahiraṃtargataistathā \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. The day, the fortnight, the month, the season, the transit of the sun and the year can be known by means of the subtle or gross, external or internal signs.

## \|\| 9.25.8 \|\|

*tattehaṃ sampravakṣyāmi śṛṇu tattvena sundari \|*  
*lokānāmupakārārthaṃ vairāgyārthamume'dhunā \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. O Umā, O beautiful lady, I shall tell that to you factually now, for the welfare of the people for bringing about their detachment from the world.

## \|\| 9.25.9 \|\|

*akasmātpāṃḍuraṃ dehamūrddhvarāgaṃ samaṃtataḥ \|*  
*tadā mṛtyuṃ vijānīyātṣaṇmāsābhyantare priye \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. O beloved, if the body becomes all of a sudden pale and white with red patches all round and above, know that death will place within six months.

## \|\| 9.25.10 \|\|

*mukhaṃ karṇau tathā cakṣurjihvāstambho yadā bhavet \|*  
*tadā mṛtyuṃ vijānīyātṣaṇmāsābhyantare priye \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. When there is paralysis in the face, ears, eyes or in the tongue, O beloved, know that death will take place within six months.

## \|\| 9.25.11 \|\|

*rauravānugataṃ bhadra dhvaniṃ nākarṇayeddrutam \|*  
*ṣaṇmāsābhyaṃtare mṛtyurjñātavyaḥ kālavedibhiḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. O gentle lady, if a man does not hear the sound accompanying a shrill cry, immediately, death within six months shall be declared by those who understand Kāla.

## \|\| 9.25.12 \|\|

*ravisomāgnisaṃyogādyadodyotaṃ na paśyati \|*  
*kṛṣṇaṃ sarvaṃ samastaṃ ca ṣaṇmāsaṃ jīvitaṃ tathā \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. When in contact with the light of the sun, moon or fire if a man does not see the brilliant lustre, if he sees everything black he will remain alive only for six months more.

## \|\| 9.25.13 \|\|

*vāmahasto yadā devi saptāhaṃ spaṃdate priye \|*  
*jīvitaṃ tu tadā tasya māsamekaṃ na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. O beloved, O goddess, if the left hand throbs for seven days, there is no doubt that he will live only for a month more.

## \|\| 9.25.14 \|\|

*unmīlayati gātrāṇi tālukaṃ śuṣyate yadā \|*  
*jīvitaṃ tu tadā tasya māsamekaṃ na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. If the limbs unfold and expand, if the palate dries up, there is no doubt that he will live only for a month more.

## \|\| 9.25.15 \|\|

*nāsā tu sravate yasya tridoṣe pakṣajīvitam \|*  
*vaktraṃ kaṃṭhaṃ ca śuṣyeta ṣaṇmāsāṃte gatāyuṣaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. When the three humours are disturbed and the nose begins to water, his life is only for a fortnight more. The mouth and the throat of a man sure to die at the end of six months, become parched.

## \|\| 9.25.16 \|\|

*sthūlajihvā bhavedyasya dvijāḥ klidyaṃti bhāmini \|*  
*ṣaṇmāsājjāyate mṛtyuścihnaistairupalakṣayet \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. O beautiful lady, if the tongue becomes thick and the teeth begin to exude, death takes place within six months. The same may be inferred through these signs.

## \|\| 9.25.17-18 \|\|

*aṃbutailaghṛtasthaṃ tu darpaṇe varavarṇini \|*  
*na paśyati yadātmānaṃ vikṛtaṃ palameva ca \|\| 17 \|\|*

*ṣaṇmāsāyussa vijñeyaḥ kālacakraṃ vijānatā \|*  
*anyacca śṛṇu deveśi yena mṛtyurvibuddhyate \|\| 18 \|\|*

17-18. If a man does not see himself reflected in water, oil, ghee or in the mirror or if he sees with distorted flesh or features he will live only for six months more. This shall be declared by the expert. O goddess, now listen to other signs whereby death can be inferred.

## \|\| 9.25.19 \|\|

*śirohīnāṃ yadā chāyāṃ svakīyāmupalakṣayet \|*  
*athavā chāyayā hīnaṃ māsamekaṃ na jīvati \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. If a man sees his shadow without the head or if he does not see the shadow at all he will not live for one full month thereafter.

## \|\| 9.25.20 \|\|

*āṃgikāni mayoktāni mṛtyucihnāni pārvati \|*  
*bāhyasthāni bruve bhadre cihnāni śṛṇu sāṃpratam \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. O Pārvatī, the signs of death evinced by bodily limbs have been explained. O gentle lady, I shall now mention the extraneous signs. Listen.

## \|\| 9.25.21 \|\|

*raśmihīnaṃ yadā devi bhavetsomārkamaṇḍalam \|*  
*dṛśyate pāṭalākāraṃ māsārde्dhena vipadyate \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. O goddess, if the zone of the sun or the moon appears devoid of rays or like the trumpet flower, the man dies within half a month.

## \|\| 9.25.22 \|\|

*aruṃdhatī mahāyānamiṃdulakṣaṇavarjitam \|*  
*adṛṣṭatārako yo'sau māsamekaṃ sa jīvati \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. If a person does not see the star Arundhatī or the moon with the black spot, or if any one does not see any star he lives only for a month.Wom

## \|\| 9.25.23 \|\|

*dṛṣṭe grahe ca diṅmohaḥ ṣaṇmāsājjāyate dhruvam \|*  
*utathyaṃ na dhruvaṃ paśyedyadi vā ravimaṇḍalam \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. If a person does not see the star Utathya or the Polar star or the solar zone or has delusion of quarters on seeing the planet he dies within six months.

## \|\| 9.25.24 \|\|

*rātrau dhanuryadāpaśyenmadhyāhne colkapātanam \|*  
*veṣṭyate gṛdhrakākaiśca ṣaṇmāsāyurna saṃśayaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. If a man sees rainbow at night or the fall of the meteor at midday or if he is surrounded by vultures and crows he lives only for six months more.

## \|\| 9.25.25 \|\|

*ṛṣayassvargapaṃthāśca dṛśyaṃte naiva cāmbare \|*  
*ṣaṇmāsāyurvijanīyātpuruṣaiḥ kālavedibhiḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. If the great Bear and the Milky way are not seen in the sky, know him to live for only six months more. This shall be declared by the experts.

## \|\| 9.25.26 \|\|

*akasmādrāhuṇā grastaṃ sūryaṃ vā somameva ca \|*  
*dikcakraṃ bhrāṃtavatpaśyetṣaṇmāsānmriyate sphuṭam \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. If any person sees the sun or the moon suddenly seized by Rāhu, or the wheel of quarters whirling, he dies within six months definitely.

## \|\| 9.25.27 \|\|

*nīlābhirmakṣikābhiśca hyakasmādveṣṭyate pumān \|*  
*māsamekaṃ hi tasyāyurjñātavyaṃ paramārthataḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. If a person is suddenly surrounded by blue bees, he will live only a month more. That is true.

## \|\| 9.25.28 \|\|

*gṛdhraḥ kākaḥ kapotaśca śiraścākramya tiṣṭhati \|*  
*śīghraṃ tu mriyate jaṃturmāsaikena na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. Surely the person dies soon within a month if a crow or a dove attacks him and settles on his head.

## \|\| 9.25.29 \|\|

*evaṃ cāriṣṭabhedastu bāhyasthaḥ samudāhṛtaḥ \|*  
*mānuṣāṇāṃ hitārthāya saṃkṣepeṇa vadāmyaham \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. Thus the evil portents of an external nature have been explained to you. For the benefit of human beings I shall mention succinctly.

## \|\| 9.25.30 \|\|

*hastayorubhayordevi yathā kālaṃ vijānate \|*  
*vāmadakṣiṇayormadhye pratyakṣaṃ cetyudāhṛtam \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. O goddess, one can see Kāla within his two hands, the left and the right. It has been directly narrated.

## \|\| 9.25.31 \|\|

*evaṃ pakṣau sthitau dvau tu samāsātsurasuṃdari \|*  
*śucirbhūtvā smarandevaṃ susnātassaṃyatendriyaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. O beautiful goddess, in brief there are two fortnights. In these one shall take bath and be clear, remember the lord with controlled senses.

## \|\| 9.25.32 \|\|

*hastau prakṣālya dugdhenālaktakena vimardayet \|*  
*gaṃdhaiḥ puṣpaiḥ karau kṛtvā mṛgayecca śubhāśubham \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. He shall wash his hands with red lac juice and milk. He shall hold scents and fragrant flowers in the hands and try to forestall his good or bad luck.

## \|\| 9.25.33-34 \|\|

*kaniṣṭhāmāditaḥ kṛtvā yāvadaṃguṣṭhakaṃ priye \|*  
*parvatrayakrameṇaiva hastayorubhayorapi \|\| 33 \|\|*

*pratipadādivinyasya tithiṃ pratipadāditaḥ \|*  
*saṃpuṭākārahastau tu pūrvadiṅmukhasaṃsthitaḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

33-34. O beloved, beginning with the little finger and ending with the thumb, one should make the Nyāsa (mystical placing) of each of the Tithis on each knot. Thus the two hands together will cover the mouth Keep the hands in the cuplike form and sit facing the east.

## \|\| 9.25.35 \|\|

*smarennavātmakaṃ maṃtraṃ yāvadaṣṭottaraṃ śatam \|*  
*nirīkṣayettato hastau pratiparvaṇi yatnataḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. The devotee then should repeat the Navātmaka mantra one hundred and eight times. Then he shall scrutinise every one of the knots in the fingers of both the hands.

## \|\| 9.25.36 \|\|

*tasminparvaṇi sā rekhā dṛśyate bhṛṃgasannibhā \|*  
*tattithau hi mṛtirjñeyā kṛṣṇe śukle tathā priye \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. O beloved, if the devotee sees a line in the form of a bee in a particular knot he shall understand that the death will take place on the Tithi represented by that knot.

## \|\| 9.25.37 \|\|

*adhunā nādajaṃ vakṣye saṃkṣepātkālalakṣaṇam \|*  
*gamāgamaṃ viditvā tu karma kuryāñchṛṇu priye \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. Now I shall succinctly mention the time of death. O beloved, listen. On the basis of hearing the sound he shall know the result.

## \|\| 9.25.38-39 \|\|

*ātmavijñānaṃ suśroṇi cāraṃ jñātvā tu yatnataḥ \|*  
*kṣaṇaṃ truṭirlavaṃ caiva nimeṣaṃ kāṣṭhakālikam \|\| 38 \|\|*

*muhūrtakaṃ tvahorātraṃ pakṣamāsartuvatsaram \|*  
*abdaṃ yugaṃ tathā kalpaṃ mahākalpaṃ tathaiva ca \|\| 39 \|\|*

38-39. O woman of slender waist, the calculation of time is based on the division of it into Kṣaṇa, Truṭi, Lava, Nimeṣa, Kāṣṭhā, Muhūrta, day, night, fortnight, month, season, year, Yuga, Kalpa and Mahākalpa.

## \|\| 9.25.40 \|\|

*evaṃ sa harate kālaḥ paripāṭyā sadāśivaḥ \|*  
*vāmadakṣiṇamadhye tu pathi trayamidaṃ smṛtam \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. Śiva calculates time by three paths: Left, right and middle.

## \|\| 9.25.41 \|\|

*dināni paṃca cārabhya paṃcaviṃśaddināvadhi \|*  
*vāmācāragatau nādaḥ pramāṇaṃ kathitaṃ tava \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. Beginning with the fifth and ending with the twenty-fifth are the calculations of sound according to the left-hand practices.

## \|\| 9.25.42 \|\|

*bhūtaraṃdhradiśaścaiva dhvajaśca varavarṇini \|*  
*vāmacāragatau nādaḥ pramāṇaṃ kālavedinaḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. O fair lady, Bhūtarandhra (?) the quarters and the banner are the sounds in the left hand practices.

## \|\| 9.25.43 \|\|

*ṛtorvikārabhūtāśca guṇāstatraiva bhāmini \|*  
*pramāṇaṃ dakṣiṇaṃ proktaṃ jñātavyaṃ prāṇavedibhiḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. O fair lady, the attributes and the aberrations of the seasons constitute that in the right-hand practices. This shall be known by those who understand the motion of vital airs.

## \|\| 9.25.44-47 \|\|

*bhūtasaṃkhyā yadā prāṇānvahaṃte ca iḍādayaḥ \|*  
*varṣasyābhyaṃtare tasya jīvitaṃ hi na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

*daśaghasrapravāheṇa hyabdamānaṃ sa jīvati \|*  
*paṃcadaśapravāheṇa hyabdamekaṃ gatāyuṣam \|\| 45 \|\|*

*viṃśaddinapravāheṇa ṣaṇmāsaṃ lakṣayettadā \|*  
*paṃcaviṃśaddinamitaṃ vahate vāmanāḍikā \|\| 46 \|\|*

*jīvitaṃ tu tadā tasya trimāsaṃ hi gatāyuṣaḥ \|*  
*ṣaḍviṃśaddinamānena māsadvayamudāhṛtam \|\| 47 \|\|*

44-47. When Iḍā and other Nāḍīs respire for five days consecutively, you shall know that the person will die within that year. If these respire for ten days he lives for a year. If they respire for fifteen days he will die after a year. If for twenty days he lives for six months. If the left Nāḍī respires for twenty five days he lives for three months; if for twenty-six days, two months.

## \|\| 9.25.48 \|\|

*saptaviṃśaddinamitaṃ vahatetyativiśramā \|*  
*māsamekaṃ samākhyātaṃ jīvitaṃ vāmagocare \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. If it respires for twenty-seven days incessantly he lives for a month.

## \|\| 9.25.49 \|\|

*etatpramāṇaṃ vijñeyaṃ vāmavāyupramāṇataḥ \|*  
*savyetare dinānyeva catvāraścānupūrvaśaḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. This shall be known in accordance with the left-hand practices. If in the right he lives for four days only.

## \|\| 9.25.50 \|\|

*catussthāne sthitā devi ṣoḍaśaitāḥ prakīrtitāḥ \|*  
*teṣāṃ pramāṇaṃ vakṣyāmi sāmprataṃ hi yathārthataḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. O goddess, these sixteen are mentioned as stationed in four places. I shall now mention their extent factually.

## \|\| 9.25.51 \|\|

*ṣaḍdinānyāditaḥ kṛtvā saṃkhyāyāśca yathāvidhi \|*  
*etadaṃtargate caiva vāmaraṃdhre prakāśitam \|\| 51 \|\|*

51\. Beginning with six days and calculating duly it happens at mentioned in the left path.

## \|\| 9.25.52 \|\|

*ṣaḍdināni yadā rūḍhaṃ dvivarṣaṃ ca sa jīvati \|*  
*māsānaṣṭau vijānīyāddinānyaṣṭa ca tāni tu \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. If it respires for six days, he lives for two yean eight months and eight days. You shall know that.

## \|\| 9.25.53 \|\|

*prāṇaḥ saptadaśe caiva viddhi varṣaṃ na saṃśayaḥ \|*  
*saptamāsānvijānīyāddinaiḥ ṣaḍbhirna saṃśayaḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. If it respires for seven days he lives for one year seven months and six days, to be sure.

## \|\| 9.25.54 \|\|

*aṣṭaghasraprabhedena dvivarṣaṃ hi sa jīvati \|*  
*caturmāsā hi vijñeyāścaturviṃśaddināvadhiḥ \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. If it flows for eight days, he lives for two yean four months and twenty-four days.

## \|\| 9.25.55 \|\|

*yadā navadinaṃ prāṇā vahaṃtyeva trimāsakam \|*  
*māsadvayaṃ ca dve māse dinā dvādaśa kīrtitāḥ \|\| 55 \|\|*

55\. If for nine days, he lives for three months, twice two months, and twelve days (i.e. seven months and twelve days).

## \|\| 9.25.56 \|\|

*pūrvavatkathitā ye tu kālaṃ teṣāṃ tu pūrvakam \|*  
*avāṃtaradinā ye tu tena māsena kathyate \|\| 56 \|\|*

56\. The time of the previous days has been mentioned before. But the intervening days of the month may be calculated thus.

## \|\| 9.25.57 \|\|

*ekādaśa pravāheṇa varṣamekaṃ sa jīvati \|*  
*māsā nava tathā proktā dinānyaṣṭamitānyapi \|\| 57 \|\|*

57\. If for eleven days, he lives for one year nine months and eight days.

## \|\| 9.25.58 \|\|

*dvādaśena pravāheṇa varṣamekaṃ sa jīvati \|*  
*māsān sapta vijānīyātṣaḍghasrāṃścāpyudāharet \|\| 58 \|\|*

58\. If for twelve days he lives for one year seven months and six days.

## \|\| 9.25.59-61 \|\|

*nāḍī yadā ca vahati trayodaśadināvadhi \|*  
*samvatsaraṃ bhavettasya caturmāsāḥ prakīrtitāḥ \|\| 59 \|\|*

*caturviśaddinaṃ śeṣaṃ jīvitaṃ ca na saṃśayaḥ \|*  
*prāṇavāhā yadā vāme caturddaśadināni tu \|\| 60 \|\|*

*samvatsaraṃ bhavettasya māsāḥ ṣaṭ ca prakīrtitāḥ \|*  
*caturviṃśaddinānyeva jīvitaṃ ca na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 61 \|\|*

59-61. If for thirteen days he lives for one year four months and twenty-four days. When the Prāṇa flows in the left side for fourteen days he lives for one year, six months and twenty-four days.

## \|\| 9.25.62 \|\|

*paṃcadaśapravāheṇa nava māsānsa jīvati \|*  
*caturviśaddinānyeva kathitaṃ kālavedibhiḥ \|\| 62 \|\|*

62\. If for fifteen days he lives for nine months and twenty-four days.

## \|\| 9.25.63 \|\|

*ṣoḍaśāhapravāheṇa daśamāsānsa jīvati \|*  
*caturviśaddinādhikyaṃ kathitaṃ kālavedibhiḥ \|\| 63 \|\|*

63\. If for sixteen days he lives for ten months and twenty-four days.

## \|\| 9.25.64 \|\|

*saptadaśapravāheṇa navamāsairgatāyuṣam \|*  
*aṣṭādaśadinānyatra kathitaṃ sādhakeśvari \|\| 64 \|\|*

64\. If for seventeen days he dies within nine months. O fond of devotees, eighteen days too are mentioned here.

## \|\| 9.25.65 \|\|

*vāmacāraṃ yadā devi hyaṣṭādaśadināvadhiḥ \|*  
*jīvitaṃ cāṣṭamāsaṃ tu ghasrā dvādaśa kīrtitāḥ \|\| 65 \|\|*

65\. O goddess, if for eighteen days he lives for eight months and twelve days.

## \|\| 9.25.66-68 \|\|

*caturviṃśaddinānyatra niścayenāvadhāraya \|*  
*prāṇavāho yadā devi trayoviṃśaddināvadhiḥ \|\| 66 \|\|*

*catvāraḥ kathitā māsāḥ ṣaḍdināni tathottare \|*  
*caturviṃśapravāheṇa trīnmāsāṃśca sa jīvati \|\| 67 \|\|*

*dinānyatra daśāṣṭau ca saṃharaṃtyeva cārataḥ \|*  
*avāṃtaradine yastu saṃkṣepātte prakīrtitaḥ \|\| 68 \|\|*

66-68. If for twenty-three days, know for certain that he lives for five months i.e. 4 months and 24+6 days=5 months. If for twenty-four days he lives for three months and eighteen days. All this I have narrated to you in brief.

## \|\| 9.25.69 \|\|

*vāmacāraḥ samākhyāto dakṣiṇaṃ śṛṇu sāṃpratam \|*  
*aṣṭāviṃśapravāheṇa tithimānena jīvati \|\| 69 \|\|*

69\. This is in accordance with the left hand practices. Now listen according to the right hand practices. If the flow is for twenty-eight days he lives for that much period.

## \|\| 9.25.70 \|\|

*pravāheṇa daśāhena tatsaṃsthena vipadyate \|*  
*triṃśaddhasrapravāhena pañcāhena vipadyate \|\| 70 \|\|*

70\. If for ten days, he dies immediately. If for thirty days he dies in five days.

## \|\| 9.25.71 \|\|

*ekatriṃśadyadā devi vahate ca niraṃtaram \|*  
*dinatrayaṃ tadā tasya jīvitaṃ hi na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 71 \|\|*

71\. O goddess, if it respires incessantly for thirty-one days, he lives for three days.

## \|\| 9.25.72 \|\|

*dvātriṃśatprāṇasaṃkhyā ca yadā hi vahate raviḥ \|*  
*tadā tu jīvitaṃ tasya dvidinaṃ hi na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 72 \|\|*

72\. When Ravi Nāḍi respires thirty-two breaths he lives for two days; there is no doubt in this.

## \|\| 9.25.73-74 \|\|

*dakṣiṇaḥ kathitaḥ prāṇo madhyasthaṃ kathayāmi te \|*  
*ekabhāgagato vāyupravāho mukhamaṇḍale \|\| 73 \|\|*

*dhāvamānapravāheṇa dinamekaṃ sa jīvati \|*  
*cakrame tatparāsorhi purāvidbhirudāhṛtam \|\| 74 \|\|*

73-74. The right path-practice has been mentioned to you. I shall now tell you about the middle one. The respiration with frequency on one part of the face makes him live for one day. Thus the time for death for a dying person has been mentioned by the experts.

## \|\| 9.25.75 \|\|

*etatte kathitaṃ devi kālacakraṃ gatāyuṣaḥ \|*  
*lokānāṃ ca hitārthāya kimanyacchrotumicchasi \|\| 75 \|\|*

75\. O goddess, this Kālacakra pertaining to the dying person has been mentioned to you for the benefit of the worlds. What more do you wish to hear?

## Chapter 26 - The deception or dodging of Kāla

## \|\| 9.26.1 \|\|

*devyuvāca \|*  
*kathitaṃ tu tvayā deva kālajñānaṃ yathārthataḥ \|*  
*kālasya vaṃcanaṃ brūhi yathā tattvena yoginaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

The goddess said:—

1\. O lord, the perfect knowledge of Kāla has been mentioned by you. Now please mention the dodging of Kāla by a Yogin, factually.

## \|\| 9.26.2-3 \|\|

*kālastu sannikṛṣṭo hi vartate sarvajaṃtuṣu \|*  
*yathā cāsya na mṛtyuśca vaṃcate kālamāgatam \|\| 2 \|\|*

*tathā kathaya me deva prītiṃ kṛtvā mamopari \|*  
*yogināṃ ca hitāya tvaṃ brūhi sarvasukhaprada \|\| 3 \|\|*

2-3. Kāla follows upon the track of all creatures. O lord, now taking pity on me, please mention how the Kāla that has come does not deceive. O bestower of happiness, please mention it, for the benefit of Yogins.

## \|\| 9.26.4 \|\|

*\|\| śaṃkara uvāca \|*  
*śṛṇu devi pravakṣyāmi pṛṣṭohaṃ yattvayā śive \|*  
*samāsena ca sarveṣāṃ mānuṣāṇāṃ hitārthataḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

Śiva said:—

4\. O Goddess Śivā listen. I shall explain briefly what I have been asked by you for the benefit of all men.

## \|\| 9.26.5 \|\|

*pṛthivyāpastathā tejo vāyurākāśameva ca \|*  
*eteṣāṃ hi samāyogaḥ śarīraṃ pāṃcabhautikam \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. The body is a conglomeration of earth, water, fire, wind and ether. It consists of five elements.

## \|\| 9.26.6 \|\|

*ākāśastu tato vyāpī sarveṣāṃ sarvagaḥ sthitaḥ \|*  
*ākāśe tu vilīyaṃte saṃbhavaṃti punastataḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. The ether is all-pervasive. The elements merge in ether and are reborn therefrom.

## \|\| 9.26.7 \|\|

*viyoge tu sadā kasya svaṃ dhāma pratipedire \|*  
*tasyā sthiratā cāsti sannipātasya suṃdari \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. When the ether is disintegrated the elements resort to their causes. O beautiful woman, there is no stability for this conglomeration.

## \|\| 9.26.8 \|\|

*jñānino'pi tathā tatra tapomaṃtrabalādapi \|*  
*te sarve suvijānaṃti sarvametanna saṃśayaḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. There is no doubt that the wise know all this perfectly through the power of austerity and mantras.

## \|\| 9.26.9 \|\|

*devyuvāca \|*  
*khaṃ tena yannaśyati ghorarūpaḥ kālaḥ karālastridivaikanāthaḥ \|*  
*dagdhastvayā tvaṃ punareva tuṣṭaḥ stotrai stutaḥ svāṃ prakṛtiṃ sa lebhe \|\| 9 \|\|*

The goddess said:—

9\. Kāla of terrible form, the awful sole lord of the gods, perishes while taking them to the Ether. He was burnt by you. When eulogised you were pleased and then you revived him to life.

## \|\| 9.26.10 \|\|

*tvayā sa coktaḥ kathayā janānāmadṛṣṭarūpaḥ pracariṣyasīti \|*  
*dṛṣṭastvayā tatra mahāprabhāvaḥ prabhorvarātte punarutthitaśca \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Kāla was addressed by you. “In the talk of the people you will be moving about in the invisible form.” He of great power was seen by you there. By means of the boon granted by you he was reborn.

## \|\| 9.26.11 \|\|

*tadadya bhoḥ kāla ihāsthi kiṃcinnihanyate yena vadasva tanme \|*  
*tvaṃ yogivaryaḥ prabhurātmataṃtraḥ paropakārāttatanurmaheśa \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Hence there is Kāla here. Something is killed by him. Please mention that to me. You are the foremost among Yogins. You are self-controlled lord. O lord Śiva, you take up physical bodies for helping others.

## \|\| 9.26.12 \|\|

*śaṃkara uvāca \|*  
*na hanyate devavaraistu daityaissayakṣarakṣoragamānuṣaiśca \|*  
*ye yogino dhyānaparāssadehā bhavaṃti te ghnaṃti sukhena kālam \|\| 12 \|\|*

Śiva said:—

12\. Kāla is not killed by the leading gods, Daityas, Yakṣas, Rākṣasas, serpents and human beings. But those embodied beings who are Yogins engrossed in meditation can easily kill Kāla.

## \|\| 9.26.13 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*etacchrutvā tribhuvanaguroḥ prāha gaurī vihasya satyaṃ tvaṃ me vada kathamasau hanyate yena kālaḥ \|*  
*śambhustāmāha sadyo hi makaravadane yogino ye kṣipaṃti kālavyālaṃ sakalamanaghāstacchṛṇuṣvaikacittā \|\| 13 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

13\. On hearing this from Śiva, the preceptor of the three worlds, Gaurī laughed and said “Please tell me the truth. How is this Kāla killed? By whom is it killed? Śiva told her immediately “O moon-faced lady, those who are sinless Yogins hurl the serpent Kāla entirely. Listen to that with concentration.”

## \|\| 9.26.14 \|\|

*śaṅkara uvāca \|*  
*paṃcabhūtātmako dehassadāyuktastu tadguṇaiḥ \|*  
*utpādyate varārohe tadvilīno hi pārthivaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

Śiva said:—

14\. O beautiful lady, the physical body consists of five elements. It arises from them and merges in them. It is always endowed with their attributes.

## \|\| 9.26.15 \|\|

*ākāśājjāyate vāyurvāyostejaśca jāyate \|*  
*tejaso'mbu vinirddiṣṭaṃ tasmāddhi pṛthivī bhavet \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. From the ether the wind is produced. From the wind, fire is generated. Water is said to be produced from fire and from it the earth comes out.

## \|\| 9.26.16-17 \|\|

*pṛthivyādīni bhūtāni gacchaṃti kramaśaḥ param \|*  
*dharā paṃcaguṇā proktā hyāpaścaiva caturguṇāḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

*triguṇaṃ ca tathā tejo vāyurdviguṇa eva ca \|*  
*śabdaikaguṇamākāśaṃ pṛthivyādiṣu kīrtitam \|\| 17 \|\|*

16-17. Each of the elements merges into the other in order. Earth has five attributes. Water has four. Fire has three. Wind has only two. Ether has only one. The attributes of the earth and other elements can be mentioned thus.

## \|\| 9.26.18 \|\|

*śabdassparśaśca rūpaṃ ca raso gandhaśca paṃcamaḥ \|*  
*vijahāti guṇaṃ svaṃ svaṃ tadā bhūtaṃ vipadyate \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. The five attributes are sound, touch, colour, taste and smell. When it is relieved of its attributes the element perishes.

## \|\| 9.26.19 \|\|

*tadā guṇaṃ vigṛhṇāti prādurbhūtaṃ taducyate \|*  
*evaṃ jānīhi deveśi paṃcabhūtāni tattvataḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. When it takes up the attributes the element manifests itself. O goddess, know thus all the elements factually.

## \|\| 9.26.20 \|\|

*tasmāddhi yoginā nityaṃ svasvakāleṃ'śajā guṇāḥ \|*  
*ciṃtanīyāḥ prayatnena devi kālajigīṣuṇā \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. Hence O goddess, the partial attributes shall be thought of by the Yogin assiduously if he is desirous of conquering Kāla.

## \|\| 9.26.21 \|\|

*devyuvāca \|*  
*kathaṃ jejīyyate kālo yogibhiryogavitprabho \|*  
*dhyānena cātha mantreṇa tatsarvaṃ kathayasva me \|\| 21 \|\|*

The goddess said:—

21\. O lord of the knowers of Yoga, how is Kāla wished to be conquered by the Yogins? Is it by meditation or incantations? Please mention everything to me.

## \|\| 9.26.22 \|\|

*śaṅkara uvāca \|*  
*śṛṇu devi pravakṣyāmi yogināṃ hitakāmyayā \|*  
*parajñānaprakathanaṃ na deyaṃ yasya kasyacit \|\| 22 \|\|*

Śiva said:—

22\. O goddess, listen. I shall explain it for the benefit of the Yogins. The perfect knowledge shall not be imparted to anyone and everyone.

## \|\| 9.26.23 \|\|

*śraddadhānāya dātavyaṃ bhaktiyuktāya dhīmate \|*  
*anāstikāya śuddhāya dharmanityāya bhāmini \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. O fair lady, it is to be imparted to the faithful, to the intelligent, to one who is endowed with devotion, to the pious who is not an atheist and to oie who is always virtuous.

## \|\| 9.26.24 \|\|

*suśvāsena suśayyāyāṃ yogaṃ yuṃjīta yogavit \|*  
*dīpaṃ vināṃdhakāre tu prajāḥ supteṣu dhārayet \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. The knower of Yoga shall practise it taking breath normally sitting on a good bed. If he practises in darkness he shall not keep any lamp. He shall practise when people are asleep.

## \|\| 9.26.25 \|\|

*tarjanyā pihitau karṇau pīḍayitvā muhūrttakam \|*  
*tasmātsaṃśrūyate śabdastudanvahnisamudbhavaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. When the ears are closed with the index finger for a short while a kind of sound is heard as that of a blazing fire.

## \|\| 9.26.26 \|\|

*sandhyāto bhuktamevaṃ hi cāvasannaṃ kṣaṇādapi \|*  
*sarvarogānnihatyāśu jvaropadravakānbahūn \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. The practice of Yoga quells all ailments, fevers and many other distressing ills.

## \|\| 9.26.27 \|\|

*yaścopalakṣayennityairākāraṃ ghaṭikādvayam \|*  
*jitvā mṛtyuṃ tathā kāmaṃ svecchayā paryyaṭediha \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. He who visualises the form of death for two Ghaṭikās conquers death and lust and can roam about as he pleases.

## \|\| 9.26.28-29 \|\|

*sarvajñassarvadarśī ca sarvasiddhimavāpnuyāt \|*  
*yathā nadati khe'bdo hi prāvṛḍadbhissusaṃyataḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

*taṃ śrutvā mucyate yogī sadyaḥ saṃsārabandhanāt \|*  
*tatassa yogibhirnnityaṃ sūkṣmātsūkṣmataro bhavet \|\| 29 \|\|*

28-29. He becomes omniscient and omniseer. He attains all Siddhis. If the Yogin hears the sound resembling that of the rain-bearing cloud he becomes rid of worldly bondage immediately. He then becomes the subtlest of all Yogins.

## \|\| 9.26.30 \|\|

*eṣa te kathito devi śabdabrahmavidhikramaḥ \|*  
*palālamiva dhānyārthī tyajedbandhamaśeṣataḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. O Goddess; thus the order and procedure of the meditation of Śabdabrahman has been narrated to you. Just as the seeker of grains eschews husks, he shall eschew bondage.

## \|\| 9.26.31 \|\|

*śabdabrahmatvidaṃ prāpya ye kecidanyakāṃkṣiṇaḥ \|*  
*ghnaṃti te muṣṭinākāśaṃ kāmayaṃte kṣudhāṃ tṛṣām \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. If after attaining the Śabdabrahman he were to aspire for other things he will be hitting the sky with his fist, he will be loving hunger and thirst.

## \|\| 9.26.32 \|\|

*jñātvā paramidaṃ brahma sukhadaṃ muktikāraṇam \|*  
*avāhyamakṣaraṃ caiva sarvopādhivivarjitam \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. After knowing the great Brahman, the cause of salvation, the bestower of happiness, the non-external, the imperishable, freed from all limitations, nothing else need be aspired after.

## \|\| 9.26.33 \|\|

*mohitāḥ kālapāśena mṛtyupāśavaśaṃgatāḥ \|*  
*śabdabrahma na jānaṃti pāpinaste kubuddhayaḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. Sinners of crooked intellect deluded by the noose of Kāla, fastened into death’s bondage do not realise Śabdabrahman.

## \|\| 9.26.34 \|\|

*tāvadbhavaṃti saṃsāre yāvaddhāma na viṃdate \|*  
*vidite tu pare tattve mucyate janmabandhanāt \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. One remains in the world as long as he has not obtained the supreme abode. When the great reality is understood he is released from the bondage of life.

## \|\| 9.26.35-36 \|\|

*nidrālasyaṃ mahā vighnaṃ jitvā śatruṃ prayatnataḥ \|*  
*sukhāsane sthito nityaṃ śabdabrahmābhyasanniti \|\| 35 \|\|*

*śatavṛddhaḥ pumāṃllabdhvā yāvadāyussamabhyaset \|*  
*mṛtyuñjayavapustambha ārogyaṃ vāyuvarddhanam \|\| 36 \|\|*

35-36. After conquering sleep and idleness—the great enemies and the great obstacles and sitting in a comfortable posture one should practise Śabdabrahman for ever. Even if he is an old man of hundred years he will gain health and virility and the power of stopping the decay of the body and of conquering death. He shall then continue the practice as long as he is alive.

## \|\| 9.26.37-38 \|\|

*pratyayo dṛśyate vṛddhe kiṃ punastaruṇe jane \|*  
*na coṃkāro na mantropi naiva bījaṃ na cākṣaram \|\| 37 \|\|*

*anāhatamanuccāryyaṃ śabdabrahma śivaṃ param \|*  
*dhyāyante devi satataṃ sudhiyā yatnataḥ priye \|\| 38 \|\|*

37-38. Proof of belief is visible even in an old man. Why not then in a youthful man? O beloved goddess, intelligent persons meditate on the great Śiva assiduously forever. The Śabdabrahman cannot be uttered, cannot be struck at. It is neither Oṃkāra nor any mantra nor any Bīja nor any syllable.

## \|\| 9.26.39 \|\|

*tasmācchabdā nava proktāḥ prāṇavidbhistu lakṣitāḥ \|*  
*tānpravakṣyāmi yatnena nādasiddhimanukramāt \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. Hence the Śabdas are ninefold as explained by those who know the science of breath. I shall explain them assiduously in the proper order according to the Siddhi of Nāda.

## \|\| 9.26.40 \|\|

*ghoṣaṃ 1 kāṃsyaṃ 2 tathā śṛṃgaṃ 3 ghaṇṭāṃ 4 vīṇā 5 divaṃśajān \|*  
*dundubhiṃ 7 śaṃkhaśabdaṃ 8 tu navamaṃ meghagarjitam 9 \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. They are Ghoṣa (sound of the soft consonant), Kāṃsya(sound of bell metal), Śṛṅga (sound of horn), Ghaṇṭā (bell), Vīṇā (lute), Vaṃśaja (flute), Dundubhi (drum), Śaṅkha (conch) and Meghagarjita (the rumbling thunder).

## \|\| 9.26.41 \|\|

*nava śabdānparityajya tuṃkāraṃ tu samabhyaset \|*  
*dhyāyannevaṃ sadā yogī puṇyaiḥ pāpairna lipyate \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. After eschewing all these sounds, one shall practise Tuṃkāra? Meditating thus for ever the Yogin is not affected by either virtues or sins.

## \|\| 9.26.42 \|\|

*na śṛṇoti yadā śṛṇvanyogābhyāsena devike \|*  
*mriyatebhyasamānastu yogī tiṣṭheddivāniśam \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. O goddess, what others do not hear the Yogin hears by the practice of Yoga. While practising, if he dies, he may stay in that posture for days and nights.

## \|\| 9.26.43 \|\|

*tasmādutpadyate śabdo mṛ tsaptabhirdinaiḥ \|*  
*sa vai navavidho devi taṃ bravīmi yathārthataḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. From him the sound of conquest of death rises up in seven days. O goddess, it is of nine varieties. I shall mention it factually.

## \|\| 9.26.44 \|\|

*prathamaṃ nadate ghoṣamātmaśuddhikaraṃ param \|*  
*sarvavyādhiharaṃ nādaṃ vaśyākarṣaṇamuttamam \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. The first sound is Ghoṣa. It purifies the soul. It dispels sickness. It is excellent. It attracts and controls.

## \|\| 9.26.45 \|\|

*dvitīyaṃ nādate kāṃsyastambhayetprāṇināṃ gatim \|*  
*viṣabhūtagrahānsarvānbadhnīyānnātra saṃśayaḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. The second sound is Kāṃsya. It stops the movement of living beings. There is no doubt that it stops the effect of poison, evil spirits and evil planets.

## \|\| 9.26.46 \|\|

*tṛtīyaṃ nādate śṛṃgamabhicāri niyojayet \|*  
*vidviḍuccāṭane śatrormāraṇe ca prayojayet \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. The third sound is Śṛṅga. It is employed in black magic for exorcizing enemies and killing them.

## \|\| 9.26.47 \|\|

*ghaṃṭānādaṃ caturtha tu vadate parameśvaraḥ \|*  
*ākarṣassarvadevānāṃ kiṃ punarmānuṣā bhuvi \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. The fourth is Ghaṇṭānāda, so says lord Śiva. It is one that attracts the gods even. What then of human beings on this earth?

## \|\| 9.26.48 \|\|

*yakṣagandharvakanyāśca tasyākṛṣṭā dadaṃti hi \|*  
*yathepsitāṃ mahāsiddhiṃ yogine kāmato'pi vā \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. Attracted by him the virgins of Yakṣas and Gandharvas give him great Siddhis as desired by him or out of their own accord.

## \|\| 9.26.49 \|\|

*vīṇā tu paṃcamo nādaḥ śrūyate yogibhissadā \|*  
*tasmādutpadyate devi dūrādarśanameva hi \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. Vīṇā is the fifth sound. It is heard by Yogins always. O goddess, from it arises the power of vision from a great distance.

## \|\| 9.26.50 \|\|

*dhyāyato vaṃśanādaṃ tu sarvatattvaṃ prajāyate \|*  
*dundubhiṃ dhyāyamānastu jarāmṛtyuvivarjitaḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. All principles are understood by one who meditates on Vaṃśa Nāda. One who meditates on Dundubhi is devoid of old age and death.

## \|\| 9.26.51 \|\|

*śaṃkhaśabdena deveśi kāmarūpaṃ prapadyate \|*  
*yogino meghanādena na vipatsaṃgamo bhavet \|\| 51 \|\|*

51\. O goddess, through the conch sound he assumes any form as he wishes. By means of Megha Nāda the Yogin wards off all adversities.

## \|\| 9.26.52-53 \|\|

*yaścaikamanasā nityaṃ tuṃkāraṃ brahmarūpiṇam \|*  
*kimasādhyaṃ na tasyāpi yathāmati varānane \|\| 52 \|\|*

*sarvajñassarvadarśī ca kāmarūpī vrajatyasau \|*  
*na vikāraiḥ prayujyeta śiva eva na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

52-53. O pretty lady, is there anything impossible for one who meditates with single-minded devotion the Tuṃkāra form of Brahman? He becomes omniscient and visualiser of all. He moves about assuming any form he wishes. He is not affected by aberrations. He is Śiva himself. There if no doubt in this.

## \|\| 9.26.54 \|\|

*etatte parameśāni śabdabrahmasvarūpakam \|*  
*navadhā sarvamākhyātaṃ kiṃ bhūyaḥ śrotumicchasi \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. O goddess, thus the form of Śabdabrahman with its nine aspects has been wholly narrated to you. What else do you wish to hear?

## Chapter 27 - Escaping death and attainment of Śiva

## \|\| 9.27.1 \|\|

*devyuvāca \|*  
*vāyostu padamāpnoti yogākāśasamudbhavam \|*  
*tanme sarvaṃ samācakṣva prasannastvaṃ yadi prabho \|\| 1 \|\|*

The goddess said:—

1\. A Yogin attains the region of wind arising from the Yogic Ether. O lord, if you are delighted let me hear from you.

## \|\| 9.27.2 \|\|

*\|\| śaṃkara uvāca \|*  
*purā me sarvamākhyātaṃ yogināṃ hitakāmyayā \|*  
*kālaṃ jigāya yassamyagvāyorliṃgaṃ yathā bhavet \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. Formerly, desirous of the welfare of Yogins I had explained everything as to how wind became a symbol for the Yogins desirous of conquering Kāla.

## \|\| 9.27.3 \|\|

*tena jñātvā dinaṃ yogī prāṇāyāmaparaḥ sthitaḥ \|*  
*sa jayatyāgataṃ kālaṃ māsārddhenaiva suṃdari \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. Knowing the day through that, the Yogin stays engaged in Prāṇāyāma. O pretty woman, within half a month he conquers the Kāla that has already come upon him.

## \|\| 9.27.4 \|\|

*hṛtstho vāyussadā vahnerdīpakasso'nupāvakaḥ \|*  
*sa bāhyābhyaṃtaro vyāpī vāyussarvagato mahān \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. The wind within the heart kindles the fire. It is favourable to the fire. It blows within and without. It is all pervasive. It is great and it spreads everywhere.

## \|\| 9.27.5 \|\|

*jñānavijñānamutsāhaḥ sarvaṃ vāyoḥ pravartate \|*  
*yeneha nirjito vāyustena sarvamidaṃ jagat \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Knowledge, perfect knowledge, zeal, everything is activised by wind. He who has conquered it has conquered the universe.

## \|\| 9.27.6 \|\|

*dhāraṇāyāṃ sadā tiṣṭhejjarāmṛtyujighāṃsayā \|*  
*yogī yogarataḥ samyagdhāraṇādhyānatatparaḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. With a desire to prevent old age and death, the Yogin engaged in the practice of Yoga, meditation and retention shall stay in the state of Dhāraṇā.

## \|\| 9.27.7 \|\|

*lohakāro yathā bhastrāmāpūryya mukhato mune \|*  
*sādhayedvāyunā karma tadvadyogī samabhyaset \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. O sage, the blacksmith blows air into the bellows and does his job by means of the wind. The Yogin shall also practise similarly.

## \|\| 9.27.8 \|\|

*devassahasrake netrapādahastasahasrakaḥ \|*  
*graṃthīnhi sarvamāvṛtya so'gre tiṣṭheddaśāṃgulam \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. The lord of thousands, the lord of thousand eyes, hands and feet encompasses the universe and stands ten Aṅgulas above.

## \|\| 9.27.9 \|\|

*gāyatrīṃ śirasā sārddhaṃ japedvyāhṛtipūrvikām \|*  
*trivāramāyataprāṇāḥ prāṇāyāmassa ucyate \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Holding the breath one shall repeat the Gāyatrī Mantra along with his Śiras (Oṃ) and Vyāhṛtis thrice. That is called Prāṇāyāma.

## \|\| 9.27.10 \|\|

*gatāgatā nivartaṃte candrasūryādayo grahāḥ \|*  
*adyāpi na nivartaṃte yogadhyānaparāyaṇāḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Planets like the sun and the moon ate evolved and dissolved. But those who are devoted to Yoga and meditation do not return still.

## \|\| 9.27.11 \|\|

*śatamabdaṃ tapastaptvā kuśāgrāpaḥ pibeddvijaḥ \|*  
*tadāpnoti phalaṃ devi viprāṇāṃ dhāraṇaikayā \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. O goddess, by a single retention of vital airs, a brahmin attains the benefit accruing to him by the penance.performed for a hundred years while sustaining himself on the water from the tips of Kuśa grass.

## \|\| 9.27.12 \|\|

*yo dvijaḥ kalyamutthāya prāṇāyāmaikamācaret \|*  
*sarvaṃ pāpaṃ nihaṃtyāśu brahmalokaṃ sa gacchati \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. The brahmin who gets up at daybreak and performs a Prāṇāyāma quells his sins and goes to Brahmā’s region.

## \|\| 9.27.13 \|\|

*yo'taṃdritassadaikāṃte praṇāyāmaparo bhavet \|*  
*jarāṃ mṛtyuṃ vinirjitya vāyugaḥ khecarīti saḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. Free from lethargy he who is engaged in Prāṇāyāma in an isolated place, conquers old age and death, masters the vital air and can traverse the sky.

## \|\| 9.27.14 \|\|

*siddhasya bhajate rūpaṃ kāṃtiṃ medhāṃ parākramam \|*  
*śauryaṃ vāyusamo gatyā saukhyaṃ ślāghyaṃ paraṃ sukham \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. He can assume the form of a Siddha. He gets lustre, intelligence, exploits, valour, happiness and the speed of the wind.

## \|\| 9.27.15 \|\|

*etatkathitamaśeṣaṃ vāyossiddhiṃ yadāpnute yogī \|*  
*yattejaso'pi labhate tatte vakṣyāmi deveśi \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. O goddess, I have thus narrated the Siddhi through the wind that the Yogin attains from fire.

## \|\| 9.27.16 \|\|

*sthitvā sukhāsane sve śete janavacanahīne tu \|*  
*śaśiraviyutayā tejaḥ prakāśayanmadhyame deśe \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. The aspirant sits in the comfortable posture in a place free from noise. In the middle place he illuminates the brilliance accompanied by the lustre of the moon and the sun.

## \|\| 9.27.17 \|\|

*vahnigataṃ bhrūmadhye prakāśate yastvataṃdrito yogī \|*  
*dīpahīnadhvāṃta paśyennyūnamasaṃśayaṃ loke \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. The Yogin who illuminates the fire existing between the eyebrows, alertfully can see in darkness without lamps to be sure.

## \|\| 9.27.18 \|\|

*netre karaśākhābhiḥ kiṃcitsaṃpīḍya yatnato yogī \|*  
*tāraṃ paśyandhyāyenmuhūrtamarddhaṃ tamekabhāvo'pi \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. The Yogin presses the eyes with the fingers a little and strives to see the star and meditate on it for a muhūrta or half that time with concentration.

## \|\| 9.27.19 \|\|

*tatastu tamasi dhyāyanpaśyate jyotiraiśvaram \|*  
*śvetaṃ raktaṃ tathā pītaṃ kṛṣṇamindradhanuṣprabham \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. Thereafter while meditating in darkness he sees the bright lustre of Śiva in colours white, red, yellow, black and the rainbow colour.

## \|\| 9.27.20 \|\|

*bhuvormadhye lalāṭasthaṃ bālārkasamatejasam \|*  
*taṃ viditvā tu kāmāṃgī krīḍate kāmarūpadhṛk \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. On seeing that brilliance on a par with the rising sun stationed in the forehead between the eyebrows, the Yogin can assume any form of body he chooses and plays about as he wishes.

## \|\| 9.27.21-22 \|\|

*kāraṇapraśamāveśaṃ parakāyapraveśanam \|*  
*aṇimādiguṇāvāptirmanasā cāvalokanam \|\| 21 \|\|*

*dūraśravaṇa vijñānamadṛśyaṃ bahurūpadhṛk \|*  
*satatābhyāsayogena khecaratvaṃ prajāyate \|\| 22 \|\|*

21-22. By repeated practice the Yogin gains various powers—subduing the senses, entry into other bodies, the attainment of eight Siddhis Aṇimā and others, mental vision, hearing from far, perfect knowledge, invisibility assuming multi-forms, and the ability to walk through the sky.

## \|\| 9.27.23 \|\|

*śrutādhyayanasaṃpannā nānāśāstraviśāradāḥ \|*  
*jñānino'pi vimuhyaṃte pūrvakarmavaśānugāḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. Persons endowed with Vedic studies, experts in different Śāstras and the perfectly wise too become deluded when subservient to the force of the previous Karmans.

## \|\| 9.27.24 \|\|

*paśyaṃto'pi na paśyaṃti śṛṇvānā badhirā yathā \|*  
*yathāṃdhā mānuṣā loke mūḍhāḥ pāpavimohitāḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. They do not see even when they gaze at it; even while listening they are no better than deaf. Deluded by sins they are like blind men in the world.

## \|\| 9.27.25 \|\|

*vedāhametaṃ puruṣaṃ mahāṃtamādityavarṇaṃ tamasaḥ parastāt \|*  
*tameva viditvātimṛtyumeti nānyaḥ paṃthā vidyate prāyaṇāyaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. I know the great Puruṣa of the colour of the sun beyond all darkness. By knowing him alone can one surmount death. There is no other path to go.

## \|\| 9.27.26 \|\|

*eṣa te kathitaḥ samyaktejaso vidhiruttamaḥ \|*  
*kālaṃ jitvā yathā yogī cāmaratvaṃ prapadyate \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. Thus the excellent mode of Yoga of the fire has been explained to you, how the Yogin conquers death and attains immortality.

## \|\| 9.27.27 \|\|

*punaḥ parataraṃ vakṣye yathā mṛtyurna jāyate \|*  
*sāvadhānatayā devi śṛṇuṣvaikāgramānasā \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. I shall now explain a greater method of Yogic practice to ward off death. O goddess, listen with attention and concentration.

## \|\| 9.27.28-31 \|\|

*turīyā devi bhūtānāṃ yogināṃ dhyānināṃ tathā \|*  
*sukhāsane yathāsthānaṃ yogī niyatamānasaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

*samunnataśarīro'pi sa baddhvā karasaṃpuṭam \|*  
*cañcvākāreṇa vaktreṇa pibanvāyuṃ śanaiśśanaiḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

*prasravaṃti kṣaṇādāpastālusthā jīvadāyikāḥ \|*  
*tā jighredvāyunādāyāmṛtaṃ tacchītalaṃ jalam \|\| 30 \|\|*

*pibannanudinaṃ yogī na mṛtyuvaśago bhavet \|*  
*divyakāyo mahātejāḥ pipāsā kṣudvivarjitaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

28-31. This Yogin, O goddess, forms the fourth one, the other three being the Bhūtas, the Yogins and the meditators. This devotee practising the fourth method sits in a comfortable posture in a convenient spot with pure mind. He keeps his body straight. His hands are clasped together cuplike. He shapes the mouth like a beak and drinks in the wind slowly. The water-drops clinging to his palate begin to exude. They are life-givers. He inhales them through the wind. Drinking this nectarine cool water daily he will never be subservient to death. He gets a divine body and great brilliance. He becomes free from hunger and thirst.

## \|\| 9.27.32 \|\|

*balena nāgasturago javena dṛṣṭyā suparṇassuśrutistu dūrāt \|*  
*ākuṃcitākuṃḍalikṛṣṇakeśo gaṃdharvavidyādharatulyavarṇaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. In strength he becomes equal to an elephant; in speed he vies with a horse; he has the keen sight of Garuḍa; he can hear from a distance. He has dark curly ringlets of hair. His complexion is as fair as that of Gandharvas and Vidyādharas.

## \|\| 9.27.33 \|\|

*jīvennaro varṣaśataṃ surāṇāṃ sumedhasā vākpatinā samatvam \|*  
*evaṃ caran khecaratāṃ prayāti yatheṣṭacārī sukhitassadaiva \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. He lives for a hundred years by the calculation of the time of gods. He attains equality with Bṛhaspati. By practising this Yoga he can traverse in the sky or any where he likes. He can always remain happy.

## \|\| 9.27.34 \|\|

*punaranyatpravakṣṇami vidhānaṃ yatsurairapi \|*  
*gopitaṃ tu prayatnena tacchṛṇuṣva varānane \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. O fair lady, I shall now explain another mode which is strenuously guarded even by gods. Please listen to It.

## \|\| 9.27.35 \|\|

*samākuṃcyābhyasedyogī rasanāṃ tālukaṃ prati \|*  
*kiṃcitkālāṃtareṇaiva kramātprāpnoti laṃbikām \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. The Yogin shall practise curving the tongue towards the palate. After sometime be can carry it to the uvula.

## \|\| 9.27.36 \|\|

*tataḥ prasravate sā tu saṃspṛṣṭā śītalāṃ sudhām \|*  
*pibanneva sadā yogī so'maratvaṃ hi gacchati \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. When touched it begins to exude cool nectar. By drinking in this the Yogin attains immortality.

## \|\| 9.27.37 \|\|

*rephāgraṃ laṃbakāgraṃ karatalaghaṭanaṃ śubhrapadmasya bindostenākṛṣṭā sudheyaṃ patati parapade devatānaṃdakārī \|*  
*sāraṃ saṃsāratāraṃ kṛtakaluṣataraṃ kālatāraṃ satāraṃ yenedaṃ plāvitāṃgaṃ sa bhavati na mṛtaḥ kṣutpipāsāvihīnaḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. The tip of the tongue touches the tip of the uvula. The hands are clasped in the form of a white lotus. Drawn by him the nectar falls drop by drop. When he attains the great region he delights even the deities. This is the essential substance enabling one to cross the ocean of worldly existence, surmount all sins, cross the Kāla along with the star. He by whom the limbs have been drenched with the nectar never dies. He is devoid of hunger and thirst.

## \|\| 9.27.38 \|\|

*ebhiryuktā caturbhiḥ kṣitidharatanaye yogibhirvai dharaiṣā dhairyyānnityaṃ kuto'ntaṃ sakalamapi jagadyatsukhaprāpaṇāya \|*  
*svapne dehī vidhatte sakalamapi sadā mānayanyacca duḥkhaṃ svarge hyevaṃ dharitryāḥ prabhavati ca tato vā sa kiñciccaturṇām \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. O daughter of the mountain, the earth possessing these four types of Yogins can bestow happiness on the entire universe. What a man creates in dream the Yogin is competent to create in heaven. This is only a part of what the four can do.

## \|\| 9.27.39 \|\|

*tasmānmaṃtraistapobhirvrataniyamayutairauṣadhairyogayuktā dhātrī raktā manuṣyairnayavinayayutairdharmavidbhiḥ krameṇa \|*  
*bhūtānāmādi devo na hi bhavati calaḥ saṃyuto vai caturṇāṃ tasmādevaṃ pravakṣye vidhimanugaditaṃ chāyikaṃ yacchivākhyam \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. Hence the earth united with mantras, penances, Vratas, restraints, medicinal herbs and Yogic practice becomes lovable to men endowed with humility, polity and sacred virtues. The primordial lord of all living beings does not become shaken when associating with the four Yogins. I shall now explain in accordance with the injunctions the Chāyāpuruṣa (the shadowy person) who is called Siva.

## Chapter 28 - The Chāyāpuruṣa

## \|\| 9.28.1-2 \|\|

*devyuvāca \|*  
*devadeva mahādeva kathitaṃ kālavaṃcanam \|*  
*śabdabrahmasvarūpaṃ ca yogalakṣaṇamuttamam \|\| 1 \|\|*

*kathitaṃ te samāsenacchāyikaṃ jñānamuttamam \|*  
*vistareṇa samākhyāhi yogināṃ hitakāmyayā \|\| 2 \|\|*

The goddess said:—

1-2. O great lord of the gods, the way of dodging Kāla has been narrated; the form of Śabdabrahman and the excellent characteristics of Yoga have been mentioned. The reference to the shadow-person has been made by you in brief. Please explain in detail with a desire for the welfare of Yogins.

## \|\| 9.28.3 \|\|

*\|\| śaṃkara uvāca \|*  
*śṛṇu devi pravakṣyāmicchāyāpuruṣalakṣaṇam \|*  
*yajjñātvā puruṣaḥ samyaksarvapāpaiḥ pramucyate \|\| 3 \|\|*

Śiva said:—

3\. O goddess, listen. I shall explain the characteristics of the Chāyāpuruṣa, on knowing which man becomes released from all sins.

## \|\| 9.28.4-5 \|\|

*sūryyaṃ hi pṛṣṭhataḥ kṛtvā somaṃ vā varavarṇini \|*  
*śuklāmbaradharassragvī gaṃdhadhūpādivāsitaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

*saṃsmarenme mahāmaṃtraṃ sarvakāmaphalapradam \|*  
*navātmakaṃ piṃḍabhūtaṃ svāṃ chāyāṃ saṃnirīkṣayet \|\| 5 \|\|*

4-5. The aspirant wears garlands and white cloth. He makes himself fragrant white scents, incense and other things. O fair lady, he then sits with his back to the sun or the moon remembering the great mantra that bestows the benefits of all desires and that consists of the nine units solidified into one. He then glances at his own shadow.

## \|\| 9.28.6-7 \|\|

*dṛṣṭvā tāṃ punarākāśe śvetavarṇasvarūpiṇīm \|*  
*sa paśyatyekabhāvastu śivaṃ paramakāraṇam \|\| 6 \|\|*

*brahmaprāptirbhavettasya kālavidbhiritīritam \|*  
*brahmahatyādikaiḥ pāpairmucyate nātra saṃśayaḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

6-7. If he can see the shadow in the sky in a white-coloured form and begins to meditate on Śiva, the great cause with single-minded attention he attains Brahman then. This has been mentioned by the experts. In this state he is rid of Brahminicide and other sins.

## \|\| 9.28.8 \|\|

*śirohīnaṃ yadā paśyetṣaḍbhirmāsairbhavetkṣayaḥ \|*  
*samastaṃ vāṅmayaṃ tasya yoginastu yathā tathā \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. If he finds the shadow-person headless, the entire power of oral expression of practising Yogin becomes eliminated within six months.

## \|\| 9.28.9 \|\|

*śukle dharmaṃ vijānīyātkṛṣṇe pāpaṃ vinirdiśet \|*  
*rakte baṃdhaṃ vijānīyātpīte vidviṣamādiśet \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. If the shadow is white, know that it is an indication of virtue; if it is black, it indicates sin; if it is red, it indicates bondage and if it is yellow it indicates an enemy.

## \|\| 9.28.10 \|\|

*vivāho baṃdhunāśassyāddvituṃḍe caiva kṣudbhayam \|*  
*vikaṭau naśyate bhāryyā vijaṃghe dhanameva hi \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. If the shadow is devoid of arms it indicates death or ruin of kinsmen; if it is without snout it indicates the fear of hunger; if it is devoid of hips, wife dies; if it is devoid of calves it indicates wealth.

## \|\| 9.28.11 \|\|

*pādābhāve videśassyādityetatkathitaṃ mayā \|*  
*tadvicāryyaṃ prayattnena puruṣeṇa maheśvari \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. If there is no foot, exile to a foreign country takes place. This has been mentioned by me. O great goddess, this shall be carefully considered by the people.

## \|\| 9.28.12 \|\|

*samyaktaṃ puruṣaṃ dṛṣṭvā saṃniveśyātmanātmani \|*  
*japennavātmakaṃ maṃtraṃ hṛdayaṃ me maheśvari \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. (Practising still further and) seeing the shadow-person perfectly and keeping it within the Ātman through the Ātman, O goddess, the aspirant shall repeat the nine-syllabled Mantra in the heart.

## \|\| 9.28.13 \|\|

*vatsare vigate maṃtrī tannāsti yanna sādhayet \|*  
*aṇimādiguṇānaṣṭau khecaratvaṃ prapadyate \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. If a year passes by there will be nothing that the repeater of the mantra cannot achieve. He attains the eight Siddhis, Aṇimā and others and the ability to walk through the sky.

## \|\| 9.28.14 \|\|

*punaranyatpravakṣyāmi śaktiṃ jñātuṃ durāsadām \|*  
*pratyakṣaṃ dṛśyate loke jñānināmagrataḥ sthitam \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. I shall mention another power which is inscrutable but is known to those possessed of perfect knowledge.

## \|\| 9.28.15-16 \|\|

*ajñeyā likhyate loke yā sarpīkṛtakuṇḍalī \|*  
*sā mātrā yānasaṃsthāpi dṛśyate na ca paṭhyate \|\| 15 \|\|*

*brahmāṇḍamūrdhnigā yā ca stutā vedaistu nityaśaḥ \|*  
*jananī sarvavidyānāṃ guptavidyeti gīyate \|\| 16 \|\|*

15-16. An unknowable figure in the form of a curling serpent is drawn and that figure placed in the vehicle, nowhere mentioned, is seen there. That which stands uppermost in the universe and that which is eulogised by the Vedas for ever is sung as a secret Vidyā and is the mother of all Vidyās.

## \|\| 9.28.17 \|\|

*khecarā sā vinirdiṣṭā sarvaprāṇiṣu saṃsthitā \|*  
*dṛśyādṛśyācalā nityā vyaktāvyaktā sanātanī \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. It is the lore of traversing the sky. It is stationed in all living beings. It is visible and invisible. It is immovable, eternal, manifest and unmanifest. It is permanent.

## \|\| 9.28.18 \|\|

*avarṇā varṇasaṃyuktā procyate biṃdumālinī \|*  
*tāṃ paśyansarvadā yogī kṛtakṛtyo'bhijāyate \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. It is devoid of colour yet it possesses colour. It is called Bindumālinī. The Yogin who sees it always remains contented.

## \|\| 9.28.19 \|\|

*sarvatīrthakṛtasnānādbhaveddānasya yatphalam \|*  
*sarvayajñaphalaṃ yacca mālinyā darśanāttadā \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. On seeing Mālinī one attains the fruit of all sacrifices, the benefit of charitable gifts and the ceremonial ablutions in all holy centres.

## \|\| 9.28.20-21 \|\|

*prāpnotyatra na saṃdehassatyaṃ vai kathitaṃ mayā \|*  
*sarvatīrtheṣu yatsnātvā dattvā dānāni sarvaśaḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

*sarveṣāṃ devi yajñānāṃ yatphalaṃ tallabhetpumān \|*  
*kiṃ bahūktyā maheśāni sarvānkāmānsamaśnute \|\| 21 \|\|*

20-21. There is no doubt in this. I mention the truth to you. The man attains the fruit derivable from all sacrifices, O goddess, as well as the benefit of making charitable gifts and taking the ceremonial ablutions in all the holy centres. O goddess, of what avail is speaking much? He attains all cherished desires.

## \|\| 9.28.22 \|\|

*tasmājjñānaṃ yathāyogamabhyasetsatataṃ budhaḥ \|*  
*abhyāsājjāyate siddhiryogo'bhyāsātpravardhate \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. Hence a sensible person shall cultivate this knowledge as well as Yoga. Siddhi is attained by practice. Yoga is enhanced by practice.

## \|\| 9.28.23 \|\|

*saṃvittirlabhyate'bhyāsādabhyāsānmokṣamaśnute \|*  
*abhyāsassatataṃ kāryo dhīmatā mokṣakāraṇam \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. Perfect realization is attained by practice. Liberation is attained by practice. An intelligent man shall always maintain practice. Practice is the cause of salvation.

## \|\| 9.28.24 \|\|

*ityetatkathitaṃ devi bhuktimuktiphalapradam \|*  
*kimanyatpṛcchyate tattvaṃ vada satyaṃ bravīmi te \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. O goddess, I have mentioned that all which bestows the benefit of worldly pleasures and salvation. What other tenet do you propose? Tell me, I shall tell you the truth.

## \|\| 9.28.25 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*iti śrutvā brahmaputravacanaṃ paramārthadam \|*  
*prasanno'bhūdati vyāsaḥ pārāśaryyo munīśvarāḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

25\. O great sages, on hearing these words of Sanatkumāra, son of Brahmā, the words bestowing the knowledge of truth, Vyāsa, son of Parāśara, was much delighted.

## \|\| 9.28.26 \|\|

*sanatkumāraṃ sarvajñaṃ brahmaputraṃ kṛpānidhim \|*  
*vyāsaḥ paramasaṃtuṣṭaḥ praṇanāma muhurmuhuḥṣa \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. The extremely delighted Vyāsa, bowed again and again to the omniscient son of Brahmā, Sanatkumāra, the storehouse of mercy.

## \|\| 9.28.27 \|\|

*tatastuṣṭāva taṃ vyāsaḥ kāleyassa munīśvaraḥ \|*  
*sanatkumāraṃ munayaḥ suravijñānasāgaram \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. O sages, then the great sage Vyāsa, eulogised Sanatkumāra, the ocean of the perfect knowledge of the deities.

## \|\| 9.28.28 \|\|

*vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*kṛtārtho'haṃ muniśreṣṭha brahmatvaṃ me tvayā kṛtam \|*  
*namaste'stu namaste'tu dhanyastvaṃ brahmavittamaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

Vyāsa said:—

28\. O great sage, I am satisfied. The position of Brahma has been assigned to me. Obeisance be to you. You are blessed. You are the most excellent of all knowers of Brahman.

## \|\| 9.28.29 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*iti stutvā sa kāleyo brahmaputraṃ mahāmunim \|*  
*tūṣṇīṃ babhūva suprītaḥ paramānaṃdanirbharaḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

29\. After eulogising the great sage the son of Brahmā, Vyāsa, the grandson of Brahmā, kept silent. He was extremely delighted. He was filled with great bliss.

## \|\| 9.28.30 \|\|

*brahmaputrastamāmaṃtrya pūjitastena śaunakaḥ \|*  
*yayau svadhāma suprīto vyāso'pi prītamānasaḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. O Śaunaka, Sanatkumāra, son of Brahmā, took leave of him. Worshipped by Vyāsa he returned to his abode. Vyāsa too left for his abode with his mind full of delight.

## \|\| 9.28.31 \|\|

*iti me varṇito viprāḥ sukhadaḥ paramārthayuk \|*  
*sanatkumārakāleyasaṃvādo jñānavarddhanaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. O brahmins, thus I have described to you the conversation between Sanatkumāra and Vyāsa. It deals with truth. It is pleasing. It enhances knowledge.

## Chapter 29 - The primeval creation

## \|\| 9.29.1 \|\|

*śaunaka uvāca \|*  
*śrutaṃ me mahadākhyānaṃ yattvayā parikīrtitam \|*  
*sanatkumārakāleyasaṃvādaṃ paramārthadam \|\| 1 \|\|*

Śaunaka said:—

1\. The great narrative of the conversation of Sanatkumāra and Vyāsa has been heard by me as narrated by you. It has bestowed the knowledge of truth.

## \|\| 9.29.2 \|\|

*atohaṃ śrotumicchāmi yathā sargastu brahmaṇaḥ \|*  
*samutpannaṃ tu me brūhi yathā vyāsācca te śrutam \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. Now I wish to hear about the creation of Brahmā, the manner of its origin and the way in which you heard it from Vyāsa.

## \|\| 9.29.3 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*mune śṛṇu kathāṃ divyāṃ sarvapāpapraṇāśinīm \|*  
*kathyamānāṃ mayā citrāṃ bahvarthāṃ śrutavistarām \|\| 3 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

3\. O sage, listen to the divine story that destroys all sins; the variegated story that is being described by me; the story full of meaning and well known details.

## \|\| 9.29.4 \|\|

*yaścaināṃ pāṭhayettāṃ ca śṛṇuyādvā'pyabhīkṣṇaśaḥ \|*  
*svavaṃśadhāraṇaṃ kṛtvā svargaloke mahīyate \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. He who teaches or hears this story repeatedly sustains his family and is honoured in the world of heaven.

## \|\| 9.29.5-6 \|\|

*pradhānaṃ puruṣo yattannityaṃ sadasadātmakam \|*  
*pradhānapuruṣo bhūtvā nirmame lokabhāvanaḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

*sraṣṭāraṃ sarvabhūtānāṃ nārāyaṇaparāyaṇam \|*  
*taṃ vai viddhi muniśreṣṭha brahmāṇamamitaujasam \|\| 6 \|\|*

5-6. Puruṣa is eternal and he is of the nature of Sat and Asat as Pradhāna and Puruṣa. The creator of the worlds created Pradhāna after becoming Puruṣa. He created the Creator of all living beings devoted to Nārāyaṇa. O great sage, understand him to be Brahma of immeasurable strength and prowess.

## \|\| 9.29.7 \|\|

*yasmādakalpayatkalpāntamagrāśśucayo yataḥ \|*  
*bhavaṃti muniśārdūla namastasmai svayambhuve \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. O leading sage, obeisance to that Self-born being from whom the Kalpas and all beings originate.

## \|\| 9.29.8 \|\|

*tasmai hiraṇyagarbhāya puruṣāyeśvarāya ca \|*  
*namaskṛtya pravakṣyāmi bhūyaḥ sargamanuttamam \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. After bowing to that Puruṣa, lord Hiraṇyagarbha, I shall explain the excellent mode of creation again.

## \|\| 9.29.9 \|\|

*brahmā sraṣṭā hariḥ pātā saṃhartā ca maheśvaraḥ \|*  
*tasya sargasya nānyo'sti kāle kāle tathā gate \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Brahmā is the creator; Viṣṇu is the protector and Śíva is the annihilator. Even when the time passes there is no other cause of that creation.

## \|\| 9.29.10 \|\|

*so'pi svayaṃbhūrbhagavānsisṛkṣurvividhāḥ prajāḥ \|*  
*apa eva sasarjādau tāsu vīryamavāsṛjat \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Being self-born himself the lord, desirous of creating various subjects, created only the waters first. He then instilled virility into them.

## \|\| 9.29.11 \|\|

*āpo nārā iti proktā āpo vai narasūnavaḥ \|*  
*ayanaṃ tasya tāḥ pūrvaṃ tena nārāyaṇaḥ smṛtaḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. The waters are called Nāra (belonging to Nara). The waters are the progenies of Nara. Since waters had been his abode he is called Nārāyaṇa.

## \|\| 9.29.12 \|\|

*hiraṇyavarṇamabhavattadaṃḍamudakeśayam \|*  
*tatra jajñe svayaṃ brahmā svayaṃbhūriti viśrutaḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. The Egg floating in the water assumed golden colour. Brahma himself famous as the self-born was born there.

## \|\| 9.29.13 \|\|

*hiraṇyagarbho bhagavānuṣitvā parivatsaram \|*  
*tadaṃḍamakaroddvaidhaṃ divaṃ bhūmi ca nirmame \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. After staying there for a year, lord Hiraṇyagarbha, dichotomised that egg and created heaven and earth.

## \|\| 9.29.14 \|\|

*adho'thorddhvaṃ prayuktāni bhuvanāni caturddaśa \|*  
*tayośśakalayormadhya ākāśamamṛjatprabhuḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. Fourteen worlds were created including those above and below. With the intervening space between those two pieces the lord created the ether.

## \|\| 9.29.15 \|\|

*apsu pāriplavāṃ pṛthvīṃ diśaśca daśadhā divi \|*  
*tatra kāle mano vācaṃ kāmakrodhāvatho ratim \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. He created the earth floating in the waters and the ten quarters in the firmament. Then he created the mind, the speech, love and anger and the sexual delight.

## \|\| 9.29.16 \|\|

*marīcimatryaṃgirasau pulastyaṃ pulahaṃ kratuma \|*  
*vasiṣṭhaṃ tu mahatejāsso'sṛjatsapta mānasān \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. The excessively brilliant creator created seven mental sons, the sages, Marīci, Atri, Aṅgiras, Pulastya, Pulaha, Kratu and Vasiṣṭha.

## \|\| 9.29.17 \|\|

*sapta bahmāṇa ityete purāṇe niścayaṃ gatāḥ \|*  
*tato'sṛjatpunarbrahmā rudrānkrodhasamudbhavān \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. They have been decisively termed the seven Brahmās in the Purāṇas. Brahmā again created the Rudras arising from anger.

## \|\| 9.29.18 \|\|

*sanatkumāraṃ ca ṛṣiṃ sarveṣāmapi pūrvajam \|*  
*sapta caite prajāyaṃte paścādrudrāśca sarvataḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. He created the sage Sanatkumāra too, the eldest of all. They are thus seven born. The Rudras were born afterwards.

## \|\| 9.29.19 \|\|

*atassanatkumārastu tejassaṃkṣipya tiṣṭhati \|*  
*teṣāṃ saptamahāvaṃśā divyā devarṣipūjitāḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. Sanatkumāra stays with controlled brilliance. From them seven great families originated, all being divine and worshipped by the celestial sages.

## \|\| 9.29.20 \|\|

*prajāyante kriyāvanto maharṣibhiralaṃkṛtāḥ \|*  
*vidyuto'śani meghāṃśca rohitendradhanūṃṣi ca \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. Endowed with sacred rites they were born along with the other Sages. The lightning, the thunderbolt, the red clouds and the rainbow were created.

## \|\| 9.29.21 \|\|

*payāṃsi ca sasarjādau parjanyaṃ ca sasarja ha \|*  
*ṛco yajūṃṣi sāmāni nirmame yajñasiddhaye \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. He created water at the outset. He created the rain-bearing cloud. He created the Ṛks, the Yajus and the Sāmans for facilitating Yajñas.

## \|\| 9.29.22 \|\|

*pūjyāṃstairayajandevānityevamanuśuśruma \|*  
*mukhāddevānajanayatpitṝṃścaivātha vakṣasaḥ \|*  
*prajanācca manuṣyānvai jaghanānnirmame'surān \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. With these he worshipped the gods worthy of being adored. This is what we had heard. He created the gods from his mouth, the manes from the chest, men through impregnation and the Asuras from his loins.

## \|\| 9.29.23 \|\|

*uccāvacāni bhūtāni gātrebhyastasya jajñire \|*  
*āpavasya prajāsargaṃ sṛjato hi prajāpateḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. Living beings high and low were born of his limbs as the patriarch Āpava generated the creation of the aquatic beings.

## \|\| 9.29.24 \|\|

*sṛjyamānāḥ prajāścaiva nāvarddhanta yadā tadā \|*  
*dvidhā kṛtvātmano dehaṃ strī caiva puruṣo'bhavat \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. When the beings created did not increase he dichotomised his body and became man and woman.

## \|\| 9.29.25 \|\|

*sasṛje'tha prajāssarvā mahimnā vyāpya viśvataḥ \|*
