*tatpurūravase prādādrājyaṃ prāpya mahāyaśāḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

*mānavo yo muniśreṣṭhāḥ strīpuṃsorlakṣaṇaḥ prabhuḥ \|*  
*nariṣyaṃtācchakāḥ putrā nabhagasya suto 'bhavat \|\| 22 \|\|*

*aṃbarīṣastu bāhleyo bāhlakaṃ kṣetrāmāptavān \|*  
*śaryātirmithunaṃ tvāsīdānarto nāma viśrutaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

*putrassukanyā kanyā ca yā patnī cyavanasya hi \|*  
*ānartasya hi dāyādo raibhyo nāma sa raivataḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

20-24. Ikṣvāku the eldest son got the central territory. At the instance of Vasiṣṭha, Sudyumna gave the virtuous territory Pratiṣṭhāna\[2\] to Purūravas. O great sages, Sudyumna had the characteristics of both man and woman. The Śakas were the sons of Nariṣyanta. Nabhaga’s son Ambarīṣa got the region Bāhlaka and so was known as Bāhleya. Śaryāti had a son and a daughter. The son was known as Ānarta and the daughter as Sukanyā who later became the wife of Cyavana. Ānarta’s son was Raibhya known also as Raivata.

## \|\| 9.36.25 \|\|

*ānartaviṣaye yasya purī nāma kuśasthalī \|*  
*mahādivyā saptapurīmadhye yā saptamī matā \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. In the territory of Ānarta he got the city Kuśasthalī which was extremely brilliant and was the seventh among seven cities.

## \|\| 9.36.26 \|\|

*tasya putraśataṃ tvāsītkakudmī jyeṣṭha uttamaḥ \|*  
*tejasvī subalaḥ pāro dharmiṣṭho brahmapālakaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. He had a hundred sons of whom the eldest and the most excellent was Kakudmin. He was brilliant, strong, virtuous and a protector of brahmins.

## \|\| 9.36.27 \|\|

*kakudminastu saṃjātā revatī nāma kanyakā \|*  
*mahālāvaṇyasaṃyuktā divyalakṣmīrivāparā \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. A daughter Revatī was born to Kakudmin. She was endowed with great beauty, She was another Lakṣmī.

## \|\| 9.36.28 \|\|

*praṣṭuṃ kanyāvaraṃ rājā kakudmī kanyayā saha \|*  
*brahmaloke vidhessamyaksarvādhīśo jagāma ha \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. The king Kakudmin the lord of all went to Brahma’s region near Brahmā, accompanied by his daughter in order to ask him about a suitable bridegroom for her.

## \|\| 9.36.29 \|\|

*āvartamāne gāṃdharve sthito labdhakṣaṇaḥ kṣaṇam \|*  
*śuśrāva tatra gāṃdharvaṃ nartane brahmaṇoṃ'tike \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. While dance and music was going on, he stood there waiting for a moment near Brahmā.

## \|\| 9.36.30 \|\|

*muhūrtabhūtaṃ tatkāle gataṃ bahuyugaṃ tadā \|*  
*na kiṃcidbubudhe rājā kakudmī munayassa tu \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. Although it was only a Muhūrta in Brahmā’s region many Yugas had passed by. But O sages, the king Kakudmin did not know anything about it.

## \|\| 9.36.31 \|\|

*tadāsau vidhimā namya svābhiprāyaṃ kṛtāṃjaliḥ \|*  
*nyavedayadvinītātmā brahmaṇe paramātmane \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. Then he bowed to Brahmā the great Ātmari, humbly and with palms joined in reverence told him about his mission.

## \|\| 9.36.32 \|\|

*tadabhiprāyamākarṇya sa prahasya prajāpatiḥ \|*  
*kakudminaṃ mahārājaṃ samābhāṣya samabravīt \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. On hearing his purpose the patriarch Brahmā laughed. Addressing the great king Kakudmin, he said.

## \|\| 9.36.33 \|\|

*brahmovāca \|*  
*śṛṇu rājanraibhyasuta kakudminpṛthivapite \|*  
*madvacaḥ prītitassatyaṃ pravakṣyāmi viśeṣataḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

Brahmā said:—

33\. Listen, O King Kakudmin, O lord of the earth, son of Raibhya, to my words with attention. I shall tell you the truth particularly.

## \|\| 9.36.34 \|\|

*kālena saṃhṛtāste vai varā ye te kṛtā hṛdi \|*  
*na tadgotraṃ hi tatrāsti kālassarvasya bhakṣakaḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. All those bridegrooms you had in view have been killed by the efflux of time. Even their family is extinct. Time is the devourer of everything.

## \|\| 9.36.35 \|\|

*tvatpuryyapi hatā puṇyajanaissā rākṣasairnṛpa \|*  
*aṣṭāviṃśaddvāpare'dya kṛṣṇena nirmitā punaḥ \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. O king, your city too has been destroyed by Puṇyajanas, the Rākṣasas. It is now the twenty-eighth Dvāpara Yuga and the city has been rebuilt by Kṛṣṇa.

## \|\| 9.36.36 \|\|

*kṛtā dvārāvatī nāmnā bahudvārā manoramā \|*  
*bhojavṛṣṇyaṃdhakairguptā vāsudevapurogamaiḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. In the name of Dvāravatī because it has many entrances, it is very beautiful. It is protected by the Bhojas, Vṛṣṇis and Andhakas with Kṛṣṇa as their leader.

## \|\| 9.36.37 \|\|

*tadgaccha tatra prītātmā vāsudevāya kanyakām \|*  
*baladevāya dehi tvamimāṃ svatanayāṃ nṛpa \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. O king, now go there and give this daughter of yours to Baladeva the son of Vasudeva.

## \|\| 9.36.38 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*ityādiṣṭo nṛpo'yaṃ taṃ natvā tāṃ ca purīṃ gataḥ \|*  
*gatānbahūnyugāñjñātvā vismitaḥ kanyayā yutaḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

38\. Thus commanded, the king bowed to him and went to that city. On realising that many Yugas had elapsed he was surprised along with his daughter.

## \|\| 9.36.39 \|\|

*tatastu yuvatīṃ kanyāṃ tāṃ ca svāṃ suvidhānataḥ \|*  
*kṛṣṇabhrātre balāyāśu prādāttatra sa revatīm \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. Then he gave his daughter, the youthful maiden, Revatī to Bala the brother of Kṛṣṇa in conformity with the Śāstric rituals.

## \|\| 9.36.40 \|\|

*tato jagāma śikharaṃ merordivyaṃ mahāprabhuḥ \|*  
*śivamārādhayāmāsa sa nṛpastapasi sthitaḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. Then he, the great lord, went to the celestial summit of Meru and propitiated Śiva observing penance.

## \|\| 9.36.41 \|\|

*ṛṣaya ūcuḥ \|*  
*tatra sthito bahuyugaṃ brahmaloke sa revataḥ \|*  
*yuvaivāgānmartyalokametannaḥ saṃśayo mahān \|\| 41 \|\|*

The sages said:—

41\. Raivata stayed in Brahmā’s region for many Yugas. He returned to the mortal world as a young man. This is my great doubt.

## \|\| 9.36.42 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*na jarā kṣutpipāsā vā vikārāstatra saṃti vai \|*  
*apamṛtyurna keṣāṃcinmunayo brahmaṇoṃ'tike \|\| 42 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

42\. O sages, near Brahmā there is no old age, hunger, thirst, aberration or premature death to any one.

## \|\| 9.36.43 \|\|

*ato na rājā saṃprāpa jarāṃ mṛtyuṃ ca sā sutā \|*  
*sa yuvaivāgatastatra saṃmaṃtrya tanayāvaram \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. Therefore the king did not attain old age or death, nor his daughter. After consulting Brahmā about the bridegroom he returned still a young man.

## \|\| 9.36.44 \|\|

*gatvā dvārāvatīṃ divyāṃ purīṃ kṛṣṇavinirmitām \|*  
*vivāhaṃ kārayāmāsa kanyāyāḥ sa balena hi \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. Going to the divine city Dvāravatī rebuilt by Kṛṣṇa, he got his daughter married to Bala.

## \|\| 9.36.45 \|\|

*tasya putraśataṃ tvāsīddhārmikasya mahāprabho \|*  
*kṛṣṇasyāpi sutā jātā bahustrībhyo'mitāstataḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. Hundred sons were born to that virtuous great lord. Through many wives Kṛṣṇa too had innumerable sons.

## \|\| 9.36.46 \|\|

*anvavāyo mahāṃstatra dvayorapi mahātmanoḥ \|*  
*kṣatriyā dikṣu sarvāsu gatā hṛṣṭāssudhārmikāḥ \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. The family of the two noble souls was very great. All the Kṣatriyas in every quarter became delighted and virtuous.

## \|\| 9.36.47 \|\|

*iti prokto hi śaryātervaṃśo'nyeṣāṃ vadāmyaham \|*  
*mānavānāṃ hi saṃkṣepācchṛṇutādarato dvijāḥ \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. Thus the race of Śaryāti has been narrated to you. O brahmins, I shall succinctly mention the details of others too. Listen attentively.

## \|\| 9.36.48 \|\|

*nābhāgo diṣṭaputro'bhūtsa tu brāhmaṇatāṃ gataḥ \|*  
*svakṣatravaṃśaṃ saṃsthāpya brahmakarmabhirāvṛtaḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. Nābhāga had a son Dhṛṣṭa. After establishing the Kṣatriya race and performing the brahminical rites he attained brahminhood.

## \|\| 9.36.49 \|\|

*dhṛṣṭāddhārṣṭamabhūtkṣatraṃ brahmabhūyaṃ gataṃ kṣitau \|*  
*karūṣasya tu kārūṣāḥ kṣatriyā yuddhadurmadāḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. Dhṛṣṭa’s race originally Kṣatriya became brahmin family on the earth. Karūṣa’s descendants the Kārūṣas were Kṣatriyas, insubjugable in war.

## \|\| 9.36.50 \|\|

*nṛgo yo manuputrastu mahādātā viśeṣataḥ \|*  
*nānāvasūnāṃ suprītyā viprebhyaśca gavāṃ tathā \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. Nṛga who was also a son of Manu was a liberal donor of riches to brahmins and of cows.

## \|\| 9.36.51 \|\|

*godātavyatyayādyastu svakubuddhyā svapāpataḥ \|*  
*kṛkalāsatvamāpannaḥ śrīkṛṣṇena samuddhṛtaḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

51\. Once due to an error in his charitable gift of a cow, prompted by his own sins and crooked intellect, he became a chameleon and was redeemed by Śrīkṛṣṇa.

## \|\| 9.36.52 \|\|

*tasyekobhūtsutaḥ śreṣṭhaḥ prayātirdharmavittathā \|*  
*iti śrutaṃ mayā vyāsāttatproktaṃ hi samāsataḥ \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. An excellent son was born to him named Prayāti, the knower of virtues. This I have heard from Vyāsa and have now briefly mentioned to you.

## \|\| 9.36.53 \|\|

*vṛṣaghnastu manoḥ putro gopālo guruṇā kṛtaḥ \|*  
*pālayāmāsa gā yatto rātryāṃ vīrāsanavrataḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. Pṛṣadhra, a son of Manu was made the keeper of cows by his preceptor. During the nights, observing the posture of heroes, he diligently looked after the cows.

## \|\| 9.36.54 \|\|

*sa ekadā''gataṃ goṣṭhe vyāghraṃ gā hiṃsituṃ balī \|*  
*śrutvā gokadanaṃ buddho haṃtuṃ taṃ khaḍgadhṛgyayau \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. Once a tiger entered the cowshed to attack the cows. On hearing the shrieks of the cows he woke up. Strong that he was he seized a sword to kill it and started.

## \|\| 9.36.55 \|\|

*ajānannahanadbabhrośśiraśśārdūlaśaṃkayā \|*  
*niścakrāma sabhīrvyāghro dṛṣṭvā taṃ khaḍginaṃ prabhum \|\| 55 \|\|*

55\. On seeing the lord armed with a sword the frightened tiger slipped away. Not knowing it and mistaking a reddish brown cow as the tiger he hit it with his sword on its head.

## \|\| 9.36.56 \|\|

*manyamāno hataṃ vyāghraṃ svasthānaṃ sa jagāma ha \|*  
*rātryāṃ tasyāṃ bhramāpanno varṣavātavinaṣṭadhīḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

56\. Due to rain and gust of wind in the night he was deluded. Thinking, that the tiger had been killed he returned to his place.

## \|\| 9.36.57 \|\|

*vyuṣṭāyāṃ niśi cotthāya prage tatra gato hi saḥ \|*  
*adrākṣītsa hatāṃ babhruṃ na vyāghraṃ duḥkhito'bhavat \|\| 57 \|\|*

57\. When the night dawned into day he got up and went there. He saw only the cow killed and not the tiger. He felt dejected.

## \|\| 9.36.58 \|\|

*śrutvā tadvṛttamājñāya taṃ śaśāpa kṛtāgasam \|*  
*akāmatovicāryyeti śūdro bhava na kṣatriyaḥ \|\| 58 \|\|*

58\. On hearing about the incident and realising that he had committed a sin unconsciously and not wilfully, the preceptor cursed him—“Become a Śūdra, not a Kṣatriya.”

## \|\| 9.36.59 \|\|

*evaṃ śaptastu guruṇā kulācāryyeṇa kopataḥ \|*  
*nissṛtaśca pṛṣadhrastu jagāma vipinaṃ mahat \|\| 59 \|\|*

59\. On being cursed by his preceptor, the family priest out of anger, Pṛṣadhra started from there and went to the great forest.

## \|\| 9.36.60 \|\|

*nirviṇṇaḥ sa tu kaṣṭena virakto'bhūtsa yogavān \|*  
*vanāgnau dagdhadehaśca jagāma paramāṃ gatim \|\| 60 \|\|*

60\. Dejected by sufferings he became detached and practised Yoga. He burnt himself in conflagration and attained the greatest goal.

## \|\| 9.36.61 \|\|

*kaviḥ putro manoḥ prājñaśśivānugrahato'bhavat \|*  
*bhuktvā sukhaṃ divyaṃ muktiṃ prāpa sudurlabhām \|\| 61 \|\|*

61\. Kavi, son of Manu, was highly intelligent due to Śiva’s blessings. After enjoying divine pleasures here he attained the rare salvation hereafter.

Notes on the nine sons of Vaivasvata Manu:

There is no consensus of opinion in the Purāṇas about the names. Pargiter who collated the Puranic texts on the topic suggested the following names Ikṣvāku, Nābhāga, Dhṛṣṭa, Śaryāti, Nariṣyanta, Karūṣa, Prāṃśu, Nābhānediṣṭa and Pṛṣadhra. Śp agrees in respect of the first five but it substitutes Śibi, Nābhāga (Dvitīya) and Priyavrata Prāṃśu for Nābhānediṣṭa and Pṛṣaghra (?). Probably the second Nābnhāga is identical with Nābhanediṣṭa. The identification of Śibi and Priyavraya (?) with Prāṃśu Prṛṣadha (?) is doubtful.

## Chapter 37 - The race of Manu

## \|\| 9.37.1 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*pūrvatastu manorjajñe ikṣvākurghrāṇatassutaḥ \|*  
*tasya putraśataṃ tvāsīdikṣvākorbhūridakṣiṇam \|\| 1 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

1\. Formerly Ikṣvāku was born as the son of Manu through the nose. He had a hundred sons who were liberal in bestowing gifts.

## \|\| 9.37.2 \|\|

*teṣāṃ purastādabhavannāryyāvarte nṛpā dvijāḥ \|*  
*teṣāṃ vikukṣirjyeṣṭhastu so'yodhyāyāṃ nṛpo'bhavat \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. Before them O brahmins, there were no kings in Āryāvarta. Of them the eldest was Vikukṣi. He became the king of Ayodhyā.

## \|\| 9.37.3-4 \|\|

*tatkarma śṛṇu tatprītyā yajjātaṃ vaṃśato vidheḥ \|*  
*śrāddhakarmmaṇi coddiṣṭo hyakṛte śrāddhakarmaṇi \|\| 3 \|\|*

*bhakṣayitvā śaśaṃ śīghraṃ śaśādatvamato gataḥ \|*  
*ikṣvākuṇā parityaktaśśaśādo vanamāviśata \|\| 4 \|\|*

3-4. Listen to one of his misdeeds. He wanted to perform Śrāddha but before performing the same he ate a rabbit and came to be known as Śaśāda. Abandoned by Ikṣvāku, he entered a forest.

## \|\| 9.37.5 \|\|

*ikṣvākau saṃsthite rājā vasiṣṭhavacanādabhūt \|*  
*śakunipramukhāstasya putrāḥ pañcadaśa smṛtāḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. When Ikṣvāku passed away he was installed a king at the instance of Vasiṣṭha. He had fifteen sons of whom the chief was Śakuni.

## \|\| 9.37.6 \|\|

*uttarāpathadeśasya rakṣitāro mahīkṣitaḥ \|*  
*ayodhasya tu dāyādaḥ kakutstho nāma vīryyavān \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. They all became kings and protectors of the northern country. Ayodha’s son was the powerful king Kakutstha.

## \|\| 9.37.7 \|\|

*arinābhaḥ kakutsthasya pṛthuretasya vai sutaḥ \|*  
*viṣṭarāśvaḥ pṛthoḥ putrastasmādiṃdraḥ prajāpatiḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. Kakutstha’s son was Arinābha. His son was Pṛthu. Pṛthu’s son was Viṣṭarāśva. From him was born Indra the lord of people.

## \|\| 9.37.8 \|\|

*iṃdrasya yuvanāśvastu śrāvastasya prajāpatiḥ \|*  
*jajñe śrāvastakaḥ prājñaḥ śrāvastī yena nirmitā \|*  
*śrāvastasya tu dāyādo bṛhadaśvo mahāyaśāḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. Indra’s son was Yuvanāśva, the ruler of Śrāvasta. His son was Śrāvastaka who built Śrāvasti. Śrāvastaka’s son was Bṛhadaśva.

## \|\| 9.37.9 \|\|

*yuvanāśvassutastasya kuvalāśvaśca tatsutaḥ \|*  
*sa hi dhuṃdhuvadhādbhūto dhuṃdhumāro nṛpottamaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. His son was Yuvanāśva and Kuvalāśva was his son. Since he slew Dhundhu he became the excellent king Dhundhumāra.

## \|\| 9.37.10-11 \|\|

*kuvalāśvasya putrāṇāṃ śatamuttamadhanvinām \|*  
*babhūvātra pitā rājye kuvalāśvaṃ nyayojayat \|\| 10 \|\|*

*putrasaṃkrāmitaśrīko vanaṃ rājā samāviśat \|*  
*tamuttaṃko'tha rājarṣi prayāṃtaṃ pratyavārayat \|\| 11 \|\|*

10-11. Kuvalāśva had hundred sons who were excellent archers. He was entrusted with the kingdom by his father. After transferring the royal glory to the son the king entered the forest. But Uttaṅka prevented him.

## \|\| 9.37.12-14 \|\|

*uttaṃka uvāca \|*  
*bhavatā rakṣaṇaṃ kāryaṃ pṛthivyā dharmataḥ śṛṇu \|*  
*tvayā hi pṛthivī rājanrakṣyamāṇā mahātmanā \|\| 12 \|\|*

*bhaviṣyati nirudvignā nāraṇyaṃ gaṃtumarhasi \|*  
*mamāśramasamīpe tu himeṣu marudhanvasu \|\| 13 \|\|*

*samudravālukāpūrṇo dānavo baladarpitaḥ \|*  
*devatānāmavadhyo hi mahākāyo mahābalaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

Uttaṅka said:—

12-14. Listen. You shall protect the earth virtuously. O king, only when protected by you can the earth be relieved of excitement and sorrow. It does not behove you to go to the forest. There is a Dānava, proud of his strength near my hermitage, in the snow-covered wilderness, full of sea-sand. He is indestructible even to the gods. He has a huge body and is very strong.

## \|\| 9.37.15 \|\|

*aṃtarbhūbhigatastatra vālukāṃtarhitaḥ sthitaḥ \|*  
*rākṣasasya madhoḥ putro dhuṃdhunāmā sudāruṇaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. He stays underground concealed by the sand. The terrible Rākṣasa son of the demon Madhu is Dhundhu by name.

## \|\| 9.37.16 \|\|

*śete lokavināśāya tapa āsthāya dāruṇam \|*  
*saṃvatsarasya paryante sa niśvāsaṃ vimuṃcati \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. He stays there performing a terrible penance for the destruction of the worlds. At the end of every year he exhales terribly.

## \|\| 9.37.17 \|\|

*yadā tadā bhūścalati saśailavanakānanā \|*  
*savisphuliṃgaṃ sāṃgāraṃ sadhūmamapi vāruṇam \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. When he exhales, the whole world including mountains, forests and wilderness, quakes. Blazing flames with pink smoke smoulder everywhere.

## \|\| 9.37.18 \|\|

*tena rāyanna śaknomi tasmiṃssthātuṃ sva āśrame \|*  
*taṃ vāraya mahābāho lokānāṃ hitakāmyayā \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. Hence, O king, I cannot stay in my hermitage. O strong one with huge arms ward him off desiring the welfare of the worlds.

## \|\| 9.37.19 \|\|

*lokāssvasthā bhavaṃtvadya tasminvinihate tvayā \|*  
*tvaṃ hi tasya vadhāyaiva samarthaḥ pṛthavīpate \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. Let the worlds become happy and peaceful after he had been killed by you. O lord of earth, you alone can slay him.

## \|\| 9.37.20 \|\|

*viṣṇunā ca varo datto mahānpūrva yuge'nagha \|*  
*tejasā svena te viṣṇusteja āpyāyayiṣyati \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. O sinless one, a great boon has been granted to you. Viṣṇu will heighten your splendour by his own splendour.

## \|\| 9.37.21 \|\|

*pālane hi mahādharmaḥ prajānāmiha dṛśyate \|*  
*na tathā dṛśyate'raṇye mā te'bhūdbuddhirīdṛśī \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. Great virtue accrues from the protection of the subjects. A similar opportunity is wanting in the forest. Let not your mind be directed that way.

## \|\| 9.37.22 \|\|

*īdṛśo nahi rājendra kvaciddharmaḥ pravidyate \|*  
*prajānāṃ pālane yādṛk purā rājarṣibhiḥ kṛtaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. O leading king, nowhere such a virtue exists as it does in the protection of the subjects. This has been pursued by the saintly kings of yore.

## \|\| 9.37.23 \|\|

*sa evamukto rājarṣiruttaṃkena mahātmanā \|*  
*kuvalāśvaḥ sutaṃ prādāttasmai dhundhunivāraṇe \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. Thus requested the saintly king entrusted his son Kuvalāśva with the task of thwarting Dhundhu.

## \|\| 9.37.24 \|\|

*bhagavannyastaśastrohamayaṃ tu tanayo mama \|*  
*bhaviṣyati dvijaśreṣṭha dhundhumāro na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. “O holy lord, O excellent brahmin, I have already laid aside my weapons. Here is my son who will surely destroy Dhundhu.”

## \|\| 9.37.25 \|\|

*ityuktvā putramādiśya yayau sa tapase nṛpaḥ \|*  
*kuvalāśvaśca sottaṅko yayau dhundhuvinigrahe \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. Having said thus and instructed his son the king proceeded with penance. Kuvalāśva accompanied by Uttaṅka went to fight with Dhundhu.

## \|\| 9.37.26 \|\|

*tamāviśattadā viṣṇurbhagavāṃstejasā prabhuḥ \|*  
*uttaṃkasya niyogādvai lokānāṃ hitakāmyayā \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. At the approach of Uttaṅka and for the benefit of the worlds, lord Viṣṇu entered him with his splendour.

## \|\| 9.37.27 \|\|

*tasminprayāte durddharṣe divi śabdo mahānabhūt \|*  
*eṣa śrīmānnṛpasuto dhundhumāro bhaviṣyati \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. When the invincible Kuvalāśva started there was a loud shout in the heaven. “This glorious prince will slay Dhundhu.”

## \|\| 9.37.28 \|\|

*divyairmālyaiśca taṃ devāssamaṃtātsamavārayan \|*  
*praśaṃsāṃ cakrire tasya jaya jīveti vādinaḥ \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. The gods surrounded him with garlands of flowers. They praised him saying “Be victorious, Be long-lived.”

## \|\| 9.37.29 \|\|

*sa gatvā jayatāṃ śreṣṭhastanayaissaha pārthivaḥ \|*  
*samudraṃ khanayāmāsa vālukārṇavamadhyataḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. The most excellent among the victorious, the king went there accompanied by his sons. He caused an ocean to be dug in the midst of that vast expanse of sand.

## \|\| 9.37.30 \|\|

*nārāyaṇasya viprarṣestejasāpyāyitastu saḥ \|*  
*babhūva sumahātejā bhūyo balasamanvitaḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. O brahminical sage, heightened in strength by the splendour of Viṣṇu he became very brilliant and stronger.

## \|\| 9.37.31 \|\|

*tasya putraiḥ khanadbhistu vālukāṃtargatastu saḥ \|*  
*dhundhurāsādito brahmandiśamāśritya paści mām \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. O brahmin, the demon Dhundhu was found out, concealed beneath the sand towards the western quarter as the sons of the king dug up the place.

## \|\| 9.37.32 \|\|

*mukhajenāgninā krodhāllokānsaṃvartayanniva \|*  
*vāri susrāva vegena vidhoḥ kadhirivodaye \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. He appeared to consume all the worlds out of fury in the fire emerging from his mouth. Water too gushed out from him as from the moon-stone at the moonrise.

## \|\| 9.37.33 \|\|

*tato'nalairabhihataṃ dagdhaṃ putraśataṃ hi tat \|*  
*traya evāvaśiṣṭāśca teṣu madhye munīśvara \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. The hundred sons were scorched and burnt in the fire. O great sage, among them only three survived.

## \|\| 9.37.34 \|\|

*tatassa rājā viprendra rākṣasaṃ taṃ mahābalam \|*  
*āsasāda mahātejā dhundhuṃ vipravināśanam \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. O leading brahmin, then the king of great splendour rushed at the very powerful Rākṣasa, the brahmin-slayer Dhundhu.

## \|\| 9.37.35 \|\|

*tasya vārimayaṃ vegamāpīya sa narādhipaḥ \|*  
*vahnibāṇena vahniṃ tu śamayāmāsa vāriṇā \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. The king quaffed off the gushing water through fiery arrows and quelled the fire through water.

## \|\| 9.37.36 \|\|

*taṃ nihatya mahākāyaṃ balenodakarākṣasam \|*  
*uttaṃkasyekṣayāmāsa kṛtaṃ karma narādhipaḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. After killing the aquatic demon of huge body with his strength, the king requested Uttaṅka to survey his work.

## \|\| 9.37.37 \|\|

*uttaṃkastu varaṃ prādāttasmai rājñe mahāmune \|*  
*adadaccākṣayaṃ vittaṃ śatrubhiścāparājayam \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. O great sage, Uttaṅka granted him boons. He gave him never-ending wealth and invincibility to enemies.

## \|\| 9.37.38 \|\|

*dharme matiṃ ca satataṃ svarge vāsaṃ tathākṣayam \|*  
*putrāṇāṃ cākṣayaṃ lokaṃ rakṣasā ye tu saṃhatāḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. He blessed him with interest in virtue, perpetual residence in the heaven and the imperishable world to his sons who were killed in the battle.

## \|\| 9.37.39 \|\|

*tasya putrāstrayaśśiṣṭāḥ dṛḍhāśvaḥ śreṣṭha ucyate \|*  
*haṃsāśvakapilāśvau ca kumārau tatkanīyasau \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. Three of his sons survived. The eldest of them was Dṛḍhāśva. Haṃsāśva and Kapilāśva were younger princes.

## \|\| 9.37.40 \|\|

*dhauṃdhumārirdṛḍhāśvo yo haryyaśvastasya cātmajaḥ \|*  
*haryaśvasya nikuṃbhobhūtputro dharmaratassadā \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. Haryaśva was the son of Dṛḍhāśva who was the son of Dhundhumāra. Nikumbha who was always engaged in sacred rites was the son of Haryaśva.

## \|\| 9.37.41 \|\|

*saṃhatāśvo nikuṃbhasya putro raṇaviśāradaḥ \|*  
*akṣāśvaśca kṛtāśvaśca saṃhatāśvasuto'bhavata \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. Saṃhatāśva an expert in war was the son of Nikumbha. Akṣāśva and Kṛtāśva were the sons of Saṃhatāśva.

## \|\| 9.37.42 \|\|

*tasya haimavatī kanyā satāṃ mānyā vṛṣadvatī \|*  
*vikhyātā triṣu lokeṣu putrastasyāḥ prasenajit \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. He had two daughters Haimavatī and Dṛṣadvatī honoured by the good. She was famous in the three worlds. Her son was Prasenajit.

## \|\| 9.37.43 \|\|

*lebhe prasenajidbhāryāṃ gaurīṃ nāma pativratām \|*  
*abhiśaptā tu sā bhartrā nadī sā bāhudā kṛtā \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. Prasenajit had a chaste wife Gaurī. She was cursed by her husband and turned into the river Bāhudā.

## \|\| 9.37.44 \|\|

*tasya putro mahānāsīdyuvanāśvo mahīpatiḥ \|*  
*māṃdhātā yuvanāśvasya triṣu lokeṣu viśrutaḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. His great son was Yuvanāśva, the lord of the earth. Māndhātā famous in the three worlds was Yuvanāśva’s son.

## \|\| 9.37.45-46 \|\|

*tasya caitrarathī bhāryā śaśabiṃdusutā 'bhavat \|*  
*pativratā ca jyeṣṭhā ca bhrātṝṇāmayutaṃ ca saḥ \|\| 45 \|\|*

*tasyāmutpādayāmāsa māndhātā dvau sutau tadā \|*  
*purukutsaṃ ca dharmajñaṃ mucukuṃdaṃ ca dhārmikam \|\| 46 \|\|*

45-46. Śaśabindu’s daughter the chaste Caitrarathī was his wife. She was the eldest sister of ten thousand brothers. Māndhātā begot of her two sons, Purukutsa, the knower of sacred rites and Mucukunda the righteous.

## \|\| 9.37.47 \|\|

*purukutsasutastvāsīdvidvāṃstrayyāruṇiḥ kaviḥ \|*  
*tasya satyavrato nāma kumāro'bhūnmahābalī \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. Purukutsa’s son was Trayyāruṇi. His son was Satyavrata.

## \|\| 9.37.48-50 \|\|

*pāṇigrahaṇamaṃtrāṇāṃ vighraṃ cakre mahātmabhiḥ \|*  
*yena bhāryyā hṛtā pūrvaṃ kṛtodvāhaḥ parasya vai \|\| 48 \|\|*

*balātkāmācca mohācca saṃharṣācca yadotkaṭāt \|*  
*jahāra kanyāṃ kāmācca kasyacitpuravāsinaḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

*adharmasaṃginaṃ taṃ tu rājā trayyāruṇistyajan \|*  
*apadhvaṃseti bahuśo'vadatkrodhasamanvitaḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

48-50. He was evil-minded. Whenever sacred mantras were recited he put obstacles. After the marriage was celebrated he abducted the brides of others with force, out of lust, delusion, fun or arrogance. He abducted the virgins to satisfy his lust. The king Trayyāruṇi forsook him for such evil practices. Infuriated he called him a disgraceful wretch.

## \|\| 9.37.51 \|\|

*pitaraṃ so'bravīnmuktaḥ kva gacchāmīti vai tadā \|*  
*vasa śvapākanikaṭe rājā prāheti taṃ tadā \|\| 51 \|\|*

51\. When cast-off he asked his father where to go. The king asked him to stay with the outcastes.

## \|\| 9.37.52 \|\|

*sa hi satyavratastena śvapākavasathāṃtike \|*  
*pitrā tyakto'vasadvīro dharmapālena bhūbhujā \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. Cast off by his father the righteous king and protector, the heroic Satyavrata lived with the outcastes.

## \|\| 9.37.53 \|\|

*tatastrayyāruṇī rājā viraktaḥ putrakarmaṇā \|*  
*sa śaṃkaratapaḥ karttuṃ sarvaṃ tyaktvā vanaṃ yayau \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. Becoming detached due to the activities of his son, the king Trayyāruṇi forsook everything and went to the forest in order to perform penance to propitiate Śiva.

## \|\| 9.37.54 \|\|

*tatastasya sva viṣaye nāvarṣatpākaśāsanaḥ \|*  
*samā dvādaśa viprarṣe tenādharmeṇa vai tadā \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. O brahmin sage, due to that sinful misdeed Indra did not rain in his kingdom for twelve years.

## \|\| 9.37.55 \|\|

*dārāṃ tasya tu viṣaye viśvāmitro mahātapāḥ \|*  
*saṃtyajya sāgarānūpe cacāra vipulaṃ tapaḥ \|\| 55 \|\|*

55\. Viśvāmitra of great penance abandoned his wife in that land and performed extensive penance in the marshy foreshore of the ocean.

## \|\| 9.37.56 \|\|

*tasya patnī gale baddhvā madhyamaṃ putramaurasam \|*  
*śeṣasya bharaṇārthāya vyakrīṇādgośatena ca \|\| 56 \|\|*

56\. His wife tied her middle son round her neck and offered him for sale in exchange for a hundred cows in order to sustain the other children.

## \|\| 9.37.57 \|\|

*tāṃ tu dṛṣṭvā gale baddhaṃ vikrīṇaṃtī svamātmajam \|*  
*maharṣiputraṃ dharmmātmā mocayāmāsa taṃ tadā \|\| 57 \|\|*

57\. On seeing her offering her own son, tied round her neck, for sale, Satyavrata released him.

## \|\| 9.37.58 \|\|

*satyavrato mahābāhurbharaṇaṃ tasya cākarot \|*  
*viśvāmitrasya tuṣṭyarthamanukrośārthameva ca \|\| 58 \|\|*

58\. The mighty Satyabrata sustained him just to satisfy Viśvāmitra and out of human sympathy.

## \|\| 9.37.59 \|\|

*tadārabhya sa putrastu viśvāmitrasya vai muneḥ \|*  
*abhavadgālavo nāma galabaṃdhānmahātapāḥ \|\| 59 \|\|*

59\. Ever since then, that son of sage Viśvāmitra came to be called Gālava because he was tied round the neck. He too performed great penance.

## Chapter 38 - From Satyavrata to Sagara

## \|\| 9.38.1 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*satyavratastu tadbhaktyā kṛpayā ca pratijñayā \|*  
*viśvāmitrakalatraṃ ca poṣayāmāsa vai tadā \|\| 1 \|\|*

1\. By his devotion to Viśvāmitra his compassionate nature and his vow, Satyavrata nurtured and looked after Viśvāmitra’s wife.

## \|\| 9.38.2 \|\|

*hatvā mṛgānvarāhāṃśca mahiṣāṃśca vanecarān \|*  
*viśvāmitrāśramābhyāśe tanmāṃsaṃ cākṣipanmune \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. O sage, he killed deer, boars and buffaloes of the forest and he cast off their flesh near the hermitage of Viśvāmitra.

## \|\| 9.38.3 \|\|

*tīrthaṃ gāṃ caiva rātraṃ ca tathaivāṃtaḥpuraṃ muniḥ \|*  
*yājyopādhyāyasaṃyogādvasiṣṭhaḥ paryyarakṣata \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. In virtue of his being the priest and the teacher the sage Vasiṣṭha looked after the holy centres, cows, clans and the harem.

## \|\| 9.38.4 \|\|

*satyavratasya vākyādvā bhāvinorthasya vai balāt \|*  
*vasiṣṭho'bhyadhikaṃ manyuṃ dhārayāmāsa nityaśaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. Vasiṣṭha nursed more and more grudge against Satyavrata forced by the gravity of what was destined to happen.

## \|\| 9.38.5 \|\|

*pitrā tu taṃ tadā rāṣṭrātparityaktaṃ svamātmajam \|*  
*na vārayāmāsa munirvasiṣṭhaḥ kāraṇena ca \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Vasiṣṭha had not prevented the father from exiling the son from the kingdom because there was sufficient cause for the same.

## \|\| 9.38.6 \|\|

*pāṇigrahaṇamaṃtrāṇāṃ niṣṭhā syātsaptame pade \|*  
*na ca satyavratasthasya tamupāṃśumabuddhyata \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. When the seventh step is taken (round the fire in the altar) the marriage becomes valid. But Satyavrata did not understand this secret.

## \|\| 9.38.7 \|\|

*tasminsa paritoṣāya piturāsīnmahātmanaḥ \|*  
*kulasya niṣkṛtiṃ vipra kṛtavānvai bhavediti \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. Only for the satisfaction of his father did he do the same thinking that the family customs should remain unviolated.

## \|\| 9.38.8 \|\|

*na taṃ vasiṣṭho bhagavānpitrā tyaktaṃ nyavārayat \|*  
*abhiṣekṣyāmyahaṃ putramasyāṃ naivābravīnmuniḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. When he was abandoned by his father Vasiṣṭha did not interfere on his behalf. The sage proclaimed that he would never install him in this kingdom.

## \|\| 9.38.9-12 \|\|

*sa tu dvādaśa varṣāṇi dīkṣāṃ tāmudvahadbalī \|*  
*avidyāmāne māṃse tu vasiṣṭhasya mahātmanaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

*sarvakāmaduhāṃ dogdhrīṃ dadarśa sa nṛpātmajaḥ \|*  
*tāṃ vai krodhācca lobhācca śramādvai ca kṣudhānvitaḥ \|\| 10 \|\|*

*dāśadharmagato rājā tāṃ jaghāna sa vai mune \|*  
*sa taṃ māṃsaṃ svayaṃ caiva viśvāmitrasya cātmajam \|\| 11 \|\|*

*bhojayāmāsa tacchrutvā vasiṣṭho hyasya cukrudhe \|*  
*uvāca ca muniśreṣṭhastaṃ tadā krodhasaṃyutaḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

9-12. During the twelve years of famine and drought Satyavrata observed his vow. When there was no meat available the prince saw the wish-yielding cow of Vasiṣṭha. O sage, the king who observed the ten tenets of Dharma killed the cow either due to anger or greed or exhaustion or hunger. The meat of the same he utilised to feed Viśvāmitra’s son. On hearing of it the sage Vasiṣṭha became angry and said.

Vasiṣṭha said

## \|\| 9.38.13 \|\|

*vasiṣṭha uvāca \|*  
*pātayeyamahaṃ krūraṃ tava śaṃkumayomayam \|*  
*yadi te dvāvimau śaṃkū naśyetāṃ vai kṛtau purā \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. If the two iron stakes thrust by me fail I shall fix another cruel one.

## \|\| 9.38.14 \|\|

*pituścāparitoṣeṇa gurordogdhrīvadhena ca \|*  
*aprokṣitopayogācca trividhaste vyatikramaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. Your transgression is threefold. You have displeased your father, killed the cow of your preceptor and used things unsprinkled with holy water.

## \|\| 9.38.15-16 \|\|

*triśaṃkuriti hovāca triśaṃkuriti sa smṛtaḥ \|*  
*viśvāmitrastu dārāṇāmāgato bharaṇe kṛte \|\| 15 \|\|*

*tena tasmai varaṃ prādānmuniḥ prītastriśaṃkave \|*  
*chandyamāno vareṇātha varaṃ vavre nṛpātmajaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

15-16. He called him Triśaṅku and ever since he is known as Triśaṅku. When Viśvāmitra returned he was pleased with Triśaṅku for having maintained his family during his absence. When pressed to choose a boon the prince chose it.

## \|\| 9.38.17 \|\|

*anāvṛṣṭibhaye cāsmiñjāte dvādaśavārṣike \|*  
*abhiṣicya pitṛ rājye yājayāmāsa taṃ muniḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. When there was drought for twelve years he had helped his family, therefore the sage anointed him in the kingdom of his father and officiated as priest in his coronation.

## \|\| 9.38.18 \|\|

*miṣatāṃ devatānāṃ ca vasiṣṭhasya ca kauśikaḥ \|*  
*saśarīraṃ tadā taṃ tu divamāroha yatprabhuḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. Ever as the gods and Vasiṣṭha were watching, the saintly lord Viśvāmitra made him ascend heaven in his physical body.

## \|\| 9.38.19 \|\|

*tasya satyarathā nāma bhāryā kekayavaṃśajā \|*  
*kumāraṃ janayāmāsa hariścandramakalmaṣam \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. His wife Satyarathā, hailing from the family of Kekayas bore him a son who was named Hariścandra.

## \|\| 9.38.20 \|\|

*sa vai rājā hariścandro traiśaṃkava iti smṛtaḥ \|*  
*āhartā rājasūyasya samrāḍiti ha viśrutaḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. That king Hariścandra is known as Traiśaṅkava. He is famous as the performer of the Rājasūya sacrifice and as an Emperor.

## \|\| 9.38.21 \|\|

*hariścandrasya hi suto rohito nāma viśrutaḥ \|*  
*rohitasya vṛkaḥ putro vṛkādbāhustu jajñivān \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. Hariścandra’s son Rohita was famous. Rohita’s son was Vṛka and Bāhu was born of Tṛka.

## \|\| 9.38.22 \|\|

*haihayāstālajaṃghāśca nirasyaṃti sma taṃ nṛpam \|*  
*nātmārthe dhārmiko vipraḥ sa hi dharmaparo'bhavata \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. Haihayas and Tālajaṅghas removed that king. O brahmin, he was very virtuous.

## \|\| 9.38.23 \|\|

*sagaraṃ sasutaṃ bāhurjajñe saha gareṇa vai \|*  
*aurvasyāśramamāsādya bhārgaveṇābhirakṣitaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. Bāhu begot a son. Sagara was born with poison. Reaching the hermitage of Aurva, he was saved by Bhārgava.

## \|\| 9.38.24 \|\|

*āgneyamastraṃ labdhvā ca bhārgavātsagaro nṛpaḥ \|*  
*jigāya pṛthivīṃ hatvā tālajaṃghānsahaihayāna \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. Securing fiery missiles from Bhārgava king Sagara conquered the earth after killing Tālajaṅghas\[3\] and Haihayas.

## \|\| 9.38.25 \|\|

*śakānbahūdakāṃścaiva pāradāṃtagaṇānkhaśān \|*  
*sudharmaṃ sthāpayāmāsa śaśāsa vṛṣataḥ kṣitim \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. He defeated Śakas,\[5\] Bahūdakas, Pāradas\[6\], Tagaṇas\[7\] and Khaśas. He established a good religious cult and ruled over the earth virtuously.

Śaunaka Said:—

## \|\| 9.38.26 \|\|

*śaunaka uvāca \|*  
*sa vai gareṇa sahitaḥ kathaṃ jātastu kṣatriyāt \|*  
*jitavānetadācakṣva vistareṇa hi sūtaja \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. How was he born with poison? How did he conquer the Kṣatriyas? O son of Sūta, please narrate this in detail.

## \|\| 9.38.27 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*pārīkṣitena saṃpṛṣṭo vaiśaṃpāyana eva ca \|*  
*yadācaṣṭa sma tadvakṣye śṛṇuṣvaikamanā mune \|\| 27 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

27\. O sage, listen with attention. I shall narrate what Vaiśampāyana said on being asked by Janamejaya, son of Pārīkṣita.

## \|\| 9.38.28 \|\|

*\|\| pārīkṣito uvāca \|*  
*kathaṃ sa sagaro rājā gareṇa sahito mune \|*  
*jātassa jaghnivānbhūyānetadākhyātumarhasi \|\| 28 \|\|*

Pārīkṣita said:—

28\. O sage, how was the king born with poison? How did he kill the kings? Please narrate this.

## \|\| 9.38.29 \|\|

*vaiśampāyana uvāca \|*  
*bāhorvyasaninastāta hṛtaṃ rājyamabhūtkila \|*  
*haihayaistālajaṃghaiśca śakaissārddhaṃ viśāṃpate \|\| 29 \|\|*

Vaiśampāyana said:—

29\. O dear, O lord of the subjects, the kingdom of Bāhu who indulged in vices was captured by Haihayas and Tālajaṅghas and the Śakas.

## \|\| 9.38.30 \|\|

*yavanāḥ pāradāścaiva kāmbojāḥ pāhnavāstathā \|*  
*bahūdakāśca paṃcaiva gaṇāḥ proktāśca rakṣasām \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. Five groups of Rākṣasas are mentioned, viz.—Yavanas,\[9\] Pāradas,\[10\] Kāmbojas,\[11\] Pahlavas\[12\] and Bahūdakas.

## \|\| 9.38.31 \|\|

*ete paṃca gaṇā rājanhaihayārtheṣu rakṣasām \|*  
*kṛtvā parākramān bāho rājyaṃ tebhyo dadurbalāt \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. O king, these five groups of Rākṣasas pursuing activities of exploit on behalf of the Haihayas seized the kingdom of Bāhu and gave it to the Haihayas.

## \|\| 9.38.32 \|\|

*hṛtarājyastato viprāḥ sa vai bāhurvanaṃ yayau \|*  
*patnyā cānugato duḥkhī sa vai prāṇānavāsṛjat \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. Having lost the kingdom, Bāhu went to the forest along with his wife. Distressed that he was he abandoned his life.

## \|\| 9.38.33 \|\|

*patnī yā yādavī tasya sagarbhā pṛṣṭhato gatā \|*  
*sapatnyā ca garastasyai dattaḥ pūrvaṃ suterṣyayā \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. One of his wives belonging to the house of Yadu followed him in pregnancy. Due to jealousy as a result of her expected son before her, the co-wife administered poison to her.

## \|\| 9.38.34 \|\|

*sā tu bhartuścitāṃ kṛtvā jvalanaṃ cāvarohata \|*  
*aurvastāṃ bhārgavo rājankāruṇyātsamavārayat \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. She made the funeral pyre of her husband ready and was about to enter the fire, Aurva Bhārgava mercifully prevented her.

## \|\| 9.38.35 \|\|

*tasyāśrame sthitā rājñī garbharakṣaṇahetave \|*  
*siṣeve munivaryaṃ taṃ smarantī śaṃkaraṃ hṛdā \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. The queen stayed in his hermitage for the sake of her child in the womb. She served the sage, mentally remembering Śiva.

## \|\| 9.38.36 \|\|

*ekadā khalu tadgarbho gareṇaiva saha cyutaḥ \|*  
*sumuhūrtte sulagne ca paṃcoccagrahasaṃyute \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. Once when the Muhūrta and the Lagna were good when the five planets were ascendant the child was born along with the poison administered to the queen.

## \|\| 9.38.37 \|\|

*tasmiṃllagne ca balini sarvathā munisattama \|*  
*vyajāyata mahābāhussagaro nāma pārthivaḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. In that auspicious Lagna, O excellent sage, the king Sagara, of mighty arms, was born.

## \|\| 9.38.38 \|\|

*aurvastu jātakarmādi tasya kṛtvā mahātmanaḥ \|*  
*adhyāpya vedaśāstrāṇi tato'straṃ pratyapādayat \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. Aurva performed the postnatal rites of that prince. He taught him Vedas and Śāstras and instructed him in the use of missiles.

## \|\| 9.38.39 \|\|

*āgneyaṃ taṃ mahābhāgo hyamarairapi dussaham \|*  
*jagrāha vidhinā prītyā sagarosau nṛpottamaḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. The blessed Sagara, earnestly learnt the lore of the fiery missile, in accordance with the rules of procedure, the missile that is unbearable even to the gods.

## \|\| 9.38.40 \|\|

*sa tenāstrabalenaiva balena ca samanvitaḥ \|*  
*haihayānvijaghānāśu saṃkuddho'strabalena ca \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. The infuriated Sagaras, equipped with this miraculous and other weapons and with his own natural strength, killed the Haihayas.

## \|\| 9.38.41 \|\|

*ājahāra ca lokeṣu kīrtiṃ kīrtimatāṃ varaḥ \|*  
*dharmaṃ saṃsthāpayāmāsa sagaro'sau mahītale \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. This Sagara became foremost among the famous, earned fame in all the worlds and established piety on the earth.

## \|\| 9.38.42 \|\|

*tataśśakāssayavanāḥ kāmbojāḥ pāhnavāstathā \|*  
*hanyamānāstadā te tu vasiṣṭhaṃ śaraṇaṃ yayuḥ \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. Then the Śakas, Yavanas, Kāmbojas and Pahlavas, being destroyed sought refuge in Vasiṣṭha.

## \|\| 9.38.43 \|\|

*vasiṣṭho vaṃcanāṃ kṛtvā samayena mahādyutiḥ \|*  
*sagaraṃ vārayāmāsa teṣāṃ dattvābhayaṃ nṛpam \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. After deceitfully compelling them to enter into an agreement, Vasiṣṭha of great brilliance offered them freedom from fear and brought them to king Sagara.

## \|\| 9.38.44 \|\|

*sagarassvāṃ pratijñāṃ tu gurorvākyaṃ niśamya ca \|*  
*dharmaṃ jaghāna teṣāṃ vai keśānyatvaṃ cakāra ha \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. At the instance of his priest Sagara maintained his vow by destroying their mode of worship and effecting alterations in their hair style.

## \|\| 9.38.45 \|\|

*arddhaṃ śakānāṃ śiraso muṃḍaṃ kṛtvā vyasarjayat \|*  
*yavanānāṃ śirassarvaṃ kāṃbojānāṃ tathaiva ca \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. He released the Śakas after shaving off half of their heads. Complete tonsure was assigned to Yavanas and Kambojas.

## \|\| 9.38.46 \|\|

*pāradā muṃḍakeśāśca pāhnavāśśmaśrudhāriṇaḥ \|*  
*nissvādhyāyavaṣaṭkārāḥ kṛtāstena mahātmanā \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. Pāradas were given close hair-cut and Pahlavas were asked to grow beard and moustache. All of them were deprived of the right of the Vedic study and the use of Vaṣaṭkāra.

## \|\| 9.38.47 \|\|

*jitā ca sakalā pṛthvī dharmatastena bhūbhujā \|*  
*sarve te kṣatriyāstāta dharmahīnāḥ kṛtāḥ purāḥ \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. All those Kṣatriyas who had been deprived of virtue were re-instated in piety. The entire earth was conquered by him virtuously.

## \|\| 9.38.48 \|\|

*sa dharmavijayī rājā vijitvemāṃ vasuṃdharām \|*  
*aśvaṃ saṃskārayāmāsa vājimedhāya pārthivaḥ \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. Thus conquering the earth virtuously the king instituted a horse-sacrifice.

## \|\| 9.38.49 \|\|

*tasya cāsyatesso'śvassamudre pūrvadakṣiṇe \|*  
*gataḥ ṣaṣṭisahasraistu tatputrairanvito mune \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. O sage, the sacrificial horse was let loose, followed by his sixty thousand sons. It reached the shore of the ocean in the south-eastern region.

## \|\| 9.38.50 \|\|

*devarājena śakreṇa so'śvo hi svārthasādhinā \|*  
*velāsamīpe'pahṛto bhūmiṃ caiva praveśitaḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. It was stealthily removed by Indra, king of the gods, for his selfish ends near the sea-shore and taken underground.

## \|\| 9.38.51 \|\|

*mahārājo'tha sagarastaddhayānveṣaṇāya ca \|*  
*sa taṃ deśaṃ tadā putraiḥ khānayāmāsa sarvataḥ \|\| 51 \|\|*

51\. In order to search out the horse, king Sagara caused the country around dug up through his sons.

## \|\| 9.38.52 \|\|

*āseduste tatastatra khanyamāne mahārṇave \|*  
*tamādipuruṣaṃ devaṃ kapilaṃ viśvarūpiṇam \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. While it was being dug near the ocean, they met the sage Kapila, the primordial Puruṣa of cosmic form.

## \|\| 9.38.53 \|\|

*tasya cakṣussamutthena vahninā pratibudhyataḥ \|*  
*dagdhāḥ ṣaṣṭisahasrāṇi catvārastvavaśeṣitāḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. As he woke up from trance all but four of the sixty thousand sons were burnt by the fire from his eyes.

## \|\| 9.38.54 \|\|

*harṣaketussuketuśca tathā dharmarathoparaḥ \|*  
*śūraḥ paṃcajanaścaiva tasya vaṃśakarā nṛpāḥ \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. The four who were spared were Harṣaketu, Suketu, Dharmaratha and Pañcajana. They became the kings establishing his line.

## \|\| 9.38.55 \|\|

*prādācca tasmai bhagavān hariḥ paṃcavarānsvayam \|*  
*vaṃśaṃ medhāṃ ca kīrtiñca samudraṃ tanayaṃ dhanam \|\| 55 \|\|*

55\. Lord Viṣṇu granted him five boons, viz. flourishing family, intelligence, fame, the ocean as son and wealth.

## \|\| 9.38.56 \|\|

*sāgaratvaṃ ca lebhe sa karmaṇā tasya tena vai \|*  
*taṃ cāśvamedhikaṃ so'śvaṃ samudrādupalabdhavān \|\| 56 \|\|*

56\. By that virtue he attained the fatherhood of ocean. He regained the sacrificial horse from the ocean.

## \|\| 9.38.57 \|\|

*ājahārāśvamedhānāṃ śataṃ sa tu mahāyaśāḥ \|*  
*īje śaṃbhuvibhūtīśca devatāstatra suvratāḥ \|\| 57 \|\|*

57\. He performed a hundred horse-sacrifices and became famous. He acquired wealth bestowed by Śiva. He propitiated the deities by performing the sacred rites.

## Chapter 39 - Kings of the solar race (sūryavaṃśa)

## \|\| 9.39.1 \|\|

*śaunaka uvāca \|*  
*sagarasyātmajā vīrāḥ kathaṃ jātā mahābalāḥ \|*  
*vikrāṃtāḥ ṣaṣṭisāhasrā vidhanā kena vā vada \|\| 1 \|\|*

Śaunaka said:—

1\. How were the heroic sons of Sagara born? By what means did the sixty thousand of them become valorous?

## \|\| 9.39.2 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*dve patnyo sagarasyāstāṃ tapasā dagdhakilviṣe \|*  
*aurvastayorvaraṃ prādāttoṣito munisattamaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

2\. Sagara had two wives. They had wiped off their sins by penance. When Aurva the sage was propitiated by them he granted them a boon.

## \|\| 9.39.3 \|\|

*ṣaṣṭiputrasahasrāṇi ekā vavre tarasvinām \|*  
*ekaṃ vaṃśakaraṃ tvekā yatheṣṭaṃ varaśālinī \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. One of them chose the boon of sixty thousand sons. The other chose one son who would maintain the line of succession in the race.

## \|\| 9.39.4 \|\|

*tatraivāgatya tāṃ labdhvā putrāñśūrānbahūṃstadā \|*  
*sā caiva suṣuve tumbaṃ bījapūrvaṃ pṛthakkṛtam \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. Having received the boon of heroic sons she gave birth to a big gourd of seeds which were separated.

## \|\| 9.39.5 \|\|

*te sarve hi svadhātrībhirvavṛdhuśca yathākramam \|*  
*ghṛtapūrṇeṣu kumbheṣu kumārāḥ prītivarddhanāḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. They were put in jars of ghee and nurtured by nursing maidens. They became princes and enhanced the delight of all.

## \|\| 9.39.6 \|\|

*kapilāgnipradagdhānāṃ teṣāṃ tatra mahātmanām \|*  
*ekaḥ paṃcajano nāma putro rājā babhūva ha \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. When they were burnt by Kapila one Pañcajana became king.

## \|\| 9.39.7 \|\|

*tataḥ paṃcajanasyāsīdaṃśumānnāma vīryavān \|*  
*dilīpastanayastasya putro yasya bhagīrathaḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. Pañcajana begot a son Aṃśumat whose son was Dilīpa. Dilīpa’s son was Bhagīratha.

## \|\| 9.39.8 \|\|

*yastu gaṃgā saricchreṣṭhāmavātārayataḥ prabhu \|*  
*samudramānayaccemāṃ duhitṛtvamakalpayat \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. It was this Bhagīratha who brought Gaṅgā to the sea and made her his daughter.

## \|\| 9.39.9 \|\|

*bhagīrathasuto rājā śrutasenaḥ iti śrutaḥ \|*  
*nābhāgastu sutastasya putraḥ paramadhārmikaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Bhagīratha’s son was Śrutasena. His son was the virtuous king Nābhāga.

## \|\| 9.39.10 \|\|

*aṃbarīṣastu nābhāgissiṃdhudvīpastato'bhavat \|*  
*ayutājittu dāyādassiṃdhudvīpasya vīryavān \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Ambarīṣa was the son of Nābhāga. Sindhudvīpa was his son. Ayutājit was the heir and successor to Sindhudvīpa.

## \|\| 9.39.11 \|\|

*āyutājitsutastvāsīdṛtuparṇo mahāyaśāḥ \|*  
*divyākṣahṛdayajño'sau rājā nalasakho'bhavat \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. His son was king Ṛtuparṇa, who knew the secret of the dice and was a friend of king Nala.

## \|\| 9.39.12 \|\|

*ṛtuparṇasutastvāsīdanuparṇo mahādyutiḥ \|*  
*tasya kalmāṣapādo vai nāmnā mitrasahastathā \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. Ṛtuparṇa’s son was Anuparṇa. His son was Mitrasaha who was known as Kalmāṣapāda also.

## \|\| 9.39.13 \|\|

*kalmāṣapādasya sutassarvakarmeti viśrutaḥ \|*  
*anaraṇyastu putro'bhūdviśrutassarvaśarmaṇaḥ \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. Kalmāṣapāda’s son was Sarvakarman. Anaraṇya was the son of Sarvakarman.

## \|\| 9.39.14 \|\|

*anaraṇyasuto rājā vidvānmuṃḍidruho'bhavat \|*  
*niṣadhastasya tanayo ratiḥ khaṭvāṅga ityapi \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. Anaraṇya’s son was king Muṇḍidruha. His sons were Niṣadha, Rati and Khaṭvāṅga.

## \|\| 9.39.15 \|\|

*yena svargādihāgatya muhūrtaṃ prāpya jīvitam \|*  
*trayo'pi saṃcitā lokā buddhyā satyena cānagha \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. Khaṭvāṅga returned from the heaven, lived for a Muhūrta and attained all the three worlds, O sinless one, through his intellect and truthfulness.

## \|\| 9.39.16 \|\|

*dīrghabāhussutastasya raghustasyābhavatsutaḥ \|*  
*ajastasya tu putro'bhūttasmāddaśaratho'bhavat \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. Dīrghabāhu was his son. Raghu was his son. Aja was his son and Daśaratha was born of him.

## \|\| 9.39.17 \|\|

*rāmo daśarathājjajñe dharmātmā yo mahāyaśāḥ \|*  
*sa viṣṇvaṃśo mahāśaivaḥ paulastyo yena ghātitaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. Rāma was born of Daśaratha. He was very pious and famous, a part of Viṣṇu and a devotee of Śiva. Rāvaṇa was killed by him.

## \|\| 9.39.18 \|\|

*taccaritaṃ ca bahudhā purāṇeṣu pravarṇitam \|*  
*rāmāyaṇe prasiddhaṃ hi nātaḥ proktaṃ tu vistarāt \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. His story has been described in the Purāṇas. It is famous in the Rāmāyaṇa. It has not been mentioned here in detail.

## \|\| 9.39.19 \|\|

*rāmasya tanayo jajñe kuśa ityapi viśrutaḥ \|*  
*atithistu kuśājjajñe niṣadhastasya cātmajaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. Rāma’s son Kuśa was very famous. Atithi was born of Kuśa. His son was Niṣadha.

## \|\| 9.39.20 \|\|

*niṣadhasya nalaḥ putro nabhāḥ putro nalasya tu \|*  
*nabhasaḥ puṃḍarīkaśca kṣemadhanvā tatasmṛtaḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. Nala was the son of Niṣadha. Nabhas was the son of Nala. Puṇḍarīka was the son of Nabhas. Kṣemadhanvan was bis successor.

## \|\| 9.39.21 \|\|

*kṣemadhanvasutastvāsīddevānīkaḥ pratāpavān \|*  
*āsīdahīnagurnāma devānīkātmajaḥ prabhuḥ \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. The powerful Devānīka was the son of Kṣemadhanvan. Devānīka’s son was the king Ahīnagu.

## \|\| 9.39.22 \|\|

*ahīnagostu dāyādassahasvānnāma vīryavān \|*  
*vīrasenātmajastasya yaścaikṣvākukulodbhavaḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. Ahīnagu’s heir was the powerful king Sahasvat. Vīrasena was his son, a scion of the family of Ikṣvāku.

## \|\| 9.39.23 \|\|

*vīrasenasya dāyādaḥ pāriyātro babhūva ha \|*  
*tato balākhyastanayassthalastasmādabhūtsutaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. Vīrasenā’s heir was Pāriyātra. From him a son Bala was born. Sthala was his son.

## \|\| 9.39.24 \|\|

*arkāṃśasaṃbhavastasmātputro yakṣaḥ pratāpavān \|*  
*tatsutastvaguṇastvāsīttasmādvidhṛtirātmajaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. His son was the valorous Yakṣa born of a part of the sun. His son was Aguṇa and from him was born Vidhṛti.

## \|\| 9.39.25 \|\|

*hiraṇyanābhastatputro yogācāryyo babhūva ha \|*  
*sa śiṣyo jaiminimunerhyātmavidyāviśāradaḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. His son Hiraṇyanābha was a great preceptor of Yogic science. He was the disciple of the sage Jaimini and an expert in the spiritual science.

## \|\| 9.39.26 \|\|

*kauśilyo yājñavalkyotha yogamadhyātmyasaṃjñakam \|*  
*yato'dhyagānnṛpavarāddhṛdayagraṃthibhedanam \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. It was from this great king that Kauśalya Yājñavalkya studied the spiritual science of Yoga that unrevels the knotty mesh of the heart.

## \|\| 9.39.27 \|\|

*tatsuto puṣpanāmā hi dhruvasaṃjñastadātmajaḥ \|*  
*agnivarṇassutastasya śīghranāmā sutastataḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. His son was Puṣyanāman. His son was Dhruva. Agnivarṇa was his son. His son was Śīghra.

## \|\| 9.39.28 \|\|

*marunnāmā sutastasya yogasiddho babhūva ha \|*  
*asāvāste'dyāpi prabhuḥ kalāpagrāmasaṃjñake \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. His son was Marut. He became a Siddha by means of Yoga. This king is alive even now in a village Kalāpa.

## \|\| 9.39.29 \|\|

*tadvāsibhiśca munibhiḥ kaleraṃte sa eva hi \|*  
*punarbhāvayitā naṣṭaṃ sūryavaṃśaṃ viśeṣataḥ \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. At the end of Kali he will revive the extinct solar race (sūryavaṃśa) along with the contemporary sages.

## \|\| 9.39.30 \|\|

*pṛthuśrutaśca tatputrassaṃdhistasya sutaḥ smṛtaḥ \|*  
*amarṣaṇassutastasya marutvāṃstatsuto'bhavat \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. Pṛthuśruta was his son. Sandhi was his son. Amarṣaṇa was his son and Marutvat was his son.

## \|\| 9.39.31 \|\|

*viśvasāhvassutastasya tatsuto' bhūtprasenajit \|*  
*takṣakastasya tanayastatsuto hi bṛhadbalaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. Viśvasa was his son. His son was Prasenajit. Takṣaka was his son and his son was Bṛhadbala.

## \|\| 9.39.32 \|\|

*eta ikṣvākuvaṃśīyā atītāḥ saṃprakīrtitāḥ \|*  
*śṛṇu tānāgatānbhūtāṃstadvaṃśyāndharmavittamān \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. These are the kings of Ikṣvāku family who had gone before. Now listen to the future kings of this family, the most excellent of those who know sacred virtue.

## \|\| 9.39.33 \|\|

*bṛhadbalasya tanayo bhavitā hi bṛhadraṇaḥ \|*  
*bṛhadraṇasutastasyorukriyo hi bhaviṣyati \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. Bṛhadbala’s son will be Bṛhadraṇa. Urukriya will be his son.

## \|\| 9.39.34 \|\|

*vatsavṛddhassutastasya prativyomasutastataḥ \|*  
*bhānustattanayo bhāvī divāko vāhinīpatiḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. His son will be Vatsavṛddha. His son will be Prativyoman. Bhānu will be his son whose son will be Divārka, lord of a vast army.

## \|\| 9.39.35 \|\|

*sahadevassutastasya mahāvīro bhavi ṣyati \|*  
*tatsuto bṛhadaśvo hi bhānumāṃstatsuto balī \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. His son will be a great hero, Sahadeva. His son will be Bṛhadaśva. The powerful Bhānumat will be his son.

## \|\| 9.39.36 \|\|

*suto bhānumato bhāvī pratīkāśvaśca vīryavān \|*  
*supratīkassutastasya bhaviṣyati nṛpottamaḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. The powerful Pratīkāśva will be the son of Bhānumat. King Supratīka will be his son.

## \|\| 9.39.37-41 \|\|

*marudevassutastasya sunakṣatro bhaviṣyati \|*  
*tatsutaḥ puṣkarastasyāṃtarikṣastatsuto dvijāḥ \|\| 37 \|\|*

*sutapāstatsuto vīro mitracittasya cātmajaḥ \|*  
*bṛhadbhājassutastasya barhināmā tadātmajaḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

*kṛtaṃjayassutastasya tatsuto hi raṇaṃjayaḥ \|*  
*saṃjayastu mayastasya tasya śākyo hi cātmajaḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

*śuddhodastanayastasya lāṃgalastu tadātmajaḥ \|*  
*tasya prasenajitputrastatsutaḥ śūdrakāhvayaḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

*ruṇako bhavitā tasya surathastatsutaḥ smṛtaḥ \|*  
*sumitrastatsuto bhāvī vaṃśaniṣṭhāṃta eva hi \|\| 41 \|\|*

37-41. Marudeva born of an auspicious star will be his son; his son will be Puṣkara; his son Antarikṣa; his son Sutapas; his son the heroic Mitrajit; his son Bṛhadbhāja and his son Barhināman; his son Kṛtañjaya; his son Raṇañjaya; his son Sañjaya; Sākya his son, Śuddhoda his son, Lāṅgala his son; his son Prasenajit; his son will be Śūdraka; Ruṇaka his son; Suratha his son and Sumitra his son. Thereafter the family becomes extinct.

## \|\| 9.39.42 \|\|

*sumitrāṃtonvayo'yaṃ vai ikṣvākūṇāṃ bhaviṣyati \|*  
*rājñāṃ vaicitravīryyāṇāṃ dharmmiṣṭhānāṃ sukarmmaṇām \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. The family of the Ikṣvākus will end with Sumitra, the family with kings of variegated exploits, sacred rites and virtuous practice.

## \|\| 9.39.43 \|\|

*sumitraṃ prāpya rājānaṃ tadvaṃśaśśubhaḥ kalau \|*  
*saṃsthāṃ prāpsyati tadbrāhme varddhiṣyati punaḥ kṛte \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. Reaching upto Sumitra the king in the Kali age that auspicious race will become merged in outside families. It will flourish again in the Kṛta age.

## \|\| 9.39.44 \|\|

*etadvaivasvate vaṃśe rājāno bhūridakṣiṇāḥ \|*  
*ikṣvākuvaṃśaprabhavāḥ prādhānyena prakīrtitāḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. These are the kings in the solar race (sūryavaṃśa) who made profuse monetary gifts. Those mainly mentioned belong to Ikṣvāku’s family.

## \|\| 9.39.45 \|\|

*puṇyeyaṃ paramā sṛṣṭirādityasya vivasvataḥ \|*  
*śrāddhadevasya devasya prajānāṃ puṣṭidasya ca \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. This is the auspicious creation of the sun Vivasvat, who is glorified as Śrāddhadeva, the bestower of nourishment to all living beings.

## \|\| 9.39.46 \|\|

*paṭhañchṛṇvannimāṃ sṛṣṭimādityasya ca mānavaḥ \|*  
*prajāvāneti sāyujyamiha bhuktvā sukhaṃ param \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. Reading and listening to this creation of Vivasvat, man attains Sāyujya after enjoying happiness here. He will be blessed with progeny.

## Chapter 40 - The power of the Manes (pitṛ)

## \|\| 9.40.1 \|\|

*vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*ityākarṇya śrāddhadevaḥ sūryānvayamanuttamam \|*  
*paryyapṛcchanmuniśreṣṭhaśśaunakassūtamādarāt \|\| 1 \|\|*

Vyāsa said:—

1\. On hearing about the excellent solar race of Śrāddhadeva, the sage Śaunaka respectfully asked Sūta.

Śaunaka said

## \|\| 9.40.2 \|\|

*śaunaka uvāca \|*  
*sūtasūta ciraṃjīva vyāsaśiṣya namostu te \|*  
*śrāvitā paramā divyā kathā paramapāvanī \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. O Sūta, disciple of Vyāsa, of long life, obeisance be to you. A divine and pious story has been narrated to us.

## \|\| 9.40.3 \|\|

*tvayā proktaḥ śrāddhadevassūryyaḥ sadvaṃśavarddhanaḥ \|*  
*saṃśayastatra me jātastaṃ bravīmi tvadagrataḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. It has been mentioned by you that Śrāddhadeva is the progenitor of the solar race. I have a doubt therein which I shall mention before you.

## \|\| 9.40.4 \|\|

*kuto vai śrāddhadevatvamādityasya vivasvataḥ \|*  
*śrotumicchāmi tatprītyā chiṃdhi me saṃśayaṃ tvimam \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. How did the sun become Śrāddhadeva? I wish to hear this. Please clear this doubt with pleasure.

## \|\| 9.40.5 \|\|

*śrāddhasyāpi ca māhātmyaṃ tatphalaṃ ca vada prabho \|*  
*prītāśca pitaro yena śreyasā yojayaṃti tam \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. O holy lord, please mention the glory and the benefit of Śrāddha rite whereby the manes are pleased and bless the performer with prosperity.

## \|\| 9.40.6 \|\|

*etacca śrotumicchāmi pitṝṇāṃ sargamuttamam \|*  
*kathaya tvaṃ viśeṣeṇa kṛpāṃ kuru mahāmate \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. I wish to hear this too, namely the creation par excellence of the manes (pitṛ). O intelligent one, please mention this particularly. Have mercy on me.

## \|\| 9.40.7-9 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*vacmi tatte'khilaṃ prītyā pitṛsargaṃ tu śaunaka \|*  
*mārkaṇḍeyena kathitaṃ bhīṣmāya paripṛcchate \|\| 7 \|\|*

*gītaṃ sanatkumāreṇa mārkaṇḍeya dhīmate \|*  
*tatte'haṃ saṃpravakṣyāmi sarvakāmaphalapadam \|\| 8 \|\|*

*yudhiṣṭhireṇa saṃpṛṣṭo bhīṣmo dharmabhṛtāṃ varaḥ \|*  
*śaraśayyāsthitaḥ proce tacchṛṇuṣva vadāmi te \|\| 9 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

7-9. O Śaunaka, I shall mention everything concerning the creation of the manes. This was narrated to the intelligent Mārkaṇḍeya by Sanatkumāra. I shall mention it to you. It bestows the benefit of all cherished desires. Bhīṣma the foremost of the virtuous lying on the bed of arrows was requested by Yudhiṣṭhira to whom he spoke thus.

## \|\| 9.40.10 \|\|

*yudhiṣṭhira uvāca \|*  
*puṣṭikāmena puṃsāṃ vai kathaṃ puṣṭiravāpyate \|*  
*etacchrotuṃ samicchāmi kiṃ kurvāṇo na sīdati \|\| 10 \|\|*

Yudhiṣṭhira said:—

10\. How is nourishment achieved by a man desirous of nourishment? I wish to hear this. What is it that makes a man free from distress?

## \|\| 9.40.11 \|\|

*\|\| sūta uvāca \|*  
*yudhiṣṭhireṇa saṃpṛṣṭaṃ praśnaṃ śrutvā sa dharmavit \|*  
*bhīṣmaḥ provāca suprītyā sarveṣāṃ śṛṇvatāṃ vacaḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

11\. On hearing the question of Yudhiṣṭhira, Bhīṣma the knower of Dharma spoke with pleasure even as every one was listening.

## \|\| 9.40.12 \|\|

*bhīṣma uvāca \|*  
*ye kurvaṃti narāśśrāddhānyapi prītyā yudhiṣṭhira \|*  
*śrāddhaiḥ prīṇāti tatsarvaṃ pitṝṇāṃ hi prasādataḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

Bhīṣma said:—

12\. O Yudhiṣṭhira, those who perform Śrāddha rites with pleasure delight everyone through them as a result of the favour of the manes.

J3-15. Men desirous of benefits perform Śrāddhas, keeping in view, the father, the grandfather and the greatgrandfather in three balls of rice. O Yudhiṣṭhira, ṃe manes bless the man desirous of piety with piety, desirous of progeny with progeny a ad desirous of nourishment with nourishment.

## \|\| 9.40.16 \|\|

*yudhiṣṭhira uvāca \|*  
*vartaṃte pitaraḥ svarge keṣāṃcinnarake punaḥ \|*  
*prāṇināṃ niyataṃ cāpi karmajaṃ phalamucyate \|\| 16 \|\|*

Yudhiṣṭhira said:—

16: Some manes are in the heaven and some are in the hell. The fruit accruing to the living beings is determined by their activity.

## \|\| 9.40.17 \|\|

*tāni śrāddhāni dattāni kathaṃ gacchanti vai pitṝn \|*  
*kathaṃ śaktāstamāharttuṃ narakasthā phalaṃ punaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. How do the Śrāddhas go to the manes? How those in the hell can derive the benefit?

## \|\| 9.40.18 \|\|

*devā api pitṝnsvarge yajaṃta iti me śrutam \|*  
*etadicchāmyahaṃ śrotuṃ vistareṇa bravīhi me \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. I have heard that even the gods worship the manes. I wish to hear this. Please mention this in detail.

## \|\| 9.40.19 \|\|

*bhīṣma uvāca \|*  
*atra te kīrtayiṣyāmi yathā śrutamarindama \|*  
*pitrā mama purā gītaṃ lokāntaragatena vai \|\| 19 \|\|*

Bhīṣma said:—

19\. O suppressor of enemies, in this context, I shall narrate an event as I have heard from my father who had left this world.

## \|\| 9.40.20 \|\|

*śrāddhakāle mama piturmayā piṃḍassamudyataḥ \|*  
*matpitā mama hastena bhittvā bhūmimayācata \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. At the time of Śrāddha, the ball of rice for my father was kept ready by me. My father split the ground and requested me to offer the same into his hand.

## \|\| 9.40.21 \|\|

*naiṣa kalpavidhirdṛṣṭa iti niścitya cāpyaham \|*  
*kuśeṣveva tataḥ piṃḍaṃ dattavānavicārayan \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. Thinking that it was not the procedure enjoined in the Kalpa texts I placed the Piṇḍa on the Kuśa grass without further thought.

## \|\| 9.40.22 \|\|

*tataḥ pitā me saṃtuṣṭo vācā madhurayā tadā \|*  
*uvāca bhārataśreṣṭha prīyamāṇo mayānagha \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. “O sinless one, O most excellent of the descendants of Bharata, gladdened by me my father spoke in sweet words.

## \|\| 9.40.23 \|\|

*tvayā dāyādavānasmi dharmajñena vipaścitā \|*  
*tāritohaṃ tu jijñāsā kṛtā me puruṣottama \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. O most excellent of men, I only tested you. I have been redeemed by you the knower of sacred rites and a good scholar. I have been blessed with a real heir in you.

## \|\| 9.40.24 \|\|

*pramāṇaṃ yaddhi kurute dharmācāreṇa pārthivaḥ \|*  
*prajāstadanu vartaṃte pramāṇācaritaṃ sadā \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. What the king does by virtuous or authoritative rites, the subjects too emulate, taking them to be backed by precedent.

## \|\| 9.40.25 \|\|

*śṛṇu tvaṃ bhārataśreṣṭha vedadharmāṃśca śāśvatān \|*  
*pramāṇaṃ vedadharmasya putra nirvarttitaṃ tvayā \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. O most excellent of the descendants of Bharata, listen to the eternal virtues of the Vedas. Proof of the Vedic rite has been shown by you-

## \|\| 9.40.26 \|\|

*tasmāttavāhaṃ suprītaḥ prītyā varamanuttamam \|*  
*dadāmi tvaṃ pratīkṣasva triṣu lokeṣu durlabham \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. Being delighted I shall grant you excellent boons lovingly. Accept them. They are difficult to obtain in the three worlds.

## \|\| 9.40.27 \|\|

*na te prabhavitā mṛtyuryāvajjīvitumicchasi \|*  
*tvattobhyanujñāṃ saṃprāpya mṛtyuḥ prabhavitā punaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. Death will have no hold on you as long as you wish to live. With your permission it may be effective again.

## \|\| 9.40.28 \|\|

*kiṃ vā te prārthitaṃ bhūyo dadāmi varamuttamam \|*  
*tad brūhi bharataśreṣṭha yatte manasi vartate \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. What is it that you wish for? I shall grant you an excellent boon. O foremost among the descendants of Bharata, what is in your mind? Mention it”.

## \|\| 9.40.29 \|\|

*ityuktavati tasmiṃstu abhivādya kṛtāñjaliḥ \|*  
*avocaṃ kṛtakṛtyo'haṃ prasanne tvayi mānada \|*  
*praśnaṃ pṛcchāmi vai kaṃcidvācyassa bhavatā svayam \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. When he had said this, I saluted him with palms joined in reverence and said—“O bestower of honour, if you are pleased, I am content. I ask you a question. Please answer it.

## \|\| 9.40.30 \|\|

*sa māmuvāca tad brūhi yadīcchasi dadāmi te \|*  
*ityuktetha mayā tatra pṛṣṭaḥ provāca tannṛpaḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. He said to me—“Tell me. If you wish I shall grant it to you”. When I asked him again the king told me.

## \|\| 9.40.31 \|\|

*śaṃtanuruvāca \|*  
*śṛṇu tāta pravakṣyāmi praśnaṃ te'haṃ yathārthataḥ \|*  
*pitṛkalpaṃ ca nikhilaṃ mārkaṇḍeyena me śrutam \|\| 31 \|\|*

Santanu said:—

31\. O dear, listen. I shall explain your question factually. The entire “Pitṛkalpa” has been heard by me from Mārkaṇḍeya.

## \|\| 9.40.32 \|\|

*yattvaṃ pṛcchasi māṃ tāta tadevāhaṃ mahāmunim \|*  
*mārkaṇḍeyamapṛcchaṃ hi sa māṃ provāca dharmavit \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. O dear, what you ask me now I had asked the sage Mārkaṇḍeya. He, the knower of sacred rites, replied to me.

## \|\| 9.40.33 \|\|

*mārkaṇḍeya uvāca \|*  
*śṛṇu rājanmayā dṛṣṭaṃ kadācitpaśyatā divam \|*  
*vimānaṃ mahādāyāṃtamantareṇa girestadā \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. O king listen. Once as I looked up into the sky, I saw a great aerial chariot coming over the mountain.

## \|\| 9.40.34-35 \|\|

*tasminvimāne paryakṣaṃ jvalitāṃgāravarcasam \|*  
*mahātejaḥ prajvalaṃtaṃ nirviśeṣaṃ manoharam \|\| 34 \|\|*

*apaśyaṃ caiva tatrāhaṃ śayānaṃ dīptatejasam \|*  
*aṃguṣṭhamātraṃ puruṣamagnāvagnimivāhitam \|\| 35 \|\|*

34-35. I saw in the aerial chariot a great blazing mass of splendour as brilliant as glowing coal and very pleasing to the mind having nothing to excel. it. I saw a man of the size of a thumb lying therein. He was very brilliant as if fire had been placed over fire.

## \|\| 9.40.36 \|\|

*so'haṃ tasmai namaḥ kṛtvā praṇamya śirasā prabhum \|*  
*apṛcchaṃ caiva tamahaṃ vidyāmastvāṃ kathaṃ vibho \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. I bowed to him. With my head bent down, I asked the holy lord. “O holy lord, how may we know you?”

## \|\| 9.40.37 \|\|

*māmuvāca dharmātmā tena tadvidyate tapaḥ \|*  
*yena tvaṃ budhyase māṃ hi mune vai brahmaṇassutam \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. The pious soul told me “you have not that penance and austerity, O sage, whereby you may know me, the son of Brahmā.

## \|\| 9.40.38 \|\|

*sanatkumāramiti māṃ viddhi kiṃ karavāṇi te \|*  
*ye tvanye brahmaṇaḥ putrāḥ kanīyāṃsastu te mama \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. I am Sanatkumāra. What can I do for you? The other sons of Brahmā are younger to me.

## \|\| 9.40.39 \|\|

*bhrātarassapta durdharṣā yeṣāṃ vaṃśāḥ pratiṣṭhitāḥ \|*  
*vayaṃ tu yatidharmāṇassaṃyamyātmānamātmani \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. My seven brothers are invincible and their families are established. But we pursue the rites of ascetics restraining the Ātman in the Ātman.

## \|\| 9.40.40 \|\|

*yathotpannastathaivāhaṃ kumāra iti viśrutaḥ \|*  
*tasmātsanatkumāraṃ me nāmaitatkathitaṃ mune \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. Even as I was born I became famous as Kumāra. O sage, hence I am called Sanatkumāra (the eternal bachelor).

## \|\| 9.40.41 \|\|

*yadbhaktyā te tapaścīrṇaṃ mama darśanakāṃkṣayā \|*  
*eṣa dṛṣṭo'smi bhadraṃ te kaṃ kāmaṃ karavāṇi te \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. Since you have piously performed penance with a desire for my vision I have appeared before you. Welfare be to you. What wish of yours shall I carry out?”

## \|\| 9.40.42 \|\|

*ityuktavantaṃ taṃ cāhaṃ prāvocaṃ tvaṃ śṛṇu prabho \|*  
*pitṝṇāmādisargaṃ ca kathayasva yathātatham \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. When he said this I told him—“O holy lord, listen. Please narrate factually the original creation of the Pitṛs.

## \|\| 9.40.43 \|\|

*ityuktassa tu māṃ prāha śṛṇu sarvaṃ yathātatham \|*  
*vacmi te tattvatastāta pitṛsargaṃ śubhāvaham \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. When requested thus he said to me. Listen to everything factually. O dear one, I shall explain to you the auspicious creation of the Pitṛs truthfully.

## \|\| 9.40.44 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*devānpurāsṛjadbrahmā māṃ yakṣadhvaṃ sa cāha tān \|*  
*tamutsṛjya tamātmānamayajaṃste phalārthinaḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

44\. Formerly Brahmā created the gods and said to them, “Worship me”. But they who sought benefits eschewed him and worshipped the Ātman.

## \|\| 9.40.45-46 \|\|

*te śaptā brahmaṇā mūḍhā naṣṭasaṃjñā bhaviṣyatha \|*  
*tasmātkiṃcidajānaṃto naṣṭasaṃjñāḥ pitāmaham \|\| 45 \|\|*

*procustaṃ praṇatāssarve kuruṣvānugrahaṃ hi naḥ \|*  
*ityuktastānuvācedaṃ prāyaścittārthameva hi \|\| 46 \|\|*

45-46. They were cursed by Brahmā thus. You will be deluded and become senseless”. Hence unable to know any thing and being insensible they bowed to Pitāmaha and said “Please bless us.” Being requested thus he said to them for the sake of expiation.

## \|\| 9.40.47 \|\|

*putrānsvānparipṛcchadhvaṃ tato jñānamavāpsyatha \|*  
*ityuktā naṣṭasaṃjñāste putrānpapracchurojasā \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. “Ask your sons. You will then attain perfect knowledge”. Urged thus the senseless ones asked their sons boldly.

## \|\| 9.40.48-50 \|\|

*prāyaścittārthamevādhilabdhasaṃjñā divaukasaḥ \|*  
*gamyatāṃ putrakā evaṃ putrairuktāśca te'nagha \|\| 48 \|\|*

*abhiśaptāstu te devāḥ putrakāmena vedhasam \|*  
*papracchuruktāḥ putraiste gatāste putrakā iti \|\| 49 \|\|*

*tatastānabravīddevo devānbrahmā sasaṃśayān \|*  
*śṛṇudhvaṃ nirjarāssarve yūyaṃ na brahmavādinaḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

48-50. They were asked to perform expiatory rites. O sinless one, the gods were thus addressed by the sons. “Let the sons be approached.” The gods who had been cursed by Brahmā told him that the sons had gone away. Then Brahmā told the gods thus “O gods, listen, You are not Brahmavādins, the believers in Brahma’s cult.

## \|\| 9.40.51 \|\|

*tasmādyaduktaṃ yuṣmākaṃ putraistairjñānisattamaiḥ \|*  
*maṃtavyaṃ saṃśayaṃ tyaktvā tathā na ca tadanyathā \|\| 51 \|\|*

51 Hence what has been mentioned by the sons, the most excellent of the perfectly wise shall be followed without hesitation and not otherwise.

## \|\| 9.40.52 \|\|

*devāśca pitaraścaiva yajadhvaṃ tridivaukasaḥ \|*  
*parasparaṃ mahāprītyā sarvakāmaphalapradā \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. O dwellers of heaven, let the gods and the Pitṛs worship one another with great delight. This will confer the cherished desires.”

## \|\| 9.40.53 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*tataste chinnasaṃdehāḥ prītimaṃtaḥ parasparam \|*  
*babhūvurmuniśārdūla brahmavākyātsukhapradāḥ \|\| 53 \|\|*

53\. Sanatkumāra said:—O great sage, then their doubts were cleared. With delight they became the mutual bestowers of happiness at the instance of Brahmā.

## \|\| 9.40.54 \|\|

*tato devā hi procustānyaduktāḥ putrakā vayam \|*  
*tasmādbhavaṃtaḥ pitaro bhaviṣyatha na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. Then the gods said “Since you addressed us as sons you will undoubtedly become Pitṛs.

## \|\| 9.40.55 \|\|

*pitṛśrāddhe kriyāṃ kaścitkariṣyati na saṃśayaḥ \|*  
*śrāddhairāpyāyitassomo lokānāpyāyayiṣyati \|\| 55 \|\|*

55\. In the Śrāddha of the Pitṛs, undoubtedly the performer will propitiate Soma who being delighted and enhanced by the offering shall gladden the worlds.

## \|\| 9.40.56-58 \|\|

*samudraṃ parvatavanaṃ jaṃgamājaṃgamairvṛtam \|*  
*śrāddhāni puṣṭikāmaiśca ye kariṣyaṃti mānavāḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

*tebhyaḥ puṣṭipradāścaiva pitaraḥ prīṇitāssadā \|*  
*śrāddhe ye ca pradāsyaṃti trīnpiṃḍānnāmagotrataḥ \|\| 57 \|\|*

*sarvatra vartamānāste pitaraḥ prapitāmahāḥ \|*  
*bhāvayiṣyaṃti satataṃ śrāddhadānena tarpitāḥ \|\| 58 \|\|*

56-58. They will delight the ocean, the mountain and the forest, all consisting of the mobile and immobile beings. Those who desire nourishment and perform Śrāddha rites are given nourishment by the Pitṛs who are propitiated. Those who give three Piṇḍas in the Śrāddha mentioning the names and Gotras delight the Pitṛs wherever they be and are looked after by them.

## \|\| 9.40.59-60 \|\|

*iti tadvacanaṃ satyaṃ bhavatvatha divaukasaḥ \|*  
*putrāśca pitaraścaiva vayaṃ sarve parasparam \|\| 59 \|\|*

*evaṃ te pitaro devā dharmataḥ putratāṃ gatāḥ \|*  
*anyonyaṃ pitaro vai te prathitāḥ kṣitimaṇḍale \|\| 60 \|\|*

59-60. Let these words be truthful. Whether gods or Pitṛs we are father and sons successively. Thus the Pitṛs who became sons virtuously were famous in the world as mutual beneficiaries.

## Chapter 41 - The attainment of the seven hunters

## \|\| 9.41.1 \|\|

*sanatkumāra uvāca \|*  
*sapta te tapatāṃ śreṣṭha svarge pitṛgaṇāssmṛtāḥ \|*  
*catvāro mūrttimaṃto vai trayaścaiva hyamūrtayaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

Sanatkumāra said:—

1\. O foremost among the performers of penance, the Pitṛs in the heaven are seven in number. Four of them are embodied and three bodiless.

## \|\| 9.41.2 \|\|

*tānyajaṃte devagaṇā ādyā viprādayastathā \|*  
*āpyāyayaṃti te pūrvaṃ somaṃ yogabalena vai \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. The primordial groups of gods, brahmins and others worship them. With the power of their Yoga they strengthen and gladden Soma.

## \|\| 9.41.3-6 \|\|

*tasmācchrāddhāni deyāni yogināṃ tu viśeṣataḥ \|*  
*sarveṣāṃ rājataṃ pātramatha vā rajatānvitam \|\| 3 \|\|*

*dattaṃ svadhāṃ purodhāya śrāddhe prīṇāti vai pitṝn \|*  
*vahnerāpyāyanaṃ kṛtvā somasya tu yamasya vai \|\| 4 \|\|*

*udagāyanamapyagnāvagnyabhāve'psu vā punaḥ \|*  
*pitṝnprīṇāti yo bhaktyā pitaraḥ prīṇayaṃti tam \|\| 5 \|\|*

*yacchaṃti pitaraḥ puṣṭiṃ prajāśca vipulāstathā \|*  
*svargamārogyavṛddhiṃ ca yadanyadapi cepsitam \|\| 6 \|\|*

3-6. Hence people shall offer Śrāddhas especially to the Yogins. A silver vessel or a vessel with silver when offered with Svadhā, delights the Pitṛs. When the sun is in the northern transit he shall make offerings in the fìre or in its absence in water. He shall propitiate fire-god, Soma or Yama. Those who delight the Pitṛs with devotion are delighted by Pitṛs. The Pitṛs bestow nourishment, offspring, heaven, health and other desired objects.

## \|\| 9.41.7 \|\|

*devakāryādapi mune pitṛkāryyaṃ viśiṣyate \|*  
*pitṛbhakto'si viprarṣe tena tvamajarāmaraḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. O sage, the rites of Pitṛs are better than the rites of the gods. O brahmin sage, since you are a devotee of the Pitṛs you shall be free from old age and death.

## \|\| 9.41.8 \|\|

*na yogena gatissā tu pitṛbhaktasya yā mune \|*  
*pitṛbhaktirviśeṣeṇa tasmātkāryā mahāmune \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. O sage, the goal attained by devotion to the Pitṛs is not attained by the practices of Yoga. O great sage, so devotion to the Pitṛs shall be pursued with care.

## \|\| 9.41.9 \|\|

*mārkaṇḍeya uvāca \|*  
*evamuktvā''śu deveśo devānāmapi durlabham \|*  
*cakṣurdattvā savijñānaṃ jagāma yaugikīṃ gatim \|\| 9 \|\|*

Mārkaṇḍeya said:—

9\. After saying thus and immediately bestowing the vision of perfect knowledge the lord of the gods disappeared.

## \|\| 9.41.10 \|\|

*śṛṇu bhīṣma purā bhūyo bhāradvājātmajā dvijāḥ \|*  
*yogadharmamanuprāpya bhraṣṭā duścaritena vai \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. O Bhīṣma, listen again. Formerly, even after learning the code of Yoga, the brahmin sons of Bhāradvāja were degraded due to their evil course.

## \|\| 9.41.11 \|\|

*vāgduṣṭaḥ krodhano hiṃsraḥ piśunaḥ kavireva ca \|*  
*svasṛṣaḥ pitṛvartī ca nāmabhiḥ karmabhistathā \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Their names which indicate their activities are Vāgduṣṭa (defiled in speech), Krodhana (angry), Hiṃsra (violent), Piśuna (backbiter), Kavi(poet), Svasṛṣa(self-creating) and Pitṛvartin (worshipping the pitṛs)

## \|\| 9.41.12 \|\|

*kauśikasya sutāstāta śiṣyā gargasya cābhavan \|*  
*pitaryuparate sarve pravasaṃtastadābhavan \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. O dear, the sons of Kauśika became the disciples of Garga. When their father had expired they went in exile.

## \|\| 9.41.13 \|\|

*viniyogādgurostasya gāṃ dogdhrīṃ samakālayan \|*  
*samānavatsāṃ kapilāṃ sarve'nyāyāgatāstadā \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. At the instance of their preceptor they looked after his cow and her calf. All of them were unjust in their actions.

## \|\| 9.41.14 \|\|

*teṣāṃ pathi kṣudhārtānāṃ bālyānmohācca bhārata \|*  
*krūrā buddhissamutpannā tāṃ gāṃ tai hiṃsituṃ tadā \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. O descendant of Bharata, once in their coarse of wandering in the forest, they were oppressed by hunger. They had the cruel intention of injuring the cow then.

## \|\| 9.41.15 \|\|

*tāṃ kavisasvasṛpaścaiva yācete naiti vai tadā \|*  
*na cāśakyāstu tābhyāṃ vā tadā vārayituṃ nijāḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. Kavi and Svasṛṣa forbade them do so. But they could not be prevented from that act.

## \|\| 9.41.16 \|\|

*pitṛvartī tu yasteṣāṃ nityaṃ śrāddhāhniko dvijaḥ \|*  
*sa sarvānabravītkopātpitṛbhaktisamanvitaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. The brahmin Pitṛvartin who performed Śrāddha everyday with devotion to the Pitṛs spoke to them angrily.

## \|\| 9.41.17 \|\|

*yadyaśakyaṃ prakartavyaṃ pitṝnuddiśya sādhyatām \|*  
*prakurvaṃto hi śrāddhaṃ tu sarva eva samāhitāḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. If it is not possible to stop this do it with the Pitṛs in view. Perform the Śrāddha, ye all cautiously.

## \|\| 9.41.18 \|\|

*evameṣā ca gaurdharmaṃ prāpsyate nātra saṃśayaḥ \|*  
*pitṝnabhyarcya dharmeṇa nādharmo no bhaviṣyati \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. If performed thus, the cow will attain piety undoubtedly. By worshipping Pitṛs we shall not be affected by sin.

## \|\| 9.41.19 \|\|

*evamuktāśca te sarve prokṣayitvā ca gāṃ tadā \|*  
*pitṛbhyaḥ kalpayitvā tu hyupāyuṃjata bhārata \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. O descendant of Bharata, on being advised thus, they all sprinkled the cow with sacred water, dedicated it to the Pitṛs and used it as their food.

## \|\| 9.41.20 \|\|

*upayujya ca gāṃ sarve gurostasya nyavedayan \|*  
*śārdūlena hatā dhenurvatsā vai gṛhyatāmiti \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. After eating the cow they said to the preceptor saying—“The cow is killed by a tiger. Let the calf be accepted.”

## \|\| 9.41.21 \|\|

*ārtavatsa tu taṃ vatsaṃ pratijagrāha vai dvijaḥ \|*  
*mithyopacārataḥ pāpamabhūtteṣāṃ ca goghnatām \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. The sage accepted the calf with great distress. The killers of the cow became sinful by their false reverence and service.

## \|\| 9.41.22 \|\|

*tataḥ kālena kiyatā kāladharmamupāgatāḥ \|*  
*te sapta bhrātarastāta babhūvussvāyuṣaḥkṣaye \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. O dear, in due course, when their term of life expired the seven brothers passed away.

## \|\| 9.41.23-24 \|\|

*te vai krūratayā haiṃstryātsvānāryyatvādgurostathā \|*  
*ugrahiṃsāvihārāśca jātāssapta sahodarāḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

*lubdhakasya sutāstāvadbalavaṃto manasvinaḥ \|*  
*jātā vyādhā daśārṇeṣu sapta dharmavicakṣaṇāḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

23-24. They were reborn as the sons of a hunter as a result of their cruelty, violence, being ignoble towards the preceptor and their over-indulgence in fierce violence. They were reborn in the country of Daśārṇas. They were strong, intelligent and experts in piety.

## \|\| 9.41.25 \|\|

*svadharmaniratāssarve mṛgā mohavivarjitāḥ \|*  
*āsannudvegasaṃvignā ramye kālaṃjare girau \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. They were engaged in the practice of sacred rites. They were free from the delusion of hunting animals. On the beautiful mountain Kālañjara they passed their time with distress.

## \|\| 9.41.26 \|\|

*tamevārthamanudhyāya jñānaṃ maraṇasaṃbhavam \|*  
*āsanvanacarāḥ kṣāṃtā nirdvaṃdvā niṣparigrahāḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. Recollecting the event of their death the forest-roamers became forbearers, free from Dvandvas and averse to taking gifts.

## \|\| 9.41.27 \|\|

*te sarve śubhakarmāṇassaddharmāṇo vanecarāḥ \|*  
*vidharmācaraṇairhīnā jātismaraṇasiddhayaḥ \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. The hunters performed auspicious rites, and holy deeds, disassociating themselves from the wicked. They had the power of the memory of the previous birth.

## \|\| 9.41.28 \|\|

*pūrvajātiṣu yo dharmaḥ śruto gurukuleṣu vai \|*  
*tathaiva cāsthitā buddhau saṃsāre'pya nivartane \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. Whatever sacred rites they had heard in the preceptor’s hermitage in the previous births were retained in their minds. So also the goal of non-return to this world.

## \|\| 9.41.29-30 \|\|

*girimadhye jahuḥ prāṇāṃllabdhāhārāstapasvinaḥ \|*  
*teṣāṃ tu patitānāṃ ca yāni sthānāni bhārata \|\| 29 \|\|*

*tathaivādyāpi dṛśyaṃte girau kālañjare nṛpa \|*  
*karmaṇā tena te jātāḥ śubhāśubhavivarjakāḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

29-30. They performed their penance, had their food and finally cast off their lives on that mountain. O descendant of Bharata, O king, the different places where they fell dead are still seen in the same manner on the mount Kālañjara. Thanks to their activities neither auspicious nor inauspicious, they were reborn in a life neither auspicious nor inauspicious.

## \|\| 9.41.31 \|\|

*śubhā'śubhatarāṃ yoniṃ cakravākatvamāgatāḥ \|*  
*śubhe deśe śaradvīpe saptaivāsañjalaukasaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. On an island in an auspicious spot the seven became aquatic birds. They were reborn as Cakravāka birds in a life that is neither auspicious nor inauspicious.

## \|\| 9.41.32 \|\|

*tyaktvā sahacarīdharmaṃ munayo dharmadhāriṇaḥ \|*  
*nissaṃgā nirmamāśśāṃtā nirdvaṃdvā niṣparigrahāḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. They abandoned the contact with their mates. They were like sages practising sacred rites, free from associations and Egotistic feelings. They remained calm. They did not accept gifts. They were free from Dvandvas.

## \|\| 9.41.33 \|\|

*nivṛttinirvṛtāścaiva śakunā nāmataḥ smṛtāḥ \|*  
*te brahmacāriṇassarve śakunā dharmadhāriṇaḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. They were birds only in name. They were holy b?xhelors delighted in renunciation. They were birds practising sacred rites.

## \|\| 9.41.34 \|\|

*jātismarāssusaṃvṛddhāssaptaiva brahmacāriṇaḥ \|*  
*sthitā ekatra saddharmā vikārarahitāssadā \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. They could remember their previous births. They grew old even as they were bachelors. They remained together free from aberrations and performed good rites.

## \|\| 9.41.35 \|\|

*viprayonau tu yanmohānmithyāpacaritaṃ gurau \|*  
*tiryyagyonau tathā janma śrāddhājjñānaṃ ca lebhire \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. When they were born as brahmins they acted falsely to their preceptor. Still in their birth as birds they attained knowledge as a result of the Śrāddha they had performed.

## \|\| 9.41.36 \|\|

*tathā tu pitṛkāryyārthaṃ kṛtaṃ śrāddhaṃ vyavasthitaiḥ \|*  
*tadā jñānaṃ ca jātiṃ ca kramātprāptaṃ guṇottaram \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. They had performed the Śrāddha for the Pitṛs with due rituals. They retained memory of previous noble birth.

## \|\| 9.41.37 \|\|

*pūrvajādiṣu yadbrahma śrutaṃ gurukuleṣu vai \|*  
*tathaiva saṃsthitajñānaṃ tasmājjñānaṃ samabhyaset \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. The knowledge of Brahma practised by the ancients or found in the preceptors’ families stands as of yore even today. One shall practise that knowledge therefore.

## \|\| 9.41.38 \|\|

*sumanāśca suvākchuddhaḥ pañcamaśchidradarśakaḥ \|*  
*svataṃtraśca suyajñaśca kulīnā nāmataḥ smṛtāḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. They were of noble birth and were named Svatantra. Suyajña, Sumanas, Suvākśuddha (Suvākṣuddha/Suvākchuddha?) and the fifth one Chidradarśaka.

## \|\| 9.41.39 \|\|

*teṣāṃ tatra vihaṃgānāṃ caratāṃ dharmacāriṇām \|*  
*suvṛttamabhavattatra tacchṛṇuṣva mahāmune \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. While they were practising sacred rites an auspicious event happened there. O great sage, please listen to that.

## \|\| 9.41.40 \|\|

*nīpānāmīśvaro rājā prabhāveṇa samanvitaḥ \|*  
*śrīmānantaḥpuravṛto vanaṃ tatrāviveśa ha \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. The prosperous king of Nīpas, endowed with strength, and accompanied by his harem entered that forest.

## \|\| 9.41.41 \|\|

*svataṃtraścakravākassaspṛhayāmāsa taṃ nṛpam \|*  
*dṛṣṭvā yāṃtaṃ sukhopetaṃ rājyaśobhāsamanvitam \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. The Cakravaka Svatantra yearned much, on seeing the happy king endowed with the glory of the kingdom pass along.

## \|\| 9.41.42-43 \|\|

*yadyasti sukṛtaṃ kiṃcittapo vā niyamo'pi vā \|*  
*khinnohamupavāsena tapasā niścalena ca \|\| 42 \|\|*

*tasya sarvasya pūrṇena phalenāpi kṛtena hi \|*  
*sarvasaubhāgyapātraśca bhaveyamahamīdṛśaḥ \|\| 43 \|\|*
