42-43. I have become weary with the observance of fasts and steady penance. If there is a merit accruing from penance or the observance of checks and restraints let me become like him the abode of fortune and bliss.

## \|\| 9.41.44 \|\|

*mārkaṇḍeya uvāca \|*  
*tatastu cakravākau dvāvāsatussahacāriṇau \|*  
*āvāṃ vai sacivau syāva tava priyahitaiṣiṇau \|\| 44 \|\|*

Mārkaṇḍeya said:—

44\. Then two of his comrades said. “We shall be your ministers, your delighters and well-wishers.”

## \|\| 9.41.45 \|\|

*tathetyuktvā tu tasyāsīttadā yogātmano gatiḥ \|*  
*evaṃ tau cakravākau ca svavākyaṃ pratyabhāṣatām \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. After saying “So be it”, the Yogic soul attained his goal. The two Cakravākas replied to him.

## \|\| 9.41.46 \|\|

*yasmātkarmabruvāṇasvaṃ yogadharmamavāpya tam \|*  
*evaṃ varaṃ prārthayase tasmādvākyaṃ nibodha me \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. After renouncing action by the Yogic practices why do you long for such a boon? Hence hear my statement.

## \|\| 9.41.47 \|\|

*rājā tvaṃ bhavitā tāta kāṃpilye nagarottame \|*  
*etau te sacivau syātāṃ vyabhicārapradharṣitau \|\| 47 \|\|*

47\. O dear, you will become a king in the excellent city of Kāmpilya. These too will be your ministers who will not go astray.

## \|\| 9.41.48 \|\|

*na tānūcustrayo rājyaṃ caturassahacāriṇaḥ \|*  
*saprasādaṃ punaścakre tanmadhye sumanābravīt \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. The three did not speak about the kingdom to their four comrades. Being delighted Sumanas said again.

## \|\| 9.41.49 \|\|

*aṃtarvo bhavitā śāpaḥ punaryogamavāpsyatha \|*  
*sarvasattvaḥ suyajñaśca svataṃtro'yaṃ bhaviṣyati \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. When the curse is over you will attain Yoga. Sarvasattva, Suyajña and Svatantra too will attain yogic powers.

## \|\| 9.41.50 \|\|

*pitṛprasādādyuṣmābhissaṃprāptaṃ sukṛtaṃ bhavet \|*  
*gāṃ prokṣayitvā dharmeṇa pitṛbhyaścopakalpitāḥ \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. Due to their favour you will attain merit. You have sprinkled the cow and offered it to the Pitṛs.

## \|\| 9.41.51 \|\|

*asmākaṃ jñānasaṃyogassarveṣāṃ yogasādhanam \|*  
*idaṃ ca kāryaṃ saṃrabdhaṃ ślokamekamudāhṛtam \|\| 51 \|\|*

51\. We shall acquire knowledge which shall work as the means of Yoga for all. Thīs statement is bold and spirited and is quoted as a verse.

## \|\| 9.41.52 \|\|

*puruṣāntaritaṃ śrutvā tato yogamavāpsyatha \|*  
*ityuktvā sa tu maunobhūdvihaṃgassumanā budhaḥ \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. After attaining human life you will attain “Yoga”. After saying this the learned bird Sumanas became silent.

## \|\| 9.41.53 \|\|

*mārkaṇḍeya uvāca \|*  
*lokānāṃ svastaye tāta śantanupravarātmaja \|*  
*ityuktaṃ taccaritraṃ me kiṃ bhūyaśśrotumicchasi \|\| 53 \|\|*

Mārkaṇḍeya said:—

53\. Thus I have mentioned their story to you. What more do you wish to hear?

## Chapter 42 - Power of the Pitṛs

## \|\| 9.42.1 \|\|

*\|\| bhīṣma uvāca \|*  
*mārkaṇḍeya mahāprājña pitṛbhaktibhṛtāṃ vara \|*  
*kiṃ jātaṃ tu tato brūhi kṛpayā munisattama \|\| 1 \|\|*

Bhīṣma said:—

1\. Mārkaṇḍeya of great intellect, O foremost among the devotees of the Pitṛs what happened after that? O excellent sage, please tell me.

## \|\| 9.42.2 \|\|

*mārkaṇḍeya uvāca \|*  
*te dharmayoganiratāssapta mānasacāriṇaḥ \|*  
*vāyvaṃbubhakṣāssatataṃ śarīramupaśoṣayan \|\| 2 \|\|*

Mārkaṇḍeya said:—

2\. Those seven traversers of the lake Mānasa engaged in sacred rites and Yoga got their bodies withered up taking in only air and water.

## \|\| 9.42.3 \|\|

*sa rājāṃtaḥpuravṛto nandane maghavā iva \|*  
*krīḍitvā suciraṃ tatra sabhāryyassvapuraṃ yayau \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. After sporting about for a long time there, like Indra in Nandana, the king, accompanied by his wives and the members of his harem returned to his city.

## \|\| 9.42.4 \|\|

*anūho nāma tasyāsītputraḥ paramadhārmikaḥ \|*  
*taṃ vaibhrājaḥ sutaṃ rājye sthāpayitvā vanaṃ yayau \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. A son Anūha was born to him. He was highly virtuous. King Vaibhrāja established his son in the kingdom and went to forest.

## \|\| 9.42.5 \|\|

*tapaḥ kartuṃ samārebhe yatra te sahacāriṇaḥ \|*  
*sa vai tatra nirāhāro vāyubhakṣo mahātapāḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Without taking in any food, breathing in only air he began to perform a great penance in the spot where those comrades were present.

## \|\| 9.42.6 \|\|

*tato vibhrājitaṃ tena vibhrājaṃ nāma tadvanam \|*  
*babhūva suprasiddhaṃ hi yogasiddhipradāyakam \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. Then that forest Vibhrāja shone splendidly. It became very famous as the bestower of Yogic Siddhi.

## \|\| 9.42.7 \|\|

*tatraiva te hi śakunāścatvāro yogadharmiṇaḥ \|*  
*yogabhraṣṭāstrayaścaiva dehatyāgakṛto'bhavan \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. It was there itself that the four birds of Yogic rites and the three that fell from Yoga cast off their bodies.

## \|\| 9.42.8 \|\|

*kāṃpilye nagare te tu brahmadattapurogamāḥ \|*  
*jātāssaptamahātmānassarve vigatakalmaṣāḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. They were reborn in Kāmpilya as seven noble souls Brahmadatta and others. All of them were free from sins.

## \|\| 9.42.9 \|\|

*smṛtimaṃto'tra catvārastrayastu parimohitāḥ \|*  
*svatantrasyāhvayo jāto brahmadatto mahaujasaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Four of them had the memory of their previous births but three of them were deluded. That Svatantra of great Yogic power was now called Brahmadatta.

## \|\| 9.42.10 \|\|

*chidradarśī sunetrastu vedavedāṃgapāragau \|*  
*jātau śrotriyadāyādau pūrvajātisahāṣitau \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Chidradarśin and Sunetra were the masters of the Vedas and Vedāṅgas. They were born as sons of brahmins well-versed in the Vedas. They had the memory of the previous births.

## \|\| 9.42.11 \|\|

*paṃcālo bahvṛcastvāsīdācāryatvaṃ cakāra ha \|*  
*dvivedaḥ puṃḍarīkaśca chaṃdogo'dhvaryureva ca \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Pañcāla was conversant with many Ṛk mantras. He became a preceptor. Puṇḍarīka became the master of two Vedas. He was a Chandoga (master of prosody) and an Adhvaryu (priest of sacrifice).

## \|\| 9.42.12 \|\|

*tato rājā sutaṃ dṛṣṭvā brahmadattamakalmaṣam \|*  
*abhiṣicya svarājye tu parāṃ gatimavāptavān \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. On seeing his son Brahmadatta free from sin, the king crowned him in kingdom and attained the supreme goal.

## \|\| 9.42.13 \|\|

*paṃcālaḥ puṇḍarīkastu putrau saṃsthāpya mandire \|*  
*viviśaturvanaṃ tatra gatau paramikāṃ gatim \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. Pañcāla and Puṇḍarīka established their sons in the house and went to forest. There they attained the great goal.

## \|\| 9.42.14 \|\|

*brahmadattasya bhāryyā tu sannitirmāma bhārata \|*  
*sā tvekabhāvasaṃyuktā reme bhartrā sahaiva tu \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. O descendant of Bharata, Brahmadatta’s wife Sannati sported with her husband with single-minded devotion.

## \|\| 9.42.15 \|\|

*śeṣāstu cakravākā vai kāṃpilye sahacāriṇaḥ \|*  
*jātāḥ śrotriyadāyādā daridrasya kule nṛpa \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. The other three Cakravāka comrades were born as brahmin sons in the family of a poor man, O king.

## \|\| 9.42.16 \|\|

*dhṛtimānsumahātmā ca tattvadarśīṃ nirutsukaḥ \|*  
*vedādhyayana sampannāścatvāraśchidradarśinaḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. The four sons of Chidradarśin were endowed with Vedic study. They were Dhṛtimat Sumahātman, Tattvadarśin and Nirutsuka.

## \|\| 9.42.17 \|\|

*te yoganiratāssiddhāḥ prasthitāssarva eva hi \|*  
*āmaṃtrya ca mithaḥ śaṃbhoḥ padāmbhojaṃ praṇamya tu \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. They were engaged in Yogic practice. They took leave of one another, bowed at the lotus-like feet of Śiva and set out.

## \|\| 9.42.18 \|\|

*śūrā ye samprapadyante apunarbhavakāṃkṣiṇaḥ \|*  
*pāpampraṇāśayantvadya tacchambhoḥ paramampadam \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. The enthusiasts desiring freedom From rebirth resort to Śiva’s feet. May those feet of Śiva destroy sins.

## \|\| 9.42.19 \|\|

*śārīre mānase caiva pāpe vāgje mahāmune \|*  
*kṛte samyagidambhaktyā paṭhecchraddhāsamanvitaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. O great sage, if any physical, mental or verbal sin is committed, one shall read this narrative with full devotion.

## \|\| 9.42.20-22 \|\|

*mucyate sarvapāpebhyaśśivanāmānukīrtanāt \|*  
*uccāryamāṇa etasmindevadevasya tasya vai \|\| 20 \|\|*

*vilayaṃ pāpamāyāti hyāmabhāṇḍamivāmbhasi \|*  
*tasmāttatsaṃcite pāpe samanaṃtarameva ca \|\| 21 \|\|*

*japtavyametatpāpasya praśamāya mahāmune \|*  
*naraiḥ śraddhālubhibhūryassarvakāmaphalāptaye \|\| 22 \|\|*

20-22. By repeating the names of Śiva, one gets rid of all sins soon. As soon as the name Śiva, the lord of the gods, is uttered sins are quelled like an unbaked pot in water, O great sage. In proportion to the sins committed and in order to quell them the Japa of Śiva’s names shall be performed by the faithful. In order to achieve the fruits of all desires too, this Japa shall be performed accordingly.

## \|\| 9.42.23 \|\|

*puṣṭyarthamimamadhyāyaṃ paṭhedenaṃ śṛṇoti vā \|*  
*mucyate sarvapāpebhyo mokṣaṃ yāti na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. He who reads or hears this for prosperity is liberated from sins and attains salvation. There is no doubt in this.

## Chapter 43 - The mode of worshipping Vyāsa

## \|\| 9.43.1 \|\|

*śaunaka uvāca \|*  
*ācāryyapūjanaṃ brūhi sūta vyāsaguro'dhunā \|*  
*granthasya śravaṇānte hi kiṃ kartavyaṃ tadapyaho \|\| 1 \|\|*

Śaunaka said:—

1\. O Sūta having Vyāsa as preceptor, please tell me now the mode of worshipping the preceptor. What shall be done at the end of listening to the holy book? Please mention that also.

## \|\| 9.43.2 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*pūjayedvidhivadbhaktyācāryyaṃ śrutvā kathāṃ parām \|*  
*granthānte vidhivaddadyādācāryyāya prasannadhīḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

2\. After hearing the story the devotee shall worship the preceptor duly with devotion. Delighted in mind he shall make liberal gifts duly to the preceptor at the conclusion of the holy book.

## \|\| 9.43.3 \|\|

*tato vaktāramānamya saṃpūjya ca yathāvidhi \|*  
*bhūṣaṇairhastakarṇānāṃ vastraissaumyādibhissudhīḥ \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. The intelligent devotee shall bow to the propounder and worship him duly. Gifts of ornaments for hands and ears and fine garments shall be made.

## \|\| 9.43.4-5 \|\|

*śivapūjāsamāptau tu dadyāddhenuṃ savatsikām \|*  
*kṛtvāsanaṃ suvarṇasya palamānasya sāmbaram \|\| 4 \|\|*

*tatrāsthāpya śubhaṃ graṃthaṃ likhitaṃ lalitākṣaraiḥ \|*  
*ācāryāya sudhīrdadyānmuktaḥ syādbhavabandhanaiḥ \|\| 5 \|\|*

4-5. After the worship of Śiva he shall present to the preceptor a cow and its calf. He shall make a book-seat with gold, a Pala in weight and cover it with a good cloth. The manuscript of the holy book written in beautiful hand shall be placed on it and given to the preceptor. The intelligent devotee will be liberated from worldly bondage.

## \|\| 9.43.6 \|\|

*grāmo gajo hayaścāpi yathāśaktyaparāṇi ca \|*  
*mune sarvāṇi deyāni vācakāya mahātmane \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. O sage, a village, an elephant or a horse or other things shall be given as possible to the noble preceptor who reads and propounds the story.

## \|\| 9.43.7 \|\|

*vidhānasahitaṃ samyakchataṃ hi saphalaṃ smṛtam \|*  
*purāṇaṃ śaunakamune satyamevoditaṃ mayā \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. O sage Śaunaka, Purāṇa is efficacious if it is listened to in accordance with the rules. I speak truth to you.

## \|\| 9.43.8 \|\|

*tasmādvidhānaduktaṃ tu śṛṇuyādbhaktito mune \|*  
*purāṇaṃ nigamārthāḍhyaṃ puṇyadaṃ hṛdayaṃ śruteḥ \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. O sage, the Purāṇa full of the meanings of the Vedas, the heart of the Vedas, the meritorious holy book shall be listened to with devotion and in accordance with the rules.

## Chapter 44 - The birth of Vyāsa

## \|\| 9.44.1-3 \|\|

*munaya ūcuḥ \|*  
*vyāsotpattiṃ mahābuddhe brūhi sūta dayānidhe \|*  
*kṛpayā parayā svāminkṛtārthānniṣkuru prabho \|\| 1 \|\|*

*vyāsasya jananī proktā nāmnā satyavatī śubhā \|*  
*vivāhitā tu sā devī rājñā śantanunā kila \|\| 2 \|\|*

*tasyāṃ jāto mahāyogī kathaṃ vyāsaḥ parāśarāt \|*  
*sandeho'tra mahāñjātastaṃ bhavāñchettumarhati \|\| 3 \|\|*

The sages said:—

1-3. O Sūta of great intellect, O store-house of mercy, please narrate the birth of Vyāsa. O holy lord, please satisfy our curiosity. Vyāsa’s mother is known as Satyavatī. She was married to king Śantanu. How was Vyāsa the great Yogin born of her from Parāsara? There is a great doubt in regard to that. It behoves you to remove that doubt.

## \|\| 9.44.4 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*ekadā tīrthayātrāyāṃ vrajanyogī parāśaraḥ \|*  
*yadṛcchayā gato ramyaṃ yamunāyāstaṭaṃ śubham \|\| 4 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

4\. Once, while he was making his pilgrimage, Parāśara the yogin casually came to the auspicious and beautiful tanks of the Yamunā.

## \|\| 9.44.5 \|\|

*niṣādamāha dharmātmā kurvantaṃ bhojanantadā \|*  
*nayasva yamunāpāraṃ jalayānena māmaram \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. The righteous soul spoke to the ferryman who was taking his meals “Take me across the Yamunā quickly in your boat”.

## \|\| 9.44.6 \|\|

*ityukto muninā tena niṣādassvasutāṃ jagau \|*  
*matsyagandhāmamuṃ bāle pāraṃ nāvā naya drutam \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. When the ferryman was thus urged by the sage he spokí to his daughter Matsyagandhā:—“Girl, quickly take this sage to the other bank in the boat.

## \|\| 9.44.7 \|\|

*tāpaso'yaṃ mahābhāge dṛśyantīgarbhasaṃbhavaḥ \|*  
*titīrṣurasti mardhābdhiścaturāmnāyapāragaḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. This sage, O fortunate girl, is born of the womb of Dṛśyantī. He is the ocean of sacred rites. He has mastered the four Vedas. Now he desires to cross this river.

## \|\| 9.44.8 \|\|

*iti vijñāpitā pitrā matsyagandhā mahāmunim \|*  
*saṃvāhayati naukāyāmāsīnaṃ sūryyarociṣam \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. Thus directed by her father, Matsyagandhā began to tale the great sage of solar splendour seated in the boat across the river.

## \|\| 9.44.9 \|\|

*kālayogānmahāyogī tasyāṃ kāmāturo'bhavat \|*  
*dṛṣṭvā yo'psarasāṃ rūpaṃ na kadāpi vimohitaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. That great Yogin who had never been fascinated even on seeing the beauty of the celestial damsels became overwhelmed by lust towards her, in the circumstances.

## \|\| 9.44.10 \|\|

*grahītukāmaḥ sa munirdāśakanyāṃ manoharām \|*  
*dakṣiṇena kareṇaitāmaspṛśaddakṣiṇe kare \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Desirous of clasping the beautiful daughter of the fisherman, the sage touched her in the right hand with his right hand.

## \|\| 9.44.11 \|\|

*tamuvāca viśālākṣīṃ vacanaṃ smitapūrvakam \|*  
*kimidaṃ kriyaye karma vācaṃyama vigarhitam \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. The wide-eyed lass spoke to him smilingly—“O sage of controlled speech, why do you intend to perpetrate this despicable deed?

## \|\| 9.44.12 \|\|

*vasiṣṭhasya kule ramye tvaṃ jāto'si mahāmate \|*  
*niṣādajā tvahambrahmankathaṃ saṃgo ghaṭeta nau \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. O most intelligent one, you are born of the beautiful family of Vasiṣṭha. O brahmin, I am the daughter of a Niṣāda. How can our union be proper?

## \|\| 9.44.13 \|\|

*durlabhaṃ mānuṣaṃ janma brāhmaṇatvaṃ viśeṣataḥ \|*  
*tatrāpi tāpasatvaṃ ca durlabhaṃ munisattama \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. O excellent sage, birth as a man is very difficult to obtain; especially that as a brahmin. Even there the state of an ascetic is very rarely achieved.

## \|\| 9.44.14 \|\|

*vidyayā vapuṣā vācā kulaśīlena cānvitaḥ \|*  
*kāmabāṇavaśaṃ yāto mahadāścaryamatra hi \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. It is extremely surprising that you endowed with learning, good physique, speech, noble birth and good conduct have become subservient to the arrows of Kāma!”

## \|\| 9.44.15 \|\|

*pravṛttamapyasatkarma kartumenaṃ na ko'pi ha \|*  
*bhuvi vārayituṃ śaktaḥ śāpabhītyāsya yoginaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. Then she thought within: “If this Yogin engages himself in the vicious act there is none in the world to prevent him due to his power of inflicting curse.”

## \|\| 9.44.16 \|\|

*iti saṃcintya hṛdaye nijagāda mahāmunim \|*  
*tāvaddhairyaṃ kuru svāminyāvattvāṃ pārayāmi na \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. After thinking in the mind thus she said to the great sage—“O holy lord, please forbear till I take you to the other bank.”

## \|\| 9.44.17 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*iti śrutvā vacastasyā yogirājaḥ parāśaraḥ \|*  
*tatyāja pāṇiṃ tarasā sindhoḥ pāraṃ gataḥ punaḥ \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. On hearing her words the king of Yogins Parāśara left off her hand. He reached the other shore.

## \|\| 9.44.18 \|\|

*punarjagrāha tāṃ bālāṃ muniṃ kāmaprapīḍitaḥ \|*  
*kaṃpamānā tu sā bālā tamuvāca dayānidhim \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. Afflicted with lust the sage caught hold of the lass. Tremblingly the lass spoke to the merciful sage.

## \|\| 9.44.19 \|\|

*durgandhāhaṃ muniśreṣṭha kṛṣṇavarṇā niṣādajā \|*  
*bhavāṃstu paramodāravicāro yogisattamaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. “O great sage, I have foul smell and black complexion, I am the daughter of a Niṣāda. You are the most excellent of Yogins of extremely liberal thoughts.

## \|\| 9.44.20 \|\|

*nāvayorghaṭate saṅgo kācakāṃcanayoriva \|*  
*tulyajātyākṛtikayoḥ saṃgaḥ saukhyaprado bhavet \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. The union between us is not proper as of a piece of worthless glass with gold. The sexual inion of two persons of the same class and features may be conducive to bliss.”

## \|\| 9.44.21 \|\|

*ityuktena tayā tena kṣaṇamātreṇa kāminī \|*  
*kṛtā yojanagaṃdhā tu ramyarūpā manoramā \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. As the sage addressed her thus, the damsel was instantly converted into Yojanagandhā, (one whose fragrant odours spread to a Yojana), one of fine features and beauty.

## \|\| 9.44.22 \|\|

*punarjagrāha tāṃ bālāṃ sa muniḥ kāmapīḍitaḥ \|*  
*grahītukāmaṃ taṃ dṛṣṭvā punaḥ provāca vāsavī \|\| 22 \|\|*

22\. Afflicted by lust the sage caught hold of her again. On seeing him bent upon seizing her she said again.

## \|\| 9.44.23 \|\|

*rātrau vyavāyaḥ kartavyo na diveti śrutirjagau \|*  
*divāsaṃge mahāndoṣo nindā cāpi durāsadā \|\| 23 \|\|*

23\. “It is mentioned in the Vedas that sexual intercourse shall be indulged in at night; not during the day. There is great harm in having sexual intercourse during the day time. It is censured.

## \|\| 9.44.24 \|\|

*tasmāttāvatpratīkṣasva yāvadbhavati yāminī \|*  
*paśyanti mānavāścātra pitā me ca taṭe sthitaḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. Hence please wait till the night falls. Now all the men will see us and so shall my father who stands at the other bank.”

## \|\| 9.44.25 \|\|

*tayoktamidamākarṇya vacanaṃ munipuṃgavaḥ \|*  
*nīhāraṃ kalpayāmāsa sadyaḥ puṇyabalena vai \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. On hearing the words uttered by her, the leading sage immediately created a screen of snow, thanks to the strength of his merit.

## \|\| 9.44.26 \|\|

*nīhāre ca samutpanne tamasā rātrisaṃnibhe \|*  
*vyavāyacakitā bālā punaḥ provāca tammunim \|\| 26 \|\|*

26\. When the sheet of mist spread and it looked just as night, the girl, afraid of submitting herself to sexual intercourse, spoke to the sage again.

## \|\| 9.44.27 \|\|

*yoginnamoghavīryyastvaṃ bhuktvā gantāsi māṃ yadi \|*  
*sagarbhā syāṃ tadā svāminkā gatirme bhavediti \|\| 27 \|\|*

27\. “O Yogin, your semen never fails. You may enjoy me and go away. O holy lord, I shall become pregnant, what will be my fate then?

## \|\| 9.44.28 \|\|

*kanyāvrataṃ mahābuddhe mama naṣṭaṃ bhaviṣyati \|*  
*hasiṣyati tadā lokāḥ pitaraṃ kiṃ bravīmyaham \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. O intelligent sage, my virginity will be ruined, People will laugh at me. What will I tell my father?”

## \|\| 9.44.29 \|\|

*\|\| parāśara uvāca \|*  
*rama bāle mayā sārddhaṃ svacchandaṃ kāmajai rasaiḥ \|*  
*svīyābhilāṣamākhyāhi pūrayāmyadhunā priye \|\| 29 \|\|*

Parāśara said:—

29\. O maiden, O beloved girl, sport with me freely with loving emotion. You tell me what you desire. I shall fulfil it.

## \|\| 9.44.30 \|\|

*madājñāsatyakaraṇānnāmnā satyavatī bhava \|*  
*vandanīyā tathāśeṣairyogibhistridaśairapi \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. By making my advances fruitful you will be known as Satyavatī. You will deserve the respect of all Yogins and even the gods.

## \|\| 9.44.31-32 \|\|

*satyavatyuvāca \|*  
*jānate na pitā mātā na vānye bhuvi mānavāḥ \|*  
*kanyādharmo na me hanyādyadi svīkuru māntadā \|\| 31 \|\|*

*putraśca tvatsamo nātha bhavedadbhutaśaktimān \|*  
*saugandhyaṃ sarvadāṃge me tāruṇyaṃ ca navaṃnavam \|\| 32 \|\|*

Satyavatī said:—

31-32. If neither my father nor mother nor anyother person on the earth comes to know of this, if my virginity is not affected, O lord, if the son born of me attains wonderful power like you, if there be sweet odour in my body for ever and if there be fresh youth in me, accept me then.

## \|\| 9.44.33 \|\|

*parāśara uvāca \|*  
*śṛṇu priye tavābhīṣṭaṃ sarvaṃ pūrṇaṃ bhaviṣyati \|*  
*viṣṇvaṃśasaṃbhavaḥ putro bhavitā te mahāyaśāḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

Parāśara said:—

33\. Listen to me O beloved girl, all your desires will be fulfilled. A son of great fame of Viṣṇu’s part will be born to you.

## \|\| 9.44.34 \|\|

*kiṃcidvai kāraṇaṃ viddhi yato'haṃ kāmapīḍitaḥ \|*  
*dṛṣṭvā cāpsarasārūpaṃ nāmuhyanme namaḥ kvacit \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. Know that there is a reason that I have become lustful. Even on seeing the beauty of celestial damsels my mind was never deluded anywhere before.

## \|\| 9.44.35 \|\|

*mīnagandhāṃ samālakṣya tvāṃ mohavaśago'bhavam \|*  
*na bāle bhālapaṭṭastho brahmalekho'nyathā bhavet \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. On seeing you of fish odour I was enamoured. O lass, lines drawn by Brahmā on the forehead cannot prove untrue.

## \|\| 9.44.36 \|\|

*purāṇakartā putraste vedaśākhāvibhāgakṛt \|*  
*bhaviṣyati varārohe khyātakīrtirjagattraye \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. O comely lady, your son will be the author of the Purāṇas, the classifier of the Vedas and renowned in the three worlds.

## \|\| 9.44.37 \|\|

*ityuktvā tāṃ suramyāṅgīṃ bhuktvā yogaviśāradaḥ \|*  
*vavrāja śīghraṃ yamunājale snātvā mahāmune \|\| 37 \|\|*

37\. O great sage, after saying this and enjoying the beautiful lass, the sage, an expert in Yoga, took his bath in the Yamunā and went away quickly.

## \|\| 9.44.38 \|\|

*sāpi garbhaṃ dadhārāśu dvādaśātmasamaprabham \|*  
*asūta sūryyajādvīpe kāmadevamivātmajam \|\| 38 \|\|*

38\. She conceived and immediately gave birth in an island on the Yamunā to a son with the lustre of the sun and resembling Kāmadeva.

## \|\| 9.44.39 \|\|

*vāme kamaṇḍaluṃ bibhraddakṣiṇe daṇḍamuttamam \|*  
*piśaṃgībhirjaṭābhiśca rājito mahasāṃ cayaḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. In his left hand he was holding the water pot and in his right he had an excellent staff. He had matted hair of tawny colour and shone like a mass of splendour.

## \|\| 9.44.40 \|\|

*jātamātrastu tejasvī mātaraṃ pratyabhāṣata \|*  
*gaccha mātaryathākāmaṃ gacchāmyahamataḥ param \|\| 40 \|\|*

40\. Immediately after birth the brilliant sage spoke to his mother “O mother, go as you please and I also go at my will

## \|\| 9.44.41 \|\|

*mātaryadā bhavetkāryaṃ tava kiṃciddhṛdīpsitam \|*  
*saṃsmṛtaścāgamiṣyāmi tvadicchāpūrtihetave \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. O mother, whenever you have some work, whenever you desire in your mind, please remember me. Immediately I shall come to you to fulfil your desire.”

## \|\| 9.44.42 \|\|

*ityuktvā mātṛcaraṇāvabhivādya taponidhiḥ \|*  
*jagāma ca tapaḥ karttuṃ tīrthaṃ pāpaviśodhanam \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. After saying this and worshipping his mother’s feet the sage, a storehouse of penance, went away to perform penance to holy centres quelling sins.

## \|\| 9.44.43 \|\|

*sāpi pitrantikaṃ yātā putrasnehākulā satī \|*  
*smarantī caritaṃ sūnorvarṇayantī svabhāgyakam \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. -She too returned to her father. The chaste lady was overwhelmed by her love for her son. She recollected his activities and described the event as her great fortune.

## \|\| 9.44.44 \|\|

*dvīpe jāto yato bālastena dvaipāyano'bhavat \|*  
*vedaśākhāvibhajanādvedavyāsaḥ prakīrtitaḥ \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. As the boy was born in an island he was known as Dvaipayana. Since he classified the various branches of the Vedas he is glorified as Vedavyāsa.

## \|\| 9.44.45-49 \|\|

*tīrtharājaṃ prathamato dharmakāmārtha mokṣadam \|*  
*naimiṣaṃ ca kurukṣetraṃ gaṅgādvāramavantikām \|\| 45 \|\|*

*ayodhyāṃ mathurāṃ caiva dvārakāmamarāvatīm \|*  
*sarasvatīṃ siṃdhusaṅgaṃ gaṃgā sāgarasaṃgamam \|\| 46 \|\|*

*kāñcīṃ ca tryambakaṃ cāpi saptagodāvarītaṭam \|*  
*kālañjaraṃ prabhāsaṃ ca tathā badarikāśramam \|\| 47 \|\|*

*mahālayantathoṃkārakṣetraṃ vai puruṣottamam \|*  
*gokarṇaṃ bhṛgukacchaṃ ca bhṛgutuṃgaṃ ca puṣkaram \|\| 48 \|\|*

*śrīparvatāditīrthāni dhārātīrthaṃ tathaiva ca \|*  
*gatvāvagāhya vidhinā cacāra paramantapaḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

45-49. He went to these holy centres:—Tīrtharāja \[2\] at the outset, which bestows virtue, love, wealth and salvation, Naimiṣa, \[3\] Kurukṣetra,\[4\] Gaṅgadvara,\[5\] Avantika,\[6\] Ayodhya,\[7\] Mathurā,\[8\] Dvaraka,\[9\] Amarāvatī,\[10\] Sarasvatī,\[11\] Sindhusaṅga,\[12\] the confluence of the Gaṅgā with the ocean,\[13\] Kāñcī,\[14\] Tryambaka,\[15\] the seven holy banks of the Godāvarī,\[16\] Kālañjara, Prabhāsa,\[17\] Badarikāśrama,\[18\] Mahālaya,\[19\] Oṃkārakṣetra,\[20\] Pauruṣottama,\[21\] Gokarṇa,\[22\] Bhṛgukaccha,\[23\] Bhṛgutuṅga,\[24\] Puṣkara,\[25\] Śrīparvata and Dhārātīrtha He took ablutions duly at these places and performed penance.

## \|\| 9.44.50-51 \|\|

*evantīrthānyanekāni nānādeśasthitāni ha \|*  
*paryyaṭankālikāsūnuḥ prāpadvārāṇasīmpurīm \|\| 50 \|\|*

*yatra viśveśvaraḥ sākṣādannapūrṇā maheśvarī \|*  
*bhaktānāmamṛtandātuṃ virājete kṛpānidhī \|\| 51 \|\|*

50-51. Wandering thus over the various, holy centres situated in different countries, the son of Kālikā reached the city of Vārāṇasī where Viśveśvara himself and Annapūrṇā the great goddess, the storehouses of mercy shine in order to give salvation to the devotees.

## \|\| 9.44.52 \|\|

*prāpya vārāṇasītīrthaṃ dṛṣṭvātha maṇikarṇikām \|*  
*koṭijanmārjitaṃ pāpaṃ tatyāja sa munīśvaraḥ \|\| 52 \|\|*

52\. After reaching the holy centre of Vārāṇasī and visiting Maṇikarṇikā the great sage discarded the sin accumulated in crores of birth.

## \|\| 9.44.53-57 \|\|

*dṛṣṭvā liṃgāni sarvāṇi viśveśapramukhāni ca \|*  
*snātvā sarveṣu kuṇḍeṣu vāpīkūpasarassu ca \|\| 53 \|\|*

*natvā vināyakānsarvāngaurīḥ sarvāḥ praṇamya ca \|*  
*sampūjya kālarājaṃ ca bhairavaṃ pāpabhakṣaṇam \|\| 54 \|\|*

*daṇḍanāyakamukhyāṃśca gaṇānstutvā prayatnataḥ \|*  
*ādikeśavamukhyāṃśca keśavānparitoṣya ca \|\| 55 \|\|*

*lolārkamukhyasūryāṃśca praṇamya ca punaḥpunaḥ \|*  
*kṛtvā piṇḍapradānāni sarvatīrtheṣvatandritaḥ \|\| 56 \|\|*

*sthāpayāmāsa puṇyātmā liṃgaṃ vyāseśvarābhidham \|*  
*yaddarśanādbhavedvipra naro vidyāsu vākpatiḥ \|\| 57 \|\|*

53-57. After seeing Viśveśa and other Liṅgas, taking bath in puddles, tanks, wells and lakes, after bowing to all the Vināyakas, after kneeling before all Gaurīs, after worshipping Kālarāja and Bhairava the devourer of all sins, after strenuously eulogising Daṇḍanāyaka and other important Gaṇas, after propitiating Ādikeśava and other important Keśavas, after repeatedly bowing to Lolārka and other important suns and after offering balls of rice in all the holy spots alertly, the meritorious soul installed the Liṅga Vyāseśvara on seeing whom, O brahmins, man becomes a master of all lores like Bṛhaspati.

## \|\| 9.44.58-59 \|\|

*liṃgānyabhyarcya viśveśapramukhāni subhaktitaḥ \|*  
*asakṛccintayāmāsa kiṃ ligaṃ kṣiprasiddhidam \|\| 58 \|\|*

*yamārādhya mahādevaṃ vidyāḥ sarvā labhemahi \|*  
*purāṇakartṛtāśaktirmamāstu yadanugrahāt \|\| 59 \|\|*

58-59. After worshipping devoutly Viśveśa and other Liṅgas he began to think frequently—“What is that Liṅga that bestows Siddhi instantaneously and worshipping whom the great god, we shall be able to attain all lores and by whose blessings I may have the power to compose the Purāṇas.

## \|\| 9.44.60-73 \|\|

*śrīdamoṃkāranāthaṃ vā kṛttivāseśvaraṃ kimu \|*  
*kedāreśantu kāmeśaṃ candreśaṃ vā trilocanam \|\| 60 \|\|*

*kāleśaṃ vṛddhakāleśaṃ kālaśeśvarameva vā \|*  
*jyeṣṭheśaṃ jambukeśaṃ vā jaigīṣavyeśvarantu vā \|\| 61 \|\|*

*daśāśvamedhamīśānaṃ drumicaṇḍeśameva vā \|*  
*dṛkkeśaṃ garuḍeśaṃ vā gokarṇeśaṃ gaṇeśvaram \|\| 62 \|\|*

*prasannavadaneśaṃ vā dharmmeśaṃ tārakeśvaram \|*  
*nandikeśaṃ nivāseśaṃ patrīśaṃ prītikeśvaram \|\| 63 \|\|*

*parvateśaṃ paśupatiṃ hāṭakeśvarameva vā \|*  
*bṛhaspatīśvaraṃ vātha tilabhāṇḍeśameva vā \|\| 64 \|\|*

*bhārabhūteśvaraṃ kiṃ vā mahālakṣmīśvaraṃ tu vā \|*  
*maruteśantu mokṣeśaṃ gaṃgeśaṃ narmadeśvaram \|\| 65 \|\|*

*kṛṣṇeśaṃ parameśānaṃ ratneśvaramathāpi vā \|*  
*yāmuneśaṃ lāṃgalīśaṃ śrīmadviśveśvaraṃ vibhum \|\| 66 \|\|*

*avimukteśvaraṃ vātha viśālākṣīśameva vā \|*  
*vyāghreśvaraṃ varāheśaṃ vidyeśvaramathāpi vā \|\| 67 \|\|*

*varuṇeśaṃ vidhīśaṃ vā harikeśeśvarantu vā \|*  
*bhavānīśaṃ kaparddīśaṃ kandukeśa majeśvaram \|\| 68 \|\|*

*viśvakarmeśvaraṃ vātha vīreśvaramathāpi vā \|*  
*nādeśaṃ kapileśaṃ ca bhuvaneśvarameva vā \|\| 69 \|\|*

*bāṣkulīśaṃ mahādevaṃ siddhīśvaramathāpi vā \|*  
*viśvedeveśvaraṃ vīrabhadreśaṃ bhairaveśvaram \|\| 70 \|\|*

*amṛteśaṃ satīśaṃ vā pārvatīśvarameva vā \|*  
*siddheśvaraṃ mataṃgeśaṃ bhūtīśvaramathāpi vā \|\| 71 \|\|*

*āṣāḍhīśaṃ prakāmeśaṃ koṭirudreśvarantathā \|*  
*madālaseśvaraṃ caiva tilaparṇeśvaraṃ kimu \|\| 72 \|\|*

*kiṃ vā hiraṇyagarbheśaṃ kiṃ vā śrīmadhyameśvaram \|*  
*ityādikoṭiliṃgānāṃ madhye'haṃ kimupāśraye \|\| 73 \|\|*

60-73. There are crores of Liṅgas; from the midst of these what shall I install, what shall I resort so? Can it be Oṅkāranātha, or Kṛttivāseśvara or Kedāreśa, or Kāmeśa, or Candreśa, or Trilocana, or Kāleśa, or Vṛddhakāleśa, or Kalaśeśvara, or Jyesṭheśa, or Jambukeśa, or Jaigīṣavyeśvara, or Daśāśvamedhesana, or Drumacaṇḍesa, or Dṛkkeśa or Garuḍeśa or Gaṇeśvara, or Prasannavadaneśa, or Dharmeśa, or Tārakeśvara or Nandikeśa or Nivāseśa, or Patrīśa, or Prītikeśvara, or Parvateśa, or Paśupati, or Hāṭakeśvara or Bṛhaspatīśvara, or Tilabhāṇḍeśa or Bhārabhūteśvara, or Mahālakṣmīśvara or Maruteśa, or Mokṣeśa, or Gaṅgeśa, or Narmadeśvara, or Kṛṣṇeśa, or Parameśāna, or Ratneśvara, or Yāmuneśa, or Lāṅgalīṣa, or Viśveśvara or Avimukteśvara, or or Viśālākṣīśa, or Vyāghreśvara, or Varāheśa, or Vidyeśvara or Varuṇeśa, or Vidhīśa, or Harikeśeśvara, or Bhavānīsa, or Kapardīśa, or Kandukeśa or Ajeśvara or Viśvakarmeśvara, or Vīreśvara, or Nādeśa, or Kapileśa, or Bhuvaneśvara, or Vāṣkulīśa or Mahādeva, or Siddhīśvara or Viśvedeveśvara or Vīrabhadreśa or Bhairaveśvara, or Amṛteśa; or Satīśa or Pārvatīśvara or Siddheśvara, Mataṅgeśa or Bhūtīśvara, or Āṣāḍhīsa, Prakāśeśa, or Koṭirudreśvara, or Madālaseśvara, or Tilaparṇeśvara, Hiraṇyagarbheśa or shall it be Madhyameśvara?”

## \|\| 9.44.74 \|\|

*iti cintāturo vyāsaḥ śivabhaktiratātmavān \|*  
*kṣaṇaṃ vicārayāmāsa dhyānasusthiracetasā \|\| 74 \|\|*

74\. Pondering thus, Vyāsa, devoted to Śiva with his mind steady in meditation, thought for a short while.

## \|\| 9.44.75-77 \|\|

*ājñātaṃ vismṛtaṃ tāvanniṣpanno me manorathaḥ \|*  
*siddhaiḥ saṃpūjitaṃ liṃgaṃ dharmmakāmārthamokṣadam \|\| 75 \|\|*

*darśanātsparśanādyasya ceto nirmalatāmiyāt \|*  
*uddhāṭitaṃ sadaivāsti dvāraṃ svargasya yatra hi \|\| 76 \|\|*

*avimukte mahākṣetre siddhakṣetre hi tatparam \|*  
*yatrāste paramaṃ liṃgaṃ madhyameśvarasaṃjñakam \|\| 77 \|\|*

75-77. “O I remember it now. I had forgotten it before. My desire has been realised. There is a Liṅga which is worshipped by the Siddhas. It bestows virtue, love, wealth and salvation. Its sight and touch purifies the mind. It opens the door to heaven. It is in the great holy centre, the holy centre of the Siddhas, in Avimukta. There is the great Liṅga Madhyameśvara by name.

## \|\| 9.44.78 \|\|

*na madhyameśvarādanyalliṃgaṃ kāśyāṃ hi vidyate \|*  
*yaddarśanārthamāyānti devāḥ parvaṇiparvaṇi \|\| 78 \|\|*

78\. There is no other Liṅga at Kāśī than Madhyameśvara for the sight of which the gods come here on every festive occasion.

## \|\| 9.44.79 \|\|

*ataḥ sevyo mahādevo madhyameśvarasaṃjñakaḥ \|*  
*asyārādhanato viprā bahavaḥ siddhimāgatāḥ \|\| 79 \|\|*

79\. Hence lord Madhyameśvara shall be resorted to. Many brahmins have attained Siddhis by propitiating Him.

## \|\| 9.44.80 \|\|

*yaḥ pradhānatayā kāśyā madhye tiṣṭhati śaṅkaraḥ \|*  
*svapurījana saukhyārthamato'sau madhyameśvaraḥ \|\| 80 \|\|*

80\. Śiva is called Madhyameśvara since he is stationed mainly in the centre of Kāśī for bestowing happiness on the people.

## \|\| 9.44.81 \|\|

*tumbururnāma gaṃdharvo devarṣirnāradastathā \|*  
*amumārādhya saṃpanno gānavidyāviśāradau \|\| 81 \|\|*

81\. It is by worshipping this deity that the Gandharva Tumburu and the celestial sage Nārada became proficient in the art of music.

## \|\| 9.44.82 \|\|

*amumeva samārādhya viṣṇurmokṣaprado'bhavat \|*  
*brahmā viṣṇuśca rudraśca sraṣṭṛpālakahārakāḥ \|\| 82 \|\|*

82\. It is by propitiating Him that Viṣṇu acquired the art of bestowing salvation; and Brahmā, Viṣṇu and Rudra became the creator, sustainer and the annihilates respectively.

## \|\| 9.44.83 \|\|

*dhanādhīśaḥ kubero'pi vāmadevo hi śaivarāṭ \|*  
*khaṭvāṃgo nāma bhūpālo'napatyo'patyavānabhūt \|\| 83 \|\|*

83\. Kubera became the lord of wealth; Vāmadeva the head of the devotees of Śiva; the childless Khaṭvāṅga was blessed with children.

## \|\| 9.44.84 \|\|

*apsarāścandrabhāmākhyā nṛtyantī nijabhāvataḥ \|*  
*sadehā kokilālāpā liṃgamadhye layaṃ gatā \|\| 84 \|\|*

84\. The celestial damsel Candrabhāmā of cuckoolike sweet voice was merged into the Liṅga even as she was dancing emotionally.

## \|\| 9.44.85 \|\|

*śrīkaro gopikāsūnuḥ sevitā madhyameśvaram \|*  
*gāṇapatyaṃ samālebhe śivasya karuṇātmanaḥ \|\| 85 \|\|*

85\. Śrīkara, son of the cowherdess, resorted to Madhyameśvara and attained the chieftainship of the Gaṇas of the benevolent Śiva.

## \|\| 9.44.86 \|\|

*bhārgavo gīṣpatiścobhau devau daityasurārcitau \|*  
*vidyāpāraṃgamau jātau prasādānmadhyameśituḥ \|\| 86 \|\|*

86\. Thanks to the favour of Madhyameśvara the two gods Śukra and Bṛhaspati honoured and respected by the Asuras and gods became the master of all lore.

## \|\| 9.44.87 \|\|

*ahamapyatra saṃpūjya madhyameśvaramīśvaram \|*  
*purāṇakartṛtāśaktiṃ prāpsyāmi tarasā dhuvam \|\| 87 \|\|*

87\. I too shall worship lord Madhyameśvara and attain the power to compose the Purāṇas immediately.

## \|\| 9.44.88 \|\|

*iti kṛtvā matiṃ dhīro vyāsaḥ satyavatīsutaḥ \|*  
*bhāgīrathyambhasi snātvā jagrāha niyamaṃ vratī \|\| 88 \|\|*

88\. After resolving thus the sage Vyāsa, son of Satyavatī, took his bath in the waters of the Gaṅgā and performed the sacred rites and observances.

## \|\| 9.44.89 \|\|

*kvacitparṇāśano bhūttvā phalaśākāśanaḥ kvacit \|*  
*vātabhugjalabhukkvāpi kvacinniraśanavratī \|\| 89 \|\|*

89\. Sometimes he took in only leaves, sometimes he lived on fruit and vegetable diet, sometimes on wind or water and sometimes he observed complete fast and performed the sacred rites.

## \|\| 9.44.90 \|\|

*ityādi niyamairyogī trikālaṃ madhyameśvaram \|*  
*pūjayāmāsa dharmmātmā nānāvṛkṣodbhavaiḥ phalaiḥ \|\| 90 \|\|*

90\. Thus by these observances of restraints the holy sage worshipped Madhyameśvara thrice a day with flowers of various trees.

## \|\| 9.44.91 \|\|

*itthaṃ bahutithe kāle vyatīte kālikāsutaḥ \|*  
*snātvā tripathagātoye yāvadāyāti sa prage \|\| 91 \|\|*

91\. A long time elasped thus. One day early in the morning the sage was returning after his bath in the waters of the Gaṅgā.

## \|\| 9.44.92 \|\|

*madhyameśvaramīśānaṃ bhaktābhīṣṭavarapradam \|*  
*tāvaddadarśa puṇyātmā madhyeliṃgaṃ maheśvaram \|\| 92 \|\|*

92\. The holy sage saw lord Madhyameśvara, the bestower of desires of the devotees, in the midst of the Liṅga.

## \|\| 9.44.93 \|\|

*umābhūṣitavāmāṃgaṃ vyāghracarmmottarīyakam \|*  
*jaṭājūṭacaladgaṃgātaraṃgaiścāruvigraham \|\| 93 \|\|*

93\. His left side was adorned by Umā. He was wearing the hide of a tiger as his upper cloth. His body appeared beautiful with the surging waves of the Gaṅgā amidst his matted hair.

## \|\| 9.44.94 \|\|

*lasacchāradabālenducandrikācanditālakam \|*  
*bhasmoddhūlitasarvāṅgaṃ karpūrārjunavigraham \|\| 94 \|\|*

94\. The moonlight of the autumnal crescent moon was forming bright patches in his forelocks. Bhasma was smeared all over his body. His body was as white as camphor or the Arjuna tree.

## \|\| 9.44.95 \|\|

*karṇāntāyatanetraṃ ca vidrumāruṇadacchadam \|*  
*paṃcavarṣākṛti bālaṃ bālakocitabhūṣaṇam \|\| 95 \|\|*

95\. He had assumed the form of a five-year-old boy with eyes extending upto his ears. His lips were as red as the coral. He was wearing ornaments befitting a boy.

## \|\| 9.44.96 \|\|

*dadhānaṃ koṭikandarppadarpahāni tanudyutim \|*  
*nagraṃ prahasitāsyābjaṃ gāyantaṃ sāma līlayā \|\| 96 \|\|*

96\. The lustre of his body subdued the arrogance of a crore Cupids. He was naked. His lotus-face was beaming with smiles. He was sportively singing Sāman hymns.

## \|\| 9.44.97-98 \|\|

*karuṇāpārapāthodhiṃ bhaktavatsalanāmakam \|*  
*āśutoṣamumākāntaṃ prasādasumukhaṃ haram \|\| 97 \|\|*

*samālokya stutiṃ cakre premagadgayā girā \|*  
*yogīnāmapyagamyantaṃ dīnabandhuṃ cidātmakam \|\| 98 \|\|*

97-98. On seeing Śiva, the lord of Umā, the shoreless ocean of mercy, known for his fondness of devotees and easily propitiated with delighted pleasing face, inaccessible even to the Yogins, the kinsman of the distressed and the Cit-formed lord, the sage sang the song of prayer with words choked with devotion.

## \|\| 9.44.99 \|\|

*vedavyāsa uvāca \|*  
*devadeva mahābhāga śaraṇāgatavatsala \|*  
*vāṅmanaḥ karmaduṣpāpa yogināmapyagocara \|\| 99 \|\|*

Vyāsa said:—

99\. “O lord of gods, O fortunate one who are favourably disposed to the refugees, O lord inaccessible to words, mind and activities, and the one that are invisible even to the Yogins.

## \|\| 9.44.100 \|\|

*mahimānaṃ na te vedā vidāmāsurumāpate \|*  
*tvameva jagataḥ kartā dhartā hartā tathaiva ca \|\| 100 \|\|*

100\. O lord of Umā, the Vedas too do not realise our greatness. You alone are the creator of the universe, the sustainer and the annihilator too.

## \|\| 9.44.101 \|\|

*tvamādyaḥ sarvadevānāṃ saccidānaṃda īśvaraḥ \|*  
*nāmagotre na vā te staḥ sarvajño'si sadāśiva \|\| 101 \|\|*

101\. You are the first and foremost of all gods. You are the existence, knowledge and bliss. You have no name or family lineage. O Sadāśiva you are omniscient.

## \|\| 9.44.102 \|\|

*tvameva paramaṃ brahma māyāpāśanivartakaḥ \|*  
*guṇatrayairna liptastvaṃ padmapatramivāṃbhasā \|\| 102 \|\|*

102\. You alone are the great Brahman; the untier of the knot of Māyā, unsullied by the three attributes as the leaf of the lotus unaffected by water.

## \|\| 9.44.103 \|\|

*na te janma na vā śīlaṃ na deśo na kulaṃ ca te \|*  
*itthaṃ bhūtopīśvaratvaṃ trilokyāḥ kāmamāvahe \|\| 103 \|\|*

103\. You have neither birth nor conduct of life. You have neither a native land nor a family. Even so you are the lord of the three worlds. You fulfil the desires of the three worlds.

## \|\| 9.44.104 \|\|

*na ca brahmā na lakṣmīśo na ca sendrā divaukasaḥ \|*  
*na yogīndrā vidustattvaṃ yasya taṃ tvāmupāsmahe \|\| 104 \|\|*

104\. Neither Brahmā nor Viṣṇu nor the gods including Indra nor the leading Yogins know your reality. We worship you of such features.

## \|\| 9.44.105 \|\|

*tvattaḥ sarvaṃ tvaṃ hi sarvaṃ gaurīśastvaṃ purāntakaḥ \|*  
*tvaṃ bālastvaṃ yuvā vṛddhastaṃ tvāṃ hṛdi yunajmyaham \|\| 105 \|\|*

105\. Everything originates from you. You are all—the lord of Gaurī, the slayer of the Tripuras, a boy, a youth, an aged man. I unite you with my heart.

## \|\| 9.44.106 \|\|

*namastasmai maheśāya bhaktadhyeyāya śambhave \|*  
*purāṇapuruṣāyāddhā śaṃkarāya parātmane \|\| 106 \|\|*

106\. Obeisance to lord Śiva, who deserves the worship of devotees, Obeisance to the Ancient Being; to Śiva the great Ātman,”

## \|\| 9.44.107 \|\|

*iti stutvā kṣitau yāvaddaṇḍavannipapāta saḥ \|*  
*tāvatsa bālo hṛṣṭātmā vedavyāsamabhāṣata \|\| 107 \|\|*

107\. After eulogising thus when the sage Vyāsa prostrated on the ground the delighted boy spoke to him.

## \|\| 9.44.108 \|\|

*varaṃ vṛṇīṣva bho yoginyaste manasi vartate \|*  
*nādeyaṃ vidyate kiṃcidbhaktādhīno yato'smyaham \|\| 108 \|\|*

108\. O Yogin, choose your boon, whatever be in your mind. There is nothing which cannot be granted to you since I am subservient to my devotees.

## \|\| 9.44.109 \|\|

*tata utthāya hṛṣṭātmā munirvyāso mahātapāḥ \|*  
*pratyabravītkimajñātaṃ sarvajñasya tava prabho \|\| 109 \|\|*

109\. Then the delighted sage Vyāsa of great penance stood up and replied—“O lord what is hidden from you the omniscient?”

## \|\| 9.44.110 \|\|

*sarvāntarātmā bhagavāñcharvaḥ sarvaprado bhavān \|*  
*yāñcāṃ pratiniyuṅkte māṃ kimīśo dainyakāriṇīm \|\| 110 \|\|*

110\. You are the immanent Soul and bestower of everything. Why does the lord force me for begging which is the cause of misery?

## \|\| 9.44.111 \|\|

*iti śrutvā vacastasya vyāsasyāmalacetasaḥ \|*  
*śuci smitvā mahādevo bālarūpadharo'bravīt \|\| 111 \|\|*

111\. On hearing these words of Vyāsa of pious mind, lord Śiva who had assumed the form of boy smiled and said:—

## \|\| 9.44.112 \|\|

*bāla uvāca \|*  
*tvayā brahmavidāṃ śreṣṭha yo'bhilāṣaḥ kṛto hṛdi \|*  
*acireṇaiva kālena sa bhaviṣyatyasaṃśayaḥ \|\| 112 \|\|*

The boy said:—

112\. O most excellent of those who know brahman, the desire you have cherished in your heart will undoubtedly be realised ere long.

## \|\| 9.44.113 \|\|

*kaṇṭhe sthitvā tava brahmannantaryāmyahamīśvaraḥ \|*  
*setihāsapurāṇāni samyaṅniryāpayāmyaham \|\| 113 \|\|*

113\. I the immanent lord shall station myself in your throat, O brahmin, and shall make you compose the Itihāsa and the Purāṇas efficiently.

## \|\| 9.44.114 \|\|

*abhilāṣāṣṭakaṃ puṇyaṃ stotrametattvayeritam \|*  
*varṣaṃ trikālaṃ paṭhanātkāmadaṃ śambhusadmani \|\| 114 \|\|*

114\. The holy hymn “Abhilāṣāṣṭaka” (Eight Verses of Desire) that you uttered now shall fulfil the desires of men who read or recite it in Śiva’s temple thrice a day for a year.

## \|\| 9.44.115 \|\|

*etatstotrasya paṭhanaṃ vidyābuddhivivarddhanam \|*  
*sarvasaṃpatkaraṃ proktaṃ dharmadaṃ mokṣadaṃ nṛṇām \|\| 115 \|\|*

115\. The recital of this hymn is conducive to the increase of learning and intellect. It is the cause of all riches and the bestower of virtue and salvation to men.

## \|\| 9.44.116 \|\|

*prātarutthāya susnāto liṃgamabhyarcya śāṃkaram \|*  
*varṣaṃ paṭhannidaṃ stotraṃ mūrkho'pi syādbṛhaspatiḥ \|\| 116 \|\|*

116\. Even a fool, getting up early in the morning taking his bath, worshipping the liṅga of Śiva and reciting this hymn for a year shall attain the status of Bṛhaspati (preceptor of the gods.)

## \|\| 9.44.117 \|\|

*striyā vā puruṣeṇāpi niyamālliṃgasannidhau \|*  
*varṣaṃ japtamidaṃ stotraṃ buddhiṃ vidyāñca varddhayet \|\| 117 \|\|*

117\. This hymn repeatedly recited for a year in the presence of the Liṅga whether by a woman or a man, duly observing restraints, shall increase the intellect and learning.

## \|\| 9.44.118 \|\|

*ityuktvā sa mahādevo bālo liṃge nyalīyata \|*  
*vyāso'pi muṃcannaśrūṇi śivapremākulo'bhavat \|\| 118 \|\|*

118\. After saying this the boy Śiva merged himself into the Liṅga. Shedding tears of love, Vyāsa was overcome with emotions.

## \|\| 9.44.119 \|\|

*evaṃ labdhavaro vyāso maheśānmadhyameśvarāta \|*  
*aṣṭādaśa purāṇāni praṇināya svalīlayā \|\| 119 \|\|*

119\. Vyāsa who thus secured the boon from the great lord Madhyameśvara composed the eighteen Purāṇas sportively.

## \|\| 9.44.120-122 \|\|

*brāhmaṃ pādmaṃ vaiṣṇavañca śaivaṃ bhāgavataṃ tathā \|*  
*bhaviṣyaṃ nāradīyaṃ ca mārkaṃḍeyamataḥ param \|\| 120 \|\|*

*āgneyaṃ brahmavaivarta liṃgaṃ vārāhameva ca \|*  
*vāmanākhyaṃ tataḥ kaurmaṃ mātsyaṃ gāruḍameva ca \|\| 121 \|\|*

*skāndaṃ tathaiva brahmāṇḍākhyaṃ purāṇaṃ ca kīrtitam \|*  
*yaśasyaṃ puṇyadaṃ nṝṇāṃ śrotṝṇāṃ śāṃkaraṃ yaśa \|\| 122 \|\|*

120-122. The eighteen Purāṇas Brāhma, Pādma. Vaiṣṇava, Śaiva, Bhāgavata, Bhaviṣya, Nāradīya, Mārkaṇḍeya, Āgneya, Brahmavaivarta, Laiṅga, Vārāha, Vāmana Kaurma, Mātsya, Gāruḍa, Skānda and Brahmāṇḍa are conducive to fame and merit to those who listen to the glory of Śiva.

## \|\| 9.44.123 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*aṣṭādaśapurāṇānāmpūrvaṃ nāmoditantvayā \|*  
*kuru nirvacanaṃ teṣāmidānīṃ vedavittama \|\| 123 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

123\. You have enumerated the eighteen Purāṇas. O foremost among the knowers of the Vedas please define them now.

## \|\| 9.44.124 \|\|

*vyāsa uvāca \|*  
*ayameva kṛtaḥ praśnastaṇḍinā brahmayoninā \|*  
*nandikeśvaramuddiśya sa yadāha bravīmi tat \|\| 124 \|\|*

Vyāsa said:—

124\. This selfsame question was put to Nandikeśvara by Taṇḍi the Brahminical Yogin. I shall tell you what he had said.

## \|\| 9.44.125 \|\|

*nandikeśvara uvāca \|*  
*yatra vaktā svayantaṇḍe brahmā sākṣāccaturmukhaḥ \|*  
*tasmādbrahmaṃ samākhyātaṃ purāṇaṃ prathamaṃ mune \|\| 125 \|\|*

Nandikeśvara said:—

125\. O Taṇḍi, Brahma the four-faced deity is the main speaker. Hence, O sage, the first Purāṇa is called Brāhma.

## \|\| 9.44.126 \|\|

*padmakalpasya māhātmyantatra yasyāmudāhṛtam \|*  
*tasmātpādmaṃ samākhyātaṃ purāṇaṃ ca dvitīyakam \|\| 126 \|\|*

126\. The second Purāṇa is called Padma. It is so called because it mentions the greatness of the Padmakalpa.

## \|\| 9.44.127 \|\|

*parāśarakṛtaṃ yattu purāṇaṃ viṣṇubodhakam \|*  
*tadeva vyāsakathitaṃ putrapitrorabhedataḥ \|\| 127 \|\|*

127\. Another Purāṇa composed by Parāśara and enlightening the details of Viṣṇu is called Vaiṣṇava Purāṇa. It is said to be composed by Vyāsa since there is no difference between father and son.

## \|\| 9.44.128 \|\|

*yatra pūrvottare khaṇḍe śivasya caritaṃ bahu \|*  
*śaivametatpurāṇaṃ hi purāṇajñā vadanti ca \|\| 128 \|\|*

128\. Those who know the Purāṇas speak that purāṇa as Śiva Purāṇa wherein there are many stories of Śiva in its earlier and later forms.

## \|\| 9.44.129 \|\|

*bhagavatyāśca durgāyāścaritaṃ yatra vidyate \|*  
*tattu bhāgavataṃ proktaṃ nanu devīpurāṇakam \|\| 129 \|\|*

129\. Where the stories of the goddess Durgā are mentioned, it is said to be Bhāgavata Purāṇa as well as Devīpurāṇa.

## \|\| 9.44.130-131 \|\|

*nāradoktaṃ purāṇantu nāradīyaṃ pracakṣate \|*  
*yatra vaktā'bhavattaṇḍe mārkaṇḍeyo mahāmuniḥ \|\| 130 \|\|*

*mārkaṇḍeyapurāṇaṃ hi tadākhyātaṃ ca saptamam \|*  
*agniyogāttadāgneyaṃ bhaviṣyokterbhaviṣyakam \|\| 131 \|\|*

130-131. The Purāṇa narrated by Nārada is called Nāradīya. The seventh Purāṇa is called, O Taṇḍi, Mārkaṇḍeya because the great sage Mārkaṇḍeya is the speaker therein. Since it is related to the fire-god, the Purāṇa is called Āgneya. Since it recounts future events the Purāṇa is called the Bhaviṣya Purāṇa.

## \|\| 9.44.132 \|\|

*vivartanādbrahmaṇastu brahmavaivartamucyate \|*  
*liṃgasya caritoktatvātpurāṇaṃ liṃgamucyate \|\| 132 \|\|*

132\. Since the transformation of Brahman is narrated the Purāṇa is called Brahmavaivarta. Since the story of Liṅga is mentioned it is called Liṅga Purāṇa.

## \|\| 9.44.133-135 \|\|

*varāhasya ca vārāhaṃ purāṇaṃ dvādaśaṃ mune \|*  
*yatra skandaḥ svayaṃ śrotā vaktā sākṣānmaheśvaraḥ \|\| 133 \|\|*

*tattu skāndaṃ samākhyātaṃ vāmanasya tu vāmanam \|*  
*kaurmaṃ kūrmasya caritaṃ mātsyaṃ matsyena kīrtitam \|\| 134 \|\|*

*garuḍastu svayaṃ vaktā yattadgāruḍasaṃjñakam \|*  
*brahmāṇḍacaritoktatvādbrahmāṇḍaṃ parikīrtitam \|\| 135 \|\|*

133-135. O sage, the twelfth Purāṇa Varāha is so called because it contains the story of Varāha, the great Boar. In the Skanda Purāṇa the speaker is lord Śiva himself and the listener is Skanda. In the Vāmana Puraṇa the story of Vāmana (the Dwarf-god) is mentioned. The Kūrma Purāṇa contains the story of Kūrma (the tortoise-god). The Matsya Purāṇa is so called because it is expounded by Matsya. The Garuḍa Purāṇa is so called because the speaker is Garuḍa himself. Since the story of the entire cosmic egg is mentioned, the last Purāṇa is called Brahmāṇḍa Purāṇa.

## \|\| 9.44.136 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*ayameva mayā'kāri praśno vyāsāya dhīmate \|*  
*tataḥ sarvapurāṇānāṃ mayā nirvacanaṃ śrutam \|\| 136 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

136\. This question was put by me to the intelligent Vyāsa. From him the definitions of the Purāṇas were heard by me.

## \|\| 9.44.137 \|\|

*evaṃ vyāsasamutpannaḥ satyavatyāṃ parāśarāt \|*  
*purāṇasaṃhitāścakre mahābhāratamuttamam \|\| 137 \|\|*

137\. Thus was Vyāsa born of Satyavatī and Parāśara. He composed the excellent Mahābhārata and the Purāṇa Saṃhitās.

## \|\| 9.44.138 \|\|

*parāśareṇa saṃyogaḥ punaḥ śantanunā yathā \|*  
*satyavatyā iva brahmannaḥ saṃśayitumarhasi \|\| 138 \|\|*

138\. O brahmin, you shall not entertain a doubt as to why Satyavatī had sexual intercourse with Parāśara and Śantanu.

## \|\| 9.44.139 \|\|

*sakāraṇeyamutpattiḥ kathitāścaryyakāriṇī \|*  
*mahatāṃ carite caiva guṇā grāhyā vicakṣaṇaiḥ \|\| 139 \|\|*

139\. This wondrous birth has sufficient reasons behind it. In the story of great men the good qualities shall be grasped by the intelligent people.

## \|\| 9.44.140 \|\|

*idaṃ rahasyaṃ paramaṃ yaḥ śṛṇoti paṭhatyapi \|*  
*sa sarvapāpanirmukta ṛṣiloke mahīyate \|\| 140 \|\|*

140\. He who reads or listens to this great secret is rid of all sins and is honoured in the world of sages.

## Chapter 45 - The incarnation of Mahākālikā

## \|\| 9.45.1 \|\|

*munaya ūcuḥ \|*  
*śrutvā śaṃbhoḥ kathā ramyā nānākhyānasamanvitā \|*  
*nānāvatāra saṃyuktā bhuktimuktipradā nṛṇām \|\| 1 \|\|*

Note: For the close similarity of the form and contents of the present and succeeding chapters ending with Umāsaṃhitā compare Mārkaṇḍeya-purāṇa: the narrative of Durgā.

The sages said:—

1\. The beautiful episode of Śiva with various incidental anecdotes and narratives of various incarnations, is heard. It yields both worldly pleasures and salvation to men.

## \|\| 9.45.2 \|\|

*idānīṃ śrotumicchāmastvatto brahmavidāṃ vara \|*  
*caritraṃ jagadaṃbāyā bhagavatyā manoharam \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. O foremost among those who know Brahman, we wish to hear from you the beautiful story of the mother of the universe, the goddess.

## \|\| 9.45.3 \|\|

*parabrahma maheśasya śaktirādyā sanātanī \|*  
*umā yā samabhikhyātā trailokyajananī parā \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. The first primordial Śakti of Śiva the great Brahman, who is called Umā, is the great mother of the three worlds.

## \|\| 9.45.4 \|\|

*satī hemavatī tasyā avatāradvayaṃ śrutam \|*  
*aparānavatārāṃstvaṃ brūhi sūt mahāmate \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. Her two incarnations—Satī and Haimavatī, have been heard, O intelligent Sūta. Please mention her other incarnations too.

## \|\| 9.45.5 \|\|

*ko virajyeta matimān guṇaśravaṇakarmaṇi \|*  
*śrīmāturjñānino yāni na tyajanti kadācana \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. Which intelligent man will be reluctant to hear the good attributes of the glorious mother? Wise men never abandon them.

## \|\| 9.45.6 \|\|

*sūta uvāca \|*  
*dhanyā yūyaṃ mahātmānaḥ kṛtakṛtyāḥ stha sarvadā \|*  
*yatpṛcchatha parāmbāyā umāyāścaritaṃ mahat \|\| 6 \|\|*

Sūta said:—

6\. You are all blessed and contented noble souls in as much as you inquire about the great story of Umā, the great mother.

## \|\| 9.45.7 \|\|

*śṛṇvatāṃ pṛcchatāṃ caiva tathā vācayatāṃ ca tat \|*  
*pādāmbujarajāṃsyeva tīrthāni munayo viduḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

7\. Sages consider the dust-particles of the feet of those who listen, narrate and inquire about it, on a par with holy centres.

## \|\| 9.45.8 \|\|

*te dhanyā kṛtakṛtyāḥ syurdhanyā teṣāṃ prasūḥ kulam \|*  
*yeṣāṃ cittaṃ bhavellīnaṃ śrīdevyāṃ parasaṃvidi \|\| 8 \|\|*

8\. Blessed and contented are they, their parents and their family, whose mind is merged in the goddess, the great Knowledge.

## \|\| 9.45.9 \|\|

*ye na stuvanti deveśīṃ sarvakāraṇakāraṇām \|*  
*māyāguṇairmohitāssyurhatabhāgyā na saṃśayaḥ \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Those who do not eulogise the goddess of the gods, the cause of all causes are deluded by the attributes of Māyā. They are unfortunate. There is no doubt in this.

## \|\| 9.45.10 \|\|

*na bhajanti mahādevīṃ karuṇārasasāgarām \|*  
*andhakūpe patantyete ghore saṃsārarūpiṇi \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. Those who do not worship the great goddess, the ocean of the juice of mercy, fall in the blind well in the form of the cruel worldly existence.

## \|\| 9.45.11 \|\|

*gaṃgāṃ vihāya tṛptyarthaṃ maruvāri yathā vrajet \|*  
*vihāya devīṃ tadbhinnaṃ tathā devāntaraṃ vrajet \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Abandoning the goddess and resorting to another deity is as bad as abandoning the Gaṅgā and resorting to water in the desert for the sake of satisfaction.

## \|\| 9.45.12 \|\|

*yasyāḥ smaraṇamātreṇa puruṣārthacatuṣṭayam \|*  
*anāyāsena labhate kastyajettāṃ narottamaḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. Which excellent man will abandon her whose very remembrance bestows all the four aims of life without any strain?

## \|\| 9.45.13 \|\|

*etatpṛṣṭaḥ purā medhāssurathena mahātmanā \|*  
*yaduktaṃ medhasā pūrvaṃ tacchṛṇuṣva vadāmi te \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. This same question was put to Medhas by the noble Suratha. Please listen. I shall narrate what was mentioned by Medhas.

## \|\| 9.45.14 \|\|

*svārociṣentare pūrvaṃ viratho nāma pārthivaḥ \|*  
*surathastasya putro'bhūnmahābalaparākramaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. Formerly in the Svārociṣa Manvantara there was a king Viratha. Suratha his son was of great strength and exploit.

## \|\| 9.45.15 \|\|

*dānaśauṇḍaḥ satyavādī svadharmma kuśalaḥ kṛtī \|*  
*devībhakto dayāsindhuḥ prajānāṃ paripālakaḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

15\. He was very munificent, truthful, efficient in duties, a devotee of the goddess, an ocean of kindness and the protector of his subjects.

## \|\| 9.45.16 \|\|

*pṛthivīṃ śāsatastasya pākaśāsanatejasaḥ \|*  
*babhūburnava ye bhūpāḥ pṛthvīgrahaṇatatparāḥ \|\| 16 \|\|*

16\. While he was thus ruling over the earth, with the brilliance of Indra, nine other kings became desirous of seizing his land.

## \|\| 9.45.17 \|\|

*kolānāmnīṃ rājadhānīṃ rurudhustasya bhūpateḥ \|*  
*taissamantumulaṃ yuddhaṃ samapadyata dāruṇam \|\| 17 \|\|*

17\. They laid siege to his capital Kolā. The king had a terrible war with them.

## \|\| 9.45.18 \|\|

*yuddhe sa nirjito bhūpaḥ prabalaistairdviṣadgaṇaiḥ \|*  
*ujjāsitacca kolāyā hṛtvā rājyamaśeṣataḥ \|\| 18 \|\|*

18\. That king was defeated by his powerful enemies in the war. His kingdom was seized and he was banished from his city Kolā.

## \|\| 9.45.19 \|\|

*sa rājā svapurīmetyākarodrājyaṃ svamaṃtribhiḥ \|*  
*tatrāpi ca mahaḥpakṣairvipakṣaissa parājitaḥ \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. The king went to another city of his along with his ministers. There too he was defeated by his enemies who had a large following.

## \|\| 9.45.20 \|\|

*daivācchatrutvamāpannai ramātyapramukhairgaṇaiḥ \|*  
*kośasthitaṃ ca yadvittaṃ tatsarvaṃ cātmasātkṛtam \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. His ministers and officers became inimical to him due to adverse fate. They took away all the wealth from the treasury.

## \|\| 9.45.21 \|\|

*tatassa nirgato rājā nagarānmṛgayā chalāt \|*  
*asahāyo'śvamāruhya jagāma gahanaṃ vanam \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. The king left his city pretending to go out for hunting. Alone he got up on a horse and went to the dense forest.

## \|\| 9.45.22-23 \|\|

*itastatastatra gacchanrājā munivarāśramam \|*  
*dadarśa kusumārāmabhrājitaṃ sarvatodiśam \|\| 22 \|\|*

*vedadhvanisamākīrṇaṃ śāntajantusamāśritam \|*  
*śiṣyaiḥ praśiṣyaistacchiṣyaissamantātpariveṣṭitam \|\| 23 \|\|*

22-23. Going about here and there, the king saw the hermitage of a great sage. It was shining all round with flower gardens. Everywhere the sound of Vedic hymns was heard. All the animals there were quiet and peaceful. The disciples and the disciples of their disciples moved here and there.

## \|\| 9.45.24 \|\|

*vyāghrādayo mahāvīryā alpavīryānmahāmate \|*  
*tadāśrame na bādhante dvijavaryyaprabhāvataḥ \|\| 24 \|\|*

24\. O intelligent one, tigers and other ferocious and powerful animals did not harass the animals of less strength in that hermitage, thanks to the power of the leading brahmin.

## \|\| 9.45.25 \|\|

*uvāsa tatra nṛpatirmahākāruṇiko budhaḥ \|*  
*satkṛto munināthena suvaco bhojanāsanaiḥ \|\| 25 \|\|*

25\. The king who was very kind and learned was welcomed and honoured by the leading sage with pleasing words, accommodation and food and stayed there.

## \|\| 9.45.26-27 \|\|

*ekadā sa mahārājaściṃtāmāpa duratyayām \|*  
*aho me hīnabhāgyasya durbuddherhīnatejasaḥ \|\| 26 \|\|*

*hṛtaṃ rājyamaśeṣeṇa śatruvargairmadoddhataiḥ \|*  
*matpūrvai rakṣitaṃ rājyaṃ śatrubhirbhujyate'dhunā \|\| 27 \|\|*

26-27. Once the king began to think anxiously—“Alas, my kingdom has been seized by my haughty enemies. I am unfortunate. My intellect is confounded. I have lost my splendour. The kingdom which had been well guarded by my ancestors is now enjoyed by my enemies.

## \|\| 9.45.28 \|\|

*mādṛśaścaitravaṃśesminna kopyāsīnmahīpatiḥ \|*  
*kiṃ karomi kva gacchāmi kathaṃ rājyaṃ labhemahi \|\| 28 \|\|*

28\. There was no king in this family as weak as I. What shall I do? Where shall I go? How shall I get the kingdom back?

## \|\| 9.45.29 \|\|

*amātyā maṃtriṇaścaiva māmakā ye sanātanāḥ \|*  
*na jāne kaṃ ca nṛpatiṃ samāsādyādhunāsate \|\| 29 \|\|*

29\. My hereditary ministers and counsellors have left me. I do not know which king they serve now.

## \|\| 9.45.30-32 \|\|

*vināśya rājyamadhunā na jāne kāṃ gatiṃ gatāḥ \|*  
*raṇabhūmimahotsāhā arivarganikartanāḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

*māmakā ye mahāśūrā nṛpamanyaṃ bhajanti te \|*  
*parvatābhā gajā aśvā vātavadvegagāminaḥ \|\| 31 \|\|*

*pūrvapūrvārjitaḥ kośaḥ pālyate tairnavādhunā \|*  
*evaṃ mohavaśaṃ yāto rājā paramadhārmikaḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

30-32. I do not know what plight they are in, after the ruination of the kingdom. My heroic soldiers, very enthusiastic in the battle, slayers of enemies, now serve another king. Huge elephants like mountains, horses fast as wind have gone away. Is the traditional treasury accumulated by my ancestors well guarded or not? Thus the very virtuous king became much deluded.

## \|\| 9.45.33-35 \|\|

*etasminnaṃtare tatra vaiśyaḥ kaścitsamāgataḥ \|*  
*rājā papraccha kastvaṃ bhoḥ kimarthamiha cāgataḥ \|\| 33 \|\|*

*durmanā lakṣyase kasmādetanme brūhi sāmpratam \|*  
*ityākarṇya vaco ramyaṃ narapālena bhāṣitam \|\| 34 \|\|*

*dṛgbhyāṃ vimuṃcannaśrūṇi samādhirvaiśyapuṃgavaḥ \|*  
*pratyuvāca mahīpālaṃ praṇayāvanato giram \|\| 35 \|\|*

33-35. In the meantime a certain Vaiśya came there. The king asked him—“Sir, who are you? Why have you come here? Why do you appear to be very morose and dejected? Please tell me this, now.”

On hearing the sweet words of the king, the leading Vaiśya Samādhi shed tears. He spoke to the king in words full of humility and friendship.

## \|\| 9.45.36-38 \|\|

*\|\| vaiśya uvāca \|*  
*samādhirnāma vaiśyohaṃ dhanivaṃśasamudbhavaḥ \|*  
*putradārādibhistyakto dhanalobhānmahīpate \|\| 36 \|\|*

*sa vanamabhyāgato rājanduḥkhitaḥ svena karmaṇā \|*  
*sohaṃ putraprapautrāṇāṃ kalatrāṇāṃ tathaiva ca \|\| 37 \|\|*

*bhrātṝṇāṃ bhrātṛputrāṇāṃ pareṣāṃ suhṛdāṃ tathā \|*  
*na vedmi kuśalaṃ samyakkaruṇāsāgara prabho \|\| 38 \|\|*

The Vaiśya said:—

36-38. “O king, I am a Vaiśya Samādhi, born of a rich family. Out of greed for my wealth I have been abandoned by my wife, sons and others. I am depressed by my past Karman. O king, I have come here to the forest. I am worried about my sons, grandsons, wife, brothers, their sons and friends. O lord, ocean of mercy, I am in dark about their welfare.

## \|\| 9.45.39 \|\|

*rājovāca \|*  
*niṣkāsito yaiḥ putrādyairdurvṛttairdhanagardhibhiḥ \|*  
*teṣu kiṃ bhavatā prītiḥ kriyate mūrkhajantuvat \|\| 39 \|\|*

The king said:—

39\. How is it that you love, even as senseless animals do, those sons and others who are of evil conduct and covetous for wealth and by whom you have been ousted?

## \|\| 9.45.40 \|\|

*vaiśya uvāca \|*  
*samyaguktaṃ tvayā rājanvacaḥ sārārthabṛṃhitam \|*  
*tathāpi snehapāśena mohyate'tīva me manaḥ \|\| 40 \|\|*

The Vaiśya said:—

40\. O king, words pregnant with substantial sense have been uttered by you. Still my mind is deluded by the tie of love.

## \|\| 9.45.41 \|\|

*evaṃ mohākulau vaiśyapārthivau munisattama \|*  
*jagmaturmunivaryasya medhasaḥ sannidhintadā \|\| 41 \|\|*

41\. O excellent sage, the king and the Vaiśya, both of them equally deluded then approached the sage Medhas.

## \|\| 9.45.42 \|\|

*sa vaiśyarājasahito nararājaḥ pratāpavān \|*  
*praṇanāma mahāvīraḥ śirasā yogināṃ varam \|\| 42 \|\|*

42\. The valorous king accompanied by the chief of the Vaiśyas bowed his head to the leading Yogin.

## \|\| 9.45.43 \|\|

*baddhvāñjalimimāṃ vācamuvāca nṛpatirmunim \|*  
*bhagavannāvayormohaṃ chettumarhasi sāmpratam \|\| 43 \|\|*

43\. With palms joined in reverence the king spoke to the sage:—“O holy lord, it behoves you now to dispel our delusion.

## \|\| 9.45.44 \|\|

*ahaṃ rājaśriyā tyakto gahanaṃ vanamāśritaḥ \|*  
*tathāpi hṛtarājyasya toṣo naivābhijāyate \|\| 44 \|\|*

44\. Abandoned by the royal glory I have resorted to this dense forest. Still I am not content and happy since my kingdom has been taken away.

## \|\| 9.45.45 \|\|

*ayaṃ ca vaiśyassvajanairdārādyairniṣkṛto gṛhāt \|*  
*tathāpyetasya mamatā na nivṛttiṃ samaśnute \|\| 45 \|\|*

45\. This Vaiśya has been expelled from his house by his people, wife and others. Still his sense of affinity and kinship with them does not go off.

Sivapurāṇa

## \|\| 9.45.46 \|\|

*kimatra kāraṇaṃ brūhi jñāninorapi no manaḥ \|*  
*mohena vyākulaṃ jātaṃ mahatyeṣāṃ hi mūrkhatā \|\| 46 \|\|*

46\. What is the reason hereof? Please say. Wise though we are, our minds are afflicted and agitated by delusion. This is great foolishness.

## \|\| 9.45.47 \|\|

*ṛṣi uvāca \|*  
*mahāmāyā jagaddhātrī śaktirūpā sanātanī \|*  
*sā mohayati sarveṣāṃ samākṛṣya manāṃsi vai \|\| 47 \|\|*

The sage said:—

47\. The great Māyā in the form of the eternal Śakti is the material cause of the universe. It is this that drags the minds of all and makes them deluded.

## \|\| 9.45.48 \|\|

*brahmādayassurāssarve yanmāyāmohitāḥ prabho \|*  
*na jānanti parantattvaṃ manuṣyāṇāṃ ca kā kathā \|\| 48 \|\|*

48\. O lord, Brahmā and other gods deluded by this Māyā do not realise the truth. What then is the story of men?

## \|\| 9.45.49 \|\|

*sā sṛjatyakhilaṃ viśvaṃ saiva pālayatīti ca \|*  
*saiva saṃharate kāle triguṇā parameśvarī \|\| 49 \|\|*

49\. That alone, Parameśvarī of three attributes, creates the universe; she alone sustains it and she alone destroys it at the proper time.

## \|\| 9.45.50 \|\|

*yasyopari prasannā sā varadā kāmarūpiṇī \|*  
*sa eva mohamatyeti nānyathā nṛpasattama \|\| 50 \|\|*

50\. O excellent king, only he surmounts this delusion on whom the favourite goddess who assumes forms as she pleases becomes delighted.

## \|\| 9.45.51 \|\|

*rājovāca \|*  
*kā sā devī mahāmāyā yā ca mohayate'khilān \|*  
*kathaṃ jātā ca sā devī kṛpayā vada me mune \|\| 51 \|\|*

The king said:—

51\. O sage, who is that Goddess? Who is that great Māyā who fascinates all? How was that goddess born? Please tell me.

## \|\| 9.45.52-53 \|\|

*ṛṣiruvāca \|*  
*jagatyekārṇave jāte śeṣamāstīrya yogarāṭ \|*  
*yoganidrāmupāśritya yadā suṣvāpa keśavaḥ \|\| 52 \|\|*

*tadā dvāvasurau jātau viṣṇau karṇamalena vai \|*  
*madhukaiṭabhanāmānau vikhyātau pṛthivītale \|\| 53 \|\|*

The sage said:—

52-53. When the whole universe had been one great expanse of water, a vast sea, when Keśava, the king of Yogins, resorted to Yogic slumber and was sleeping on Śeṣa, two Asuras were born of the dirt in the ears of Viṣṇu. They became notorious on the surface of the world in the names of Madhu and Kaiṭabha.

## \|\| 9.45.54 \|\|

*pralayārkaprabhau ghorau mahākāyau mahāhanū \|*  
*daṃṣdrākarālavadanau bhakṣayantau jaganti vā \|\| 54 \|\|*

54\. They were terrible with huge bodies. They had the dazzling brilliance of the sun at the time of dissolution They had huge jaw bones. Their faces were hideous with curved fangs. They seemed to devour all the worlds.

## \|\| 9.45.55 \|\|

*tau dṛṣṭvā bhagavannābhipaṅkaje kamalāsanam \|*  
*hananāyodyatāvāstāṃ kastvaṃ bhoriti vādinau \|\| 55 \|\|*

55\. On seeing the lotus-seated deity in the umbilical lotus\[3\] of the lord, the two Asuras shouted “Hey, who are you?” and attempted to kill him.

## \|\| 9.45.56 \|\|

*samālokyaṃ tu tau daityau surajyeṣṭho janārdanam \|*  
*śayānaṃ ca payombhodhau tuṣṭāva parameśvarīm \|\| 56 \|\|*

56\. On seeing the two Daityas and observing that Viṣṇu was still lying in the vast milky ocean, Brahmā eulogised Parameśvarī.

## \|\| 9.45.57 \|\|

*brahmovāca \|*  
*rakṣarakṣa mahāmāye śaraṇāgatavatsale \|*  
*etābhyāṃ ghorarūpābhyāṃ daityābhyāṃ jagadambike \|\| 57 \|\|*

Brahmā said:—

57\. O Mahāmāyā, save me, save me, O goddess favourably disposed to those who seek refuge in you, O mother of the universe, save me from these Daityas of hideous features.

## \|\| 9.45.58-59 \|\|

*praṇamāmi mahāmāyāṃ yoganidrāmumāṃ satīm \|*  
*kālarātriṃ mahārātriṃ moharātriṃ parātparām \|\| 58 \|\|*

*tridevajananīṃ nityāṃ bhaktābhīṣṭaphalapradām \|*  
*pālinīṃ sarvadevānāṃ karuṇāvaruṇālayam \|\| 59 \|\|*

58-59. I bow to the great Māyā, the Yogic slumber, Umā, Satī, Kālarātri, Mahārātri, Moharātri, greater than the greatest, the mother of the three deities, the eternal, the bestower of the fruits of the cherished desires of the devotees, the protectress of the gods and the ocean of mercy.

## \|\| 9.45.60 \|\|

*tvatprabhāvādahaṃ brahmā mādhavo girijāpatiḥ \|*  
*sṛjatyavati saṃsāraṃ kāle saṃharatīti ca \|\| 60 \|\|*

60\. It is by your power that Brahmā creates the world, Viṣṇu protects it and Śiva destroys it at the opportune time.

## \|\| 9.45.61 \|\|

*tvaṃ svāhā tvaṃ svadhā tvaṃ hrīstvaṃ buddhirvimalā matā \|*  
*tuṣṭiḥ puṣṭistvamevāmba śāntiḥ kṣāntiḥ kṣudhā dayā \|\| 61 \|\|*

61\. O Mother, you are Svāhā, Svadhā, Hrī, the unalloyed intellect, Tuṣṭi (satisfaction), Puṣṭi (nourishment), Śānti (peace), Kṣānti (forbearance), Kṣudhā (hunger) and mercy itself.

## \|\| 9.45.62 \|\|

*viṣṇu māyā tvamevāmba tvameva cetanā matā \|*  
*tvaṃ śaktiḥ paramā proktā lajjā tṛṣṇā tvameva ca \|\| 62 \|\|*

62\. O mother, you are the Māyā of Viṣṇu, the very consciousness, the great Śakti, Lajjā (bashfulness) and Tṛṣṇā (thirst).

## \|\| 9.45.63 \|\|

*bhrāntistvaṃ smṛtirūpā tvaṃ mātṛrūpeṇa saṃsthitā \|*  
*tvaṃ lakṣmīrbhavane puṃsāṃ puṇyākṣarapravartinām \|\| 63 \|\|*

63\. You are Bhrānti (Illusion). You appear in the form of Smṛti (Memory): you stay assuming the form of mother. You are Lakṣmī in the house of those who engage themselves in meritorious activities.

## \|\| 9.45.64 \|\|

*tvaṃ jātistvaṃ matā vṛttirvyāptirūpā tvameva hi \|*  
*tvameva cittirūpeṇa vyāpya kṛtsnaṃ pratiṣṭhitā \|\| 64 \|\|*

64\. You are Jāti (Birth), Vṛtti (cause of activity) and Vyāpti (pervasiveness.) You pervade everything in the form of intelligence.

## \|\| 9.45.65 \|\|

*sā tvametau durādharṣāvasurau mohayāmbike \|*  
*prabodhaya jagadyone nārāyaṇamajaṃ vibhum \|\| 65 \|\|*

65\. O Mother, please delude these unthwartable Asuras. O origin of the universe, please waken Viṣṇu the unborn lord.”

## \|\| 9.45.66-67 \|\|

*ṛṣiruvāca \|*  
*brahmaṇā prārthitā seyaṃ madhukaiṭabhanāśane \|*  
*mahāvidyājagaddhātrī sarvavidyādhidevatā \|\| 66 \|\|*

*dvādaśyāṃ phālgunasyaiva śuklāyāṃ samabhūnnṛpa \|*  
*mahākālīti vikhyātā śaktistrailokyamohinī \|\| 67 \|\|*

The sage said:—

66-67. O king, on being requested by Brahmā for the destruction of Madhu and Kaiṭabha, the mother of the universe, the great Vidyā, the presiding deity of all Vidyās, Śakti the enchantress of the three worlds manifested herself as Mahākālī on the twelfth Tithi of the bright half of the month of Phālguna.

## \|\| 9.45.68 \|\|

*tato'bhavadviyadvāṇī mā bhaiṣīḥ kamalāsana \|*  
*kaṇṭakaṃ nāśayāmyadya hatvājau madhukaiṭabhau \|\| 68 \|\|*

68\. Then a celestial voice arose:—“O lotus-seated one, do not be afraid. I shall remove the thorn after killing Madhu and Kaiṭabha in the battle.”

## \|\| 9.45.69 \|\|

*ityuktvā sā mahāmāyā netravaktrādito hareḥ \|*  
*nirgamya darśane tasthau brahmaṇo'vyaktajanmanaḥ \|\| 69 \|\|*

69\. After saying this and coming out of the eyes, mouth etc. of Viṣṇu, the great Māyā stood before Brahmā of unmanifested birth.

## \|\| 9.45.70 \|\|

*uttasthau ca hṛṣīkeśo devadevo janārdanaḥ \|*  
*sa dadarśa puro daityo madhukaiṭabhasaṃjñakau \|\| 70 \|\|*

70\. Viṣṇu, the lord of gods stood up and saw in front of him the Daityas Madhu and Kaiṭabha.

## \|\| 9.45.71 \|\|

*tābhyāṃ pravavṛtte yuddhaṃ viṣṇoratulatejasaḥ \|*  
*pañcavarṣasahasrāṇi bāhuyuddhamabhūttadā \|\| 71 \|\|*

71\. A battle ensued between Viṣṇu of unequalled splendour and the two Daityas lasting for five thousand years. Then there ensued a hand-to-hand fight.

## \|\| 9.45.72 \|\|

*mahāmāyāprabhāveṇa mohito dānavottamau \|*  
*jajalpatū ramākāntaṃ gṛhāṇa varamīpsitam \|\| 72 \|\|*

72\. Deluded by the powerful great Māyā, the excellent Dānava spoke to the lord Viṣṇu—“Ask for whatever boon you desire to choose.”

## \|\| 9.45.73 \|\|

*nārāyaṇa uvāca \|*  
*mayi prasannau yadi vāṃ dīyatāmeṣa me varaḥ \|*  
*mama vadhyāvubhau nānyaṃ yuvābhyāṃ prārthaye varam \|\| 73 \|\|*

Nārāyaṇa said:—

73\. If you are pleased with me grant me this boon that I may kill you. I do not request for any other boon.

## \|\| 9.45.74 \|\|

*ṛthiruvāca \|*  
*ekārṇavāṃ mahīṃ dṛṣṭvā procatuḥ keśavaṃ vacaḥ \|*  
*āvāṃ jahi na yatrāsau dharaṇī payasā' 'plutā \|\| 74 \|\|*

The sage said:—

74\. On seeing the earth covered by a great expanse of ocean they spoke to Keśava:—“Kill us in a spot where the earth is not covered with water.”

## \|\| 9.45.75 \|\|

*tathāstu procya bhagavāṃścakramutthāpya sūjjvalam \|*  
*ciccheda śirasī kṛtvā svakīyajaghane tayoḥ \|\| 75 \|\|*

75\. After giving his consent the lord lifted up his dazzling discus, placed their heads on his loins and cut them off.

## \|\| 9.45.76 \|\|

*evante kathito rājankālikāyāssamudbhavaḥ \|*  
*mahālakṣmyāstathotpattiṃ niśāmaya mahāmate \|\| 76 \|\|*

76\. O king, thus I have told you about the origin of Kālikā. O intelligent one, listen to the origin of Mahālakṣmī now.

## \|\| 9.45.77 \|\|

*nirvikārādi sākārā nirākārāpi devyumā \|*  
*devānāṃ tāpanāśārthaṃ prādurāsīdyugeyuge \|\| 77 \|\|*

77\. Although free from aberrations, and devoid of forms and features, goddess Umā manifested herself in different Yugas for the destruction of the distress of the gods, after assuming different forms and features.

## \|\| 9.45.78 \|\|

*yadicchāvaibhavaṃ sarvaṃ tasyā dehagrahaḥ smṛtaḥ \|*  
*līlayā sāpi bhaktānāṃ guṇavarṇanahetave \|\| 78 \|\|*

78\. Thus I have described to you her manifestation in bodily form whose wish alone manifests as everything efficaciously. Thus she conducts her sports in that way and thereby gives chance to her devotees to describe her attributes.

## Chapter 46 - Incarnation of Mahālakṣmī

## \|\| 9.46.1 \|\|

*ṛṣiruvāca \|*  
*āsīdraṃbhāsuro nāma daityavaṃśaśiromaṇiḥ \|*  
*tasmājjāto mahātejā mahiṣo nāma dānavaḥ \|\| 1 \|\|*

The sage said:—

1\. There was a demon Rambha who was the crest-jewel of the race of Daityas. From him was born Mahiṣa the Dānava of great splendour.

## \|\| 9.46.2 \|\|

*sa saṃgrāme surānsarvānnirjitya danujādhipaḥ \|*  
*cakāra rājyaṃ svarloke mahendrāsanasaṃsthitaḥ \|\| 2 \|\|*

2\. That king of Dānavas defeated all the gods in battle and ruled over the kingdom of heaven seated on the throne of lord Indra.

## \|\| 9.46.3 \|\|

*parājitāstato devā brahmāṇaṃ śaraṇaṃ yayuḥ \|*  
*brahmāpi tānsamādāya yayau yatra vṛṣākapī \|\| 3 \|\|*

3\. The defeated gods sought refuge in Brahmā. With them Brahmā went to the place where Viṣṇu and Śiva were present.

## \|\| 9.46.4 \|\|

*tatra gatvā surāssarve natvā śaṃkarakeśavau \|*  
*svavṛttaṃ kathāyāmāsuryathāvadanupūrvaśaḥ \|\| 4 \|\|*

4\. After reaching there and bowing to Śivā and Viṣṇu the gods narrated all their woeful tale in the proper order.

## \|\| 9.46.5 \|\|

*bhagavantau vayaṃ sarve mahiṣeṇa durātmanā \|*  
*ujjāsitāśca svarlokānnirjitya samarāṃgaṇe \|\| 5 \|\|*

5\. O lords, we are harassed by the wicked Mahiṣa. We have been ousted from heaven after our defeat in the battle-field.

## \|\| 9.46.6 \|\|

*bhramāmo martyaloke'sminna labhemahi śaṃ kvacit \|*  
*kāṃ kāṃ na durdaśāṃ nītā devā indrapurogamāḥ \|\| 6 \|\|*

6\. We are wandering in this world of mortals without peace or happiness anywhere. O what pitiable plights we—Indra and others—have been led to!

## \|\| 9.46.7-8 \|\|

*sūryācandramasau pāśī kubero yama eva ca \|*  
*indrāgnivātagandharvā vidyādharasucāraṇāḥ \|\| 7 \|\|*

*eteṣāmapareṣāṃ ca vidheyaṃ karma sosuraḥ \|*  
*svayaṃ karoti pāpātmā daityapakṣa bhayaṃkara \|\| 8 \|\|*

7-8. The sinful Asura who has offered freedom from fear to the partisans of the Daityas, himself performs the ordained task of these—viz, the sun, the moon, Varuṇa, Kubera, Yama, Indra, Agni, wind, the Gandharvas, Vidyādharas and the good Cāraṇas and others as well.

## \|\| 9.46.9 \|\|

*tasmāccharaṇamāpannāndevānnastrātumarhathaḥ \|*  
*vadhopāyaṃ ca tasyāśu cintayethāṃ yuvāṃ prabhū \|\| 9 \|\|*

9\. Hence it behoves you to save us the gods who have sought refuge in you. O lords, please think of the means of killing him immediately.

## \|\| 9.46.10 \|\|

*iti devavacaḥ śrutvā dāmodarasatīśvarau \|*  
*cakratuḥ paramaṃ kopaṃ roṣāghūrṇitalocanau \|\| 10 \|\|*

10\. On hearing the gods, Viṣṇu and Śiva were very angry. Their eyes rolled through anger.

## \|\| 9.46.11 \|\|

*tatotikopapūrṇasya viṣṇośśaṃbhośca vaktrataḥ \|*  
*tathānyeṣāṃ ca devānāṃ śarīrānnirgataṃ mahaḥ \|\| 11 \|\|*

11\. Then a great splendour issued forth from the mouths of the infuriated Viṣṇu and Śiva and from the bodies of other gods.

## \|\| 9.46.12 \|\|

*atīva mahasaḥ puṃjaṃ jvalantaṃ daśadikṣu ca \|*  
*apaśyaṃstridaśāssarve durgā dhyānaparāyaṇāḥ \|\| 12 \|\|*

12\. The gods who were devoted to meditation on Durgā saw the mass of splendour blazing in all the ten directions.

## \|\| 9.46.13 \|\|

*sarvadevaśarīrotthaṃ tejastadatibhīṣaṇam \|*  
*saṃghībhūyābhavannārī sākṣānmahiṣamardinī \|\| 13 \|\|*

13\. That highly terrible splendour issuing forth from the bodies of the gods joined together and became a woman Mahiṣamardinī herself.

## \|\| 9.46.14 \|\|

*śaṃbhutejasa utpannaṃ mukhamasyāḥ subhāsvaram \|*  
*yāmyena bālā abhavanvaiṣṇavena ca bāhavaḥ \|\| 14 \|\|*

14\. Her dazzlingly brilliant face constituted the splendour of Śiva, the hairs the splendour of Yama and the arms the splendour of Viṣṇu.

## \|\| 9.46.15-18 \|\|

*candramastejasā tasyāḥ stanayugmaṃ vyajāyata \|*  
*madhyame ndreṇa jaṃghorū vāruṇena babhūvatuḥ \|\| 15 \|\|*

*bhūtejasā nitaṃbobhūdbrāhmeṇa caraṇadvayam \|*  
*ārkeṇa caraṇāṃgulyaḥ karāṃgulyaśca vāsavāt \|\| 16 \|\|*

*kuberatejasā nāsā radanāśca prajāpateḥ \|*  
*pāvakīyena nayanatrayaṃ sāndhyena bhrūdvayam \|\| 17 \|\|*

*ānilena śravodvandvaṃ tathānyeṣāṃ svarokasām \|*  
*tejasāṃ saṃbhavaḥ padmālayā sā parameśvarī \|\| 18 \|\|*

15-18. Her breasts were formulated by the splendour of the moon; the waist by that of Indra; the calves and thighs by that of Varuṇa; the hips by that of the earth; the feet by that of Brahmā; the toes by that of the sun; the fingers by that of Indra; the nose by that of Kubera; the teeth by that of the patriarch; the eyes by that of fire; the eyebrows by that of the dusk; the ears by that of the wind, her other limbs by that of other heaven-dwellers. Thus the goddess manifested herself on the lotus-seat.

## \|\| 9.46.19 \|\|

*tato nikhiladevānāṃ tejorāśisamudbhavām \|*  
*tāmālokya surāssarve paraṃ harṣaṃ prapedire \|\| 19 \|\|*

19\. On seeing her thus formulated by the mass of splendour of the gods, they attained great delight.

## \|\| 9.46.20 \|\|

*nirāyudhāṃ ca tāṃ dṛṣṭvā brahmādyāstridiveśvarāḥ \|*  
*sāyudhāntāṃ śivāṃ kartuṃ manaḥ sandadhire surāḥ \|\| 20 \|\|*

20\. Observing that she had no weapons, Brahmā and other gods decided to make her fully equipped with weapons.

## \|\| 9.46.21 \|\|

*tataḥ śūlaṃ maheśāno maheśānyai samarpayata \|*  
*cakraṃ ca kṛṣṇo bhagavāñcchaṃkhaṃ pāśaṃ ca pāśabhṛta \|\| 21 \|\|*

21\. Then lord Śiva offered her a spear; Lord Kṛṣṇa gave her a discus; Varuṇa conch and noose.

## \|\| 9.46.22-23 \|\|

*śaktiṃ hutāśano'yacchanmārutaścāpameva ca \|\| \|*  
*bāṇapūrṇeṣudhī caiva vajraghaṇṭe śacīpatiḥ \|\| 22 \|\|*

*yamo dadau kāladaṇḍamakṣamālāṃ prajāpatiḥ \|*  
*brahmā kamaṇḍaluṃ prādādromaraśmīndivākaraḥ \|\| 23 \|\|*

22-23. The fire-god gave her Śakti; the wind-god a bow arid a quiver full of arrows; Indra the thunderbolt and a bell; Yama a big staff; Prajāpati a garland of beads; Brahma a water-pot and the sun the lustrous hair.

## \|\| 9.46.24-27 \|\|

*kālaḥ khaḍgandadau tasyai phalakaṃ ca samujvalam \|*  
*kṣīrābdhī ruciraṃ hāramajare ca tathāmbare \|\| 24 \|\|*

*cūḍāmaṇiṃ kuṇḍale ca kaṭakāni tathaiva ca \|*  
*arddhacandraṃ ca keyūrānnūpurau ca manoharo \|\| 25 \|\|*

*graiveyakamaṃgulīṣu samastāsvaṃgulīyakam \|*  
*viśvakarmā ca paraśuṃ dadau tasyai manoharam \|\| 26 \|\|*

*astrāṇyanekāni tathābhedyaṃ caiva tanucchadam \|*  
*suramyasarasāṃ mālāṃ paṅkajaṃ cāmbudhirdadau \|\| 27 \|\|*

24-27. Kāla gave her a sword and a shining shield, the milk ocean a beautiful necklace, two fresh pieces of cloth, the crest-jewel, ear-rings, bangles, crescent-shaped ornaments, beautiful anklets, shoulder-pieces, a necklace and finger-rings. Visvakarman gave her a beautiful axe and the ocean various weapons, an impenetrable armour, a beautiful garland and a lotus.

## \|\| 9.46.28-29 \|\|

*dadau siṃhaṃ ca himavānratnāni vividhāni ca \|*  
*surayā pūritaṃ pātraṃ kubero'syai samarpayat \|\| 28 \|\|*

*śeṣaśca bhogināṃ netā vicitrara canāñcitam \|*  
*dadau tasyai nāgahāraṃ nānāstramaṇiguṃphitam \|\| 29 \|\|*

28-29. Himavat gave her a lion and gems of various sorts. Kubera a vessel filled with wine. Śeṣa a Nāgahāra of wonderful workmanship, studded with various precious gems.

## \|\| 9.46.30 \|\|

*etaiścānyaissurairdevī bhūṣaṇairāyudhaistathā \|*  
*satkṛtoccairnanādāsau sāṭṭahāsaṃ punaḥpunaḥ \|\| 30 \|\|*

30\. Thus honoured, the goddesss with these and other similar objects, ornaments, weapons etc. shouted loudly again and again.

## \|\| 9.46.31 \|\|

*tasyā bhīṣaṇanādena pūritā ca nabhaḥsthalī \|*  
*pratiśabdo mahānāsīccukṣubhe bhuvanatrayam \|\| 31 \|\|*

31\. The sky was filled with her terrible noise that echoed tremendously and made the three worlds agitated.

## \|\| 9.46.32 \|\|

*celuḥ samudrāścatvāro vasudhā ca cacāla ha \|*  
*jayaśabdastato devairakāri mahiṣārditaiḥ \|\| 32 \|\|*

32\. The four oceans shook and rolled. The earth quaked. Cries of victory were shouted by “the gods harassed by Mahiṣa.

## \|\| 9.46.33 \|\|

*tato'mbikāṃ parāṃ śaktiṃ mahālakṣmīsvarūpiṇīm \|*  
*tuṣṭuvuste surāssarve bhaktigadgadayā girā \|\| 33 \|\|*

33\. Then the gods eulogised the great Śakti, Ambikā in the form of Mahālakṣmī with words choked with devotion.

## \|\| 9.46.34 \|\|

*lokaṃ saṃkṣubdhamālokya devatāparipanthinaḥ \|*  
*sannaddhasainikāste ca samuttasthurudāyudhāḥ \|\| 34 \|\|*

34\. On seeing the world agitated, the enemies of the gods got up with uplifted arms and with their armies ready for war.

## \|\| 9.46.35 \|\|

*mahiṣo'pi ca taṃ śabdamabhyadhāvadruṣānvitaḥ \|*  
*sa dadarśa tato devīṃ vyāptalokatrayāṃ rucā \|\| 35 \|\|*

35\. The infuriated Mahiṣa rushed at the direction of the sound and saw the goddess who had pervaded the three worlds with her beautiful lustre.

## \|\| 9.46.36 \|\|

*etasminnantare tatra mahiṣāsurapālitāḥ \|*  
*samājagmurmahāvīrāḥ koṭiśo dhṛtahetayaḥ \|\| 36 \|\|*

36\. In the meantime, crores of great heroes led by Mahiṣāsura came there with weapons..

## \|\| 9.46.37-38 \|\|

*cikṣuraścāmarodagrau karāloddhatabāṣkalāḥ \|*  
*tāmrogrāsyogravīryāśca biḍālo'ndhaka eva ca \|\| 37 \|\|*

*durdharo durmukhaścaiva trinetraśca mahāhanuḥ \|*  
*ete cānye ca bahavaḥ śūrā yuddhaviśā radāḥ \|\| 38 \|\|*

37-38. Cikṣura, Cāmara, Udagra, Karāla, Uddhaṭa, Bāṣkala; Tāmra, Ugrāsya, Ugravīrya, Biḍāla, Andhaka, Durdhara, Durmukha, Trinetra Mahāhanu—these and many others, heroic and efficient in battle, came there.

## \|\| 9.46.39 \|\|

*yuyudhuḥ samare devyā saha śastrāstrapāragāḥ \|*  
*itthaṃ kālo vyatīyāya yudhyatorbhīṣaṇastayoḥ \|\| 39 \|\|*

39\. As they, the masters of weapons and missiles, fought with the goddess in the battle a terribly long time elapsed.

## \|\| 9.46.40 \|\|

*arivargakarakṣiptā nānāśastrāstrarāśayaḥ \|*  
*mahāmāyāprabhāveṇa viphalā abhavan kṣaṇāt \|\| 40 \|\|*
